The adapter reads `ended('unavailable')` on a placed session as "not live": `remote.session.stream` becomes `idle`, and it subscribes again with O9's backoff (1 s doubling to 30 s) while the tab is open, so a session resumed by this app's send, by the machine's own app or by anyone else streams within the backoff; the pane meanwhile shows the history, which `session.events` answers for any session with a state file. Rejected: an endpoint change to hold a stream for a session with no host row (the shared catch-up's rule is the front's too), and polling the record.

*One subscription per session, `full`, from the start.* The stream's parts and events become the events the renderer already consumes, emitted with `emitFrom(machine, …)` so the renderer lifts them to the machine (`src/mawsd/front-streams.ts`'s `eventOf` is the mapping to copy): `status` → `session.status`; `queue` → `session.queue`; `catalog` → `session.catalogChanged`; `pendingItems` → `attention.changed {appSessionId, items}` for that session, the broker items turned into attention items the way main's local attention service turns them (the renderer never reads `session.brokerItems`); `ev` → `session.eventBatch`, coalesced per macrotask into one batch of at most 500 events. The first `subbed` of a subscription says nothing to the renderer. A later `subbed {replay}` says nothing either. A later `subbed {snapshot}` (a relaunch: the session was resumed, or `/clear`) and a `subbed {missing}` (a range the journal lost) each emit `remote.gap {appSessionId, launchId}` before the parts that follow, and the renderer draws the gap bar at that point of the transcript; it is the one signal lane B has for "events between were not delivered", and the local path's `historyUnavailable` draws the same bar (below). An `ev` body `{omitted: 'too-large'}` is a gap too. Rejected: re-reading the transcript from the journal at each `snapshot` (the renderer pages history itself through `session.events` over the link, as locally; a re-read would double what it holds).

*The record.* At subscribe, the adapter reads `session.get {appSessionId}` over the link (§L; a `view` call) and emits it as `session.changed {record, cause: 'live', revision}` for the machine, so the sessions store holds a record under the address and `SessionPane` takes its started branch; P5 Decision Log 101 names this switch from the listed row to the record. A `session.get` that answers null or `grade` (a fresh session whose engine hasn't reported its id yet, tracker row "A fresh lane B session has no `engineSessionId`") is retried at 0.5, 1, 2 and 4 s with the pane reading "Opening on `<machine>`…"; after the last retry the pane reads "`<machine>` can't place this session" with Try again (`remote.session`'s `stream: 'ended'`, `reason`), the row stays listed, and a later click tries again. The record stays after the tab closes. The sidebar draws one row per address: a session with a record is its record row, and its listed row is hidden (O8 M2's listed rows are their own node kind; the executor dedups where the tree is built). Rejected: a pane over the listed row alone with a parallel state as O9's `LaneAState` (every panel tool reads `SessionRecord | null` for its directory and worktree, which M4's diff, files and terminal need).

*Acts.* The adapter takes, for an RL2 machine, every act §L offers and builds it in one of two shapes. An **engine-host act** (`session.send`, `steer`, `interrupt`, `answer`, the queue and the chips) is `link.act({action, session: <bare appSessionId>, context: {lane: 'B', launchId, engineSessionId}, args, executor: 'engine-host', requestDigest?})`: the context from the last `session.status` the subscription delivered; `args` is the command's input with its ids lowered to bare ids; `requestDigest` for `session.answer` from `requestDigestOf(appSessionId, request)` over the pending request the adapter reads with `session.pendingRequest` (also what answers the renderer's `attention.request`, below). A **`machine` act** (`session.acquire` here; M4's writes, uploads and `session.spawn`) carries neither `session` nor `context` (the link and `isAction` refuse a `session` without a context, and the services' ledger claims no launch): the endpoint places it by `args.appSessionId` (or the machine), so it works on a released session as on a live one. An engine-host act on a session with no status yet (released, or opening) is not refused: the adapter first sends `session.acquire` if the renderer did not (the composer's `sendHumanPrompt` does when the record has no ready process), waits for the first status the resumed launch delivers on the subscription, and seals the act with it, all under the same 60 s bound as a send's wait; an act on a session with no open tab is refused `internal {refusal: 'not-open'}` with "open the session to drive it" (O9's `NOT_OPEN`). `session.update` is not offered: its reader fields (unread, last seen, pinned) are the owner's on the machine's index until O8 M6 keys them per member (P5 Decision Log 36), so a remote session's read marks stay in this app's memory and the executor confirms the renderer tolerates the refusal, as for drafts. Sends are serialized per session as O9's are: one `session.send` or `steer` in flight, the next created only at the outcome or at the pending bound (below), at most 8 waiting; a `session.interrupt` or `session.answer` never waits behind a send. Step-up is the link's `if-asked`. Rejected: building acts in `dispatchOverLink` from the registry alone, which has no status to fill the context from; a context on `machine` acts (refused as malformed on both sides).

*Outcomes as results* (`remote-outcomes.ts`). `done {result}` is the command's output, checked with the link surface's codec as `dispatchOverLink` checks a call's; `failed {error}` passes the error through as the command's; `refused {reason}` maps by §F (`offline` → `host_unavailable` with the message "Not sent: `<machine>` is offline", which the composer shows with Send again, A4; `too-large` → `invalid_input` "an image larger than 2.5 MiB goes to a session on another machine as a file"); `not-sent` → `host_unavailable` "not sent"; `unknown {reason}` → `timeout` with "`<machine>` took the message but its answer was lost; check the transcript". A `session.send` or `steer` answers at its outcome, and never `queued` before it; a never-sent act's hold (`ACT_FRESH_MS`, 60 s) ends it `refused offline`, while an act the channel carried and then lost is ambiguous and is queried by the link's rule, which can take as long as RL4's horizon: so the adapter answers the renderer at `SEND_PENDING_MS` (60 s) at the latest, `timeout` with "`<machine>` took the message but hasn't confirmed it; check the transcript", keeps the act querying in the background (its late outcome is logged), and lets the next waiting send go. The composer shows "Sending to `<machine>`…" on the pending submission, clears the draft only on `sent` or `queued`, and on any error keeps the text with Send again, as it does for a local `host_unavailable`; the renderer's invoke for these names to an RL2 machine carries no deadline shorter than that bound (the executor confirms `src/renderer/src/ipc/client.ts` and the router's remote path impose none, else lifts it for them). Rejected: answering `queued` at 5 s while the act goes on (O9's `sendAnswerMs`), which the composer reads as the engine's queue entry and clears the draft on; turning an ambiguous act into "not sent" (RL4: only a `not-received` query may).

*Images.* The composer sends an image inline (a base64 block in `session.send`'s content) when the sealed act stays under `MESSAGE_MAX_BYTES` less 64 KiB of headroom (about 2.9 MiB of image after the composer's 1568 px downscale, which is almost every pasted screenshot); the link refuses an act whose sealed JSON would exceed the limit locally, `refused {reason: 'too-large'}`, never sending it (today `Channel.send` rejects it and nothing reads that rejection: a case in `acts.test.ts`); the composer pre-checks the bound for a remote session and refuses a larger image with the words above until M4 uploads it as a file. Rejected: sending and reading the channel's rejection (a closed act with no answer).

*Reads.* A `list`- or `view`-graded command for an RL2 machine goes as a call through the router's existing path (`dispatchOverLink`), so the pane's reads need only their offer rows (§L): `session.events` (history, `before`, `seek`, `agentId`), `session.appEvents`, `session.status`, `session.catalog`, `session.queue.list`, `session.get`, `session.getContextUsage`, `session.getUsage`, `session.getWorkspaceDiff`, `session.getSettings`, `session.listModels`, `session.listPermissionRules`, `session.getStatus`, `session.mcpStatus`, `session.getSessionCost`, `session.history.list`. Two reads the pane makes have no row and get substitutes in the adapter: `attention.request {appSessionId, requestId}` is answered from `session.pendingRequest` over the link as `{event}`, the shaping `src/services/attention/service.ts`'s `attention.request` handler does (five lines, lifted to a shared function or copied); `engine.models` (graded `spawn`, machine-wide) is answered from `session.listModels` for the session the composer is in. Drafts: `session.drafts.get` and `.set` are not offered (a draft on another machine's index is per member, O8 M6), so the composer keeps a remote session's draft in memory for the app's run and the executor confirms the composer tolerates the refused read. `fs.agentEvents` (`view`, by `appSessionId`) is offered in M4; a spilled result on a remote session shows its placeholder (`fs.readSpill` is not offered: §L).

*The capability list* (RL7's per-session half; O10 leaves it to O7; O9's residue 8). The adapter publishes `remote.session {address, link, stream, controls, reason}` (§K) at each change: `link` as O9's `linkView` (the link's state, carrier, refusal, this member, null before the first `caps`, and its machine rights); `stream` is `opening`, `idle`, `live`, `paused` (`paused('offline')` or `no-app`), `gap` or `ended`; `controls` is the lane B port's table with the remote overlay: closed with a reason are `media` beyond an inline image that fits until M4 ("files to a session on another machine wait for uploads", `MEDIA_GAP`'s pattern), `take-over` and `reopen` (not offered remotely), `rewind` and `rewind-files` until M4's files panel can show their effect, `subagent-rows` (the remote agents feed was O9's gap, kept; lifted for lane B by PR #501, P5 Decision Log 236), and every control while `reason` stands: the device `refused`, the link `closed`, or the session unplaced (no record). A record is enough: an idle session's controls are open, and an act that needs a status gets one through the resume (above). A link that is `down` or reconnecting closes nothing: `send`, `steer`, `interrupt` and `answer` stay available and their acts wait under the link's hold (A4: 60 s, then "Not sent"), the pending submission reading "Sending to `<machine>`…"; the renderer's `connectShell` forgets a departed machine's engine statuses, so the send path reads the record, not a status, to decide whether to act. (As built, 2026-10-31: `connectShell` keeps the last status of a lane B session main still holds through a link drop, forgetting it when main reports the stream `idle` or `ended`, so Stop and Escape stay on a running turn; a remote send never acquires first and makes one `session.send` or `session.steer`, main resuming under the send's one bound.) The renderer's `laneBControls` takes the overlay for a remote session (`controls-for.ts` chooses it when the record's machine has a `remote.session` state). The member's rank on the session is not computed here (tracker row "Main cannot know a member's rank on a remote session"): the endpoint is the authority, and a `refused grade` is shown on the control that asked, as lane A shows it.

*Pending items in the pane.* The `pendingItems` part reaches the attention store as `attention.changed` for that session only (above), so the pane's docked card and the session's row show the request; Needs you, the badge and ⌘⇧J count it once the record exists (the derive requires a record). Machine-wide attention, with no tab open, is M6's.

*The gap bar.* One renderer component, `GapRow`, draws "Earlier history isn't available" as a row of the transcript, from two sources: `remote.gap` (above), and a `session.events` page that answered `historyUnavailable: true` (the local path, P5 Decision Log 77's tracker row). `TranscriptEntry` gains a `gap` kind at the position the signal names (the launch's first event for a `snapshot`, the cursor for a `missing`, the page's start for `historyUnavailable`); `RemoteTranscriptSlot`'s `older-failed` bar is the precedent for its look. Rejected: a toast (the gap is positional).

*The carrier hover* (A2, P5 Decision Log 108). `useMachineMark` gains `carrier` and `refusal` from `MachineState`; `SessionHoverCard` and `ListedHoverCard` show "direct (tailnet)" for `direct`, "via the hub" for `relay`, nothing for `socket` or local. A machine whose `refusal` is `removed` greys its rows with "This Mac is no longer allowed on `<name>`" in place of "machine offline". "Hub unreachable" is drawn once, in the sidebar's org header, from `org.status`'s `hubAccess: 'unreachable'` while any RL2 machine is `up` (P5 §3's hub-offline, channel-up); the chip itself shows no carrier.

*The step-up sheet* (P5 Decision Log 126; §P). `sheetConfirmation` is a `Confirm` (`src/main/device/step-up.ts`) that shows the prompt's `text` in a macOS sheet on the focused window (`dialog.showMessageBox` with `message: summary`, `detail: text`, buttons Continue and Cancel, Cancel the cancel button and Escape) and, on Continue, runs `promptTouchIDConfirmation`'s prompt with the summary; Cancel at the sheet resolves `{ok: false, reason: 'cancelled'}` and never prompts Touch ID; with no window, the sheet is a window-less message box. `src/main/index.ts` passes it to both `createStepUp` calls (the dev and the production session) and to `createStateSigner` (O8 M3's org-state signing: its summary and text are the signer's, so the weekly re-sign and a revocation show their words). A harness run (`MAWS_E2E=1`, unpackaged) with `MAWS_E2E_STEP_UP=accept` takes a confirmation that resolves `{ok: true}` without a sheet or Touch ID, so `e2e:remote`'s spawn (M4) runs unattended; the signature and the endpoint's verification stay real, and the seam is refused outside `MAWS_E2E` as the other e2e seams are. Rejected: an in-app sheet in the renderer (a security confirmation that must show even when no page is loaded, and a second surface to keep in step with main's words; A7 asks the owner whether the native sheet's look is acceptable).

*The `machine` executor on RL2.* `session.acquire` is a `machine` act (O3-D's ledger, §L), the first act O7 sends to that executor, so M2 adds `machine` to org-protocol's `Executor` and `FROM_DEVICE.query`, behind the capability `rl2.executor.machine` (§M): the endpoint lists it in `h2`, the device in `h1`, and the link refuses an act for an executor the peer did not list, locally, `refused {reason: 'not-offered'}`, since the peer would close the channel on the query it would later need. `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` moves to 1 at this landing (master's plus one). Rejected: waiting for M4 (a released remote session could not be resumed by a send in M2).

*The e2e scene* (§Q; the tracker row "No e2e group drives main's RL2 link in the built app"). Profile B is a private `mawsd` as `e2e:machines` starts one, with `MAWS_ENDPOINT_DIRECT=1`, a fake tailnet interface table (`src/mawsd/process-endpoint.test.ts`'s `--require` pattern, through `NODE_OPTIONS`), enrolled in a local org (`e2e/support/local-org.ts`'s `startLocalOrg().enroll`); profile A is the app under test, signed in through `MAWS_DEV_ORG`, whose directory the scene writes from A's own device keys (`loadDeviceKeys` with `excludeFromBackup` stubbed) listed in the org by the local hub's admin. The relay is the local hub; the direct carrier is a `directMap` from B's advertised tailnet address to its loopback port, read once at the dev session's load (the dev session exposes it, `src/main/index.ts` passes it to `startMachines` as `linkDeps.dialDirect`, and `src/main/machines/index.ts` hands `linkDeps` to `createRemoteSource`, which already takes them), so the spec launches A twice: without the map, proving the relay, then with it, proving direct. B's lane B records, and so its listed rows at the hub and in A's sidebar, come the way they do in production, from sminos's `member` frames on RL10: the scene plays sminos with the endpoint harness's fake (`test/endpoint/rl10/fake-sminos.ts`, which imports only `node:net` and `desk-hub/protocol`), bundled for Playwright as `local-org.ts` bundles `local-hub.ts`, connected to B's `rl10.json` socket, restating one `member` frame per session the scene starts (owners `[A's member]`, access `listed`, the engine session id read over B's `machine.sock`) and the project's presence. B's session runs the replay engine with `e2e:c7`'s fixtures (sends, a permission, a question), started over B's `machine.sock` by the spec. One thing is dev-only: `directMap` in `session.json`, honoured by `dev-session.ts` only in an unpackaged build. In production the same frames come from sminos's lane B observer (O12 item 7), which M7's live runs need on the mini and the VM (open question A11). Rejected: a fake endpoint in the spec's process in place of B's `mawsd` (it proves the app, not the machine, and M7's live run is the only other end-to-end); an `authorization.records` seam in `mawsd` under `MAWS_E2E` (it would authorize a session but list none: `endpoint.listings` and the hub's listings read `rl10.members.sessions()` alone, so A would have no row to click).

*What `connectShell` does for an RL2 machine* is unchanged: no snapshot (O8 M2 skips listed machines), rows from listings; the inferred race where `machines.changed` precedes `org.rows` and the five reads fail softly is harmless (each store starts empty and the rows load after) and is not changed here.

**Does not touch.** `git.*`, `files.*`, `scm.*`, `pty.*`, uploads, `session.spawn` and the machine picker (M3, M4); attention beyond a subscribed session, search across machines (M6); the client port's routing over RL2, artifacts, documents (M5); the link's carriers, acts and subscriptions beyond `peerCapabilities()`, the executor check and the size check; O8's rows code and `org.*`; the engine host, the pty host, `src/services/` (the `session.get` and `session.acquire` handlers exist); any `WIRE_MINOR` bump (nothing on the manifest changes: `remote.*` is main-owned).

**Proves.** Acceptance 2's conversation half (stream, send, steer, interrupt, approve and deny, answer a question, take an image), 5, 8, 10 and 11.
- **`src/main/lane-b/*.test.ts`** (fakes; no network): the tabs-to-subscriptions rule with the linger; each part's event and the batching of `ev`s; `remote.gap` at a later `snapshot`, a `missing` and an `omitted` body; the record's retry and its terminal state; an `ended('unavailable')` on a placed session retried with backoff, the stream `idle` meanwhile; the two act shapes (an engine-host act's context and digest; a `machine` act with no `session` and no `context`); a send on a session with no status preceded by `session.acquire` and sealed with the resumed launch's status; send serialization and the pending bound; every outcome's mapping, A4's, the pending bound's and the image's copy included; the capability overlay with a record and no status (sends available), the link `down` (sends available) and `refused` (closed), and `remote.session` at each change; the substitutes for `attention.request` and `engine.models`.
- **`src/main/machines/remote/acts.test.ts`**, extended: an act over `MESSAGE_MAX_BYTES` refused `too-large` locally, never sent.
- **`src/main/device/step-up-sheet.test.ts`**: the sheet shows the full text before any Touch ID; Cancel never prompts; Continue prompts with the summary; the state signer takes the same confirmation; the harness confirmation resolves without either, and is refused outside `MAWS_E2E`.
- **`test/endpoint/targets.test.ts`** (new): a lane B session the spine's record lists but the engine host holds no row for is placed (the parked-host case); one neither knows is `unknown`; a `ClientRef` per channel reaching `dispatch` and ended at the channel's close.
- **`test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`** (`node-timing`; the endpoint harness with `FakeEngineHost` and the fake spine, the link with the profile's real keys, the adapter through `routeLaneB`, as `test/lane-a/remote.test.ts` is built): the pane's opening reads and the stream, with every event's `(launchId, seq, idx)` checked against the fake host; a released session (a record, no host row) opened, its history read, its subscription ended `unavailable` by the real `SessionStreams` and retried, then resumed by a send's `session.acquire` and streaming the new launch; a send that runs once across a forced drop and answers only at its outcome; a send during a drop longer than the hold answering `host_unavailable` with A4's words and nothing run; a send the channel carried and lost, with no channel back within the bound, answering `timeout` with the pending words while the link's query later settles it; an answer whose digest the host checks; an interrupt that does not wait behind a queued send; a 2 MiB image inline and a 4 MiB image refused locally; `session.acquire` refused locally `not-offered` against a harness endpoint that lists no `rl2.executor.machine`; a relaunch's `snapshot` and a lost range's `missing` each producing one `remote.gap`; two devices on one session, an answer from one settling the other's `pendingItems` (acceptance 5); a `removed` close ending the stream and greying.
- **`e2e:remote`** (`e2e/remote.spec.ts`, §Q): from A, B's listed row (listed through the fake sminos) opens; the transcript streams the replay's deltas; a send appears in B's transcript (read over B's `machine.sock`); a permission card from the fixture answers; a pasted image reaches B's transcript; the hover reads "via the hub" in the first launch and "direct (tailnet)" in the second; the machine's link `up` on each carrier in `machines.list`. No wall-clock bound beyond Playwright's own timeouts; the measured numbers are M7's.
- Renderer unit tests (vitest's `renderer` project): `GapRow` from both sources; the hover's three texts; "Hub unreachable" drawn once from `hubAccess: 'unreachable'` with an RL2 machine `up`, and not otherwise; "This Mac is no longer allowed on `<machine>`" on a `refusal: 'removed'`; the controls overlay's reasons; one row per address.

### M4 — Diff, files, uploads and spawn anywhere

The panel's diff, file tree and editor work for a remote session through that machine's checkout services (O3-E's `git.*`, `scm.*`, `files.*` in `mawsd`) and follow its changes; a dropped file lands in the session's attachments directory on its machine before the message goes; and "+ Session" gains the machine menu (A5), so a lane B session starts on the mini or the VM with the sheet and Touch ID.

**At its end.**
- In a remote session's pane the Files tool lists and watches the checkout, the viewer opens a file and the editor saves it, on a live or an idle session; an edit made on the machine shows in the tree, the git status and the diff without a manual refresh, including one made while the link was down; the SCM tool stages, commits, pushes and opens a pull request; the review tool holds diffs. Reveal in Finder and Open externally are greyed with "on `<machine>`" (they would open on the machine, not here; M5 routes them if a reader appears).
- A file dropped into a remote session's composer uploads to `<working directory>/.claude/desk-uploads/<appSessionId>/` on the machine, with a progress mark on the chip, and the message carries the machine's path as a mention; a 32 MiB PDF arrives whole. Images still go inline when they fit (M2); a larger image uploads like a file.
- "+ Session" shows a machine menu beside the project picker, defaulting to this Mac and listing the machines where the project has a checkout; one line under it reads "Lane B on `<machine>` · `<machine>`'s inference route". Send asks Touch ID at the sheet (a spawn always needs step-up) and the new session is in the sidebar under its machine within 5 s of the engine's first event, open in the pane; a first message with attachments reaches the new session with their paths on the machine.
- The requester and the signed spawn are recorded on the session for O12's observer to claim as its owner and proof.
- A remote session's history opens with its rows however large it is (acceptance 12): `session.events` takes an optional byte budget and answers a continuation cursor, so main's paged read (`src/main/lane-b/remote-history.ts`) pages a parked session's disk window and an older `before` page within the channel's limit, and the gap bar M2 draws for such a session is no longer needed.

**Touches.**
- New: `src/services/sessions/spawn.ts` (`session.spawn`'s handler), `src/endpoint/plane-feeds.ts` (the `checkout` feed; §R), `src/main/lane-b/remote-checkout.ts` (the feed's events, the watch replay and invalidation), `src/renderer/src/composer/frame/MachineMenu.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/composer/editor/remote-uploads.ts`, tests; `test/endpoint/checkout-rule.test.ts`, `test/endpoint/feeds.test.ts`.
- Changed: `src/endpoint/offers.ts` (§L's M4 rows), `src/endpoint/targets.ts`, `authorize.ts` and `gate.ts` (the `checkout` target rule; `AuthorizeRequest` gains `session`; step 6's spawn guard over every `spawn`-graded row with the `machine` target), `src/endpoint/subscriptions.ts` (a `feed` subscription's plane), `src/endpoint/capabilities.ts` and `src/main/machines/remote/capabilities.ts` (`rl2.call-session`, `rl2.feeds`), `apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts` and `channel.ts` (`call.session?`; `sub.feed?`; `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor`), `src/preload/bridge.ts`, `src/main/ipc/router.ts` and `src/renderer/src/ipc/client.ts` (the session a path-class call is made from, carried to main: below), `src/main/machines/remote/link.ts` and `subs.ts` (`call` takes an optional `session`; `subscribe` takes a feed), `src/main/machines/remote/dispatch.ts` (a path-only command of an RL2 machine carries `MainContext.session` as `call.session`), `src/main/lane-b/remote.ts` (the M4 acts; uploads in parts; `session.spawn`), `src/shared/ipc/schema/session.ts` (`session.spawn`), `src/shared/graph/session.ts` (`SessionRecordSchema.launchedBy`), `src/shared/ipc/schema/machine.ts` (`MachineOpInputSchema.action?`), `src/shared/ipc/client.ts` (`HandlerContext.op?`), `src/shared/ipc/schema/files.ts` (`files.upload`'s `part`), `src/shared/ipc/grades/session.ts` and `names/session.ts` (`session.spawn`), `src/shared/ipc/routes.ts` (`session.spawn` machine-wide: the renderer passes `{machine}`), `src/services/files/upload.ts` (parts), `src/shared/ipc/schema/session.ts`, `src/engine-host/fs/follow.ts` and `src/shared/graph/history-window.ts` (`session.events`' byte budget and continuation cursor; acceptance 12), `src/main/lane-b/remote-history.ts` (paging with them), `src/services/machine-ops.ts` (the claim and the action into the handler context and the ledger), `src/shared/ipc/wire.ts` (`WIRE_MINOR`), the renderer: `composer/editor/controller.ts` and `paste.ts` (a drop on a remote session), `composer/submit/` (a spawn with attachments), `sessions/connect.ts` and `keymap/actions-runtime.ts` (the machine menu's flow), `composer/frame/ProjectRow.tsx`, `tools/code/**` and `tools/scm/**` (the `{machine, session}` each path-only call carries; the greyed reveal and open), `stores/git.ts`, `tools/code/files/store.ts`, `sessions/useSetupIntent.ts`.

**Interfaces.** Produces §L's M4 rows, §M's `call.session` and `feed`, §O's `session.spawn`, `files.upload` parts, `launchedBy`, `MachineOpInputSchema.action` and `HandlerContext.op`, §R's `checkout` feed, `MainContext.session`. Consumes §J, M2's `ClientRef` per channel, O3-E's `files.upload` and `files.read`, O3-D's `session.get`, the endpoint's `spawnRefusal` and `SPAWN_FIELDS` (`src/endpoint/policy.ts`), `machine.op` (`src/services/machine-ops.ts`), `src/main/sessions/watch-replay.ts`'s invalidation rule.

**Decisions.**

*The checkout rule and its boundary* (O10 left `git.*` and `fs.holders` "until O7 gives them a session or checkout rule"). `scm.*` and `files.*` name their session (`appSessionId`), so the `appSessionId` rule applies as it does to lane B's conversation, and the files service's own allowlist bounds the paths. `git.*`'s reads take `directory` or `repoRoot`, and the git service answers for the *repository* that contains the directory (`git.status` resolves the enclosing repository; `git.diff` runs `--no-relative`; `git.worktree.list` lists every worktree of the repository), so the boundary a remote reader gets is the repository, not the directory. The rule, `checkout`: `call` gains an optional `session: SessionRef` (§M, additive, behind `rl2.call-session`), `gate.ts` passes it in `AuthorizeRequest.session`, the endpoint resolves the session from it as it resolves a subscription's and takes the rank from it, reads the record with `dispatch('machine', 'session.get', {appSessionId})`, resolves each of the record's roots (`directory`, `additionalDirectories`, `worktree.path`) to its repository through `dispatch('machine', 'git.status', {directory: root})`'s `repoRoot` (cached per record revision), and admits the call when the input's `directory` or `repoRoot`, realpathed, is one of those roots or repositories or lies under one; else `no-target`. `git.worktree.status`'s and `.branch`'s second argument is bounded by the service itself (a path or branch it does not list answers null). The boundary is the repository the session's root belongs to, with its worktrees: a `view` holder of a session rooted in a subdirectory reads the repository's status and diff, which the session's transcript already spans; it is a decision for the architect's attention, with the alternative (refusing a repository-wide read for a subdirectory root) rejected because it breaks the diff for every session opened on a subfolder project. The `checkout` feed filters by the same repositories. `fs.agentEvents` takes `appSessionId` and uses that rule (an engine-host read); `fs.readSpill` is not offered: a spill lives in the engine's store, not the checkout, and a session-bound rule for it needs the engine host to attest the spill's session, which no read does today. The session a path-class call is made from reaches main with the call: the preload's third argument, today the machine, becomes `{machine, session?}` (`src/preload/bridge.ts`; main reads both forms), the router puts it on `MainContext.session`, and `dispatch.ts` sends it as `call.session` when the peer lists `rl2.call-session`; the renderer's client fills it from the panel tool's session. Rejected: an `appSessionId` added to every git input (a wire change to every caller for one consumer), and grading the reads at the machine level (`view` on any session would open every checkout).

*Changes cross as a feed, and a reconnect invalidates.* `git.changed`, `files.changed` and `scm.remote.output` are `machine`-owned events, which the endpoint's `onEvent` delivers and plane B drops (it carries engine-host events only), so nothing today carries them to a device. M4 introduces feeds (§R; `sub {feed, session?}` behind `rl2.feeds`, the mechanism M6 reuses for attention): a `checkout` feed per session, authorized at `view` on the session, opened by the adapter beside the session's `full` subscription from M4 on (M2's tabs rule), whose `ev`s are `{name, payload}` for each `files.changed` and `scm.remote.output` naming the session and each `git.changed` whose `repoRoot` is one of the session's repositories; the adapter emits them with `emitFrom(machine, …)`, so the renderer's stores follow as they do locally. Every opening is a `snapshot` with nothing to snap, and a reconnect follows `src/main/sessions/watch-replay.ts`'s two rules: a `git.watch` is replayed by the adapter (it records the live ones) and its answered status emitted as `git.changed`; a `files.watch` lists nothing, so the adapter emits `files.changed {kind: 'invalidated'}` once per live (session, directory) and the renderer's files store re-lists and re-watches, the watches on the machine having ended with the old channel's `ClientRef` (M2), so nothing double-counts. Rejected: polling `git.status` and `files.list` from the adapter (a second mechanism, late and costly); replaying `files.watch` (a pane stale for what changed while disconnected); lane B's cursor space for machine events (they have no `(launchId, seq, idx)`).

*Writes are `machine` acts under the services' ledger.* `files.write`, `files.upload`, `scm.stage`, `unstage`, `discard`, `commit`, `push`, `pr.create` and `remote.cancel` are `machine` acts in M2's second shape (no `session`, no `context`, placed by `args.appSessionId`), so the editor saves to an idle session's checkout without resuming it; their `done.result` is the command's output. `git.worktree.add`, `remove` and `deleteBranch` are not offered: a worktree for a new session is `session.spawn`'s, and a remote cleanup waits for a reader. `files.reveal` and `files.openExternal` are not offered (they act on the asking client through the client port, M5's).

*Uploads in parts.* The channel's message limit is 4 MiB (`MESSAGE_MAX_BYTES`) and an upload may be 32 MiB (`UPLOAD_FILE_MAX_BYTES`), so `files.upload` gains an optional `part: {upload, index, last}` (§O): a call with `part` carries one file's part of at most `REMOTE_UPLOAD_PART_BYTES` (2 MiB decoded), the service stages `<root>/.staging/<upload>` (0600, created exclusively at index 0), appends a part only at its expected index (else `{status: 'refused', reason}`), bounds the whole at 32 MiB, and at `last` moves it to its final `<uploadId>.<n>.<ext>` name and answers its path (a part before the last answers `paths: []`); staging older than a day is swept with the roots. Each part is its own `machine.op` claim, so a reseal after a lost channel answers the stored result and writes nothing twice. The adapter runs one upload at a time per session, parts in order, and the composer's chip shows the part count. A local upload (the renderer on its own machine) keeps the one-call form. Rejected: M3's byte streams for uploads (M4 would then wait on M3), and bounding remote uploads at one message.

*What a drop sends.* On a remote session, `classifyFile`'s three outcomes become: an image that fits inline (M2's bound) goes inline; any other file, a larger image or a PDF included, uploads and is mentioned by the machine's path, relative to the session's working directory as a local path mention is. The engine reads images and PDFs by path.

*`session.spawn`* (§O; O10's "lane B spawn" row, P5's "lane B spawn (constructing a launch spec)"). `session.launch` is already an engine-host command (the launch spec's own, `src/shared/ipc/schema/session.ts`, called by the owner's launches), and retagging it would be a major change, so the remote spawn is a new `machine`-owned command, `session.spawn`, graded `spawn`, whose input is exactly DX6's spawn fields (`checkout`, `worktree?`, `brief?`, `model?`, `effort?`, `permission_mode`) so the endpoint's `spawnRefusal` applies as it does to `sminos.spawn` (the ceiling, a known checkout, no other field); `permission_mode` is required, since `spawnRefusal` reads an absent mode as the ceiling while the machine's `session.create` would seed the user's default, which can be looser: the app sends the composer's mode. Authorization's step 6 runs the spawn guard for every `spawn`-graded row with the `machine` target (today it names `sminos.spawn` alone), after step 2 has held the args to the registry's schema. Its handler in the spine composes what the renderer does today: `session.create` with the project of `checkout` (a project row whose directory is the checkout) and the location (`directory`, or a new worktree named `worktree` on a new branch of that name from HEAD), `session.intent.patch` with the model, effort and the mode as sent, and `session.start` with the brief as the first message (or none), on the machine's own settings (the route, the launch profile); it answers `{status: 'ok', appSessionId}` once the start is `launching`. A checkout the machine lists but has not trusted answers `{status: 'refused', reason: 'untrusted'}` ("trust it on `<machine>` first"). It is claimed by `machine.op` like any `machine` act, so a replayed spawn answers the first one's id. The claim reaches the handler: `machine.op` gains an optional `action` (the sealed action as the device sent it, step-up signature included), the ledger stores it beside the claim, and `machineOps.op` runs the handler with `HandlerContext.op = {opId, opCreated, member, device, fingerprint, action}` (§O); the handler writes `launchedBy: {member, device, opId, action}` on the intent and the record, which is the handoff P5 §7 and RL §5.8 need: O12's lane B observer reads `launchedBy` and sminos claims the member as the session's owner with `action` as the proof (RL9's `ownersProof`); until O12 reads it, the session's owners are the machine's (today's rule), which for an org of one is the same person. A spawn whose composer holds attachments sends no `brief`: the adapter spawns, waits for the record (M2's retries), uploads the parts to the new session, then sends one `session.send` carrying the text and the machine's paths, so the first message the engine sees names files that exist; a failure in the uploads leaves the session started with no message and the composer's text kept with Send again. Rejected: `session.create` + `intent.patch` + `start` as three acts (an intent has no engine session, so the `appSessionId` rule can't place the second and third); a MAWS-shaped input (`spawnRefusal` would refuse every field); an optional mode (the two defaults differ); a brief that names files not yet uploaded.

*The machine menu* (A5, P5 Decision Log 108). `session.projects.list` is offered as a `list` call with the `machine` target (§L), and the composer's project chip lists, for the focused org project, this Mac and each `up` RL2 machine whose projects answer a row of the same org project id (`OrgProject.id`, O6's key), by name; the line under it reads "Lane B on `<machine>` · `<machine>`'s inference route" (the route itself is the machine's settings, which no `list`-graded read carries). Choosing another machine makes Send build `session.spawn` through the adapter (`stepUp: 'required'`, with the composer's mode) instead of the local create-and-start; the New chat tab stays local until the spawn answers, then the tab moves to the address the answer names and M2's pane takes over.

*History in pages the channel carries* (from M2's milestone review, 2026-10-31; acceptance 12). M2's main pages a live launch's `session.events` by `afterSeq` and `limit`, but a window the inputs cannot page (the engine host's disk read of a parked session, whose window is a 2 MiB soft and 16 MiB hard budget of string length that keeps a whole newest turn, and an older `before` page) can exceed the channel, and M2 answers it `historyUnavailable`, with a main-owned `remote.historyTooLarge {appSessionId, window}` that gives the gap bar words saying the history is too large to show here yet (a resume does not bring it back: it streams the new launch). M4 adds to `session.events`' input an optional byte budget (the answer's UTF-8 JSON size, which the engine host's window selection honours as a hard bound, never keeping a whole turn past it) and to its output a continuation cursor for the rows the budget left out, both additive under M4's one bump; main's paged read uses them for every window, and a single row larger than the channel stays a `remote.gap` at its place. Rejected: paging call results in RL2 (a protocol change for one read); leaving the gap bar (history a person can't read on a session they can drive).

*`WIRE_MINOR` and the RL2 minor.* `session.spawn`, `files.upload`'s `part`, `launchedBy` and `machine.op`'s `action` and `session.events`' byte budget and continuation cursor are additive: one `WIRE_MINOR` bump, to master's minor plus one at landing (13 if nothing lands first), `e2e:cross-build` on the merge tree. `rl2.call-session`, `rl2.feeds` and `rl2.events-budget` join both capability constants and `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` moves to master's plus one.

**Does not touch.** Terminals and forwarding (M3); attention and search (M6); artifacts, documents, reveal and open on the client (M5); O12's observer and sminos; the files service's read rules (O3-E's); `session.launch` and the local New chat flow for this Mac.

**Proves.** Acceptance 2's diff and file items, 4.
- **`test/endpoint/checkout-rule.test.ts`**: a `git.status` with a `call.session` the caller views and a directory under its worktree answers, and so does one naming the repository that contains a subdirectory root; a directory outside the session's repositories is `no-target`; one with no `session` is `no-target`; a sibling worktree's path on `git.worktree.status` answers null; `fs.agentEvents` by its session; `files.upload` with parts out of order refused; `session.spawn` with a field outside `SPAWN_FIELDS` refused `malformed` (step 2's strict schema, before step 6's guard; `spec-field` still guards `sminos.spawn`), with no `permission_mode` `malformed`, over the ceiling `ceiling` (a ceiling stricter than the machine's default), on an unlisted checkout `unknown-checkout`; a non-owner with a `spawn` grant admitted.
- **`test/endpoint/feeds.test.ts`**: a `checkout` feed delivering `files.changed` and `scm.remote.output` of its session and `git.changed` of its repositories only; a watch released when the channel closes (the ref ended) and absent from the front's counts.
- **`src/services/sessions/spawn.test.ts`**: the composed spawn on the spine root (`src/services/spine-root.test.ts`'s pattern): a session created, patched with the mode as sent and started on the machine's settings, with and without a brief; `launchedBy` with the action on the record; an untrusted checkout refused; a replayed `machine.op` answering the first id.
- **`src/services/machine-ops.test.ts`**, extended: the claim and the action in the handler's context and in the ledger's record.
- **`src/services/files/upload.test.ts`**, extended: parts staged, appended in order, moved at `last`, bounded at 32 MiB, swept.
- **History over the channel** (acceptance 12): an engine-host test that a disk window under a byte budget stops at the budget, never keeping a whole turn past it, and answers a cursor that reaches the rest; and in `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`, a parked session (no host row; history read from disk) whose newest window is over 4 MiB, and an older `before` page over 4 MiB, each opening with paged rows and a working cursor, not a gap bar.
- **`test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`**, extended: an upload of 5 MiB in three parts across a forced drop writing once; `files.write` as a `machine` act on an idle session (no status, not resumed); a `git.watch` and a `files.watch` whose changes arrive on the feed, and, after a forced drop with a file created meanwhile, the git watch replayed with its status emitted, the files watch invalidated and reinstalled by the renderer's path with the new file listed and the old watch released; `session.spawn` with the sheet's injected confirmation, by a non-owner with a grant, the new session streaming in the same test; a spawn with a PDF attached sending no brief, uploading, then one message naming the machine's path.
- **`e2e:remote`**, extended (the spawn under `MAWS_E2E_STEP_UP=accept`): the Files tool lists B's checkout and the editor saves a file there (read back over B's `machine.sock`); a file written on B by the spec appears in A's tree and git status without a click, and one written while B's link is cut appears after the reconnect; the SCM tool commits; a dropped text file appears under B's `desk-uploads` and the message mentions its path; "+ Session" on B starts a session that opens in the pane.

### M3 — Remote terminals and port forwarding

The panel's terminal for a remote session is a shell in that machine's worktree from its pty host; a `localhost` link in a remote session opens in the in-app browser through a port forwarded over the link (A6). Both ride one new RL2 facility, byte streams, which M5's artifact proxy and document frames reuse.

**At its end.**
- In a remote session's pane, the Terminal tool opens a shell in the session's worktree on its machine; typing, resizing, ⌘-click on links and the tab's exit state work as locally; a reload of the app re-attaches to the same shell and shows the ring's tail; closing the tab kills nothing (the shell lives with the machine until killed, as locally). A viewer on the machine's own app and one here see the same bytes, whatever either has checkpointed.
- A `localhost:<port>`, `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `0.0.0.0:<port>` link in a remote session's transcript or terminal opens in the in-app browser, its tab marked "forwarded from `<machine>`", without asking; the page loads from the machine's own loopback through the link, and a reload of the page after a Wi-Fi blip loads again. A port nothing listens on shows the browser's connection error.
- The owner sees no prompt. A non-owner with a `shell` grant confirms at the sheet and Touch ID for each shell act: a spawn, a kill, a list at a tab's open, an attach after a reopen, a forward's open; a channel loss re-opens a terminal's stream under the same act and keeps a forward for two minutes, asking nothing. A grant withdrawn while a terminal or forward is open ends it within one round trip.
- The tracker row "`hostReady` reads the local pty host even for another machine's terminal" closes: a remote terminal's readiness is its machine's link.

**Touches.**
- New: `src/pty-host/ops.ts` (`pty.op`, `pty.opQuery` over `src/shared/host/op-ledger.ts`), `src/endpoint/streams.ts` (the stream table and its planes: pty and tcp), `src/endpoint/forwards.ts` (the endpoint's forward table), `src/main/machines/remote/streams.ts` (the link's half), `src/main/lane-b/remote-pty.ts` and `remote-forward.ts` (the adapter's halves), `src/main/forwards.ts` (the local listeners), tests; `test/endpoint/streams.test.ts`.
- Changed: `apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts` and `channel.ts` (`Executor` gains `pty-host`; the stream kinds, §M; `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor`), `src/endpoint/capabilities.ts` and `src/main/machines/remote/capabilities.ts` (`rl2.streams`, `rl2.executor.pty-host`), `src/endpoint/offers.ts` (§L's M3 rows), `actions.ts` and `queries.ts` (the `pty-host` executor branch, as `machine`'s), `endpoint-actions.ts` (`forward.open` as a graded own action), `authorize.ts` and `gate.ts` (the `stream` request kind), `channels.ts` (streams re-authorized at a `caps` refresh), `endpoint.ts` (`onFrame` subscribed), `src/shared/ipc/schema/pty.ts` (`pty.op`, `pty.opQuery`, `pty.listFor`, `pty.replay`; `WIRE_MINOR`), `src/pty-host/table.ts` (`pty.listFor`, `pty.replay`), `src/mawsd/daemon.ts` (the seam's guard reads `pty.op`'s inner name), `src/shared/ipc/schema/remote.ts` (`remote.forward.open`, `.close`), `src/main/machines/remote/link.ts` (`openStream`), `src/main/lane-b/remote.ts` (the pty acts and the forward act), `src/main/index.ts` (the forwards' listeners closed at shutdown), the renderer: `terminal/registry.ts` (`pty.listFor` per remote session; `pty.ack` and `pty.checkpoint` for a remote pty stay in the adapter), `tools/terminal/index.ts` (`hostReady` by machine), `transcript/view/markdown/Link.tsx`, `pty-links` and the browser tool (the forwarded tab and its mark), `src/main/pty-links.ts` and `open-external.ts` (a loopback link of a remote session is a forward).

**Interfaces.** Produces §L's M3 rows, §M's streams and `pty-host`, §N (streams on the link and the endpoint), §O's `pty.op`, `pty.listFor`, `pty.replay` and `remote.forward.*`. Consumes §J, M2's `ClientRef` per channel, O2's pty host (`pty.attach {credit: false}`, the ring, `onFrame`), the endpoint's own ledger (`src/endpoint/endpoint-actions.ts`), the gate's query path for a binding (`src/endpoint/queries.ts`).

**Decisions.**

*Acts, then streams.* Every `pty.*` command is `shell` (OX9), so a call is `act-required` (O10's rule, kept: no `authorize.ts` change admits a `shell` call). The discrete ones are acts under a ledger the pty host keeps (`pty.op`, `pty.opQuery`, §O, mirroring `src/engine-host/ops.ts`): `pty.spawn`, `pty.attach`, `pty.kill` and `pty.listFor`; the endpoint's `actions.ts` gains a `pty-host` branch beside `machine`'s, dispatching `pty.op` with the claim, and `queries.ts` routes `pty-host` to `pty.opQuery`. `pty.op` admits exactly those four names (anything else `unavailable`), forces `credit: false` on an attach whatever the args say, and keeps and answers an attach's result as `{info, ringStart, nextOffset}` only: the ring's bytes go over the stream (below), since a 512 KiB replay as JSON is over the channel's limit and is no `Uint8Array` after a ledger reopen, and the attach's own replay starts at the *local* app's checkpoint (`replayFrom = max(checkpoint.offset, ring.start)`), which is no remote viewer's. `mawsd`'s seam guard, which reads the outer name today, reads `pty.op`'s inner name as well (belt and braces: the pty host's rule is the authority). `pty.list` answers every pty on the machine and takes no input, which an act cannot carry (`args` is an object; `z.void()` refuses `{}`), so M3 adds `pty.listFor {appSessionId}` (pty-host, `shell`, additive), filtered to that session on the machine, and the local registry keeps `pty.list`. A replayed `pty.attach` or `pty.listFor` (a reseal after a lost channel) answers the stored metadata, which the device reads as it would a fresh one. The bytes (`pty.write`, `pty.data`, `pty.resize`, `pty.ack`) are not acts: they go over a stream (§N) opened under the act that authorized the pty. Rejected: `pty.write` as an act (a keystroke is not an operation; a fsynced claim per key); admitting `shell` calls (a change to O10's decision order for two reads); widening `pty.list`'s input (a changed type is a major); the attach's replay as the remote viewer's (another viewer's checkpoint).

*Streams* (§N; RL §4.8). A stream is opened by the device under an act it sent on this channel: `streamOpen {id, kind, op, …}` names the act's `opId`, and the endpoint admits it when that executor's ledger answers `done` with a binding of this channel's member and device and a name the kind allows (`pty.spawn` or `pty.attach` for `pty`, `forward.open` for `tcp`), the pty or forward named being the one the act's result names; the authorization is the query path's, so step-up and the grade were decided at the act. Data goes as `streamData {id, offset, data}` in chunks of at most `STREAM_CHUNK_BYTES` (64 KiB, base64 in JSON), each direction under the other side's credit (`streamCredit`, granted at open and refunded as bytes are taken, at most 4 MiB), with `streamControl` for a pty's resize and `streamClose {reason}` from either side. A stream dies with its channel: a `pty` stream is reopened by the device on the next channel from its offsets, under the same act (no new prompt); a `tcp` stream is a connection and is not reopened (its local socket is reset; the browser retries, and the retry is a new stream under the same forward, below). Open streams are authorized again whenever subscriptions are (`channels.ts`'s `caps` refresh at a grant kept or ended or an owner change; every record change and new head): a `pty` or `tcp` stream whose member no longer holds `shell` (or ownership) closes `removed` at once, its held input dropped, the forward's entry removed and its socket reset. Rejected: a binary plaintext kind now (a change to the sealing layer and `Channel`, O10's package, for a 33 % saving on terminal output; M7 measures, and a binary kind stays the named follow-up if the forward's throughput needs it); trusting the app's capability view for revocation.

*The pty stream.* Output: at a `pty` stream's open the endpoint reads the ring through `pty.replay {ptyId, from}` (§O; pty-host, `internal`, non-crediting, an offset-based `ring.since` independent of any checkpoint) and sends `[max(from, ringStart), nextOffset)` as `streamData`, then, subscribed to `onFrame`, each later `pty.data` frame of that pty at the frame's `offset`, under the device's credit; frames past the credit are held up to `STREAM_BACKLOG_BYTES` (512 KiB, the ring's size) and then the stream is closed `behind`: the device reopens with `from: <its last offset>` and the ring fills the gap, or, if the ring moved past it, the terminal draws "[output lost]" as a reload's lossy restore does. The pty's own flow control is untouched: `mawsd` holds the credit when no app is attached (it acks every frame) and the attached app holds it otherwise, so a slow remote viewer never pauses the pty for a local one, and a remote attach never acknowledges on a local reader's behalf (the forced `credit: false`). Input: the device's keystrokes go as `streamData` with the device's own byte offset per pty under an `inputEpoch` the adapter mints per app run (§N); the endpoint writes them through `pty.write` and remembers the last offset it wrote per `(device, ptyId, inputEpoch)` for the resume window, dropping a repeat after a reopen (RL §4.8's dedup) and forgetting an older epoch at a new one, so a restarted app under the same device keys starts at zero without its keystrokes read as repeats. Resize is a `streamControl {resize: {cols, rows}}`, applied through `pty.resize`. The renderer's `pty.ack` becomes the stream's credit refund and `pty.checkpoint` is kept in the adapter's memory per pty for the app's life (`mawsd` refuses the endpoint's; O2's seam): a reload within the app restores exactly from the adapter's checkpoint and a `pty.replay` from its offset; a new app run restores from the ring's tail (A9).

*The terminal's machine.* `pty.spawn` goes to the session's machine already (PtyId is addressed); the registry asks `pty.listFor` for each remote session whose tab is open, once at the tab's open and again after a `pty.exit` or a spawn (so a non-owner is not prompted at each refresh), and `pty.list` with no machine for the local host as today; `pty.hostState` for an RL2 machine is answered by the adapter from the link's state (`up` is ready), which closes the tracker row. `pty.foreground` and `pty.openLink` are not offered: the remote tab's title is its label, and ⌘-click opens through main as any link.

*Forwarding* (A6, P5 Decision Log 108; §O). `remote.forward.open {machine, port}` (main-owned, renderer to main) opens a listener on `127.0.0.1:0` in main, runs the act `forward.open {port}` on the link (the endpoint's own executor, graded `shell`, target the machine, claimed in the endpoint's `ops.jsonl`; its result `{forward, port}`), and answers `{localPort}`; each accepted connection opens a `tcp` stream naming the forward, which the endpoint connects to `127.0.0.1:<port>` on the machine, bytes both ways under credit, a connect failure answering `streamRefused {reason: 'unreachable'}` and the local socket reset. A forward is the endpoint's per `(device, forward)`, not per channel: it outlives a channel loss for `RESUME_MS` (120 s), so the listener's next connections open streams naming the same forward on the next channel with no new act, and the ledger's stored result stays the one the device holds; past the window the endpoint forgets it, a stream naming it is refused `unknown`, and the adapter runs a fresh `forward.open` (which a non-owner confirms, since it is a new act). A listener lives until `remote.forward.close`, the link's close or quit. Where a link is clicked: `Link.tsx` and `pty-links` route a loopback URL of a remote session to the browser tool with `{forward: {machine, port}}` instead of `layout.openExternal`; the browser tool asks `remote.forward.open`, loads `http://127.0.0.1:<localPort><path>` and marks the tab "forwarded from `<machine>`" beside its title. The grade is `shell` (P5 Decision Log 92's item 13). Rejected: a forward as a `machine` command with sockets in `mawsd`'s core (the bytes would cross the seam twice); a forward per channel (every blip would need a new act and, for a non-owner, a new prompt).

*`WIRE_MINOR` and the RL2 minor.* `pty.op`, `pty.opQuery`, `pty.listFor` and `pty.replay` are additive: one `WIRE_MINOR` bump at landing to master's minor plus one, `e2e:cross-build` on the merge tree. `rl2.streams` and `rl2.executor.pty-host` join both capability constants and `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` moves likewise.

**Does not touch.** The pty host's table beyond `pty.listFor` and `pty.replay`, its flow control and ring; `mawsd`'s credit rule and relay beyond the inner-name guard; documents and the artifact proxy (M5 reuses §N); the local terminal's path.

**Proves.** Acceptance 2's terminal item, §5's port forwarding.
- **`src/pty-host/ops.test.ts`**: a claimed spawn runs once and a replay answers the stored result; `opQuery`'s states; the ledger's file under the pty host's directory; a claimed attach forced `credit: false` whatever its args, its stored result holding no bytes and read back whole after a reopen; a name outside the four refused `unavailable`; `pty.listFor` filtered to its session; `pty.replay` answering from an offset below the local checkpoint and never touching the credit.
- **`test/endpoint/streams.test.ts`**: a `streamOpen` under a foreign opId refused `grade`; one under a `pty.attach` of another pty refused; the ring's tail delivered at open from the device's offset while a local viewer's checkpoint stands past it, then live output under credit, held past it, closed `behind` at the backlog; an input repeat after a reopen dropped, and a new `inputEpoch` starting at zero not dropped; a `tcp` stream to a listening port carrying an HTTP round trip, one to a closed port `unreachable`; a forward surviving its channel's loss within the window and a stream naming it on the next channel, and one past the window refused `unknown`; a stream ending with its channel; a `shell` grant withdrawn while a pty stream and a forward are open closing both `removed` with the channel kept.
- **`src/mawsd/daemon-pty.test.ts`**, extended: a local reader paused on the pty's credit and a remote attach through `pty.op` leaving the local flow's acked offset and pause unchanged.
- **`test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`**, extended: a shell spawned on the harness's pty host (the endpoint harness gains a fake pty host that emits frames), typed into and read back across a forced drop with nothing repeated and no second prompt (an injected confirmation counting its calls); a new adapter under the same device keys typing without its first keystrokes dropped; a reload (a new registry) re-attaching from the ring at the adapter's offset; a forward whose local listener serves a fetch through the link, and serves it again after a forced drop with no new act within the window, and with one new act (one more confirmation for a non-owner) past it.
- **`e2e:remote`**, extended: a terminal in B's worktree runs `pwd` and shows the worktree; a dev server started in that terminal (`python3 -m http.server`) is opened from its `localhost` link in the in-app browser with the mark.

### M6 — Attention and search across machines

Overview and attention span machines (P5 §8): the dock badge, Needs you and ⌘⇧J count and open every reachable session's items on every machine, and conversation search spans them. It stands on M4's feeds.

**At its end.**
- A permission raised on the mini with no tab open shows in Needs you under its listed row, counts in the dock badge, and ⌘⇧J opens it in M2's pane; answering it there, or on the mini, clears it everywhere within one round trip. A session shared with this member while its permission waits shows it at once. A machine offline greys its items and keeps their count out of the badge.
- A notification is raised for a remote item as for a local one, once per item across the app's windows (A8).
- The sidebar's search answers from every `up` machine, each hit under its machine.

**Touches.**
- New: `src/main/lane-b/remote-attention.ts` (one feed per link), tests.
- Changed: `src/endpoint/plane-feeds.ts` (the `attention` feed beside M4's `checkout`), `src/endpoint/offers.ts` (§L's M6 rows), `targets.ts`, `authorize.ts` and `calls.ts` (the `each-session` target rule and its filters), `src/main/machines/remote/subs.ts` (a machine-wide feed), `src/shared/ipc/schema/remote.ts` (`remote.attention`), `src/main/attention/notifier.ts` (the remote counts and notifications), the renderer: `attention/derive.ts` (a listed row counts), `stores/attention.ts` (a remote snapshot; an offline machine's items greyed), the search surface (per-machine queries merged).

**Interfaces.** Produces §L's M6 rows, §R's `attention` feed. Consumes §J, M4's feeds (§R), O3-D's `attention.list` and `attention.changed` on `onEvent`, O8 M2's listed rows.

**Decisions.**

*The `each-session` rule.* `attention.list` and `session.search` answer per session for the whole machine; neither names a target, and both are graded `view` in the registry, which authorization's step 4 answers `no-target` with no session. A new `TargetRule`, `each-session`: the rows carry `grade: 'list'` (their admission, as `endpoint.listings` is admitted), `resolveTarget` answers `{kind: 'machine'}` for it, and `calls.ts` holds the input to the registry's schema (step 2 skips a row with its own grade) and filters the answer to the sessions the caller holds the registry's declared grade on (`gradeOf(name)`, `view`): `attention.list`'s items by `appSessionId`, `session.search`'s hits likewise. Rejected: a rank-filtered `session.list` (C5's refused alternative; listings are the rows), and offering `attention.list` at `list` unfiltered (it carries every session's requests).

*The feed.* The `attention` feed is machine-wide (`sub {feed: 'attention'}`, no session; §R), admitted at `list` for any member and streamed from `onEvent`'s `attention.changed` (owner `machine`): every opening is a `snapshot` whose `snap` is the filtered `attention.list`, then each change of a session the member views goes as `ev {body: {appSessionId, items}}` at a rising `seq` under the epoch `attention:<endpoint boot id>`. Its membership follows the member's rights: at every record change and new head the endpoint recomputes the sessions the member holds `view` on, emits `ev {body: {appSessionId, items: []}}` for each session that left the set (so the device clears it) and `ev {body: {appSessionId, items: <current>}}` for each that joined (a permission already waiting on a newly shared session shows at once), and never ends for an empty set: the feed lives with its channel, so a member who can view nothing today needs no reopening tomorrow. Main opens one feed per RL2 link while the link is `up` and emits `remote.attention {machine, items}` at each snapshot and `attention.changed` for the machine at each event; the renderer applies the snapshot to the machine's partition as `connectShell` applies a local one, and a machine that goes `down` keeps its items greyed and out of the badge until its next snapshot. Rejected: `cards` subscriptions per listed session (one per session against a cap of 64); ending the feed at an empty set (nothing would reopen it).

*Counting without a record.* `needsYou` requires a record; for an RL2 machine a listed row stands in, so an item of a session with no open tab is drawn under its listed row. The badge: `updateBadge` adds the remote feeds' pending counts to `allAttention()`'s, `up` machines only. Notifications: the notifier raises one for a remote item as for a local one, keyed by the item's address and raised once across windows; a click opens the session through M2's opener (A8 is the owner's call; built as recommended).

*Search.* `session.search` is offered with the `each-session` rule; the renderer's search sends the query to this Mac and each `up` RL2 machine (`{machine}`) and merges the hits under their machines; a machine that answers `host_unavailable` is left out with no error. The search's input names the sessions the caller views (`sessions`, the milestone review's fix), so the machine's 50-hit limit is spent on them; a full page the cut still took hits from answers `incomplete: true` (Decision Log, "M6 takes `WIRE_MINOR` 19").

**Does not touch.** The attention core, the notifier's local path, the phone's pushes (O13), the project channel (O8 M7, which consumes M2's cards and this feed).

**Proves.** Acceptance 9.
- **`test/endpoint/feeds.test.ts`**, extended: the attention feed's snapshot filtered to the member's sessions; a change on a session the member can't view not delivered; the snapshot again after a resubscribe; a share withdrawn on one of two sessions emitting that session's empty items with the feed kept; a share granted on a session with a waiting permission emitting its current items without a reconnect; a member who views nothing holding an open, empty feed that fills at the first share; `session.search`'s hits filtered.
- **`test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`**, extended: a request raised on the harness with no tab reaching `remote.attention`; an answer from a second device clearing it; the link forced `down` greying the items and leaving the badge count, the next `up` restoring them.
- **`src/main/attention/notifier.test.ts`**, extended: one notification per remote item across two windows; none for a greyed machine.
- **`e2e:remote`**, extended: a permission from the fixture with B's tab closed shows in Needs you and the badge (`app.dock` read through the harness), ⌘⇧J opens it, answering clears both; B's daemon stopped greys its item and the badge drops; search finds a word of B's transcript.

### M5 — Artifacts, documents and the client-bound surfaces *(re-planned 2026-11-01, executable; three landings)*

A remote session's artifacts list, open, edit and export from this Mac; its pages render here through the machine's artifact endpoint; a document edit from this Mac lands in the machine's store as a local edit does; "Open externally" on a remote file opens a copy (A10); O5's tools and O4's surfaces light or grey from the capability lists (acceptance 6); and §N gains the session-bound read open O17 M9 builds its screencast on (R1). It was advisory until O4 and O5 landed; this section was written against what they shipped (the re-check of 2026-11-01, `m5-recheck-2026-11-01.md` in the SDE workspace) and against the code at master a0a7f81a2. Where the advisory text assumed something the landings changed, the Decision Log's M5 entries say what and why.

**At its end.**
- *M5a, the rule and the reads.* In a remote session's pane the Artifacts tool lists the session's artifacts and opens one: its title, versions and metadata read from its machine, a rename or a pin lands there, a text artifact's content reads, Save As exports on the machine and saves the file here, and "Open externally" on a remote file, library item or export opens a copy on this Mac (Reveal stays greyed "on `<machine>`"). The Library tool lists another machine's library from a machine menu. The Browser tool's agent notice and the pane's capability-drawn affordances read `session.capabilities` from the session's machine and follow its changes. An `artifacts.export` of a cold machine completes (the link gives each call its command's budget, which closes the tracker row of 2026-10-31). A member who cannot view an artifact's sessions sees none of it.
- *M5b, pages and documents.* An `html`, PDF, image or media artifact of a remote session renders in the pane's frame, ranges included, from the machine's artifact endpoint over the link; a document (text, board, design) opens in the editor and an edit here reaches the machine's store and every other replica, with the host's acks, restores and provisional edits flowing back; a `view`-only member gets the editor read-only; a carrier drop reopens the page or the document with nothing lost (a Yjs resync); `artifacts.downloadAsset` saves a remote asset here. The read open and the `http` and `document` kinds are in §N, the `browser.screencast` kind and `rl2.browser-screencast` are reserved for O17 M9, and a stream whose member loses `view` closes `removed` within one round trip of the record change.
- *M5c, O4's surfaces.* A dashboard of a remote session refreshes its connector widgets here, hidden while its page is not shown, under the grade the owner answers (A13); Send to works for a remote artifact, the export and the delivery both on the machine; Connect for a route D connector opens its consent here and completes through the relayed callback (A15); the machine's `openExternal {url}` requests reach this app, its `{path}` ones answer "not here"; the Connectors sheet and Send to grey by rights and by what the machine offers, never by the lane.

**The three landings.** M5a, then M5b (it stands on M5a's rule and its artifact table), then M5c (it stands on M5a; not on M5b). Each is its own branch and PR (`p5-o7-m5a`, `m5b`, `m5c`), cut from master after the previous one lands, reviewed at doperpowers:reviewer-high (each extends the endpoint's authorization: a target rule, the read open, the consent relay). O17 M9 waits for M5b alone. The owner answered A12 to A15 as recommended on 2026-11-01 (P5 Decision Log 195), so M5a and M5c build them as written; the architect may still schedule M5c after M7 without blocking anything (acceptance 6's O4 half is then greyed with reasons from the lists, which the item allows).

**Touches.**
- *M5a.* New: `src/endpoint/artifacts.ts` (the `artifact` target rule and its per-channel cache, `ArtifactRanks`), `test/endpoint/artifact-rule.test.ts`, `test/endpoint/artifact-rows.test.ts`; `src/main/artifacts/machines.ts` (`ArtifactMachines`, the id-to-machine table), `src/main/lane-b/remote-artifacts.ts` (the artifact acts, the table's writer, the `session.capabilities` substitute, the export read, open-a-copy), `src/renderer/src/artifacts/machines.ts` (the renderer's table), tests. Changed: `src/endpoint/offers.ts` (§L's M5a rows), `targets.ts` and `authorize.ts` (the `artifact` rule, the machine-owner short cut, the orphan form; a multi-session target's rank), `actions.ts` (an engine-host act with no session), `queries.ts` (an artifact binding), `calls.ts` (the per-row filter of `library.list`), `plane-feeds.ts` (the `artifacts` feed; `session.capabilitiesChanged` on `checkout`), `capabilities.ts` and `src/main/machines/remote/capabilities.ts` (`rl2.artifacts`, `rl2.bytes`), `calls.ts` and `actions.ts` (the bytes codec on `input`/`output` and `args`/`result`) with `src/main/machines/remote/dispatch.ts` and `acts.ts` (the device's half), `apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts` and `channel.ts` (`Feed` gains `artifacts`; `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor`), `src/engine-host/ops.ts` and `src/shared/ipc/schema/host.ts` (`host.op` with no session), `src/shared/ipc/schema/library.ts` and `src/services/library/files.ts` (`library.read`'s export form), `src/shared/ipc/schema/artifacts.ts` (`artifacts.open`'s `readOnly`, main-owned), `src/shared/ipc/schema/remote.ts` (`remote.openCopy`), `src/shared/ipc/wire.ts`, `src/main/machines/remote/dispatch.ts`, `link.ts` and `src/main/machines/socket-link.ts` (per-command budgets, injected), `src/main/machines/index.ts` and `src/main/app.ts` (the budgets dep; liveness from the link), `tools/artifacts/viewers/ByteViewer.tsx`, `tools/code/viewer/viewer-store.ts`, `library/store.ts`, `library/copy.ts` and a library `MachineMenu`, `library/export.ts`, `tools/document/history-api.ts`, `tools/code/viewer/ExternalRow.tsx` and `transcript/view/rows/Delivery.tsx` (Open externally through `remote.openCopy`), `tools/browser/agent/capability.tsx` (unchanged in what it asks; Changed: `apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts` and `channel.ts` (`streamOpen` without `op`, the `http` and `document` kinds and their fields, `streamOpened.http`; `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor`), `src/endpoint/offers.ts` (`OfferRow.kind` gains `stream`; §L's M5b rows), `authorize.ts` (a `stream` row through `#command`; the read-open path beside the act path), `streams.ts` (the read open; recheck on a record change and on an artifact's relink; the kind dispatch), `endpoint.ts` (`RecordSource.onChange` and `artifacts.changed` to the streams), `capabilities.ts` and the device's (`rl2.read-open`), `src/main/machines/remote/streams.ts` (`StreamRequest` and `StreamOpened` for the two kinds), `src/main/lane-b/remote-artifacts.ts` (the fetch and the open), `src/render/scheme.ts` and `src/main/artifacts/index.ts` (the responder chain), `src/main/ipc/router.ts` (`artifacts.downloadAsset` answered here for any machine), `src/mawsd/engine-requests.ts` (an open endpoint document channel is a park need; a shared file of O5 and O16, one line), `test/endpoint/fake-services.ts` (a real `artifacts.fetch` over a scratch store; `documents` over a fake host port), the renderer's document editor (read-only on `readOnly`) and `artifacts/host.ts` (reconnect on `remote.session`'s link state).

Changed: `apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts` and `channel.ts` (`client`, `clientAnswer`; the minor), `src/endpoint/capabilities.ts` and the device's (`rl2.client-port`), `src/endpoint/services.ts` and `src/mawsd/endpoint-seam.ts` (`clientPort.serve(ref, target)`), `src/mawsd/front.ts` (`route` consults the endpoint's targets after its own), `src/mawsd/daemon.ts` (the seam member), `src/endpoint/offers.ts` (§L's M5c rows; `client.windowShown` as an endpoint call; `connectors.callback` a registry command), `calls.ts`, `plane-feeds.ts` (the `artifacts` feed carries the connector and delivery events), `src/shared/ipc/schema/{connectors,delivery}.ts`, `grades/{connectors,delivery}.ts` and `names/`, `src/shared/host/socket-protocol.ts` and `src/shared/ipc/wire.ts` (`delivery.send` retagged, `callback` fields, `machine.delivery`; `WIRE_MINOR`), `src/services/connectors/{oauth-flow,sign-in,index}.ts` (the receiver chosen per request), `src/services/delivery/index.ts` (`delivery.send` on the machine), `src/main/delivery/send.ts` (the shim kept behind the welcome capability), `src/main/connectors/windows.ts` and `index.ts` (reports per machine; `connectors.reset {machine}` at a link's reconnection), `src/main/lane-b/remote.ts` (the M5c acts; `MACHINE_CALLS` for the six lease reads), `src/endpoint/artifacts.ts` (the leases' re-authorization beside the streams'), the renderer: `artifacts/connectors.ts` and `connector-owner.ts` (the artifact's machine on every call; the rebuild per machine), `delivery/run.ts` and `store.ts`, the Connectors sheet and Send to (greyed by rights and offer), `e2e/remote.spec.ts` and its scene (a dashboard with live data through route D's fixture on B; the OAuth fixture's callback through A).

**Interfaces.** Produces §L's M5 rows and its not-offered list, §M's `rl2.artifacts`, `rl2.read-open` and `rl2.client-port` and the reserved `rl2.browser-screencast`, §N's read open and its two kinds, §O's M5 names, §R's `artifacts` feed and the `checkout` feed's `session.capabilitiesChanged`, §K's `remote.openCopy`, the `artifact` target rule and `EndpointServices.clientPort`. Consumes §J, M2's `ClientRef` per channel, M3's §N machinery and `StreamWindow`, M4's `files.read` paging (`src/main/lane-b/remote-files-read.ts`) and `call.session`, O5's `EndpointServices.artifacts.fetch` and `session.capabilities`, O2's `EndpointServices.documents`, O4's `listen` seam (its Interfaces §E), the library's rows with their links (`LibraryRowSchema.links`) and `library.entry`, `src/services/command-budgets.ts`, the router's allow-list pattern (M3's `BROWSER_TOOL_HERE_FOR_ANY_MACHINE`).

**Decisions.**

*The `artifact` target rule* (M5a; `src/endpoint/artifacts.ts`, `targets.ts`, `authorize.ts`). An artifact names no session: its URL is `maws-artifact://<id>/<rev>/<path>`, its commands carry `artifactId`, and its links to sessions are store rows, many to many (`ArtifactSessionLink {artifactId, appSessionId, role}`), which `library.entry {artifactId}` (engine-host, `internal`, main-only; `{artifact, links}` from the store) already answers in one read. The rule: `resolveTarget('artifact', …)` reads the id from `input.artifactId` (or from the URL of an `http` open, or `input.filter.appSessionId` for a listing that names a session, which it places as the `appSessionId` rule would), dispatches `library.entry` on the asker's behalf, and answers a new `Resolved` form, `{kind: 'artifact', artifactId, sessions: SessionTarget[], orphan}`, each linked session placed through `laneB` and nothing else (the spine's record, then the engine host, as M2 placed them: the target is `laneB`'s own `{lane: 'B', session, appSessionId}` with the record's `launchedBy`, never one built by hand, since O12 M11's `handedOff` keys on the app session and a target with an empty one could re-open the bridge after a `/clear`; a session that places `unavailable` or `unknown` is left out). M5a places the links, so M11's bridge applies to artifacts as it does to sessions (the other option, placing none and passing no `launchedBy`, would fail closed until sminos's record arrives; it is not taken). Standing (`#stands`): a member who is one of the machine's owners (`machineOwnerMembers`) holds `manage` on every artifact of the machine, read before any link is resolved (the machine's own app shows anyone at the machine everything; the owner's own machines are the daily case, and the short cut keeps a listing from costing a placement per row); any other member's rank is the highest `sessionRank` over the linked sessions' records, each read through `Reads.record(target, launchedBy)` and never `records.record` directly, so the bridge's standing is read with the record's and `recordsMoved` re-decides the artifact when a record arrives mid-decision, and the spawner ranks `manage` on its spawn's artifacts in the gap before sminos lists the session, as it does on the session (a member who may view a session the artifact is linked to may view the artifact: that session's transcript already shows it); an artifact with no link (`orphan`: the person's own `artifacts.create` or `duplicate`, an import with no links, a seeded design system) is the machine owners' alone, as a session owned by `@machine` is; an artifact `library.entry` answers null for is `unknown` (refused `grade`, as an unknown session is). `artifacts.create` (no id yet) resolves to the orphan form, so only the machine's owners create there. `artifacts.copyAsset {from, ref, to}` names two artifacts and no `artifactId`: its registry grade becomes `{floor: 'manage', byEffect: true}` (`src/shared/ipc/grades/artifacts.ts`; `#effectiveGrade` consults `BY_EFFECT` only for a by-effect declaration, so the literal `manage` would never run the rule), the rule reads `to` as the target (`manage`, the write) and `BY_EFFECT['artifacts.copyAsset']` reads `from` through the same cache and refuses `grade` unless the member holds `view` on it, since the store checks only that both are live (`store.ts`'s `copyAsset`); both artifacts are on the machine the act goes to, and the adapter refuses a copy whose `from` and `to` the table places on different machines (`invalid_input`, "an asset can't be copied between machines yet").
