The decision's `target` for an artifact is the artifact, and it is made durable where the act is claimed: the endpoint writes the resolved artifact into the claim as `target: 'artifact:<id>'` (`'artifact:'` alone for the orphan form of `create`), an additive optional field of `HostOpInputSchema`'s and `MachineOpInputSchema`'s claim (§O), which the two ledgers store beside the claim and their `opQuery` answers as the binding's `target` (today a session id or null), so a query after a lost acknowledgement or an endpoint restart re-places the artifact through the rule (`queries.ts` reads the `artifact:` prefix) with no channel-local mapping needed, lease and run acts included (M5c's `connectors.call {leaseId}` and `delivery.cancel {runId}` are claimed with the artifact the endpoint resolved them to); a query whose member no longer holds `view` on the artifact is refused `grade`, as a session's is. The resolution is cached per channel and artifact (`ArtifactRanks`, 256 entries, FIFO, keyed by the artifact id; a record change naming one of its sessions, an `artifacts.changed {change: 'linked'}` for it, a `caps` refresh and a new head each drop the entry; a record's first arrival is a record change, so the bridge's end needs nothing more), so a page's many requests and a listing's rows cost one `library.entry` each. This is the owner's A12, answered as recommended on 2026-11-01: the residual (a member's agent linking another's artifact into their session) is accepted until the multi-person coordination work, in the owner's words "and it will be completely solved then". Rejected: grading on `createdBy` alone (an artifact an agent edited into a second session, and every seeded or imported one, would be unreadable); `machine-admin` for orphans (a read graded there is `act-required`, and every act there asks Touch ID; the machine's owners already stand apart in `sessionRank`); refusing orphans (`no-target`), the conservative alternative A12 names; a new engine-host read for links (`library.entry` is it).

*What crosses, as what* (M5a; §L). Reads graded `view` keyed by `artifactId` cross as calls under the rule: `artifacts.get`, `versions`, `versionState`, `attributions`, `readDataset`, `datasetFiles`, `connectorGrants`, `markViewed` (it writes `lastViewedAt`, graded `view` by O1; kept), `library.get`, `library.content`, `library.read` (with its export form, below), and `artifacts.export` (a `view` call by O5's ruling, with the budget below). Listings: `artifacts.list` is offered only with `filter.appSessionId` (the pane's list; a listing without a session is `no-target`, since `library.list` is the library's listing), and `library.list` and `library.spaces` are offered with the `machine` target at row grade `list`, `calls.ts` filtering `library.list`'s rows to the artifacts the member holds `view` on through the rule, each row's own `sessions` (`LibraryRowSchema.sessions`, the index's view of the store's links: `{appSessionId, title, roles, lastAt}` per session) the input, placed and ranked as the rule places `library.entry`'s links, and the machine-owner short cut the common case (the index can lag the store by a moment; a listing is a listing, and each row's content is read through the rule's own `library.entry` at the request); `artifacts.search` is not offered (its rows carry no sessions; `library.list`'s `query` filter is the search). Bytes: `artifacts.versionState` and `attributions` answer `Uint8Array`s, `artifacts.uploadAsset` and `artifacts.create {content: {bytes}}` carry them, and RL2 writes messages with plain `JSON.stringify` (`channel.ts`'s `send`), which turns a `Uint8Array` into an object the schema refuses. M5a carries bytes on RL2 the way the machine-local socket does: `src/shared/host/json-bytes.ts`'s tagged form (`{"$bytes": "<base64>"}`, with its `$plain` escape), applied with the codec's own `toWire`/`fromWire` to a call's `input` and `output` and an act's `args` and `result`, behind `rl2.bytes` (§M). The tagged form is a JSON value, so it travels *unchanged* through everything that requires one: the device encodes before it seals an action or sends a call; the canonical action (`canonical.ts` refuses a typed array) is signed and fingerprinted over the tagged JSON, the same bytes on both sides; the endpoint authorizes and validates against the registry's schema a copy decoded with `fromWire` (step 2), but forwards the *tagged* `args` as the op's `input` and the sealed action as `machine.op`'s `action` (`HostOpInputSchema.input` and `MachineOpInputSchema.input` are `JsonValueSchema`, which refuses a typed array, and the ledgers' JSONL keeps a JSON value), and the two ledgers (`src/engine-host/ops.ts`, `src/services/machine-ops.ts`) decode a copy with `fromWire` *before their own `validateCommandInput`*, which precedes the handler in both (`ops.ts:60`, `machine-ops.ts:53`; a decode after it would still refuse the tagged input `malformed`), run the handler with the validated value, and keep the stored claim and action tagged; `json-bytes.ts` exports `toWire` and `fromWire`, private today; a call's `input` the endpoint decodes before its raw dispatch (no ledger) and a call's `output` and an act's `result` it encodes with `toWire` before `result`/`done`; the device decodes an answer before the link's codec check. A peer that lists no `rl2.bytes` gets a bytes-carrying call or act refused locally `not-offered`. (A tagged input reaching a local handler through `machine.op` from the machine's own app never happens: main's socket already decodes the line, and a `$bytes` object a local caller sends on purpose is what the schema refuses today.) (`files.read` and `library.read` need none of it: `FilesReadOutput` already carries binary as base64 with `encoding`.) Writes graded `manage` cross as acts: `artifacts.update`, `delete`, `duplicate`, `saveVersion`, `nameVersion`, `restore`, `pruneVersions`, `acceptSuggestion`, `rejectSuggestion`, `uploadAsset`, `copyAsset`, `rereadDatasetFile`, `create`; `artifacts.open` is `main`-owned and is M5b's (the adapter answers it; M5a refuses it for a remote artifact with "editing an artifact on `<machine>` arrives with the next landing"). `artifacts.uploadAsset` and `artifacts.create {content: {bytes}}` above `REMOTE_INLINE_MAX_BYTES` are refused locally with M2's words (parts for assets are a follow-up, not a bound the owner's documents hit); `artifacts.create {content: {path}}` names a path on this Mac and is refused `malformed` for another machine. Rejected: offering `artifacts.search` (a filter the endpoint can't apply to its rows); asset parts now.

*Artifact writes are engine-host acts with no session* (M5a; `actions.ts`, `src/engine-host/ops.ts`, `host.op`). The writes are engine-host commands keyed by `artifactId`; `host.op` runs only a command whose input names a session this host holds a row for and checks the claim's context against it (`ops.ts:62-67`), and `actions.ts` refuses an engine-host act with no lane B context `malformed` (`:199`). M5a adds a second entry beside it, additively (the architect's ruling of 2026-11-01; the Decision Log): `host.artifactOp`, an engine-host command whose input is the claim with no `context` (`opId`, `opCreated`, `member`, `device`, `fingerprint`, `target`, `name`, `input`), served by the same ledger, which runs only the artifact writes of §L's M5a act row (`ARTIFACT_OP_COMMANDS`, `src/shared/ipc/schema/host.ts`, an explicit list that fails closed; the offer table's `artifact` act rows are pinned equal to it), with no status check, `host.opQuery` answering ops of both; `host.op` and its required `context` are unchanged; `actions.ts`'s engine-host branch, for a row whose target rule is `artifact`, sends `host.artifactOp` and skips the status read, the artifact rule having placed the target at step 4. The adapter's third act shape: `link.act({action, args, executor: 'engine-host', sendBy})`, neither `session` nor `context` (the link and `isAction` admit it). An engine host kept across the update knows no `host.artifactOp`: its runtime answers `{ok: false, error: {code: 'unknown_command'}}` (`src/shared/host/runtime.ts`), which the endpoint's engine-host branch maps, for that command alone, to `{state: 'refused', reason: 'unavailable'}` and logs (an `invalid_input` stays our own fault and surfaces), and the adapter shows "`<machine>`'s engine host predates remote artifact edits; it is replaced when idle" (O17's pattern for `host.browserWork`); the test runs a fake host whose runtime has no `host.artifactOp` registered, never a hand-written refusal. Rejected: `machine`-owned wrappers in the spine for the writes (new commands for a dozen names, and the store is the engine host's); routing the writes through `machine.op` (it claims `machine`-owned commands only, by design); a session-bound write (an artifact belongs to no one session).

*Events that cross* (M5a; §R). `artifacts.changed` (engine-host; `{artifactId, kind, revision, change}`) and `library.changed` (`machine`; `{revision}`) are client events locally and reach no device: plane B carries a session's stream parts, the `checkout` feed a session's machine events. M5a adds the machine-wide feed `artifacts` (`sub {feed: 'artifacts'}`, `session` absent, admitted at `list`, behind `rl2.artifacts`, built on M4's feed mechanism beside the `attention` feed M6 is building): no `snap`; each `artifacts.changed` whose artifact the member holds `view` on (the rule's cache; a `created` change for an artifact nobody can view yet is resolved once) and every `library.changed` go as `ev {body: {name, payload}}`; the adapter emits them `emitFrom(machine, …)`, so the renderer's artifact and library stores follow as locally. `session.capabilitiesChanged` (`machine`; `{appSessionId, capabilities}`) is per session and rides the `checkout` feed as `session.changed` does (one `else if` in `plane-feeds.ts`), so the Browser tool's reader hears it for a remote session as it does locally. Rejected: a per-artifact subscription (the cap of 64 against a library of hundreds); polling `library.get` (late and costly); `session.capabilitiesChanged` on its own feed (the checkout feed is the session's machine-event feed).

*The link gives each call its command's budget* (M5a; `src/main/machines/remote/dispatch.ts`, `socket-link.ts`; the tracker row of 2026-10-31). `artifacts.export` on a cold machine is a render host's start plus the export, budgeted at 120 s in `COMMAND_BUDGETS_MS`, against the link's 10 s `CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, so A gave up while B finished (O5's landing 1). `dispatchOverLink` passes `timeoutMs: budget(name) + RTT_MARGIN_MS` (one second) where the table names the command, else the default; the socket link does the same (`LINK_TIMING.requestTimeoutMs` stays its default). The table is injected (`MachinesDeps.budgets`, from `src/main/app.ts`, which already composes the services' entry), so the link module imports nothing of `src/services/`. The endpoint has no call deadline of its own (`calls.ts`), so the device's bound is the one that matters. Rejected: raising `CALL_TIMEOUT_MS` for every call (a dead read would wait two minutes); a per-call deadline carried on the wire (the endpoint doesn't time calls).

*Which machine an artifact is on* (M5a; `src/main/artifacts/machines.ts`, `src/renderer/src/artifacts/machines.ts`). Artifact ids are UUIDs (`src/shared/artifacts/entity.ts`) and the scheme's origin is the id, so an id stays the key everywhere and the machine is a property of the artifact, not part of its key: main's `ArtifactMachines` records `artifactId → MachineRef` for every artifact-keyed request the adapter dispatches to an RL2 machine and every row or record a link answers (`artifacts.get`, `artifacts.list`, `library.list`, `library.get`, `create`, `duplicate`), and the renderer keeps the same table from the rows it lists, so every artifact-keyed invoke passes `{machine}` (`createArtifactHost` takes the machine; ByteViewer, the viewer store, the library's export path and the document history API read it from the table). The pane's own listing already routes by its session's address (`artifacts.list {filter: {appSessionId}}`, the optional-address rule of `src/renderer/src/ipc/client.ts`). The Library tool lists one machine at a time: this Mac by default, or an `up` RL2 machine chosen in a machine menu (the composer's `MachineMenu` pattern), its rows from `library.list {machine}` and `library.spaces`, so A's library row for B's artifact (O5's acceptance 5) exists. A local id never enters the table; an id answered by two machines keeps the later answer (a v4 collision does not happen; a copied profile directory could make one, and then the artifact opens from the machine that answered last, which the executor records as a known residual). This is an exception to OX2's letter ("every machine-bound id travels as `{machine, id}`; layout, drafts and stores key by it"), kept to its intent: the machine *travels* with every artifact (each store entry and tab param carries `machine` beside the row, every invoke passes it, and a row is never shown without it), and only the *key* stays the id, because the scheme's origin is the id and every artifact store and tab is keyed by it today. `[parent-impact]`: OX2's line, with the alternative (address-keyed artifact stores, tabs and layout entries, and a scheme URL carrying the machine) and the residual above; accepted by the architect on 2026-11-01 (P5 Decision Log 195: an artifact id is a v4 UUID and the scheme's origin, and a copied profile copies `machine.json` too, so the residual is not new), and OX2's text is amended. Rejected: the machine in the URL (`maws-artifact://<ref>@<id>/…`: a changed origin for every remote page, a parse in every consumer, and a host part the scheme lowercases); address-keyed artifact stores in the renderer (every artifact store and tab keyed by bare id today, for a key that is unique already; the OX2-literal alternative).

*Open a copy, and Save As of a remote artifact* (M5a; A10, the owner's answer of 2026-10-31; §K's `remote.openCopy`). The machine's `files.openExternal`, `files.reveal`, `library.openExternally`, `library.reveal` and `session.revealPath` call the client port with a path on the machine, which means nothing on this Mac, so none is offered: the renderer's remote branch opens a copy through main's own `remote.openCopy {machine, source}` (`source`: `{appSessionId, path}` for a file of the checkout, `{artifactId, revision?}` for a library file, `{artifactId, export: {revision, format, exportId?}}` for an export), which pages the bytes through the link (`files.read` by M4's `remote-files-read.ts`; `library.read` paged the same way) into `<temp>/maws-remote-copies/<machine>/<sha256 of the source>/<name>` (a fresh copy per open, 64 MiB at most, larger refused with "too large to copy from `<machine>`") and opens it with `shell.openPath`; Reveal stays greyed "on `<machine>`" (nothing here to reveal). `library.read` gains an optional `export?: {revision, format, exportId?}` (additive; §O), under which it resolves the export's path as `library.saveAs` does (`artifactExportName`'s grammar under `<root>/<artifactId>/exports/`, a regular file, no link on the way, the admission `delivery.deliver` applies) and reads it with `readResolved`'s rules, so an export reaches a device at `view` on the artifact. `library.saveAs` (`main`, `client`) is answered here for any machine (the router's allow-list, M3's pattern): the renderer exports with `artifacts.export {machine}` as today (without `ownFile` for a remote artifact, so the export stays a row of that machine's library, as its own Export leaves one; a remote own-file would need a remove the wire has no reason to carry), then `saveAs {export}` pages the file through `library.read` into a temporary file and saves it through the port's `save` as locally. `artifacts.downloadAsset` (`main`, `client`) is M5b's, through the `http` stream. Rejected: offering the five path commands and carrying `{path}` port requests back to this Mac (two more round trips to learn a path this app already knows, and a read the device must still make); an `ownFile` remove command (a wire name for one tidy-up); copies under `userData` (A10 says the temporary directory).

*The capability lists* (M5a; acceptance 6; RL7). RL7 has two lists: per device, the machine's `caps` (the member's rights: M2's `remote.session.link.rights`, from `caps.machine.grades`); per session, the session's capabilities, which in MAWS are two records: M2's control overlay (`remote.session.controls`, the conversation's affordances, computed by main from the stream and the link) and O5's `session.capabilities` (the tools: `artifact_screenshot`, `artifact_export`, `browser`), read over the link as a `view` call and kept current by `session.capabilitiesChanged` on the `checkout` feed. The renderer draws each surface from the list that owns it and greys what either lacks, never from the lane or the machine: the Browser tool's agent notice and the pane's tool affordances from `session.capabilities` (its reasons are the machine's own words); the Connectors sheet, Send to and the connector grants from `link.rights` (`machine-admin`, until M5c's grades apply) and from the row's offer (a `not-offered` refusal greys the surface with "`<machine>` doesn't offer this", the link's `lacking` mechanism). The adapter substitutes `session.capabilities` for a peer that refuses it `not-offered` (every tool `{available: false, reason: '<machine> predates session capabilities; update it'}`) and while the link is down (`'<machine> is offline'`), as M2 substitutes `attention.request`, so the reader always holds a reading. O4 shipped no capability entries for its surfaces (its §J); M5 defines none on the machine: the connectors, delivery and library exist on every post-O4 machine, and what varies is the member's rights and the machine's offer, which the two lists already carry. Rejected: entries for O4's surfaces in `session.capabilities` (always `available: true` on a post-O4 machine; the grade is the endpoint's to decide at the request); a fourth list.

*The read open* (M5b; R1, O17's §K; §N; `authorize.ts`, `streams.ts`, `offers.ts`). M3's streams open under an act, their authorization that act's ledger binding, and `#stream` admits only a single machine grade (`authorize.ts:405`): a stream at `view` on a session, which an artifact page, a read-only document and O17's screencast need, is refused `grade` today. M5b adds a second way in, the stream row: `OfferRow.kind` gains `stream`, a row names the kind's authorizing name (`artifacts.fetch` for `http`, `documents.open` for `document`; endpoint-executor names, not registry ones, with `accepts` over the open's fields), its target rule and its grade, and a `streamOpen` that carries no `op` is authorized as a call is, by `#command`'s eight steps with `kind: 'stream'` and the open's fields as the input: offered, the fields checked, the class, standing at the floor through the row's target rule (the artifact rule for both kinds), the effect grade (`documents.open` declares `{floor: 'view', byEffect: true}`, `mode: 'edit'` taking `manage`), policy (`read_only` refuses an edit open `read-only`), presence (`removed`); no step-up, since nothing a stream row admits needs one (`manage` never does, `src/endpoint/step-up.ts`). An open that carries `op` keeps M3's path. Re-authorization: a read stream is re-run through the same steps with its stored open input at every `caps` refresh, as every stream is, and from M5b also at every record change naming one of its target's sessions and at an `artifacts.changed {change: 'linked'}` of its artifact (`endpoint.ts` subscribes `Streams` to `RecordSource.onChange` and to the event; the artifact cache drops the entry first); a refusal closes it `removed` and drops its image or its held bytes. The device's `StreamRequest` takes `op?` and the two kinds' fields; `rl2.read-open` gates the form (an older endpoint reads a missing `op` as `malformed` and closes the channel). The reservation: `browser.screencast` is a kind name and `rl2.browser-screencast` a capability name that §M and §N carry and no validator admits until O17 M9 adds the kind, its envelope, its controls, its refusal words and its one-frame backlog on this path (a controlling open names `control.op`, a done `machine.browserControl` act, beside the row: M9 adds that combination). The constraint "a byte flow is a §N stream opened under an act" reads from M5 "under an act, or under a stream row at the row's grade": the act path exists for run-once and step-up, and a page fetch or a document sync needs neither (a repeated fetch is a fetch; a replayed Yjs update is a no-op). Rejected: a read token minted by a `view` call (O17's R1 named it: a second credential with its own expiry and revocation); `#stream` admitting session ranks for the act path alone (an act per page request, for a read); opening documents under an endpoint-own act (`forward.open`'s pattern) for the step-up it would carry (none is due at `manage`).

*The `http` stream* (M5b; `stream-http.ts`; `EndpointServices.artifacts.fetch`). One stream per request: `streamOpen {id, kind: 'http', credit, http: {url, method: 'GET' | 'HEAD', range?}}`; the endpoint parses the URL with `src/shared/artifacts/scheme.ts` (anything else `malformed`), takes the artifact id from it for the rule, and at `decide` calls `artifacts.fetch`, which resolves through the engine host's `artifacts.content` (a parked host wakes); `streamOpened {id, credit, http: {status, headers}}` carries the answer's status and headers (the scheme handler's own: content type, `nosniff`, `no-store`, `Accept-Ranges`, `Content-Length`, `Content-Range`, the frame CSP, the CORS header; the bridge script already inserted on the machine), then the body as `streamData` from offset 0, pulled from the `ReadableStream` only within the device's credit (no backlog, never `behind`), then `streamClose {reason: 'closed'}`; a `HEAD`, a 304, a 404 or a 416 closes right after `streamOpened`. The device sends no data on it (a `streamData` from the device is `malformed`). On this Mac, `serveArtifacts`'s responder chain becomes: the presenter's embeds, then M5b's remote responder (`ArtifactMachines` names an RL2 machine for the URL's id, else the referrer's machine when the referrer is a scheme URL of a remote artifact, for a page's embeds), then the local path; the responder answers a `Response` whose body is the stream, status and headers as received, `Content-Length` checked against the bytes delivered (a short body is an error the frame shows), at most 8 open `http` streams per link (the rest queued; `MAX_STREAMS` is 32 per channel for everything), a `503` with "`<machine>` is offline" while the link is down and a `504` when nothing opened within 30 s. Rejected: one `http` stream per artifact with multiplexed requests (a framing of our own over a framing we have; the per-request open costs one cached rule read); a call carrying the bytes (a 16 MiB page against the 4 MiB message); the artifact endpoint's socket proxied as a `tcp` stream (a credential the endpoint would have to trust and a path the artifact rule couldn't read).

*The `document` stream* (M5b; `stream-document.ts`; `EndpointServices.documents`; `src/main/artifacts/remote-open.ts`). `streamOpen {id, kind: 'document', credit, document: {artifactId, mode: 'edit' | 'view', doc?: 'suggestions'}}`. At `decide` the plane opens an endpoint document channel (`documents.open {channel: <the endpoint's range>, artifactId, actor: {kind: 'human'}, doc}`; every IPC and port write is the human's, there is no actor kind for a member, and attribution per member is O8 M6's), answers `streamOpened {id, credit}`, and relays frames: each direction's bytes carry the document frames (`src/artifacts/provider/frames.ts`: step 1, step 2, update, awareness, and the host's ack, restored and provisional) each prefixed by its u32be length, cut into `streamData` chunks under the other side's credit; the host's frames come from `onFrame`'s `{owner: 'engine-host', channel, bytes}`, the device's go through `documents.frame(channel, bytes)` as each completes. In a `view` open the plane forwards only step 1 and awareness from the device and drops update and step 2 (counted, logged), so a `view` holder's editor, which main marks read-only, can sync and show presence but never writes. The channel's end (`documents.onClosed {reason: 'host' | 'reset' | 'upstream'}`) closes the stream with that word; a carrier loss closes it `carrier`; the device reopens a document on the next channel or after a close with the ArtifactHost's own backoff (250 ms, 1 s, 3 s), and the provider's next step 2 carries the edits the host had not acked (the port's rule), so nothing typed is lost; a park of the engine host closes it `host` and the reopen's rule read wakes the host again, and an open endpoint document channel is itself a park need in `src/mawsd/engine-requests.ts` (a client's forwarded request is one; a document someone edits remotely must not park under them). On this Mac, `artifacts.open` for a remote artifact is the adapter's: `remote-open.ts` preflights `artifacts.get` over the link as `openDocument` does, mints a `MessageChannelMain`, posts one end to the window on `maws:doc-port` as locally and bridges the other to a `document` stream opened `edit` first and, on a `grade` or `read-only` refusal (`read-only` is a new `STREAM_REFUSALS` word, sent only in answer to a read open, which only a peer listing `rl2.read-open` can make; §N), `view`, answering `{channel, readOnly?: true}` (an additive output of a main-owned command), which the editor reads; the renderer's provider is unchanged otherwise. The ArtifactHost's liveness for a remote artifact follows `remote.session`'s link state instead of the local `engine.hostState`. Rejected: Yjs updates as acts (a claim per keystroke, as `pty.write` was refused); the document channel multiplexed with pages (two kinds with different lifetimes); a member actor (no such kind in the store, and O8 M6 owns attribution).

*O4's surfaces: the grade* (M5c; A13, A14; OX9). Every `connectors.*` and `delivery.*` command is `machine-admin`, and `stepUpRequired` asks Touch ID for every `machine-admin` act, so offered as they are, a remote dashboard's every widget refresh and a Send to sheet's every status read would prompt. O4's D22 handed the floor question to M5. Built as recommended unless the owner answers otherwise: the eight page-lease commands (`connectors.open`, `declare`, `call`, `watch`, `unwatch`, `refresh`, `visibility`, `close`) take `{floor: 'view', byEffect: true}` on the artifact (the lease names it at `open`; the others name the lease, which the endpoint resolves to its artifact from the `open` act's result, kept per channel), `connectors.call` and `connectors.refresh` grading `converse` by effect (they run the owner's connector tools, within the grants the owner declared for that artifact) and the rest `view`; so a member who may view a shared dashboard may use its widgets as its owner granted them, with no prompt, and the two effectful ones cross as `machine` acts (`machine.op` claims a by-effect grade) and the six as calls. The device side of that split is the adapter's, not the dispatcher's: `dispatchOverLink` refuses any grade that is not literally `list` or `view` as `act-required` before a round trip (`dispatch.ts`), so the adapter's `handles` gains `MACHINE_CALLS`, the six lease reads by name, sent through `link.call` as calls (a by-effect command crosses as a call only when it is on that list; nothing else is widened), and `MACHINE_ACTS` gains `connectors.call` and `connectors.refresh`. Delivery's reads `delivery.status` and `delivery.receipts` take `view` on the artifact (`receipts` lists runs of every artifact: offered with the `machine` target at `list`, filtered per row through the rule), and `delivery.send` and `delivery.cancel` take `manage` on the artifact (its owners send it where the machine's credential allows, with no prompt; `manage` never steps up). `connectors.servers`, `connect`, `reconnect`, `disconnect`, `signIn`, `signInCancel`, `artifacts.grantConnectorTool` and `revokeConnectorTool` keep `machine-admin` (credential and grant management; Touch ID each, deliberate acts), and `catalog.engine` and `engineRefresh` are not offered (the Discover surface is this Mac's and reads its own machine; a remote read at `machine-admin` would prompt per catalog refresh). These are grade changes to twelve commands of OX9's "connectors … are `machine-admin`" line, in the registry (`src/shared/ipc/grades/{connectors,delivery}.ts`, the floor declared, the by-effect rule in `authorize.ts`'s `BY_EFFECT`), and a `[parent-impact]` for P5's writer. The alternative A13 and A14 name: keep `machine-admin` and offer none of them (a remote dashboard's widgets read "connectors on `<machine>` need its app", Send to greys "on `<machine>`"). Rejected: `machine-admin` offered as is (a prompt per widget); grading `connectors.call` by the tool's mutability (the endpoint can't read a tool's annotations from `{leaseId, request}`; `converse` is what driving the session's agent to call the same tool already needs).

*O4's surfaces: leases, the window, delivery on the machine* (M5c). A lease's owner on the machine is the asker's `ClientRef`, which over RL2 is the channel's one ref (M2), so every page of a device is one owner there: lease ids stay per open, watches per lease, and one window report stands for the device. A lease outlives the decision that admitted it: the broker's refresh loop runs and publishes on its own (`src/services/connectors/broker.ts`), ended only by the owner's close or the ref's end (`connectors/index.ts`), so the endpoint keeps, per channel, each lease's artifact (from the `open` act's args) and re-authorizes every lease as it re-authorizes streams: at a `caps` refresh, a new head, a record change naming one of the artifact's sessions and a relink, the rule is run again for the lease's artifact, and a lease whose member no longer holds `view` is closed by the endpoint itself (`dispatch('machine', 'connectors.close', {leaseId}, channel.client)`), its mapping dropped; the `artifacts` feed forwards a `connectors.watchUpdate` only while its lease's artifact is still viewable by the cache, so a result in flight at the withdrawal reaches no one. A channel's end ends its ref, and the service closes every lease of that owner, so a reconnection must rebuild them: the adapter, once the new channel is `up` and the machine's window report has been taken, emits main's `connectors.reset {machine}`, and the renderer's connectors client rebuilds *that machine's* leases, declarations, visibility and watches under new ids, calls in flight at the drop answering `host_unavailable`. From M5c every reset names its machine: main's local emission (the machine host's streaming after the first, `src/main/connectors/index.ts`) says `machine: <local>`, the renderer rebuilds only the named machine's surfaces, and an absent `machine` reads as the local machine; a reset never touches another machine's leases, since those are live on the machine and their watches running (O4's rebuild opens a new lease without closing the old one, which was safe only because the ended ref had closed it: a rebuild of a live lease would leave its watches running under ids the renderer forgot, spending quotas and the broker's watch limit). The report: `connectors.windowShown` is `internal` and the front has no main-only gate (O4's observation; the Decision Log's 2026-10-31 entry), so the endpoint offers an own call `client.windowShown {shown}` (endpoint executor, `machine` target, row grade `list`, no registry name; `calls.ts` answers it by `dispatch('machine', 'connectors.windowShown', {shown}, channel.client)`), and main's window reporter, whose router hook runs on the local path alone, reports per RL2 machine through the adapter before the first lease act of a page on that machine and at every change, `shown` being whether any followed page holding a lease on that machine is shown (a hidden dashboard beside a shown one keeps refreshing: O4's bounded residual, now per machine). `delivery.send` moves to the machine, where the export already runs (O4's D14 named this step for O5, which closed without it): retagged `machine` (an owner change from `main` is additive, as O4's 25 were; `WIRE_MINOR` at M5c's landing), served by the spine's delivery part as export (`RenderService.exportForLibrary` on the daemon shape; main's export service injected on the embedded one) then `deliver` in process, cancel during the export honoured by the service's own run table; main's `delivery/send.ts` stays as the shim for a `mawsd` whose welcome lists no `machine.delivery` (a new daemon welcome capability, O5's `machine.render` pattern), and `delivery.deliver` stays served for the OX1 window. The connector and delivery events (`connectors.watchUpdate` for leases this channel opened, the endpoint keeping the lease ids from the `open` results; `connectors.serversChanged`, `connectors.signInChanged` and `delivery.receipt` for a member holding `machine-admin` or, for a receipt, `view` on its artifact) ride the `artifacts` feed. The renderer's connectors client, connector owner, delivery run and store pass the artifact's machine on every call. Rejected: forwarding `connectors.windowShown` itself through a row (a main-only `internal` name forwarded through a front that won't refuse it); a remote lease assumed shown (the veto exists to stop hidden dashboards from spending quotas); a `delivery.deliver` row at `machine-admin` with the export here (the path admission would hold, but Send to would be three acts and two prompts).

*Consent and the client port* (M5c; A15; C7, OX4; `rl2.client-port`). The machine's services ask the port for two things only: `openExternal {url}` (a route D connector's consent, the engine's MCP sign-in, `gws`'s login) and `openExternal {path}` or `reveal path` (the five path commands, not offered). So the port across the channel is built with its one real caller, the consent relay. The seam: `EndpointServices.clientPort.serve(ref: ClientRef, target: PortTarget): RemotePortHandle` (and `DaemonServices` the same, `src/mawsd/endpoint-seam.ts`), the handle `{answer(input: {id, answer}): void; close(): void}`: the endpoint registers a `PortTarget` for each channel's ref at admission, the front's `route(ref)` consults those after its own connections, and the handle's `answer` settles the pending request through `createClientPort`'s own `answer(from, input)` with the registered target as `from` (the identity it checks), so the request, the deadline (`CLIENT_PORT_DEADLINE_MS`, 10 s for `openExternal` and `reveal`) and the correlation stay as they are and `clientPort.answer` stays a `machine.sock` client's own; `close` at the channel's end settles its pending requests `noClient` (`gone(target)`). The wire: `client {id, kind, request}` endpoint to device and `clientAnswer {id, answer}` device to endpoint, behind `rl2.client-port`, sent only to a device whose `h1` lists it (else the target posts false and the port answers `noClient` as today); the device answers `openExternal {url}` by opening the URL here (`src/main/open-external.ts`, which refuses a loopback URL of a remote session as M3 taught it), `openExternal {path}` and `reveal` with `{opened: false, reason: 'a path on <machine>'}` (`reveal` has no failure form; it answers `noClient`), and any other kind `noClient`. The callback: the consent's redirect lands on the browser's own loopback, this Mac's, where nothing listens. O4's seam (`ConnectorOAuthFlowDeps.listen`, `SignInDeps.listen`) chooses the receiver, and M5c adds the relayed one: `connectors.connect`, `reconnect` and `signIn` gain an optional `callback?: {port}` (additive; §O), which the adapter fills from a loopback listener main binds on `127.0.0.1:0` before the act goes (`src/main/lane-b/remote-port.ts`); the flow, seeing `callback`, takes a `ListenLoopback` that binds nothing and answers that port (`src/services/connectors/relayed-callback.ts`), registers the client for `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/callback`, opens the URL through the port (above), and waits for the relayed request. The act and the flow have different lives: `connectors.connect` and `reconnect` acknowledge at once (`{accepted: true}`, `owner.ts`) and the OAuth work goes on for up to `CONNECT_CALLBACK_TIMEOUT_MS` (120 s), so the listener lives from before the act until the act is refused, or the flow ends on the machine (`connectors.serversChanged` with `ended: {sourceHandle}` naming this act's action, once a relayed request settled the flow, or `signInChanged` naming the operation), or its time has passed (amended as built, 2026-11-02: 331 s from acceptance for a Connect, `OWNER_ACTION_DEADLINE_MS` plus a round trip, and 311 s for a sign-in, `CONFIG_MS` plus `CALLBACK_MS` plus a round trip, under a hard cap from the bind; the 120 s `CONNECT_CALLBACK_TIMEOUT_MS` runs from the flow's bind on the machine, after discovery, which this side cannot see), or the channel that accepted the act ends (the machine ends a flow whose asker ended, and a new channel's ref reaches no flow), whichever first, relaying every request it receives meanwhile (an answered request does not end it: the machine's handler answers 404 and 405 without settling the flow, and after a successful callback the flow still owns its receiver through the token exchange, `oauth-flow.ts`'s `finally`); one listener per flow, and its port is held on this Mac until the listener ends, never rebound meanwhile, so a `(client, port)` names at most one live flow on the machine. The machine arms a receiver per flow with its own id, refuses `connectors.connect`, `reconnect` or `signIn` whose `callback.port` this client already has armed (`{accepted: false, reason: 'busy'}` for the owner commands; the sign-in's receipt then a `signInChanged` failure), and a flow's cleanup removes only the registration it owns, so a late callback never reaches a successor and a successor is never removed by its predecessor's end. A port is unique on one device, not across the machine's clients, so the armed receiver on the machine is keyed by the asking `ClientRef` *and* the port (the flow has the asker: `openBrowser(url, asker)`), and `connectors.callback` is dispatched under the channel's ref as every request is, so a callback reaches only a flow its own channel armed, two devices arming one port number never meet, and a member guessing a port cannot settle another's consent (the shipped handler marks a flow settled before it rejects a wrong `state`, so routing, not `state`, is the isolation). The relayed request carries the redirect whole: `connectors.callback {port, method, url}` (`url` the request's path and query as received, 4 KiB at most; a `machine` command graded `list`), which main sends as a call when the listener receives the redirect; the machine runs the flow's own callback handler over it (`oauth-flow.ts`'s, which checks the method, refuses duplicate parameters, reads an `error` response and a wrong issuer, and answers an HTTP status and body) and answers `{status, headers?, body}`, which the listener writes to the browser, so a denied consent, a replayed state and a wrong issuer behave as they do locally; a request for a `(client, port)` no flow armed answers `{status: 404, body: ''}`; PKCE's verifier never leaves the machine. The engine's sign-in whose engine keeps its own `localhost` listener, and `gws`'s login, listen on the machine and cannot be relayed: `connectors.signIn` answers its receipt `{opId}` as today and the flow ends at once with `connectors.signInChanged`'s failure, its reason "sign in on `<machine>`", when the asker is remote (a `callback` is present) and the engine takes no custom redirect; `delivery.connect` for Drive is not offered (D38: no `gws` on the mini; "Connect Drive on `<machine>`"). `show_pane` (`maws.pane.show`) is not carried: it is an engine-host event gated on an app attached at that machine, nothing records which device sent the turn's input (P5 §6's rule), and its answer is a `converse` act; the follow-up is named in the Decision Log, with the parent-impact. Rejected: building the port relay without the consent relay (no caller); a device-side listener bound after the flow registered (the redirect URI is in the registration); a listener bound to the act's life (the act answers before the person consents); a normalized `{state, code}` callback (it could not carry a denied consent or a duplicate parameter, and the browser's reply is the handler's); the port relay carrying page identity (one owner per device is enough for leases, above).

*`WIRE_MINOR` and the RL2 minor.* M5a: `host.artifactOp`, `machine.op`'s claim `target`, `library.read`'s `export` and `artifacts.copyAsset`'s grade (one bump at landing, master's plus one; `artifacts.open`'s `readOnly` and `remote.openCopy` are main-owned, outside the manifest) and `rl2.artifacts` and `rl2.bytes` (`RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` to master's plus one). M5b: no manifest change; `rl2.read-open` and `STREAM_REFUSALS`' `read-only` under it. M5c: `delivery.send`'s owner, the three `callback` fields, `connectors.callback`, `machine.delivery` (one bump; `connectors.reset`'s `machine` is main-owned) and `rl2.client-port`. Each landing re-reads both at its merge and runs `e2e:cross-build` on the merge tree; the recorded handshake vectors stay pinned (M2's `HandshakeOptions.protocol`).

**Does not touch.** The engine's binary and its tool server; the artifact store's schema and the document service's authorization (the actor stays the human's); the scheme handler's headers and bridge (the machine inserts them); `pty` and `tcp` streams and their act path; the browser kind beyond its name (O17 M9); the catalog's person half and Discover; `show_pane`'s targeting (a follow-up); O8's per-member attribution; the local app's paths for a local artifact.

**Proves.** Acceptance 2's artifact item (open and edit; screenshot and export on the machine are O5's, shown remotely here through `session.capabilities` and `artifacts.export`), 6, and A10's answer.
- *M5a.*
  - **`test/endpoint/artifact-rule.test.ts`**: a machine owner `manage` on an artifact with no links and on one linked to a session they don't own; a member with `view` on one of two linked sessions admitted at `view` and refused `manage`; a member with no rank on any linked session refused `grade`; an orphan refused `grade` to a non-owner; an unknown id `grade`; `artifacts.create` by a non-owner refused; `copyAsset` under its production declaration refused `grade` with `view` lacking on `from` and with `manage` lacking on `to`, admitted with both; a query of an artifact act after the endpoint's restart re-placed from the claim's `artifact:` target and admitted, and refused `grade` once the member's last share is withdrawn; a share withdrawn dropping the cache entry (the next request refused); an `artifacts.changed {linked}` dropping it; a `library.entry` that fails answering `unavailable`; the spawner of a session the source lists no record for (M11's bridge) holding `manage` on that session's artifacts, and only what the record grants once it arrives, the entry dropped by the arrival; every linked session's record read through `Reads.record` (a record arriving mid-decision re-deciding the artifact).
  - **`test/endpoint/artifact-rows.test.ts`**: each M5a row admitted for a holder and refused for a non-holder (the constraint's rule); `artifacts.list` with `filter.appSessionId` placed on that session, without a filter `no-target`; `library.list` filtered to the member's artifacts, whole for a machine owner, and `library.spaces` at `list`; `artifacts.update` as an engine-host act with no context running once through `host.op` and a reseal answering the stored result; an `uploadAsset` act whose tagged `args` pass the real `HostOpInputSchema` and, decoded, the ledger's own `validateCommandInput`, the handler receiving a `Uint8Array` and the ledger's record staying tagged JSON after a reopen; a context-less `host.op` answered `invalid_input` by a fake host that validates with the pre-M5 `HostOpInputSchema` (the old schema, not a hand-written refusal) read as `refused unavailable`; a `library.list` whose rows carry production-shaped `sessions` filtered for member B and whole for the owner; `session.capabilities` admitted at `view`; `library.read` with `export` admitted for an export under the artifact's directory and refused `malformed` for a name outside the grammar or a path elsewhere.
  - **`test/endpoint/feeds.test.ts`**, extended: the `artifacts` feed delivering `artifacts.changed` for a viewable artifact and not for another's, `library.changed` always; `session.capabilitiesChanged` on the `checkout` feed of its session only.
  - **`src/engine-host/ops.test.ts`**, extended: `host.artifactOp` running a session-less command once and refusing a session command; `host.opQuery` answering its `target`.
  - **`src/main/machines/remote/dispatch.test.ts`** and **`src/main/machines/socket-link.test.ts`**: a budgeted name waiting past 10 s and an unbudgeted one timing out at 10 s.
  - **`src/main/lane-b/remote-artifacts.test.ts`**: the table filled from calls and rows; the third act shape; the substitute for `session.capabilities` on `not-offered` and on a link down; `remote.openCopy` paging a file, a library file and an export into the temporary directory and opening it, refusing one over the bound; `library.saveAs` of a remote artifact paging the export and saving.
  - **`src/services/library/files.test.ts`**, extended: `library.read`'s export form.
  - **`test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`**, extended: a remote session's artifacts listed, one read, renamed across a forced drop once, exported with a 20 s fake render and answered (the budget), and the capabilities read and changed through the feed; bytes through the real channel: `artifacts.versionState` answered as a `Uint8Array` the renderer's schema accepts, and a 64 KiB `uploadAsset` sealed, verified and written by the fake host as the bytes sent (never through a fake dispatch), both refused locally `not-offered` against an endpoint listing no `rl2.bytes`.
  - Renderer suites: the Artifacts tool, viewer and library passing the machine; the library's machine menu; the ExternalRow and Delivery rows opening a copy and Reveal greyed.
  - **`e2e:remote`**, extended: from A, B's session's text artifact lists and reads; a rename shows in B's library (read over B's `machine.sock`); Save As of a PDF export lands in A's scratch directory (the save dialog's e2e seam); Open externally on a file of B's checkout opens a copy (the open-log seam); the Browser tool's notice reads B's reason. Hosted groups: `e2e:remote`, `e2e:library`, `e2e:artifacts`, `e2e:code`, `e2e:machines`, with `e2e:cross-build` on the merge tree.
- *M5b.*
  - **`test/endpoint/streams.test.ts`**, extended: an `http` open with no `op` admitted at `view` and refused `grade` for a non-holder; a malformed URL `malformed`; a GET's status, headers and body under credit with the body paused while credit is spent; a `Range` answering 206; a HEAD closing after `streamOpened`; a 404 from the fake endpoint passed through; an `edit` document open at `manage` and a `view` one at `view`, an `edit` by a `view` holder refused `grade`, one in read-only mode refused `read-only` (the word passing the validator of a peer listing `rl2.read-open`); frames relayed both ways across chunk boundaries; a `view` open dropping the device's update; a share withdrawn closing an open stream `removed` within the record change (no `caps` refresh); a relink changing the verdict; `documents.onClosed {host}` closing the stream `host`; an open naming `op` still taking M3's path; a `browser.screencast` open refused `malformed` by the validator (the reservation).
  - **`src/main/artifacts/remote-scheme.test.ts`**: the responder chain's order; the referrer rule; the concurrency cap; `Content-Length` checked; 503 and 504.
  - **`src/main/artifacts/remote-open.test.ts`**: the port bridged to a stream; `edit` then `view` on refusal with `readOnly`; a reopen after `carrier` and after `host` with the provider's unacked updates carried.
  - **`test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`**, extended: an html page fetched through the real endpoint (a scratch store under the fake services) and served to a `Request`; a document edited from two devices converging; a drop mid-edit and the edit landing.
  - **`e2e:remote`**, extended: an html artifact of B's session renders in A's frame; a document typed in A reads back from B's store (the scene's one member is the owner; the `view`-only member's read-only editor is `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`'s, over the harness's member B). Hosted groups: `e2e:remote`, `e2e:document`, `e2e:artifacts`, `e2e:dashboard`, `e2e:machines`.
- *M5c.*
  - **`test/endpoint/client-port.test.ts`**: a port request for a channel's ref reaching the device as `client` and its `clientAnswer` settling the service's call through the handle (the front's `answer` with the registered target as `from`; an answer under another channel's handle refused); a device listing no `rl2.client-port` answered `noClient`; the deadline; a channel's close settling its pending requests `noClient`.
  - **`test/endpoint/artifact-rows.test.ts`**, extended: each lease command at its floor and `connectors.call` at `converse` by effect; a `view` holder admitted to `open` and refused `call`; the last qualifying share withdrawn while a watch is active and a result in flight: the lease closed by the endpoint, the result not forwarded, the next `connectors.call` refused `grade`; `delivery.status` at `view`, `delivery.send` at `manage` by an owner with no step-up, `connectors.servers` at `machine-admin` with step-up; `client.windowShown` reaching the broker under the channel's ref; two channels arming the same port number each completed by their own callback alone, another channel's callback for the port answering 404 and settling nothing; `catalog.engine` `not-offered`.
  - **`src/services/connectors/oauth-flow.test.ts`** and **`sign-in.test.ts`**, extended: the relayed receiver registering the device's port, the connect acknowledging `{accepted: true}` before any callback and completing on a relayed request that arrives later; a denied consent (`error=access_denied`), a duplicate `state`, a replayed callback and a wrong `iss` each answered as the local handler answers them (status and body) and sealing nothing; two successive flows on one channel naming the same port before the first ends: the second refused `busy`, a late callback of the first reaching only the first, and the first's cleanup leaving a later registration alone; the engine-held listener ending a remote asker's sign-in with `signInChanged`'s failure after its receipt.
  - **`src/services/delivery/index.test.ts`**, extended: `delivery.send` on the machine exporting then delivering, a cancel during the export honoured, the embedded root's injected export.
  - **`src/main/connectors/windows.test.ts`**, extended: reports per machine, the OR over pages, before the first lease act.
  - **`src/main/lane-b/remote-port.test.ts`**: the listener bound before the act and living past the act's acknowledgement and past an answered request (a 404 relayed, then the real callback) to the act's refusal, the flow's end on the feed, the bound or the link's close, whichever first; its port never rebound while held; the browser's reply written as answered; `{url}` opened here; `{path}` answered not here.
  - **`src/main/lane-b/remote-artifacts.test.ts`**, extended: the six lease reads sent as calls through the real `routeLaneB` and `dispatchOverLink` (never refused `act-required` locally) and `connectors.call` as an act; a `copyAsset` between machines refused.
  - **`test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`**, extended: a dashboard's lease on the harness refreshing while shown and held while hidden; a forced carrier drop with a live watch: the new channel reports the window, `connectors.reset {machine}` for that machine alone, the watch resumed under new ids, the local machine's leases untouched; the local machine host's own reconnect while a remote dashboard watches: its reset names the local machine and the remote lease and its watch stay as they were; a Connect through the relayed callback sealing a grant on the fake route D; Send to on the harness's delivery.
  - **`e2e:remote`**, extended: a dashboard with live data on B (the dashboard-live-data fixture) refreshes in A; a Notion send from A for B's artifact makes one fake `notion-create-pages` call on B; Connect from A completes through A's loopback against the OAuth fixture. Hosted groups: `e2e:remote`, `e2e:dashboard-live-data` (on a Mac desktop by its gate-table placement), `e2e:connectors`, `e2e:connectors-oauth`, `e2e:delivery`, `e2e:machines`, with `e2e:cross-build` on the merge tree.

### M7 — Acceptance and the remote bench

Everything above is run from the MacBook against the mini and the Linux VM, and the bench of OX7 gains a remote mode that measures item 3's numbers on each carrier.

**At its end.**
- Acceptance 2, 4, 5, 8, 9, 10 and 11 hold live, recorded in Outcomes with the owner's sitting notes.
- `e2e:remote-latency`, a measured group, reports on the mini, for the direct and the relay carrier, remote delta-to-paint, command round trip, open-to-first-row and resume after a drop, each within item 3's bound against the same run's local numbers.
- The live bench from the MacBook against a scratch machine on the mini and one on the VM records the same numbers over the real tailnet, delta-to-paint judged over the clock offset's whole interval, or marked inconclusive where the interval is wider than the budget allows.
- P5 acceptance 7's app half (M1's F8; not this document's item 7): after a sleep and wake, every remote subscription resumes within 1 s of the network returning (the link's probes), read from the link's log lines in the live sitting and from the forced-drop case of the measured group.

**Touches.** New: `e2e/remote-latency.spec.ts`, `test/support/remote-latency.ts`; changed: `apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts`, `channel.ts` and `src/endpoint/channels.ts` (`pong` gains `received` and `sent`; `rl2.pong-clock` in `src/endpoint/capabilities.ts` and `src/main/machines/remote/capabilities.ts`; `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor`), `src/main/machines/remote/link.ts` (a probe's four stamps kept for the bench), `package.json`, `.github/workflows/gate.yml` (`MEASURED_E2E`), this document's Outcomes.

**Decisions.**

*The measured group* (§S). `e2e:remote-latency` reuses `e2e:remote`'s scene with the latency stub engine (`test/support/latency-engine-stub.mjs`) on profile B and the local-latency measures (`e2e/local-latency.spec.ts`) taken through M2's pane: 3 turns of 300 deltas, the first discarded; 220 `session.getContextUsage` invokes; the time from the row's click to the first transcript row; a forced carrier drop and the time from its restore to the first event after the gap. It runs the local case first on the same tree, then direct, then relay (two launches of A, as `e2e:remote` does), and records all three to `test-results/remote-latency.json` with the link's own round trip (the median of 100 `ping`/`pong` pairs) per carrier; the bounds are item 3's, each relative to the same run's local number and round trip. One machine, one clock, as the local bench. It is `MEASURED_E2E` only, on the mini under CI-opt's hold, on the architect's go; validity is Rule B's, read as the latency bench's is.

*The live bench.* The same spec in a `remote` mode against a real machine: a scratch profile's `mawsd` on the mini (`mini-bench`) and on the acceptance VM (`vm-bench`), each running the latency stub, enrolled through the owner's app with a code and revoked after (O9 M10's scratch-device pattern), so no production session is touched. Two clocks: `pong` echoes the device's `at` today and carries nothing of the endpoint's clock, so M7 adds two optional fields to it, `received` and `sent` (the endpoint's milliseconds at the ping's arrival and the pong's send; `readMessage` passes unknown fields of a known kind, and the endpoint lists `rl2.pong-clock` so the bench knows to expect them), and the spec estimates the offset NTP-style from at least 50 exchanges at the run's start and end: the estimate is `((received − at) + (sent − arrived)) / 2`, and its error is bounded by half the round trip of that exchange, whatever the spread, since a route's asymmetry hides inside it (the local bench's own clock check requires the whole possible interval to fit its tolerance, and this one does the same). So delta-to-paint is judged over the interval `[estimate − rtt/2, estimate + rtt/2]`, the bound holding only when its worst edge holds, and the run reports `inconclusive` for a carrier whose interval is wider than the bound's own allowance (10 ms plus the round trip): on the tailnet (a few milliseconds) it decides; through the hub (tens of milliseconds) it is expected to be inconclusive, and the measured group on one clock is the relay's number. The spread across exchanges is reported as well; a spread over 5 ms voids the run. It runs on the owner's go, from the MacBook, and never on the gate.

*The live sitting.* Acceptance 2 and the rest run by hand against the owner's real sessions, as O9 M10 did: the checklist is in Concrete Steps, each item ticked with its evidence in Outcomes. It needs the mini and the VM cut over (done), the MacBook signed in (O8 M1), lane B records on both machines (A11), and the tailnet listener reachable from the MacBook.

**Does not touch.** The product beyond `pong`'s two fields and the capability that announces them.

**Proves.** Item 3, P5 acceptance 7's app half, and the live run of items 2, 4, 5, 8, 9, 10 and 11.

### M8 — The sitting's failures *(planned 2026-11-04, from M7's live sitting)*

The live sitting failed two of O7's own acceptance lines. Answering a question from another Mac failed (acceptance 2, P5 acceptance 4). A remote-spawned session had no sidebar row on the Mac that spawned it, and so no way back once its tab closed (acceptance 4, P5 acceptance 5). The other failures were remote surfaces showing an error where local works. M8 removes each observed failure, each proved red first. Where the sitting left a cause unknown, the milestone's first task finds it before anything is built on it.

**At its end.**
- A lane B session spawned from this app on another machine shows within its org project's visible rows within 5 s of the engine's first event, even in a project group already holding more busy local records than the cap, and stays reachable through Resume… after its tab closes. A second client, one that did not spawn it, sees and opens it the same way.
- A question raised on a remote MAWS-launched session can be read and answered from this Mac. These sessions only ever raise the MAWS ask. The answer settles the card on the machine and in every attached app.
- Every command the renderer issues for a session is either offered over RL2 at its kind and grade, or its control is drawn disabled for a remote session, with its reason. A contract test fails on any session-scoped command that is neither offered nor declared remote-unavailable.
- An operation on a remote session (`/btw`, Reload plugins, MCP Reconnect and toggle, Generate title) completes in the app. Its `session.operation` and the panels' own change events cross the channel.
- A remote turn's end clears what the app draws as working. The engine host's `activity` clears at a turn's end, local and remote. An event the device can't read leaves a gap bar, never silence.
- A PDF dropped on a remote session reaches the model the way a local one does, as a `document` block.
- The session's MCP widget offers Sign in on a remote server that needs auth, and runs M5c's relayed sign-in. Reconnect stays a restart.
- Review shows an untracked file's content as added, local and remote.
- Scheduled jobs on a machine with none reads "no jobs", not an error. A refusal from the machine's services carries its message to the device.

**Touches.**
- Endpoint:
  - `src/endpoint/offers.ts`: §U's M8 rows;
  - `src/endpoint/calls.ts`: the forwarded failure's message;
  - `src/endpoint/targets.ts`: the `task-list` target rule;
  - `src/endpoint/rl10/members.ts`: the lane B listing id;
  - `src/shared/host/stream-catchup.ts` and `src/endpoint/subscriptions*.ts`: the operation and panel parts.
- Main:
  - `src/main/lane-b/remote.ts`: `ENGINE_HOST_ACTS` and `handles` for the new acts;
  - `src/main/lane-b/remote-session.ts`: the parts emitted as their events; an unreadable event as a gap;
  - `src/main/machines/remote/dispatch.ts`: no change in behaviour, pinned by the contract test.
- Engine host:
  - `src/engine-host/session-status.ts`: `activity` at a turn's end;
  - `src/engine-host/operations.ts`: completed outcomes kept by `opId`, and `session.operations`.
- The machine's send: the endpoint's expansion of `uploads` through the files service (`src/services/files/upload.ts`'s resolver), behind `rl2.upload-blocks`.
- Shared: `src/shared/ipc/remote-unavailable.ts` (new), the declared set with each command's reason.
- Renderer:
  - the stores and surfaces named in §U: the sidebar join (`stores/org-projects.ts`, `sidebar/derive.ts`), the question panel, the side panels, the widgets and Session info, which grey from the declared set;
  - `widgets/McpWidget.tsx`'s Sign in;
  - `tools/code/review/` (untracked rows), `tools/agents/background/scheduled-file.ts`.
- `src/services/connectors/sign-in.ts`: the entry resolved on the session's machine.
- Tests: `test/lane-b/offer-coverage.test.ts` (new), and the red-first tests each Decision names.

**Interfaces.** Produces §U's M8 rows, the `remote-unavailable` set, the `operation` and panel parts. Consumes §L, §R and M2's pane and adapter.

**Decisions.**

*The task order.* Task m8-1 is a red-first diagnosis pass. It writes the failing tests for each item whose cause the sitting did not settle, and records each cause in the Decision Log before any fix:
- the sidebar's listing id;
- the stale "Working…";
- the @ reference's probe;
- `files.read`'s `internal`.
The fixes follow in tasks grouped by layer, so that no two executors edit one file at once:
- m8-2: the endpoint and the shared rows;
- m8-3: main's adapter and relays;
- m8-4: the renderer;
- m8-5: the contract test and the e2e cases.

m8-1's first commit also pins a clock. `test/support/remote-bench.test.ts`'s "the mini's removal on this Mac…" builds its `BenchRun` with no clock, so it refuses on the real time inside the mini's 06:45–07:00Z quiet window. Any unit gate that falls in that window fails (the desk track's #464, run 41788418951 at 06:56Z). That test, and any sibling built the same way, takes a clock pinned outside the window. One added case, pinned inside the window, expects the refusal.

*The sidebar row* (acceptance 4). *(Rewritten 2026-11-04 after m8-1.)* The id-mismatch premise of the first plan is withdrawn. The sitting's listing (`1c155c56`, lane B) is the app id, and the device's join by app id meets it (m8-1's Decision Log entry). The row was lost to the sidebar's cut:
- A project group shows `SESSIONS_FIRST` (5) rows, or `SESSIONS_CAP` (8) after Show more (`src/renderer/src/sidebar/derive.ts`, `shownCount`).
- Under the default `priority` sort, pinned, waiting, unread and working local records lead, while a listed row ranks only by its `issued` time.
- The Resume… picker lists records only (`resume/model.ts`, `chooserRows`).

A local session past the cap is reached through Resume…, but a listed one has no way back. The fix is parity with local (the architect's call, 2026-11-04):
- **Resume… lists listed rows.** `chooserRows` includes the listed rows of the person's org projects, each under its machine, and opening one goes through lane B's opener (`ListedRow.activate`'s path, `remoteOpenerFor`).
- **The priority sort ranks a listed row by its own state, as it ranks a local record.**
  - Working, idle and the rest come from the listing's status.
  - Waiting comes from M6's attention feed (the listed row's address in `remote.attention`).
  - The listed row's last activity is its listing's latest `issued` or status change.

  A just-spawned, working remote session then leads the way a working local one does.
- **A session spawned from this app leads its group until its first turn ends, on any machine, local and remote alike** (the architect's ruling of 2026-11-04 under P5 acceptance 5, a sidebar-order change made in the owner's place, which the owner can overrule). Today local ranks unread above working (`src/renderer/src/overview/derive.ts`), so a just-spawned local session in a group holding more unread sessions than the cap is cut too. After the first turn it ranks by its state, and Resume… reaches it past the cap. Red first in `derive.test.ts`, for both lanes: a group already holding more unread sessions than the cap, then a just-spawned local session and a just-spawned remote one, each within the visible rows until its first turn ends.
- "Always drawn" (listed rows exempt from the cap) is rejected: a member with many listed sessions would get an uncapped group.

The acceptance is the failure the owner saw. In a project group already holding more busy local records (pinned, waiting, unread, working) than the cap, a just-spawned remote session shows within the visible rows within 5 s and stays reachable through Resume… after its tab closes.

Red first:
- `src/renderer/src/sidebar/derive.test.ts`: m8-1's `it.fails`, flipped by m8-4, plus the Resume… case in `resume/model.test.ts` or its nearest test;
- `e2e:remote`: more than eight busy local sessions in one project, a session spawned on B within the visible rows, its tab closed, and the session reopened through Resume… (m8-5).

Rejected: keying the device's records by engine id, and treating an engine id as lane B at the endpoint (m8-1 found no evidence for either).

*The offer audit and its contract.* A command reaches a remote machine only through `dispatchOverLink`. A name with no row is `not-offered` at the endpoint. A name graded above `list`/`view` that the adapter doesn't `handle` is refused on the device, before any round trip, as "refused act-required" (`src/main/machines/remote/dispatch.ts`). That is the sitting's `session.reloadPlugins`: it has no row at all, so the brief's "a call against an act row" is the device's own refusal. *2026-11-13 (UTC):* Amended by the UX wave (U9; O-48, S-02; `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-12-ux-wave-U9-other-machines.md`), on the owner's words: the observation behind O-48 ("I cannot create a fork session from the remote machine …") and "Approve and start", 2026-11-12. From the UX wave (U9), behind `rl2.session-acts`: a fork in place (the machine's owners), archive, unarchive, rename, the owner's read of archived sessions, dismissing an attention item, taking back a waiting message and a pane's answer are offered. Launch, start, the setup of a new session, delete, take over and replays stay unoffered.
- **The rule.** Every command whose route class is `addressed` by an `AppSessionId` and that is on the link (`src/main/machines/link-surface.ts`) is one of two things:
  - offered at its registry grade, as `laneB('call')` when graded `list` or `view`, or as `laneB('act')` named in `ENGINE_HOST_ACTS`;
  - declared in `REMOTE_UNAVAILABLE` (`src/shared/ipc/remote-unavailable.ts`) with its reason. The renderer draws its control disabled with that reason for a session on another machine.
- **The contract test.** `test/lane-b/offer-coverage.test.ts` iterates `REGISTRY.commands` with `routeOf` and asserts the rule. It also asserts:
  - every act row is in the adapter's `handles`;
  - every unhandled call row is graded plain `list` or `view`;
  - nothing is both offered and declared.
- **M8's rows** are §U's table: the MAWS ask, the side panels, the side question, Reload plugins and the widget reads.
- **`fs.tasks` is offered** (P5 §5: what the panel shows for a local session works for a remote one). A task list belongs to its session: the renderer derives the list id from the session's status (`taskListId`, else its sanitised engine session id; `src/renderer/src/tools/agents/model/task-list.ts`). So the call names the session too: `fs.tasks`'s input gains `appSessionId?`, which the remote call always carries. A new target rule, `task-list`, resolves that session as the `checkout` rule does and admits the call only when `listId` is that session's own list id, at the registry's `view`; anything else is refused `no-target`. `fs.tasksChanged` crosses as the `tasks` part. The added input field, `session.operations` and `session.send`'s `uploads?` are additive wire changes, so M8 takes `WIRE_MINOR` master's plus one at its landing (P5 Decision Log 44).
- **`mods.list`** (machine-wide, no session target) is declared, with its reason, and gets a tracker line.
- **The MAWS ask's grades.** `maws.asks.list` is `view`. `maws.ask.answer` is plain `converse`, the same as `session.answer`'s for a question (`src/endpoint/by-effect.ts`, `QUESTION_REQUESTS` already holds `maws.ask.opened`). The answer runs through the host as a `next` steer (`src/engine-host/session-maws.ts`), so the adapter resumes a released session first, as it does for `session.send`.
- **An older peer.** An older peer answers `not-offered` for a new row. The renderer greys a surface for a session on that machine once it hears a refusal for it, extending `heardRefusal` (`src/renderer/src/artifacts/remote-surfaces.ts`) from the connector surfaces to every session surface.
- Rejected:
  - a capability key per surface (the `CONTROL_KEYS` list is fixed, and each key would be a WIRE change);
  - offering everything unfiltered (a machine-wide read leaks other sessions).

*Operations and panel events* (`/btw`, Reload plugins, MCP Reconnect). An operation answers a receipt, and its completion is the `session.operation` event (`src/renderer/src/widgets/operation.ts`). The lane B stream carries only status, queue, broker items, the catalog and event batches (`src/shared/host/stream-catchup.ts`, `partOf`), so no remote operation ever completes. That is the sitting's `/btw` at "Answering…".
- An `operation` part joins the stream: the engine host's `session.operation` for that session, emitted by `remote-session.ts` as `session.operation` under the machine's address. Operations are not replace-whole state: the stream keeps only the latest value per part while opening (`stream-catchup.ts`) and a wholly unsent snapshot with the same key is replaced (`src/endpoint/subscriptions.ts`, `snap`), so each completion is its own part keyed `operation:<opId>`, never one slot.
- Outcomes survive a reconnect. `OperationTable.complete` (`src/engine-host/operations.ts`) emits and forgets, and `useOperation` waits for its own `opId` indefinitely, so the engine host keeps each completed outcome by `opId` (at most 64 per session, 10 minutes). The engine host also tracks the opIds in flight (`OperationTable.start` separates the receipt from the completion). A new `session.operations {appSessionId, opIds}` (`view`, `laneB('call')`) answers each one as its event (completed), `pending` (still in flight) or `unknown`. At every reopen the adapter asks it for the opIds its waiters hold. It emits what has completed, keeps `pending` waiters alive for their completion part, and settles an `unknown` one as failed with "the machine lost this operation's result; check the transcript". A late completion for a settled opId is dropped.
- Red first, beside those below: two completions while the subscription has no credit (both delivered); a completion during the opening; a disconnect between the receipt and the completion, with the operation completed (recovered), still running (`pending`, completed later by its part), or forgotten (settled `unknown`).
- So do the panels' own change events, each a part of the same kind: `fs.subagentsChanged`, `fs.workflowChanged`, and the side question's changes.
- `side.question.list` (call) and `side.question.clear` (act) are offered, so `refreshSide` works.
- The parts ride the existing stream with no new capability. The task proves it on the device reader that matters: `remote-session.ts` at master's build given an unknown part leaves it out (or marks the gap the rule below requires); `stream-catchup.ts`'s `partOf` is the producer's classifier, not that reader.
- Red first:
  - `src/main/lane-b/remote-session.test.ts`: an operation part emitted as `session.operation`;
  - `test/endpoint/subscriptions-b.test.ts`: the part carried;
  - the fixture `pass3/p1-side-question-session.jsonl`.

*What draws "Working…".* `activity` is set on a main-lane `system/status` frame and cleared only by another status frame (`src/engine-host/session-status.ts`). The fixtures end 389 of 401 turns with `requesting` as the last status, tool or no tool. So the VM's `activity: requesting` after `end_turn` is every turn's state, local too. No renderer code reads it: "Working…" is an open turn in the transcript store or the composer's submit state.
- `activity` clears at a main-lane `result` (red first in `src/engine-host/session-status.test.ts` over `permission-roundtrip.jsonl`). It is cheap and right, but it is not the sitting's symptom.
- The rule, whatever m8-1 finds: an event the device can't read is a visible gap, never a silent drop. `remote-session.ts` marks `remote.gap` for it and re-reads the session's status (`session.status`, and the record through `session.get`), so a turn left open by a dropped `turn.ended` heals at once. Red first in `src/main/lane-b/remote-session.test.ts`: an unknown-shaped `turn.ended` leaves a gap, the status re-read closes the turn, and the pane stops drawing "Working…".
- The symptom is a `turn.ended` the device never folded. `remote-session.ts` drops an event that fails `readTolerant(SessionEventSchema)` with a log line and no gap, and the sitting's app (01d4488cf) read a VM on 75944744d.
- m8-1 decides it red first, by feeding the older build's `turn.ended` shape to the newer reader. If the shapes differ, the reader also takes the older one.

*The attachment* (P5 acceptance 4's "take an image"; the sitting's "@ is not working"). The brief's premise is corrected by the code:
- Locally, a dropped PDF is a `document` content block (`composer/editor/paste.ts`, `serialize.ts`); only a file the API can't take becomes an `@path` mention.
- Remotely, the PDF is uploaded to the machine (`files.upload`) and the message carries `@<path relative to cwd>` (`remoteMessage`, `remote-uploads.ts`, tested). Whether the engine expands an `@path` in a stream-json user frame is unrecorded (no fixture), and the agent had to read the file with a tool.
- m8-1 adds a probe (`spikes/protocol-probe/`, run on CI's probe lane, never on this MacBook) that sends `@<pdf>` in a user frame and records what the engine attaches.
- The fix is parity, with a typed reference, never by parsing mentions. `session.send`'s input gains `uploads?: [{uploadId, as: 'document'}]`, an additive property behind the endpoint capability `rl2.upload-blocks`. The device sends it for an uploaded PDF, with the `@` mention kept in `text` for a reader, and without it to a machine that doesn't list the capability (today's behaviour).
- The endpoint expands it before the act reaches the engine host, so before the durable queue accepts the message. Its resolver is the machine's files service, which owns the confined per-session upload roots and their sweep (`src/services/files/upload.ts`). An `uploadId` resolves only inside the sending session's own upload root, by its real path with no symlink, to a file that still exists. The expansion reads it as a `document` block, checks the block against the engine's size limit, and appends it to `content`. A missing, expired, foreign or oversized upload refuses the send `malformed` with its reason, and nothing is queued.
- The queue holds the expanded blocks, so a queued send survives a restart and the upload's sweep.
- Red first (`test/endpoint/` beside the send rows): a cross-session `uploadId`, a traversal or symlink, an expired upload, an oversized one, and a queued send across a restart.
- Rejected: base64 over the channel (2 MiB parts already carry the bytes once), and the engine host parsing `@` mentions into reads (it would bypass the files service's confinement).

*Sign in from the session's MCP widget.* `startSignIn` needs `{name, scope, cwd, path}` and the session's address (`customize/connectors/sign-in.ts`). The widget has the name, scope and status from `mcp_status` and the cwd from the session's status, but not `path`: on a remote machine `customization.serverEndpoints` is main-only and not offered.
- `connectors.signIn` resolves the entry on the session's machine from `{name, scope, cwd}`, with `path` optional and the machine's own `customization.serverEndpoints` read there.
- The widget offers Sign in on `needs-auth`, beside Reconnect.
- Show on the remote Connectors sheet is meant to ask for Touch ID, and does: M5c's sheet is "Show, then Touch ID, then Refresh for any re-read" (M5c's Outcomes), because `connectors.servers` is `machine-admin` (A13 and A14 keep credential and grant management there; F17), and `stepUpRequired` asks for every `machine-admin` act whoever owns the machine (`src/endpoint/step-up.ts`; asserted by `test/endpoint/artifact-rows.test.ts`). The owner saw no Touch ID because they were on the MCP widget, whose read is `session.mcpStatus`, a `view` call that asks nothing. No fix.
- Red first: `src/renderer/src/widgets/McpWidget.test.tsx`, `src/services/connectors/sign-in.test.ts`.
- Amended 2026-11-13: UX wave U11 (l8-settings-01): a server the engine reports in scope `dynamic` (a plugin's, or one of MAWS's own list) is signed in from a session that holds it; its identity is its machine and its name.

*Untracked files.* Review lists untracked rows with no hunks and says "not in the diff" (`tools/code/review/ReviewShell.tsx`, asserted by `review.test.tsx`). Source control already asks `git.diff {scope: 'untracked', path}`, served by the git service with `--no-index /dev/null <path>`.
- Review's `loadFile` asks the same, so an untracked file shows as added. One path serves local and remote (`git.diff` is offered under `checkout`).
- The `/diff` widget is the engine's `get_workspace_diff`, whose untracked gap is the engine's (D-1): a tracker line, not a change.
- Red first: `review.test.tsx`, its `:685` expectation reversed.

*Scheduled jobs and `internal`.* The panel reads `<root>/.claude/scheduled_tasks.json` and counts a `not found:` message as no jobs (`tools/agents/background/scheduled-file.ts`). The machine's files service raises every failure as `internal` with its message. The endpoint's `#forward` passes only the code (`src/endpoint/calls.ts`), so a missing file on the VM reached the device as a bare `internal`.
- The forwarded failure carries its message, capped at 512 characters, as the local IPC carries it.
- m8-1 confirms that the VM's file was absent (as opposed to a root outside the session's directories) red first, in `test/endpoint/calls.test.ts` or the nearest forward test.

**Does not touch.** The engine (D-1). The A11 owners' verifier (O10's, the desk track's). M9's routes. The bench (M10).

**Proves.** Acceptance 2's question and the remote surfaces, 4's row, 5's second client, 6's greying with the reason, and 9's answer from this Mac, in tests and `e2e:remote`; live in M10. Review: reviewer-high, since offer rows extend the endpoint's authorization.
