### M9 — Spawn on the machine's own terms *(planned 2026-11-04)*

The owner's ruling: "We shouldn't be using ClaudeUsage, We should be just using what machine uses as its default inference." Their two answers: the `machine` route is the default on every machine, this app's own included ("Every machine (Recommended)"), and a project that isn't set up on the target machine is set up from the spawning app ("Set it up from here (Recommended)").

**At its end.**
- A third route, `machine`, launches the engine with nothing injected: no gateway variables, and none of the direct route's blanking `--settings`. The engine's own user settings, managed settings, environment and login decide, as `claude` in that machine's terminal would.
- MAWS records what `get_status` reports and names it: ClaudeUsage when the base URL is that machine's relay, the Anthropic login, or the host it names. It never calls that a mismatch.
- `machine` is every machine's default. Existing settings files read as `machine` unless they chose `direct`. No file is rewritten, and the choice lives in a new `machine-route.json`, and `gateway` and `direct` stay as per-session choices.
- ClaudeUsage's features (usage windows, accounts, gateway models) follow the observed route. Where the machine's own settings point at its relay, the pre-launch relay check and its sheet stay.
- "+ Session" on a machine with no checkout of the org project asks which folder on that machine holds it, browsing that machine's folders, and registers it there. A folder the machine doesn't trust shows that machine's trust card. Both answers are acts only the machine's owners hold.
- An older peer, an older retained engine host included, never sees a value it would read as malformed: every change is an additive property, and the legacy fields stay written.

**Touches.**
- Shared:
  - the route enum, one shared `RouteSchema` in `src/shared/engine/route.ts` (new), consumed by `launch-spec.ts`, `sessions/intent.ts`, `ipc/schema/session.ts` (`StartOptionsSchema`), `engine/status.ts`, `ipc/schema/engine.ts` and `ipc/schema/settings.ts`;
  - `src/shared/graph/session.ts`: `gateway.route` and `gateway.observed`, beside `routed`;
  - `src/shared/settings/defaults.ts`, `ipc/schema/settings.ts`, `src/services/settings/machine.ts` and `split.ts`: the new `machine-route.json` and the default's resolution, `machine-settings.json` unchanged;
  - `src/shared/ipc/wire.ts` and `wire-manifest.json`: `WIRE_MINOR`.
- Engine host:
  - `launch.ts` (`buildEnvironment`, `protectedSettings`, `LaunchPlan`);
  - `session.ts` (`verifyGateway`);
  - `holder/restore.ts`, `models-cache.ts`, `launcher.ts` (`checkRelay`).
- Services: `src/services/sessions/` (`service.ts`, `record-actions.ts`, `resume.ts`, `launch-mapping.ts`, `start-guards.ts`, `spawn.ts`).
- Endpoint:
  - `src/endpoint/offers.ts`: §U's M9 rows, and the `owners` row flag;
  - `authorize.ts`: the owner check;
  - `policy.ts`: `setupRefusal`, and `trust` in `spawnRefusal` for an owner only;
- The engine host's hello: the `engine.route-machine` capability (`src/shared/ipc/wire.ts`'s capability lists).
- Renderer:
  - `stores/usage.ts`, `composer/list-models.ts`, `gateway/*`, `settings-tab/GatewaySection.tsx`, `tools/session-info/`;
  - the spawn sheet (`sessions/remote-spawn.ts`, its sheet), with the folder picker and `TrustCard` for a remote machine.

**Interfaces.** Produces `RouteSchema`, the additive `route?` and `observed?` properties, `machine-route.json`, §U's M9 rows, `engine.route-machine`. Consumes D-19 as amended (P5 Decision Log 211, and its follow-up entry at M9's landing, which the architect writes).

**Decisions.**

*The wire rule binds the shape.* Inside a wire major, a change has to be additive. An added enum member and a changed `const` are majors (`src/shared/ipc/wire.ts`'s header). `e2e/cross-build.spec.ts` holds two builds of one major to zero breaking changes, and `machineSettings.get`/`set`/`changed` carry `MachineSettingsSchema` over the local daemon's wire. So M9 adds no enum member and changes no `version` literal anywhere. Every existing route enum keeps `['gateway', 'direct']` and machine settings stay at version 1. The third route travels as new optional properties, and an older peer reads the legacy fields it already knows. M9's bump is `WIRE_MINOR` master's plus one, taken at its landing (P5 Decision Log 44). Rejected: a wire major for one route (every peer and retained host would need the upgrade at once).

*The route.*
- `RouteSchema = z.enum(['gateway', 'direct', 'machine'])` is one definition (`src/shared/engine/route.ts`). It is used only by the new `route?` properties. The legacy `gateway` fields keep their two-member enums and are always written too, with the value an older reader should act on, called the legacy route below.
- The legacy route is `gateway` when the machine route's effective base URL is `RELAY_URL`, and `direct` otherwise. The effective base URL is resolved as in *The relay check and the features*.
- Carriers of the new `route?`: `LaunchSpec` (`launch-spec.ts`), the setup intent (`sessions/intent.ts`), `StartOptionsSchema`, `engine.models`'s input, and the record's `gateway`.
- A reader takes `route` when present, and the legacy field otherwise.
- On `route: 'machine'`:
  - `buildEnvironment` passes the captured shell environment's `ANTHROPIC_*` and `CLAUDE_*` keys through unchanged. It neither sets nor deletes `GATEWAY_KEYS`.
  - `protectedSettings` adds no `env`.
- An engine host that predates M9 ignores `route` and would launch on the legacy route. That is equivalent only in two cases: the resolved base URL is `RELAY_URL` (the gateway route reaches the same relay), or none of the keys the direct route deletes and blanks (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH`) resolves from the machine's environment or settings, so the blanking empties nothing the machine set. An uncertain resolution is never equivalent. *(Revised 2026-11-04 at m9-2's review: the URL alone missed a machine-set token; see the Decision Log.)* Main sends `route: 'machine'` only to an engine host whose hello lists the new capability `engine.route-machine`. For any other host, a retained older one included, main launches the legacy route only in those two cases. When the machine names another host, the start refuses with `route-unsupported`: "This machine's engine host predates the machine route; restart MAWS there, or choose gateway or direct for this session." It never silently changes inference.

*What the machine reports.*
- On `machine`, `verifyGateway` writes the status's existing fields with existing values:
  - `mode` is the legacy route;
  - `verified` is `routed` when the base URL is `RELAY_URL`, and `direct` otherwise, never `mismatch`;
  - no `gateway:mismatch` notice is emitted.
- It also writes two new optional status properties:
  - `gateway.route: 'machine'`;
  - `gateway.observed`, one of:
    - `{kind: 'claudeusage', baseUrl}` when the base URL is `RELAY_URL`;
    - `{kind: 'login', login?, organization?}` from the `Login` and `Organization` rows when there is no base URL;
    - `{kind: 'host', baseUrl}` otherwise.
  The fixtures `reads.jsonl`, `pass2/routing-user-settings.jsonl` and `pass2/auto-unrouted.jsonl` show these shapes.
- The record's `gateway` gains `route?` and `observed?`. It keeps `routed`: true when the session is observed through ClaudeUsage, false otherwise. So an older reader resuming it picks a route that reaches the same inference.
- Every reader that maps `routed` to a route reads `route` first: `record-actions.ts`, `resume.ts`, `launch-mapping.ts`, `holder/restore.ts`.
- All of these are additive, so RL2 needs no capability and no downgrade: an older device reads the legacy fields.
- Rejected: a `machine` route that also sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` when the machine's settings name the relay. That would be the gateway route under another name.

*The default, without a v2.* The new choice lives in a file of its own, `machine-route.json` beside `machine-settings.json` (`{version: 1, defaultRoute: Route}`), which only builds from M9 on read. An older build strict-parses `machine-settings.json` (`migrateMachineSettings`, `src/shared/settings/defaults.ts`), and a property it doesn't know there would make it substitute defaults for the whole file and refuse updates (`src/shared/settings/file-store.ts`). So `machine-settings.json` is never given a new property. A reader resolves the machine's default as follows:
- `machine-route.json`'s `defaultRoute` when the file is present;
- `direct` when `defaultRoute` is `direct`, which was never a default and so is the owner's choice;
- `machine` otherwise, which includes the old default `gateway` that every strict v1 file holds explicitly.

This rule is the migration. It covers both legacy entry paths without rewriting a file:
- a v1 machine file read through `machineSettingsRules.read` (`src/services/settings/machine.ts`);
- a unified v1–20 profile split by `splitSettingsFiles` (`src/services/settings/split.ts`, through `splitSettings`), which copies the old `gateway` choice into the machine file.

When a person sets the default:
- choosing any route writes `machine-route.json`. It also writes `machine-settings.json`'s `defaultRoute`: `gateway` for `gateway`, `direct` for `direct`, and the legacy route for `machine`. An older build on the same machine therefore reads a valid file with the nearest route it knows.

Tests cover:
- the on-disk results through the real file store: a v1 file holding `gateway`, one holding `direct`, a split unified profile, a set-then-reopen, a refused write, and an older build's reader (master's `migrateMachineSettings`) parsing the `machine-settings.json` M9 writes;
- the pure resolution.

Rejected: machine settings v2 (a changed `version` literal is a wire major), and leaving `gateway` as the default (contrary to the owner's answer).

*The relay check and the features.*
- On `machine`, the pre-launch relay check is advisory and never refuses. The effective base URL is resolved with the precedence `models-cache.ts` already implements:
  1. the spawn environment;
  2. the global config's `env`;
  3. the user, project and local settings, then the flag settings, for the launch's cwd and config home;
  4. `CLAUDE_CODE_RESTRICTED` emptying the setting sources;
  5. managed settings last, through the per-OS managed-settings path the desk track's `resolve-style.ts` fix introduces. That fix is its own PR and a prerequisite. M9 reuses its path, adds no second one, and does not touch `resolve-style.ts`.
- When the resolved URL is `RELAY_URL` and the relay doesn't answer, the relay sheet shows with "Start anyway". Otherwise there is no check. A resolution that fails or is uncertain shows nothing and defers to the engine.
- `get_status` stays authoritative after the launch.
- Red-first cases:
  - user settings naming the relay that project settings override to another host;
  - the opposite override;
  - restricted mode;
  - a missing managed file.
- Usage, accounts and the model catalogue key on `observed.kind`, falling back to the legacy fields, not on the chosen route. The files are `stores/usage.ts`, `statusline/direct-fallback.ts`, `composer/list-models.ts` (`routeOf`) and `models-cache.ts`.
- A gateway model on a `machine` launch is refused only when `observed` isn't ClaudeUsage.

*Remote setup.* The machine refuses a spawn whose checkout isn't a registered project directory (`unknown-checkout`) or isn't trusted there (`untrusted`, `spawn.ts`).

When `readCandidates` (`src/renderer/src/sessions/remote-spawn.ts`) finds no registered checkout of the org project, the sheet asks for the folder on that machine.

The folder list:
- `machine.folders.list {path?, open?, cursor?}` (new, `machine` owner) lists directories under the machine's home, a page at a time, names only.
- It never raises a privacy prompt by itself. On macOS, the TCC-protected folders (`~/Desktop`, `~/Documents`, `~/Downloads`, `~/Pictures`, `~/Movies`, `~/Music`, `~/Library` and anything under `~/Library/Mobile Documents`) are listed as entries marked `protected: true`. Nothing inside one is read unless an owner opens it explicitly (`open: true`).
- The sheet warns first that the machine may show a privacy prompt; on the mini, such a dialog holds CI. No test or e2e opens one.
- The sheet lists first the paths the project's other checkouts use (for example `~/Developer/GitHub/<name>`), read from the org project's known checkouts, since that is usually the answer.

Registering and trusting:
- `session.projects.addFolder {directory}` registers the answer.
- When the chosen folder isn't trusted, the sheet reads `session.trust.check {directory}` and shows `TrustCard`.
- The answer goes as `session.spawn`'s new optional `trust: 'accepted'`. The machine runs the attested bootstrap launch the local path runs (`launch-mapping.ts`'s `bootstrapSpec`). MAWS still never writes trust itself.

Authorization keeps the setup policy apart from the spawn guard:
- The three setup rows carry a new `OfferRow` field, `owners: true`. `authorize.ts` checks it before the grade, against `ranks.ownsMachine()`, and refuses `owners-only` to anyone else, a grant-holder included.
- `machine.folders.list` and `session.trust.check` are calls at a row grade of `list`. Owners read their own machine's folder names and trust state without a Touch ID per page. Their registry grade stays `spawn`, which `dispatchOverLink` refuses before sending (`src/main/machines/remote/dispatch.ts`). So the adapter sends both through the explicit call path that the other row-grade overrides use: they join `MACHINE_CALLS` (`src/main/lane-b/remote-connectors.ts`), sent by `callOverLink`. At the endpoint the row's `list` admits them, and step 6's guard applies to `spawn`-graded rows only, so neither meets `spawnRefusal`.
- `session.projects.addFolder` is an act graded `machine-admin`. It always steps up, and it is named in `MACHINE_ACTS` so the adapter `handles` it. A `machine-admin` row never enters `spawnRefusal`. Its own policy, in `src/endpoint/policy.ts`'s new `setupRefusal`, requires all of:
  - a directory that exists;
  - its real path under the machine's home;
  - no symlink escape;
  - not inside a protected folder unless that folder was opened.
- `session.spawn`'s `trust: 'accepted'` is added to `spawnRefusal`'s accepted fields only for a current machine owner (`ownsMachine()` at the decision). For anyone else it is `spec-field`, and the existing checks stay as they are for ordinary grant-holder spawns.
- Tests through the real gate (`test/endpoint/`): each of the three setup actions for an owner, refused for a grant-holder and for a member, and `trust: 'accepted'` for an owner against a non-owner.

Rejected:
- browsing through `files.list` (it is confined to a session's roots);
- a grant-holder setting up another person's machine;
- MAWS writing `~/.claude.json` trust (only the engine records it);
- the setup reads as acts (a Touch ID per folder page).

**Does not touch.** The engine (D-1); ClaudeUsage itself; the local spawn sheet beyond the shared `TrustCard`; O10's attestation.

**Proves.** P5 acceptance 5's spawn on a machine with no setup, in tests and `e2e:remote` (a scene machine with an unregistered checkout); the launch's environment in `src/engine-host/launch.test.ts`'s cases for `machine` (nothing injected, nothing blanked); `verifyGateway`'s naming over the three fixtures; the default's resolution on disk; `cross-build`'s zero breaking changes against master; an older engine host given the legacy route; the setup rows through the real gate; live in M10. Review: reviewer-high.

### M10 — The live re-check *(planned 2026-11-04)*

O7 closes when M10 is recorded in Outcomes.

**At its end.**
- The bench is fit for a live run:
  - one status client per run on the mini;
  - the key read and the listing promoted on demand;
  - the scratch hosts ended with the daemon on the mini;
  - the runner refuses to start on a loaded runner.
- A short sitting has recorded M8's items, M9's spawn on both machines (on `machine`, with the setup path) and Settings › Connectors' Sign in. It has recorded the live bench only if the precheck passed, otherwise as carried with its reason. M7's measured acceptance stays CI's rl-4.

**Touches.** `test/support/remote-bench.ts`, `remote-bench-agent.ts`, `remote-bench.test.ts`, `e2e/remote-latency-live.spec.ts`, `src/endpoint/cli.ts` (`endpoint sync`), `src/mawsd/cli.ts`, this document's Outcomes.

**Decisions.**

*The key read.* Attempt 1's key step could never pass. `#keyId()` parses `{key: {keyId}}`, while `mawsd keys --json` answers flat: `keysAnswer` gives `{enrollment, name, org, ref, delegated, keyId, heldKeys}` (`src/endpoint/answers.ts`; `src/mawsd/process-endpoint.test.ts`). The fakes carry the same wrong shape (`remote-bench.test.ts`).
- The reader takes the flat shape. The fakes are generated from `keysAnswer`, so they can't drift again.

*The listing on demand.* After an enroll, the endpoint syncs once, then every 10 minutes (`SYNC_EVERY_MS`). Its hub link, whose welcome syncs, opens only once it is `org-listed`, so the owner's y is seen up to 10 minutes later.
- A new `mawsd endpoint sync` verb calls `syncNow()` and then `promoteIfListed`, and the bench polls it after showing the key.
- Rejected: `enroll --wait` beside the key read (two enroll processes on one profile).

*One status client.* Each poll is a fresh ssh plus an Electron-as-Node `mawsd` (`STATUS_EVERY_MS`).
- The agent gains a `watch` mode, started at the stage's start: it retries `reachMachine` until the daemon answers, then answers `{status}` and `{keys}` lines on that one link.
- The runner reads status from it for `stage`, `enroll` and `#keyId`.

*The mini's hosts.* On the mini, teardown prints mini-ops' stop line and registers no host cleanup, so a detached scratch engine host outlives it.
- After mini-ops' stop, the runner waits for the daemon's absence and runs the agent's `stop-hosts` and the reap, as the VM path does (`#endScratchProcesses`). These are the runner's non-TTY calls under the scratch profile, which the desk track's reach allows.

*The precheck.* Before the local case (`e2e/remote-latency-live.spec.ts`, after `benchMachine()`), the spec reads this Mac's load1, and for the mini its `sysctl -n vm.loadavg` through the run. It refuses to start with the reason when either is at or over Rule B's 5. A refused precheck is a recorded outcome, not a failure.

*The sitting.* Like M7's sitting, from the MacBook against the mini and a fresh acceptance VM (dc-acceptance-bd31 goes down on 2026-11-04). The owner's checklist:
- M8's seven items;
- the spawn on each machine on `machine`, one through the setup path;
- Settings › Connectors' Sign in on Notion, and a Notion send;
- the bench, after its precheck.
Prerequisite for the VM's re-check: the desk track's fix that lets lane B sessions on a devcloud host keep `mcp__to-human__message` after the engine's first plugin update (`resolve-style.ts` counting the `managed` scope, per OS). The owner keeps the network and the lid; the sleep and wake items stay with the measured group.

*The sitting's three defects* (added 2026-11-05 by the architect, from M10's live sitting; three tasks on `p5-o7-m10` after the bench fixes, each red first).

*A. A remote session ranks by a stale activity time* (a bug; acceptance 5 still fails live).
- **Evidence (2026-11-05).** A session spawned from the MacBook on the mini (app id cb9e796a, first turn ended 05:29:09Z) never shows under the MAWS row, not even after Show more. Neither does one spawned on the VM (cf463a17, 05:42Z). Show more's 8 rows include sessions last active 1 and 2 weeks ago, and the two appear only in Resume….
- **The architect's diagnosis**, from the code at 5f2bc8964 (the task verifies it and records what it finds in the Decision Log):
  - No lane B module emits `session.digest`, so a remote record's `lastActivity` falls back to `record.updatedAt` (`src/renderer/src/overview/derive.ts`).
  - The record hides its listing, so the listing's fresher `issued` is never used.
  - Once the spawned-here lead ends (`spawned-here.ts`), the session drops past `SESSIONS_CAP` (`derive.ts`, `shownTrees`).
  - M8's e2e (`e2e/remote.spec.ts`) used nine pinned sessions, which always outrank, so activity ranking never decided. It even asserts that the row goes below the cut after the first turn.
- **The fix is parity.** A remote session's activity time is its true latest activity: the newest of the record's `updatedAt` as this Mac last received it, the listing's `issued`, and the turn events the app saw on its stream. It then ranks among local sessions by the same recency rule. The task checks that this Mac actually receives the remote record's `updatedAt` bump at a turn's end after the tab has closed.
- **Red first:**
  - a unit case in the sidebar's derive tests: eight or more older idle local sessions, no pins, and one remote session whose turn just ended;
  - M8's e2e case changed, so that after the first turn the remote row stays visible when it is the most recent activity.

*B. No Touch ID for a spawn on a machine the member owns.* The owner's ruling (2026-11-05, about 05:5xZ): "Not on my machines (Recommended)". *2026-11-13 (UTC):* Amended by the UX wave (U9; O-48; `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-12-ux-wave-U9-other-machines.md`), on the owner's words: the observation behind O-48 ("I cannot create a fork session from the remote machine …") and "Approve and start", 2026-11-12; this ruling's own reason extends to a fork on a machine the member owns. A `session.fork` on a machine the member owns is signed unasked, as `session.spawn` is; nothing else is.
- **Today.** `src/main/lane-b/remote-spawn.ts` acts with `stepUp: 'required'`, and main's step-up (`src/main/device/step-up.ts`) asks Touch ID for every execution-grade act.
- **The ruling.** A `session.spawn` on a machine whose owners include the signed-in member is signed with the same step-up key, with no confirmation prompt.
- **The machines don't change.** The signature is the same, and owners still verify (`attest.ts`'s `SPAWN_ACTIONS`).
- **Touch ID stays for:**
  - a spawn on a machine the member doesn't own;
  - an approval on a session the member doesn't own;
  - owner and share changes;
  - machine admin;
  - org state signing (root);
  - a non-owner's shell.
- **Who decides.** Main decides ownership from the org state, never from the renderer's word. The renderer can't ask for an unconfirmed signature of any other act.
- **Tests.** The skip case, and one refusal case per act that keeps Touch ID.
- **Review.** reviewer-high with a security lens: this moves the step-up's trust boundary.

*C. "Thinking · 0s" never counts up* (the owner's screenshot, a local session on the MacBook, engine 2.1.318, model claude-opus-5-5).
- **The symptom.** While the agent thinks for more than 5 s, the transcript row stays "Thinking · 0s". The likely cause is a duration computed from thinking deltas that this model doesn't stream; the task finds the cause in the renderer's thinking row.
- **The fix.** While the block is open, the row ticks from the block's start with a live clock. Once it closes, it shows the final duration.
- **Red first.** A unit test.

*D. A read session behind stale unread ones* (the follow-up, 2026-11-05; tracker row "a read session behind stale unread ones").
- **Evidence.** In the owner's live MAWS group nearly every row carries an unread dot, rows 5 days to 2 weeks old included. Under `priority`, `priorityRank` put every unread session ahead of every read one, and a group draws at most 8 rows. At the sitting the owner watched the remote session's turn end on screen, so it was read, and it fell behind 8 or more stale unread rows; any local session they read does the same. A covered only a turn ending off screen.
- **The owner's ruling, verbatim:** "Fresh unread first (Recommended)". "An unread session ranks ahead only for 24 h after the turn that made it unread. Older unread rows keep their dot but rank by last activity with the rest, so a session you just used stays in view. Needs-you and working still rank first."
- **The rule, in one place.** `priorityRank` (`overview/derive.ts`), shared by the Overview's priority order and the sidebar. An unread session takes the unread tier only while `now` less its `unreadAt` is under `UNREAD_FRESH_MS` (24 h). `unreadAt` is the time the tier already keyed on: the digest's `lastTurnEndedAt`, or for a session with no digest its `lastActivity` with the seen activity. Past the window it falls through to working or the rest, and keeps its dot.
- **`now`.** Derive stays pure: `now` is an argument. The sidebar reads it from the shared 30 s tick (`useNow`) floored to 5 minutes (`useNowEvery`), so a row crossing 24 h moves within 5 minutes or on the next store change, with no timer per row.
- **Tests.** Red first in `overview/derive.test.ts` and `sidebar/derive.test.ts`; `e2e:remote`'s M8 case watches the first turn end on screen and asserts the read row first above the nine stale unread ones. The off-screen case stays in the unit tests.

**Proves.** O7's acceptance live; M10's bench fixes in `remote-bench.test.ts`; A, B and C in their red-first tests and `e2e:remote`; D in its red-first tests and `e2e:remote`'s M8 case. Review: reviewer-medium for the bench fixes and C, reviewer-high (security lens) for A and B together.

Expected:
- Each focused file first fails on the failure it names, then ends `Test Files  1 passed`.
- `offer-coverage.test.ts` lists by name any session-scoped command that is neither offered nor declared.
- `e2e:remote` adds:
  - a spawned session's row under its org project, reopened after its tab closes;
  - a MAWS ask answered from A;
  - `/btw` answered;
  - an operation completed;
  - (M9) a spawn on B through the folder and trust path.
- M10's sitting is recorded in Outcomes.

### Interfaces and Dependencies

No new dependency. M1 uses `ws` 8.22.0 (in the tree), `org-protocol` (workspace) and zod 4.

**§A. The remote link** (`src/main/machines/remote/link.ts`; reached through `Machines.remote`). This is the interface O9 and every later consumer use by name. Nothing else opens a channel.

    import type { ActionContext, RequestDigest, Cursor, SessionRef, Executor, RefusalReason, CapsMessage, ByteDuplex, Channel, EndpointRecord, Signed } from 'org-protocol';
    import type { MachineRef } from '@shared/machine/ref';
    import type { OrgSession, SignedIn } from '../../org/session';
    import type { MachineLink } from '../manager';

    export type Carrier = 'direct' | 'relay';
    export type ProbeReason = 'wake' | 'unlock' | 'focus' | 'network' | 'person';
    export type DeviceCaps = Omit<CapsMessage, 't'>;                                  // RL7's device half: { member, device, clientClass, machine: { grades }, stateVersion }

    export interface RemoteLink extends MachineLink {
      readonly machine: MachineRef;                                                   // known from the org state at creation, never null
      readonly name: string;                                                          // the org state's name, updated on a rename
      carrier(): Carrier | null;                                                      // null while no channel is current
      refusal(): string | null;                                                       // the permanent verdict holding it `refused`, else null
      caps(): DeviceCaps | null;                                                      // the latest `caps`, null before the first
      onCaps(listener: (caps: DeviceCaps) => void): () => void;
      call(name: string, input: unknown, options?: { timeoutMs?: number }): Promise<CallResult>;
      act(request: ActRequest): ActHandle;
      subscribe(request: SubscribeRequest, sink: SubscriptionSink): Subscription;
      probe(reason: ProbeReason): void;                                               // `person`: a person acted on this machine (also lifts `refused`)
      // MachineLink.onState fires on a reported-state change, and also when the carrier or the refusal changes under the
      // same reported state (a relay-to-direct switch), so the manager re-emits machines.changed; neither is a new generation.
    }

    export type CallResult = { ok: true; output: unknown } | { ok: false; error: { code: RefusalReason | 'offline' | 'timeout' | string; message?: string } };

    export interface ActRequest {
      action: string;                         // an offered name: a registry command, 'sminos.<rpc>', 'endpoint.grant' | 'endpoint.state', O8's 'record.*'
      session?: string;                       // the bare target: lane B's appSessionId, lane A's engine session id (lane A also names it in args.session)
      context?: ActionContext;                // mandatory with `session` (RL4)
      args: unknown;
      requestDigest?: RequestDigest;
      stepUp?: 'required' | 'if-asked' | 'never';   // default 'if-asked'
      executor?: Executor;                    // default by name: 'sminos.' → 'sminos', 'endpoint.' → 'endpoint', else 'engine-host'
    }
    export type ActProgress = 'waiting' | 'sent' | 'accepted' | 'unknown';            // waiting: not yet on a channel; unknown: lost track, querying
    export type ActOutcome =
      | { state: 'done'; result: unknown }                                            // it ran
      | { state: 'failed'; error: unknown }                                           // it was tried and failed
      | { state: 'refused'; reason: RefusalReason | 'offline' | 'step-up-cancelled' | string }   // nothing ran (the endpoint's, the executor's or the link's word)
      | { state: 'not-sent' }                                                         // query said not-received: tombstoned; nothing ran or will
      | { state: 'unknown'; reason: 'expired' | 'grade' | 'class' | 'removed' | 'closed' | string };  // final, outcome unknown
    export interface ActHandle {
      readonly opId: string;
      readonly opCreated: string;
      progress(): ActProgress;
      onProgress(listener: (progress: ActProgress) => void): () => void;
      readonly outcome: Promise<ActOutcome>;                                          // never rejects
    }

    export interface SubscribeRequest { session: SessionRef; view?: 'full' | 'cards'; cursor?: Cursor | null }
    export interface SubscriptionSink {
      subbed(mode: 'replay' | 'snapshot' | 'missing', at: Cursor): void;              // a snapshot or missing starts the stream over from `at`
      snapshot(at: Cursor, value: unknown): void;                                     // one whole snapshot or replace-whole part, its pages joined and parsed
      event(at: Cursor, body: unknown): void;                                         // lane B: a SessionEvent (or { omitted: 'too-large', bytes }); lane A: one sminos plane message
      paused(reason: string): void;                                                   // the endpoint's ('no-app') or the link's ('offline')
      resumed(): void;
      ended(reason: string): void;                                                    // final; the link won't resume it
    }
    export interface Subscription {
      readonly id: string;                                                            // stable across channels; a lane A read names it in input.sub
      cursor(): Cursor | null;
      close(): void;                                                                  // sends unsub
    }

    export interface RemoteLinkDeps {
      machine: { ref: MachineRef; name: string };
      session: OrgSession;                                                            // read at every attempt
      records: EndpointRecords;                                                       // endpoint-records.ts
      dialDirect?(url: string, timeoutMs: number): Promise<ByteDuplex>;               // carriers.ts's by default; tests map the tailnet address to loopback
      openRelay?(url: string, headers: Record<string, string>): Promise<RelaySocket>; // carriers.ts's by default
      handshake?(dial: () => Promise<ByteDuplex>, signedIn: SignedIn, machine: MachineRef): Promise<Channel>;
                                                                                      // connectChannel(dial, signedIn.identity, signedIn.view, { machine }) by default;
                                                                                      // link.test.ts passes an in-memory Channel, so the state machine runs with no crypto
      timing?: Partial<RemoteTiming>;
      now?(): number;
      random?(): number;                                                              // the jitter, for tests
      log(line: string): void;
    }
    export function createRemoteLink(deps: RemoteLinkDeps): RemoteLink & { rename(name: string): void };   // the source renames on a head's rename

    // carriers.ts
    export function dialDirect(url: string, timeoutMs: number): Promise<ByteDuplex>; // one ws:// WebSocket, binary messages; rejects past timeoutMs
    export interface RelaySocket {                                                   // one app socket to the machine's object; at most MAX_CHANNELS_PER_SOCKET channels
      dial(): Promise<ByteDuplex>;                                                    // a new channel: its first send is the hub's `open [tag][payload]`
      onClose(listener: (reason: string) => void): void;                              // every channel on it has ended by then
      close(): void;
    }
    export function openRelaySocket(url: string, headers: Record<string, string>, timeoutMs?: number): Promise<RelaySocket>;  // rejects on a failed upgrade (RelayUpgradeError, its status)
    // Both carriers end every ByteDuplex as 'carrier' (CARRIER_CLOSED), whatever the socket or the relay said.

    // endpoint-records.ts
    export interface EndpointRecords {
      // The newest record for `ref` that verifies against `signedIn.view` now: fetched from the hub within RECORD_FETCH_MS when
      // it answers, else the cache (memory, then the file); null when neither holds one. A fetched record replaces the cached
      // one only when its `issued` is later.
      resolve(ref: MachineRef, signedIn: SignedIn): Promise<Signed<EndpointRecord> | null>;
    }
    export function createEndpointRecords(deps: { file: string; fetch?: typeof fetch; now?(): number; log(line: string): void }): EndpointRecords;

    // timing.ts: §G's table as one object; RemoteTiming is its type
    export const REMOTE_TIMING: RemoteTiming;

    // src/main/machines/index.ts
    export interface Machines {
      dispatch: RemoteDispatch;
      remote(machine: MachineRef): RemoteLink | null;                                 // the one link to that machine, or null when the org lists none
      onRemote(listener: (machines: readonly MachineRef[]) => void): () => void;      // the roster changed: links were created or closed
      serve(): void;
      stop(): Promise<void>;
    }

**§B. The org session** (`src/main/org/session.ts`). O7 M1 defines it. O8's sign-in implements it for production.

    import type { DeviceIdentity, OrgView } from 'org-protocol';
    import type { StepUpRequest, StepUpResult } from '../device/step-up';           // a type-only import: no Electron at runtime

    export interface OrgSession {
      current(): SignedIn | null;                                                     // null: signed out, or this profile's device not listed in the held state
      onChanged(listener: () => void): () => void;                                    // sign-in, sign-out, a new head adopted by any path (a link's handshake `advance`
                                                                                      // included), the hub's address or credentials changed
    }
    export interface SignedIn {
      identity: DeviceIdentity;                                                       // clientClass 'native-mac'; channelKey from loadDeviceKeys; build: main's build string; capabilities []
      view: OrgView;                                                                  // verified; its provider persists what `advance` adopts
      stepUp: { sign(request: StepUpRequest): Promise<StepUpResult> };                // createStepUp over the same keys file's step-up key
      hub: HubAccess | null;                                                          // null: no hub; the direct carrier only
    }
    export interface HubAccess {
      url: string;                                                                    // https://… in production; http://127.0.0.1:<port> in tests and dev runs
      headers(): Promise<Record<string, string>>;                                     // what an authenticated member's request to the hub carries (Access, or the local stand-in's)
    }
    export function signedOut(): OrgSession;

    // src/main/org/dev-session.ts (unpackaged builds only)
    export function createDevOrgSession(input: { dir: string; userData: string; packaged: boolean; build: string;
      stepUp: (keys: DeviceKeys, device: { org: string; device: string }) => SignedIn['stepUp'];   // main passes (keys, device) => createStepUp({ device, key: keys.stepUp })
      loadKeys?: typeof loadDeviceKeys;                                                            // tests inject one with excludeFromBackup stubbed
      log(line: string): void }): OrgSession & { loaded: Promise<void> };   // returns at once, signed out; signs in when its load succeeds (onChanged fires), else stays out with a log line; tests await `loaded`

**§C. The manager's second source** (`src/main/machines/manager.ts`, `src/main/machines/remote/source.ts`).

    export interface RemoteSource {
      machines(): readonly { ref: MachineRef; name: string }[];                       // the roster: the head's machines and live certificates, local and contested refs left out
      onChanged(listener: () => void): () => void;
      connect(machine: { ref: MachineRef; name: string }): MachineLink;               // a RemoteLink; the source keeps it for `link`
    }
    // ConnectionManagerDeps gains `remote?: RemoteSource`; every other field is unchanged.
    export function createRemoteSource(deps: { session: OrgSession; userData: string; local: MachineRef; log(line: string): void; now?(): number;
      networkPollMs?: number; interfaces?(): NodeJS.Dict<NetworkInterfaceInfo[]>;   // os.networkInterfaces by default; tests inject the watch
      linkDeps?: Partial<RemoteLinkDeps> }): RemoteSource & {
      link(machine: MachineRef): RemoteLink | null;
      probeAll(reason: ProbeReason): void;
      close(): void;
    };

**§D. `machines.*`** (`src/shared/ipc/schema/machines.ts`; main-owned, outside the wire manifest).

    // MachineStateSchema gains:
    carrier: z.enum(['socket', 'direct', 'relay']).optional(),   // while up
    refusal: z.string().optional(),                              // while a permanent verdict holds (an RL2 link `refused`)

**§E. Files.**
- `<userData>/endpoint-records.json` is the client's own: `{ "version": 1, "records": { "<ref>": Signed<EndpointRecord> } }`. It is 0600 and written through a temporary file and a rename. A file that doesn't read is logged and read as empty.
- `MAWS_DEV_ORG=<dir>` is honoured only in an unpackaged build. The directory holds:

      session.json  { "version": 1, "org": "<org id>", "member": "<member id>", "device": "<device id>",
                      "pins": { "roots": ["<key id>"], "recoveryPub": "<B64>" },
                      "hub": { "url": "http://127.0.0.1:<port>", "standinUser": "<email>" } | null }
      chain.json    { "chain": Signed<OrgState>[], "transitions": Signed<RootTransition>[], "certificates": Certificates }

  A harness makes it by loading the scratch profile's keys first (`loadDeviceKeys`), listing that device's `channelPub` and `stepUpPub` in the org through `OrgAdmin` or the org fixtures, and writing both files.

**§F. Refusals on the router path.** `{ ok: false, error: { code: 'internal', message: '<name>: refused <reason> by machine <name>', issues: { refusal: '<reason>' } } }`, with `offline` and `unavailable` → `host_unavailable`, `timeout` → `timeout`, and an `IPC_ERROR_CODES` member passed through.

**§G. Timing** (`src/main/machines/remote/timing.ts`; each a field of `RemoteTiming`, overridable in tests).

| Name | Value | From |
|---|---|---|
| `DIRECT_DIAL_MS` | 1000 | P5 §3's short timeout |
| `DIRECT_BUCKET` | burst 3, 5 a minute | `HANDSHAKE_LIMITS.perSource` |
| `DIRECT_RECHECK_MS` | 60 000 | — |
| `RECORD_FETCH_MS` | 2000 | — |
| `UP_GRACE_MS` | 2000 | — |
| `BACKOFF_MS` | 0, 1000, 2000, 4000, then 5000; ±20% | RL §4.6 |
| `RESUME_WINDOW_MS` | 120 000, then 30 000 between attempts | `RESUME_MS` |
| `PING_SILENCE_MS` | 5000–7000 | `PING_MS` |
| `PROBE_PONG_MS` | 2000 | — |
| `NETWORK_POLL_MS` | 2000 | — |
| `CALL_TIMEOUT_MS` | 10 000 | the socket link's |
| `ACT_FRESH_MS` | 60 000 | `FRESH_MS` |
| `ACK_TIMEOUT_MS` | 10 000 | — |
| `DONE_QUERY_MS` | 60 000 | — |
| `QUERY_BACKOFF_MS` | 1000, 2000, 4000, 8000, then 15 000 | — |
| `SUB_INITIAL_CREDIT` | 1 MiB | ≤ `SUB_CREDIT_MAX` |
| `CREDIT_BATCH` | 256 KiB or 250 ms | — |

**§H. Contract requests** (for P5's writer to carry into each child's decomposition). These say what O7 needs, not how to build it.

*To O3*, the services host and the spine:
- **C1. An app-less lane B event source.** The endpoint's `onEvent` must see every lane B session's events whether or not an app is attached, through O3's per-client subscriptions, so a remote viewer is never `paused {no-app}` (O10 M8, P5 Decision Log 29). Keep `historyUnavailable` (P5 Decision Log 77).
- **C2. The moved namespaces cross builds and name their target.** `git.*`, `files.*`, `scm.*`, drafts, `session.search` and `session.deletions.list` become `machine`-owned on the wire manifest. For each, O7 needs a way to bind a path to a session's checkout on the machine: the RL6 record (or `session.status`) carries the session's `cwd` and worktree, and the machine's checkouts are known to the endpoint's `CheckoutSource` with MAWS's projects added (O10 M5).
- **C3. Ledgers and an executor name for moved acts.** Every act-graded command that moves into `mawsd` keeps an op ledger (`src/shared/host/op-ledger.ts`, O10 M7), and O3 names the executor that RL4's `query` routes to. O7 adds that name to RL2's `query.executor` behind a capability (Constraints, "RL2's vocabulary").
- **C4. A machine-side spawn.** An entry point that starts a lane B session from a remote request: checkout, worktree choice, model, effort, permission mode and brief. It builds the launch spec from the machine's own settings (settings' machine half), writes the RL6 record with the requester as owner and the spawn action as its proof (RL §5.8), and keeps a ledger. O7 offers it and builds the picker. O10's ceiling and checkout rules apply at the endpoint.
- **C5. Per-member reads.** A remote caller is a member, not the machine's owner. O7 needs either listings as the only source of another machine's rows (P5 finding F5), or a `session.list`-shaped read filtered by the caller's rank. Drafts and reader state are kept per member, which is O8's rule (P5 Decision Log 36); O3's index must allow those keys.
- **C6. A per-member attention feed.** The attention core in `mawsd` exposes the items on sessions a member may `view`, as a stream the endpoint can serve as a machine-wide subscription. O7 defines that view on RL2.
- **C7. The client port across the channel.** The daemon's OX4 port routes a call to the client that asked, including a client attached over RL2 through the endpoint. Today a request with no local client answers `noClient`. O7 defines the RL2 messages. O3 defines which client asked, a `ClientRef` for a remote device.
- **C8. Uploads.** A `machine`-owned write into a session's attachments directory, bounded in size and kept under a ledger.
- **C9. Interest from remote viewers.** A remote device's lane B subscriptions count in OX5's union, which O10 M8 already reports through `interest.report`. O3's sweep, which replaces `layout.json`, reads that union, so a session someone is watching from another machine is not released as idle.
- **C10. Artifact ownership.** On the machine, an artifact id resolves to its session, so a remote read or edit is graded on that session.
- **Budget.** Whatever O3 adds to `mawsd` for C1–C10 counts against acceptance 14's bound (P5's Risks row).

*To O4.* Capability entries for connectors, Send to and the library on a session, so the app greys and lights them by capability. Consent flows opened on the client through C7.

*To O5.* The artifact HTTP endpoint and the render service's capability entries (screenshot, export). U3's outcome for the agent's browser on a remote session: if it is a screencast to the app, O7 carries the stream as an RL2 kind.

*To O8.*
- **The production `OrgSession`** (§B): Access's login transfer, device registration, the held org chain persisted with `advance`, and the hub's address and headers.
- **The remote-rows source** (P5 finding F5): OX8's verified listings, merged per machine into the stores, if O8 takes it.

*To O9.*
- Consume `Machines.remote(ref)` (§A) for remote lane A: `act('sminos.<rpc>', …)` with lane A's context, `subscribe({lane: 'A', id}, sink)`, and `call('sminos.rows', {session, sub: subscription.id, …})`.
- Build no channel. `boundary.test.ts` refuses one.
- Share the remote-rows source with O7, under whichever child P5 names.

**§I. Contract additions.**
- **Now (M1):**
  - `RemoteLink` and `Machines.remote` (§A);
  - `OrgSession` (§B);
  - `RemoteSource` (§C);
  - `MachineState.carrier` and `.refusal` (§D, no wire bump);
  - `endpoint-records.json` and `MAWS_DEV_ORG` (§E);
  - the router-path refusal mapping (§F);
  - the boundary test.

  M1 adds no `WIRE_MINOR` bump, no settings version, no offer-table row and no e2e group.
- **Later, as M1 foresaw it (superseded by §L and §M at the re-plan of 2026-10-31; kept as the record of what M1 expected):**
  - Offer-table rows: lane B spawn (C4), `pty.*` and port forwarding (M3), documents and artifacts (M5), `git.*`, `files.*`, `scm.*`, `fs.holders`, uploads, `session.search`, `session.deletions.list` (M4), and the lane B panel reads (M2).
  - RL2 minor bumps with capabilities: binary kinds for pty output, document frames and forwarded bytes; `query.executor` values; an optional `session` on `call`; endpoint-to-device `client` requests; a machine-wide attention view.
  - Likely `WIRE_MINOR` bumps for a pty-host ledger command and for any engine-host read O7 adds.
  - One e2e group, `e2e:remote` (M2).
  - No settings version.
The sections below were added at the re-plan of 2026-10-31. §H's requests to O3 were answered in O3's composite (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-28-p5-o3-spine.md`, "Contract requests answered"): C1, C3, C5, C6, C7's machine half and C9 by O3-D, C2 and C8 by O3-D and O3-E, C4 in part (the spawn's requester-as-owner goes to M4 with O12), C10 refused as existing.

**§J. The remote lane B adapter** (`src/main/lane-b/remote.ts`; M2, extended by M3, M4 and M6). Main's one consumer of `RemoteLink` for lane B, reached by the router through `routeLaneB`. It owns every subscription, feed, act and stream for lane B sessions on RL2 machines; the renderer keeps the local driver's commands and events.

    import type { MachineRef } from '@shared/machine/ref';
    import type { CommandName, CommandResult, EventName, EventPayload } from '@shared/ipc/schema';
    import type { MainContext, RemoteDispatch } from '../ipc/router';
    import type { RemoteLink } from '../machines/remote/link';
    import type { LayoutFileStore } from '../layout/store';

    export interface RemoteLaneBDeps {
      local: MachineRef;
      layout: Pick<LayoutFileStore, 'get' | 'onChange'>;                    // main.tabs: the addresses of RL2 machines are the interest
      remote(machine: MachineRef): RemoteLink | null;                        // Machines.remote
      onRemote(listener: (machines: readonly MachineRef[]) => void): () => void;   // Machines.onRemote: a link closed ends its sessions
      emit<N extends EventName>(machine: MachineRef, name: N, payload: EventPayload<N>): void;   // emitFrom
      timing?: Partial<RemoteLaneBTiming>;
      now?(): number;
      log(line: string): void;
    }
    export interface RemoteLaneBTiming {
      unsubscribeLingerMs: number;   // 10 000: a closed tab's subscription outlives it this long
      recordRetryMs: readonly number[];   // 500, 1000, 2000, 4000: session.get retried for a session the machine can't place yet; then "can't place"
      idleRetryMs: readonly [number, number];   // 1000, 30 000: a placed session's stream ended `unavailable` (not live) is subscribed again, doubling
      sendPendingMs: number;         // 60 000: a send the channel carried answers the renderer by then, `timeout`, while its act keeps querying
      sendQueueMax: number;          // 8: sends waiting behind the one in flight
      batchMax: number;              // 500 events per session.eventBatch
    }
    export const REMOTE_LANE_B_TIMING: RemoteLaneBTiming;

    export interface RemoteLaneB {
      /** Whether the adapter takes `name` for an RL2 machine (§L's lane B acts, the substitutes, M3's pty and forward names, M4's writes). */
      handles(name: CommandName): boolean;
      dispatch(link: RemoteLink, name: CommandName, input: unknown, ctx: MainContext): Promise<CommandResult<CommandName>>;
      stop(): void;                  // every subscription, feed and stream closed before the links (shutdown order)
    }
    export function createRemoteLaneB(deps: RemoteLaneBDeps): RemoteLaneB;
    /** The router's `remote`: a name the adapter handles, for a machine `remote` answers, goes to it; everything else to `base` (routeLaneA, then the manager). */
    export function routeLaneB(base: RemoteDispatch, remote: (machine: MachineRef) => RemoteLink | null, laneB: RemoteLaneB): RemoteDispatch;

    // remote-session.ts: one per subscribed session
    export interface RemoteSessionState {
      machine: MachineRef; app: string;                                      // the bare appSessionId
      status: SessionStatus | null;                                          // the last `status` part: an engine-host act's context
      record: SessionRecord | null;                                          // session.get's answer, emitted as session.changed
      stream: 'opening' | 'idle' | 'live' | 'paused' | 'gap' | 'ended';      // idle: placed, not live; subscribed again on idleRetryMs
      launch: string | null;                                                 // the current launchId
      sends: number;                                                         // acts waiting
    }
    // remote-outcomes.ts
    export function outcomeAsResult(name: CommandName, machine: string, outcome: ActOutcome): CommandResult<CommandName>;   // §F's mapping, A4's, the pending bound's and the image's words
    export function controlsOverlay(state: RemoteSessionState, link: LinkView): Record<ControlKey, Capability>;           // the remote reasons over laneBControls' table

Composition in `src/main/index.ts`: `installRouter({ …, remote: routeLaneB(routeLaneA(machines.dispatch, remote, remoteLaneA), remote, remoteLaneB) })`, `shutdown.add('remote lane B', () => remoteLaneB.stop())` before `'remote lane A'`. `MainContext` gains `session?: AppSessionId` (M4; `src/main/ipc/router.ts`): the session a path-class command was made from, read from the preload envelope's third argument, today a `MachineRef`, from M4 a `MachineRef | { machine: MachineRef; session?: AppSessionId }` (`src/preload/bridge.ts`, both forms read).

**§K. `remote.*`** (`src/shared/ipc/schema/remote.ts`; main-owned, outside the wire manifest, as `machines.*` and `org.*`; its ids are addresses as the IPC boundary lifts them).

    // events, main to renderer
    'remote.session': { address: AppSessionAddress; link: { state: 'up' | 'down' | 'connecting'; carrier: 'direct' | 'relay' | null; refusal: string | null; member: string | null; rights: ('spawn' | 'shell' | 'machine-admin')[] };
                        stream: 'opening' | 'idle' | 'live' | 'paused' | 'gap' | 'ended'; controls: Record<ControlKey, { available: true } | { available: false; reason: string }>; reason: string | null }
                        // member: null before the first caps; reason: why no control acts (the device refused, the link closed, the session unplaced); null when they do
    'remote.gap':     { appSessionId: AppSessionAddress; launchId: string; at?: { seq: number; idx?: number } }   // at: the gap's next event is the first at or after it (a lost range, an omitted event); absent for a relaunch's snapshot (the launch's start)   // M2: a later snapshot, a missing range or an omitted event: the transcript draws the bar here
    'remote.attention': { machine: MachineRef; items: AttentionItem[] }      // M6: a feed's snapshot for the machine; attention.changed follows per session
    // commands, renderer to main (M3)
    'remote.forward.open':  { machine: MachineRef; port: number } → { localPort: number }   // a listener on 127.0.0.1:<localPort> forwarding to the machine's 127.0.0.1:<port>
    'remote.forward.close': { machine: MachineRef; port: number } → void
    // commands, renderer to main (M5a)
    'remote.openCopy': { machine: MachineRef; source: { appSessionId: AppSessionId; path: string } | { artifactId: string; revision?: number } | { artifactId: string; export: { revision: number; format: string; exportId?: string } } }
                        → { opened: true } | { opened: false; reason: string }   // the bytes paged through the link into <temp>/maws-remote-copies/<machine>/<sha256 of the source>/<name> (64 MiB at most), then shell.openPath; A10
    // artifacts.open (main-owned) answers { channel, readOnly?: true } from M5b: readOnly when the document opened at `view`

**§L. The offer rows O7 adds** (`src/endpoint/offers.ts`). Each row: kind, executor, target rule, grade source. Unless noted, a registry command takes its grade from O1's declaration. Lane B's `appSessionId` placement reads the spine's record first from M2 (M2's first decision). Every request on a channel carries that channel's `ClientRef` into `dispatch` (M2).

| Milestone | Names | Kind | Executor | Target | Note |

| M2 | `session.get`, `session.appEvents`, `session.history.list` | call | machine | `appSessionId` | `calls.ts` dispatches a `machine` call row as an `engine-host` one |

| M5c | `artifacts.releaseAssets` | call | endpoint | `machine` | `list`; only the asking channel's own hold, any other id `{released: false}` with the host not asked (added 2026-11-02) |

Not offered, and why: `git.worktree.add`, `remove`, `deleteBranch` (a new session's worktree is `session.spawn`'s; no remote reader for a cleanup); `fs.holders` (its one caller is the take-over flow, disabled remotely; P5's O7 contracts line names it, see the Decision Log's F9); `fs.readSpill` (its paths are the engine store's, and no read attests a spill's session); `session.deletions.list` (`manage`-graded, a Settings surface of the machine's owner); `session.drafts.*` and `session.update` (per member, O8 M6); `pty.list`, `pty.replay` (the endpoint's own in-process read), `pty.foreground`, `pty.openLink`, `pty.hostState`; `session.create`, `intent.*`, `start`, `fork`, `launch` (the remote spawn is `session.spawn`; `session.launch` is the engine host's launch-spec command); `engine.models` (`spawn`; `session.listModels` stands in). Added at M5's re-plan: `files.reveal`, `files.openExternal`, `library.openExternally`, `library.reveal`, `session.revealPath` (they ask the port to open a path on the machine, which means nothing here; the renderer opens a copy through `remote.openCopy`, A10); `artifacts.search` (its rows carry no links to filter by; `library.list`'s `query` is the search); `artifacts.open` (main-owned; the adapter answers it with a `document` stream, M5b); `library.saveAs`, `artifacts.downloadAsset` (main's, `client`; answered here for any machine through the router's allow-list and the link's reads); `artifacts.content`, `state`, `assets`, `setDefaults`, `captureDatasetState`, `datasetRefreshRequest`, `holdDatasets`, `releaseDatasets`, `library.fileLinkedPaths`, `library.snapshot`, `entry`, `text`, `testImport`, `testLoopDelay`, `testWrite` (`internal`, main-only or e2e); `catalog.engine`, `catalog.engineRefresh` (Discover reads this Mac's own catalog, and a remote read at `machine-admin` would prompt per refresh); `delivery.connect` (Drive's login listens on the machine; D38: "Connect Drive on `<machine>`"); `delivery.deliver`, `connectors.windowShown`, `connectors.adoptGrant` (`internal`, main-only; the window report crosses as the endpoint's `client.windowShown`); `connectors.add`, `remove` and the engine host's `connectors.*` (`internal`); `maws.pane.answer` (`show_pane` is not carried to a remote device: A16 and the Decision Log's M5 entry). *2026-11-13 (UTC):* Amended by the UX wave (U9; O-48, S-02; `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-12-ux-wave-U9-other-machines.md`), on the owner's words: the observation behind O-48 ("I cannot create a fork session from the remote machine …") and "Approve and start", 2026-11-12. From the UX wave (U9), behind `rl2.session-acts`: a fork in place (the machine's owners), archive, unarchive, rename, the owner's read of archived sessions, dismissing an attention item, taking back a waiting message and a pane's answer are offered. Launch, start, the setup of a new session, delete, take over and replays stay unoffered.

**§M. RL2 additions** (`apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts`, `channel.ts`), each behind a capability a side lists in its handshake. The device's are `DEVICE_CAPABILITIES` (`src/main/machines/remote/capabilities.ts`), the endpoint's `ENDPOINT_CAPABILITIES` (`src/endpoint/capabilities.ts`); both list every capability their build implements, and `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` moves to master's plus one at each landing that adds one.

- `rl2.upload-blocks` (M8; endpoint; RL2 1.9 on the branch, re-bumped at landing if master has moved): `session.send`'s `uploads?`, expanded at the endpoint through `files.readUploads` before the act reaches the engine host. A device sends `uploads` only to an endpoint listing it.

| Capability | Milestone | What it admits |
|---|---|---|
| `rl2.executor.machine` | M2 | `Executor` gains `machine` (`FROM_DEVICE.query` admits it); `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` 1 |
| `rl2.call-session` | M4 | `call` gains `session?: SessionRef`, read by the `checkout` target rule |
| `rl2.feeds` | M4 | `sub` gains `feed?: 'checkout' | 'attention'` (`session` present for `checkout`, absent for `attention`); §R |
| `rl2.events-budget` | M4 | `session.events` takes `maxBytes` and answers `omitted` (acceptance 12); the device sends `maxBytes` only to an endpoint listing it, and otherwise keeps M2's path (no budget; `remote.historyTooLarge`) |
| `rl2.executor.pty-host` | M3 | `Executor` gains `pty-host` |
| `rl2.streams` | M3 | the stream kinds of §N: `streamOpen`, `streamOpened`, `streamRefused`, `streamData`, `streamControl`, `streamCredit`, `streamClose` |
| `rl2.pong-clock` | M7 | `pong` carries `received` and `sent` (the endpoint's clock), for the live bench |
| `rl2.artifacts` | M5a | `Feed` gains `artifacts` (`sub {feed: 'artifacts'}`, `session` absent; §R) |
| `rl2.bytes` | M5a | a call's `input` and `output` and an act's `args` and `result` may carry `Uint8Array`s in `src/shared/host/json-bytes.ts`'s tagged form (`{"$bytes": "<base64>"}`, `$plain` escaped); the device sends a bytes-carrying call or act only to an endpoint listing it |
| `rl2.read-open` | M5b | `streamOpen` without `op` (the read open, §N); the kinds `http` and `document` with their `http` and `document` fields; `streamOpened.http` |
| `rl2.client-port` | M5c | `client {id, kind, request}` endpoint to device; `clientAnswer {id, answer}` device to endpoint |
| `rl2.asset-holds` | M5c | the endpoint keeps a device's dashboard snapshot holds per channel (`artifacts.holdAssets`, `releaseAssets`, main's link's calls alone); a device asks them only of an endpoint that listed it, and otherwise draws unheld, said once per machine |
| `rl2.browser-screencast` | reserved by M5b; built by O17 M9 | the `browser.screencast` kind, its envelope, controls, refusal words and one-frame backlog (O17's §K); listed by neither constant until M9 |

A kind a peer did not list is never sent to it; the link and the endpoint each refuse locally. An unknown kind still closes a channel (`readMessage`), which the capability rule makes unreachable between two conforming builds. An optional field on a known kind passes an older reader (`readMessage` checks named fields and lets others through), so `pong`'s two fields need no gate; the capability is for the reader to know they are meant.

**§N. Streams** (M3, extended by M5b; `src/endpoint/streams.ts`, `stream-http.ts`, `stream-document.ts`, `src/main/machines/remote/streams.ts`). A byte flow between a device and its machine, inside the sealed channel, opened under an act the device sent on that channel (the act open: `pty`, `tcp`) or under a stream row of the offer table at the row's grade (the read open, M5b: `http`, `document`; O17 M9's `browser.screencast`).

    // messages (JSON, base64url data), behind rl2.streams; the read open's form behind rl2.read-open
    { t: 'streamOpen', id, kind: 'pty' | 'tcp' | 'http' | 'document', op?: string /* the authorizing act's opId; absent for a read open */, credit: number,
      pty?: { ptyId: string; from: number /* output offset */; inputEpoch: string /* 16 random bytes base64url, per app run */; inputFrom: number /* this epoch's input offset */; inputSent?: number /* RL2 1.3: the epoch's input sent so far */ },
      tcp?: { forward: string },
      http?: { url: string /* a maws-artifact: URL, the scheme's exact shape */; method: 'GET' | 'HEAD'; range?: string },
      document?: { artifactId: string; mode: 'edit' | 'view'; doc?: 'suggestions' } }
    { t: 'streamOpened', id, credit: number,                      // the endpoint's credit for the device's bytes
      pty?: { info: PtyInfo; ringStart: number; nextOffset: number },   // a pty's ring from max(from, ringStart) follows as streamData
      http?: { status: number; headers: Record<string, string> } }      // the artifact endpoint's answer; the body follows as streamData from offset 0, then streamClose 'closed'
    { t: 'streamRefused', id, reason: 'grade' | 'unknown' | 'unreachable' | 'busy' | 'malformed' | 'unavailable' | 'read-only' }   // 'unavailable' since M3 as built; 'read-only' (M5b) only in answer to a read open, so only a peer listing rl2.read-open ever receives it (an older validator would close the channel on it); no-target reads as 'grade', a bad URL 'malformed'
    { t: 'streamData', id, offset: number, data: string }        // ≤ STREAM_CHUNK_BYTES (64 KiB) decoded; offset: the sender's byte count before this chunk
    { t: 'streamControl', id, control: unknown }                 // pty: { resize: { cols, rows } }; none for http or document
    { t: 'streamCredit', id, bytes: number }                     // ≤ 4 MiB; the receiver's refund
    { t: 'streamClose', id, reason: 'closed' | 'behind' | 'exit' | 'carrier' | 'removed' | 'host' | 'reset' | 'upstream' | 'read-only' | string }   // document: the channel's own end words; read-only: a view open the device wrote to past tolerance

Rules. `id` is the device's, 16 random bytes base64url, unique on its channel; at most `MAX_STREAMS` (32) per channel (`busy`). The endpoint admits an open when the act `op` names answers `done` from its executor's ledger (`pty.opQuery`, or the endpoint's own) with a binding of this channel's member and device and a name the kind allows (`pty.spawn` or `pty.attach` for `pty`, `forward.open` for `tcp`), and the pty or forward named is the one that act's result names; else `grade`. Each side sends data only within the other's credit; output past the device's credit is held up to `STREAM_BACKLOG_BYTES` (512 KiB) and then the stream closes `behind`. A `pty` stream's output offsets are the pty host's (`pty.data`'s): its opening delivers the ring from `max(from, ringStart)` to `nextOffset`, read through `pty.replay` (§O) and so independent of any viewer's checkpoint, before live frames; its input offsets are the device's, numbered per pty from zero in each `inputEpoch`, and the endpoint keeps the last offset it wrote per `(device, ptyId, inputEpoch)` for `RESUME_MS`, drops a repeat, and forgets an older epoch when a new one opens. A `tcp` stream connects to the machine's `127.0.0.1:<port>` at open (`streamOpened` after the connect; `unreachable` on failure) and ends whole with either side's close; a forward (the `forward.open` act's result) is kept per `(device, forward)` for `RESUME_MS` after its channel's loss, so a stream on the next channel may name it with no new act, and past the window a stream naming it is `unknown`. A stream dies with its channel (`carrier`): a `pty` stream is reopened by the device from its offsets under the same act; a `tcp` stream is a connection and is not: a request made at the instant of a link drop, its connection opened on the dying channel or in flight on it, may be reset, and the next request after the reconnect succeeds, so a caller retries (the browser's reload). Open streams are authorized again at every `caps` refresh and new head (M3 as built: `channels.refreshCaps` → `streams.recheck`; the policy CLI too), and from M5b at every record change naming a stream's session and at an `artifacts.changed {change: 'linked'}` of its artifact (`Streams` subscribes to `RecordSource.onChange` and the event): one whose member no longer holds the grade closes `removed`, its held input dropped and its socket reset. Streams carry no liveness of their own.

The read open (M5b; R1). A `streamOpen` with no `op` names no act: it is admitted by a row of kind `stream` in the offer table (§L: `artifacts.fetch` for `http`, `documents.open` for `document`), authorized by `authorize.ts`'s eight steps as a call is, with `kind: 'stream'` and the open's kind fields as the input: offered (a `stream` row for the kind, else `not-offered`, which the device reads as `grade`), the fields held to the row's `accepts` (`malformed`), the class, standing through the row's target rule (`artifact` for both: the id from the URL or from `document.artifactId`), the effect grade (`documents.open` is `{floor: 'view', byEffect: true}`, `mode: 'edit'` taking `manage`), policy (`read_only` refuses an `edit` open `read-only`), presence (`removed`); no step-up (nothing a stream row admits needs one). The verdict and the publication are one step, as `decide` makes them for the act open. A re-authorization re-runs the same steps with the stored open input. An `http` stream is one request: the endpoint resolves the URL through `artifacts.fetch` at the decision, answers the status and headers in `streamOpened`, sends the body under the device's credit (pulled from the `ReadableStream` only within it: no backlog, never `behind`), then `closed`; the device sends no data on it. A `document` stream is one endpoint document channel (`EndpointServices.documents.open {channel: <the endpoint's range>, artifactId, actor: {kind: 'human'}, doc}`): each direction carries document frames (`src/artifacts/provider/frames.ts`), each prefixed by its u32be length, under the other side's credit; in a `view` open the endpoint forwards only step 1 and awareness from the device and drops update and step 2 (counted; a device that keeps writing past 64 KiB of dropped frames is closed `read-only`); the channel's end (`documents.onClosed`) closes the stream with its word (`host`, `reset`, `upstream`); the device reopens after any close with the provider's own backoff and its unacked updates in the next step 2. Reserved, built by O17 M9 on this path: the kind `browser.screencast` and the capability `rl2.browser-screencast`, with the open `browser: {appSessionId, epoch, tabId, control?: {op}}` (a controlling open naming a done `machine.browserControl` act beside the row, the combination M9 adds), `streamControl {ack: {seq}} | {input: {seq, event}}`, frames of a u32be metadata length, JSON metadata of at most 4 KiB and a JPEG of at most 1 MiB, a backlog of one frame plus 4 KiB in place of `STREAM_BACKLOG_BYTES`, and its own refusal and close words (O17's §K); no validator admits the name until M9.

    // the link's half
    export interface StreamRequest { kind: 'pty' | 'tcp' | 'http' | 'document'; op?: string; credit?: number; pty?: { ptyId: string; from: number; inputEpoch: string; inputFrom: number; inputSent?: number }; tcp?: { forward: string };
                                     http?: { url: string; method: 'GET' | 'HEAD'; range?: string }; document?: { artifactId: string; mode: 'edit' | 'view'; doc?: 'suggestions' } }   // M5b: op absent for a read open, sent only to a peer listing rl2.read-open (else closed('not-offered'))
    export interface StreamSink { opened(opened: StreamOpened): void; data(offset: number, bytes: Uint8Array): void; credit(bytes: number): void; closed(reason: string): void }   // StreamOpened gains http?: { status, headers } (M5b)
    export interface RemoteStream { readonly id: string; send(bytes: Uint8Array): void; control(control: unknown): void; credit(bytes: number): void; close(reason?: string): void }
    // RemoteLink gains:
    openStream(request: StreamRequest, sink: StreamSink): RemoteStream;      // closed('not-offered') at once when the peer lists no rl2.streams
    peerCapabilities(): readonly string[] | null;                             // the current channel's h2 list; null while no channel is current (M2)

**§O. Commands and fields O7 adds to the wire** (each an additive manifest change).

- `pty.op`, `pty.opQuery` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/pty.ts`, owner `pty-host`, grade `internal`; M3): the shapes of `machine.op` and `machine.opQuery` (`MachineOpInputSchema`, `HostOpOutputSchema`, `HostOpQueryOutputSchema`), over `<pty host directory>/pty-ops.jsonl` (`src/pty-host/ops.ts`); runs exactly `pty.spawn`, `pty.attach`, `pty.kill` and `pty.listFor` through the host's table (any other name `unavailable`), forces `credit: false` on an attach, and keeps and answers an attach's result as `{info, ringStart, nextOffset}` (no `replay`, no `checkpoint`).
- `pty.listFor` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/pty.ts`, owner `pty-host`, grade `shell`; M3): input `{appSessionId}`, output `pty.list`'s, filtered to that session's ptys.
- `pty.replay` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/pty.ts`, owner `pty-host`, grade `internal`, main-only like `pty.recaptureEnv`; M3): input `{ptyId, from}`, output `{replay: Uint8Array, replayFrom: number /* max(from, ring.start) */, nextOffset}`; `ring.since` from an offset, touching no credit and no checkpoint. The endpoint's in-process read at a `pty` stream's open.
- `session.spawn` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/session.ts`, owner `machine`, grade `spawn`, route class machine-wide (the renderer passes `{machine}`); M4):

      input:  { checkout: string; worktree?: string; brief?: string; model?: string; effort?: string; permission_mode: string }   // DX6's spawn fields, exactly; the mode required
      output: { status: 'ok'; appSessionId: AppSessionId } | { status: 'refused'; reason: 'untrusted' | 'unknown-checkout' | 'worktree-exists' | string }

- `SessionRecordSchema.launchedBy?: { member: string; device: string; opId: string; action: JsonValue }` (`src/shared/graph/session.ts`; M4): set by `session.spawn`'s handler from `HandlerContext.op`; `action` is the sealed spawn action as the device sent it, RL9's proof for O12's observer. Stored in `sessions.launched_by`, an `ADDED_COLUMNS` entry (no index version; Decision Log, M4a1 fix wave 2).
- `MachineOpInputSchema.action?: JsonValue` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/machine.ts`; M4): the sealed action beside the claim, sent by the endpoint's `actions.ts` for every `machine` act except `files.upload`, whose claim's fingerprint binds it (`KEPT_WITHOUT_ACTION`), and stored with the ledger's claim record.
- `HandlerContext.op?: { opId: string; opCreated: string; member: string; device: string; fingerprint: string; action?: unknown }` (`src/shared/ipc/client.ts`; M4): set by `machineOps.op` for the claimed run; absent for a local client's own command.
- `MainContext.session?: AppSessionId` and the preload envelope's third argument `MachineRef | { machine: MachineRef; session?: AppSessionId }` (`src/main/ipc/router.ts`, `src/preload/bridge.ts`; main-to-renderer, outside the manifest; M4): the session a path-class command is made from, which `dispatch.ts` sends as `call.session`.
- `files.upload`'s `part?: { upload: string; index: number; last: boolean }` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/files.ts`; M4): with it, `files` holds one entry of at most `REMOTE_UPLOAD_PART_BYTES` (2 MiB decoded); staging `<root>/.staging/<upload>`; a part at an unexpected index is `{status: 'refused', reason: 'part out of order'}`; the last part answers the file's path, earlier ones `paths: []`.
- `forward.open` (an endpoint action name, not a registry command; M3): args `{ port: number }` (1 to 65535); result `{ forward: string; port: number }`; the forward is kept per `(device, forward)` and survives a channel loss for `RESUME_MS`.
- `pong`'s `received?: number` and `sent?: number` (`apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts`; M7): the endpoint's clock at the ping's arrival and the pong's send, milliseconds.
- `host.artifactOp` (engine-host; `src/shared/ipc/schema/host.ts`, `src/engine-host/ops.ts`; M5a): the claim with no `context` (`opId`, `opCreated`, `member`, `device`, `fingerprint`, `target`, `name`, `input`), served by `host.op`'s ledger; it runs only the names in `ARTIFACT_OP_COMMANDS` (the thirteen artifact writes; anything else `unavailable`), with no status check, and `host.opQuery` answers its ops; an engine host that predates it answers `unknown_command`, which the endpoint answers `refused unavailable` for this command alone. `host.op` keeps its required `context`.
- The claim's `target?: string` (`host.artifactOp`'s and `MachineOpInputSchema`'s, `src/shared/ipc/schema/{host,machine}.ts`; `src/engine-host/ops.ts`, `src/services/machine-ops.ts`; M5a): the target the endpoint resolved for an artifact-targeted act, `'artifact:<id>'` (`'artifact:'` for the orphan form), stored beside the claim and answered by `host.opQuery` and `machine.opQuery` as the binding's `target` where today a session id or null stands; `queries.ts` re-places an `artifact:` target through the rule.
- Bytes in the op's `input` (M5a): a tagged `{"$bytes": …}` value is a JSON value and passes `JsonValueSchema`; `host.op` and `machine.op` decode a copy with `json-bytes`' `fromWire` before their own `validateCommandInput` (which runs before the handler in both ledgers), invoke the validated value, and store the claim and action tagged; `src/shared/host/json-bytes.ts` exports `toWire` and `fromWire` beside `encodeJsonLine` and `decodeJsonLine`.
- `library.read`'s `export?: { revision: number; format: string; exportId?: string }` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/library.ts`, `src/services/library/files.ts`; M5a): the read of an export under `<root>/<artifactId>/exports/`, its name from `artifactExportName`'s grammar, a regular file with no link on the way (`delivery.deliver`'s admission), through `readResolved`'s rules (`offset`, `length` ≤ 8 MiB per call, `hash`); without `export`, the read of a `file` artifact as today.
- `artifacts.open`'s output `readOnly?: true` and `remote.openCopy` (main-owned, outside the manifest; §K; M5a, M5b).
- `delivery.send` retagged `machine` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/delivery.ts`; grade `manage` on the artifact, A14; M5c): served by the spine's delivery part (export, then deliver, in process); main's `delivery/send.ts` stays as the shim for a `mawsd` whose welcome lists no `machine.delivery`; `delivery.deliver` stays served for the OX1 window.
- `machine.delivery` (`src/shared/host/socket-protocol.ts`, `PEER_CAPABILITIES.daemon`, `WIRE_CAPABILITIES`; M5c): the daemon's welcome says it serves `delivery.send`.
- `connectors.connect`, `connectors.reconnect` and `connectors.signIn` gain `callback?: { port: number }` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/connectors.ts`; M5c): the asking device's loopback port for the consent's redirect; the flow registers `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/callback`, binds no listener, and waits for `connectors.callback`.
- `connectors.callback` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/connectors.ts`, owner `machine`, grade `list`; M5c): `{ port: number; method: string; url: string /* path and query as received, ≤ 4096 chars */ }` → `{ status: number; headers?: Record<string, string>; body: string }`; the flow armed for `port` runs its own callback handler over the request (method, duplicate parameters, `error`, `state`, `iss`) and answers what it would have written to the browser; no flow armed for the port answers `{status: 404, body: ''}`.
- `connectors.reset`'s `machine?: MachineRef` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/connectors.ts`'s events; main-owned, off the manifest; M5c): the renderer rebuilds only the named machine's surfaces; main names the local machine on its own emission, and an absent `machine` reads as the local machine; a reset never rebuilds a surface of another machine.
- `artifacts.copyAsset`'s grade `{floor: 'manage', byEffect: true}` (`src/shared/ipc/grades/artifacts.ts`; M5a): `manage` on `to`, and `view` on `from` by the endpoint's `BY_EFFECT` rule.
- `STREAM_REFUSALS` gains `read-only` (`apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts`; M5b): sent only in answer to a read open (§N).
- Endpoint action and call names outside the registry (M5b, M5c): `artifacts.fetch` and `documents.open` (stream rows, §N), `client.windowShown {shown: boolean}` → `{ok: true}` (a call, `list`).

**§P. The step-up sheet** (`src/main/device/step-up-sheet.ts`; M2).

    import type { Confirm } from './step-up';
    export function sheetConfirmation(deps: {
      window(): BrowserWindow | null;                 // the focused window, else any; null: a window-less box
      dialog: Pick<typeof dialog, 'showMessageBox'>;
      prompt: Confirm;                                // promptTouchIDConfirmation(systemPreferences) in production
    }): Confirm;
    // message: prompt.summary; detail: prompt.text; buttons ['Continue', 'Cancel'], defaultId 0, cancelId 1; type 'question'.
    // Continue → prompt(prompt); Cancel or a closed sheet → { ok: false, reason: 'cancelled' }.
    export function harnessConfirmation(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv, packaged: boolean): Confirm | null;   // MAWS_E2E=1, unpackaged, MAWS_E2E_STEP_UP=accept → resolves { ok: true }; else null

`src/main/index.ts` passes `harnessConfirmation(…) ?? sheetConfirmation(…)` as `confirm` to both `createStepUp` calls and to `createStateSigner`.

**§Q. The e2e scene** (`e2e/support/remote-scene.ts`, `e2e/support/fake-sminos.ts`; M2, extended by M3, M4, M6, M7).

    export interface RemoteScene {
      org: LocalOrg;                                  // e2e/support/local-org.ts
      b: ProfileDaemon & { ref: MachineRef; directPort: number };   // profile B: private mawsd, MAWS_ENDPOINT_DIRECT=1, a fake tailnet table, enrolled as 'B'
      sminos: { list(session: { engine: string; app: string; owners: string[]; project: string }): Promise<void>; gone(engine: string): Promise<void> };
                                                      // the harness's fake sminos on B's RL10 socket: a `member` frame and the project's presence per session
