      devOrgDir(directMap: boolean): string;          // A's MAWS_DEV_ORG: session.json (hub: the local org, standinUser: the admin; directMap when asked) and chain.json listing A's device
      stop(): Promise<void>;
    }
    export async function startRemoteScene(opts: { scratch: string; aUserData: string }): Promise<RemoteScene>;

- `session.json` gains `directMap?: Record<string, string>` (an advertised `ws://` address to the one to dial), honoured by `dev-session.ts` only in an unpackaged build and exposed on the dev session, from which `src/main/index.ts` builds `linkDeps.dialDirect` for `startMachines` (`MachinesDeps.linkDeps`, passed to `createRemoteSource`, which already takes them); it is read once at the session's load, so a spec that wants both carriers launches A twice.
- `e2e/support/fake-sminos.ts`: `test/endpoint/rl10/fake-sminos.ts` bundled with esbuild to CommonJS (the `local-org.ts` pattern), started against B's `<profile>/mawsd/endpoint/rl10.json`. The endpoint lists what it says at the local hub (the production path), and A's `org.rows` carry the row.
- A is launched with `MAWS_E2E_STEP_UP=accept` (§P) for the scenes that spawn or act as a non-owner; the signatures and the endpoint's checks stay real.
- Profile B's engine is the replay engine with `e2e:c7`'s fixtures; the spec starts B's session over `machine.sock` (`src/mawsd/machine-client.ts`'s `reachMachine`, which `profile-daemon.ts` imports), lists it through `scene.sminos.list`, and reads B's transcript the same way to confirm what A sent.

**§R. Feeds** (M4, M6; `src/endpoint/plane-feeds.ts`). A feed is a subscription to a machine's events that have no session cursor: `sub {sub, feed, session?, cursor, credit}` behind `rl2.feeds`; every opening is `subbed {mode: 'snapshot', epoch: '<feed>:<endpoint boot id>', seq}`, and each later event is an `ev` at the next `seq`. The device dedups by `(epoch, seq)`; a reconnect opens again from a snapshot. A feed lives with its channel; it ends with the channel, `removed` with the device, or `behind` once its unread backlog passes plane B's bound (M4a2), which the device treats as a reconnect.
- `checkout` (M4; `session` present; authorized at `view` on it): no `snap`; each `files.changed` and `scm.remote.output` naming the session and each `git.changed` whose `repoRoot` is one of the session's repositories (the repository of each of its record's roots, as the `checkout` rule resolves them) goes as `ev {body: {name, payload}}`; so does each `session.changed` of the session (M4b, from O5's landing audit), which is how a device holding no live stream learns the session runs again without placing it on the engine host or reporting interest, either of which keeps a parked host from parking. While a change of roots is being read, `git.changed` is held and released only against the new bounds.
- `attention` (M6; `session` absent; admitted at `list`): the `snap` is `attention.list`'s answer filtered to the sessions the member holds `view` on; each `attention.changed` of such a session is `ev {body: {appSessionId, items}}`; at every record change and new head the set is recomputed, a session that left it gets `ev {body: {appSessionId, items: []}}` and one that joined gets its current items at once; an empty set is an open, empty feed.
- `artifacts` (M5a; `session` absent; admitted at `list`; behind `rl2.artifacts`): no `snap`; each `artifacts.changed` (engine-host; `{artifactId, kind, revision, change}`) whose artifact the member holds `view` on by the `artifact` rule (its per-channel cache; a `created` change resolved once) and every `library.changed` (`machine`; `{revision}`) go as `ev {body: {name, payload}}`; from M5c also `connectors.watchUpdate` for a lease this channel opened (the endpoint keeps the lease ids from its `connectors.open` results), `connectors.serversChanged` and `connectors.signInChanged` for a member holding `machine-admin`, and `delivery.receipt` for a member holding `view` on its artifact. The adapter opens one per `up` link and emits each event `emitFrom(machine, …)`.
- The `checkout` feed also carries its session's `session.capabilitiesChanged` (`machine`; `{appSessionId, capabilities}`; M5a), as it carries `session.changed`.
- The `browser.inventory` feed (`session` present; at `view`; a snapshot at open, then a complete snapshot at each change) is O17 M9's to add on this mechanism, not M5's.
The link's `subscribe` takes `{feed, session?}` in place of `session` alone.

**§S. The remote bench** (M7; `e2e/remote-latency.spec.ts`, `test/support/remote-latency.ts`). `test-results/remote-latency.json`:

    { local: Measures, direct: Measures & { rtt: Stats }, relay: Measures & { rtt: Stats }, bounds: { deltaToFrameP50Ms, roundTripP50Ms, openToFirstRowMs: 1500, resumeMs }, invalidReasons: string[], budgetMisses: string[], load: … }
    // Measures: { deltaToFrame: Stats; roundTrip: Stats; openToFirstRowMs: number; resumeMs: number; engineStartMs: number }; Stats: { p50, p95, n }

`e2e:remote-latency-live` writes `remote-latency-live.json` with, per carrier, `clock: { offsetMs, intervalMs: [low, high] /* offset ± rtt/2 of the exchange */, spreadMs, samples }` from `pong`'s `received` and `sent` (§O), a `verdict: 'pass' | 'miss' | 'inconclusive'` per bound (delta-to-paint judged at the interval's worst edge; `inconclusive` when the interval is wider than the bound's allowance), and voids the run when `spreadMs > 5`.

**§T. M5's seams** (added at M5's re-plan, 2026-11-01).

    // src/endpoint/offers.ts (M5b): a third row kind
    export interface OfferRow { kind: 'call' | 'act' | 'stream'; executor: OfferExecutor; target: TargetRule; grade?: GradeSpec | null; accepts?(input: unknown): boolean; stream?: StreamKind /* the kind a stream row admits */ }
    export type TargetRule = 'appSessionId' | 'session' | 'machine' | 'checkout' | 'artifact';   // M5a adds `artifact` (M6 adds `each-session` beside it; the later lander keeps both)

    // src/endpoint/targets.ts (M5a): the artifact form of Resolved
    { kind: 'artifact'; artifactId: string | null /* null: artifacts.create, the orphan form */; sessions: SessionTarget[] /* each placed through laneB; unavailable or unknown ones left out */; orphan: boolean }
    // src/endpoint/artifacts.ts (M5a)
    export class ArtifactRanks { of(channel, artifactId): Promise<ArtifactResolution>; drop(artifactId): void; dropSession(appSessionId): void; dropChannel(channel): void }   // 256 entries per channel, FIFO; library.entry on the asker's behalf

    // src/endpoint/services.ts and src/mawsd/endpoint-seam.ts (M5c): the port's remote targets
    clientPort: { serve(ref: ClientRef, target: PortTarget): RemotePortHandle };   // PortTarget is src/mawsd/client-port.ts's {post(request): boolean}; the front's route(ref) consults these after its own connections
    interface RemotePortHandle { answer(input: { id: string; answer: unknown }): void; close(): void }   // answer settles through createClientPort's answer(from, input) with the registered target as `from`; close is gone(target)

    // src/main/machines/index.ts (M5a): the budgets dep, from src/main/app.ts
    MachinesDeps.budgets?: (name: string) => number | undefined;   // COMMAND_BUDGETS_MS[name]; the link and the socket link add RTT_MARGIN_MS (1000) and fall back to their defaults

    // src/main/artifacts/machines.ts (M5a)
    export interface ArtifactMachines { of(artifactId: string): MachineRef | null; record(artifactId: string, machine: MachineRef): void; forget(machine: MachineRef): void /* a link closed */ }

    // src/main/artifacts/remote-scheme.ts (M5b): the responder serveArtifacts runs after the presenter's embeds and before the local path
    export function remoteArtifactResponder(deps: { machines: ArtifactMachines; fetch(machine: MachineRef, request: { url: string; method: 'GET' | 'HEAD'; range?: string }): Promise<RemoteFetch> }): SchemeResponder;
    // RemoteFetch: { status, headers, body: ReadableStream<Uint8Array> | null }; at most 8 http streams per link in flight, the rest queued; 503 while the link is down, 504 past 30 s with nothing opened

    // src/main/artifacts/remote-open.ts (M5b): artifacts.open for a remote artifact
    export function openRemoteDocument(deps: { machines: ArtifactMachines; open(machine: MachineRef, request: StreamRequest, sink: StreamSink): RemoteStream; get(machine, artifactId): Promise<Artifact | null> }, input: { artifactId: string; doc?: 'suggestions' }, window: WebContents): Promise<{ channel: number; readOnly?: true }>;
    // edit first; on `grade` or `read-only`, view with readOnly; one MessageChannelMain per open, the window's end posted on maws:doc-port as openDocument does

    // src/main/lane-b/remote-port.ts (M5c): the device's half of the port
    export function createRemotePort(deps: { openExternal(url: string): Promise<boolean>; listen(): Promise<CallbackListener> }): RemotePort;
    interface CallbackListener { port: number; close(): void; onRequest(handler: (request: { method: string; url: string }) => Promise<{ status: number; headers?: Record<string, string>; body: string }>): void }
    // answers `client` messages: openExternal {url} opened here; {path} and reveal answered not here; every other kind noClient.
    // A listener per consent flow: bound before the act, its port in the act's `callback`; it relays each request as `connectors.callback` and writes the answer;
    // it relays every request until the act's refusal, the flow's end on the feed, CONNECT_CALLBACK_TIMEOUT_MS plus one round trip, or the link's close (an answered request does not end it),
    // and its port stays bound and never reused until then; the machine arms one receiver per (client, port), refuses a second `busy`, and each flow removes only its own registration.

**§U. The fix wave's rows and shapes** (M8, M9; planned 2026-11-04). Rows are `src/endpoint/offers.ts` entries. `laneB` rows run on the engine host under the session's target. Every act is named in `ENGINE_HOST_ACTS` (or `MACHINE_ACTS`) for the adapter's `handles`.

| Command | Row | Grade (registry) | Milestone |
|---|---|---|---|
| `maws.asks.list` | `laneB('call')` | `view` | M8 |
| `maws.ask.answer` | `laneB('act')` | `converse` | M8 |
| `side.question.list` | `laneB('call')` | `view` | M8 |
| `side.question.clear` | `laneB('act')` | `converse` | M8 |
| `session.reloadPlugins` | `laneB('act')` | `manage` | M8 |
| `fs.subagents`, `fs.workflows` | `laneB('call')` | `view` | M8 |
| the session-scoped `view` reads the audit finds unoffered (`session.getHooksListing`, `getMemoryDialog`, `getSandboxDialog`, `getSkillsDialog`, `session.drafts.get`, `session.observedFacts`, …) | `laneB('call')`, or declared | `view` | M8 |
| `fs.tasks` | `laneB('call')` under the `task-list` target rule (`listId` must be the named session's own list) | `view` | M8 |
| `mods.list` (no session target) | declared in `REMOTE_UNAVAILABLE`, tracker line | `view` | M8 |
| `machine.folders.list` (new) | `{call, executor 'machine', target 'machine', owners: true}`, row grade `list` | `spawn` | M9 |
| `session.projects.addFolder` | `{act, executor 'machine', target 'machine', owners: true}` in `MACHINE_ACTS`, row grade `machine-admin` | `spawn` | M9 |
| `session.trust.check` | `{call, executor 'machine', target 'machine', owners: true}`, row grade `list` | `spawn` | M9 |
| `session.operations` (new) | `laneB('call')` | `view` | M8 |
| `connectors.signInServer` (new; sign-in by a server's name and scope, the entry read on the session's machine) | `machineAdmin`, offered as `connectors.signIn` is, in `MACHINE_ACTS` and `CONSENT_COMMANDS` (m8-3) | `machine-admin` | M8 |
| `files.readUploads` (new, `internal`: the endpoint's resolver for `uploads`, the sending session's own upload root only) | not offered; called by the endpoint | `internal` | M8 |
| `fs.unfollow` | `laneB('call')` with `accepts: namesAnAgent` (the caller's own follow of a named agent; the session-wide form refused) | `view` | M8 |

  *(m8-3.)* The device turns a session-wide `fs.unfollow {agentId: null}` of a remote session into one named unfollow for each agent it follows there. It records each from its own `fs.agentEvents` reads, waits for any read still out, and counts a read that failed as possibly followed. An agent is removed only when its unfollow succeeds, and a refusal is the answer.

- **`REMOTE_UNAVAILABLE`** (`src/shared/ipc/remote-unavailable.ts`): `Record<CommandName, string>`, each command's reason in the words its control shows ("Not on another machine yet: …"). It is read by the renderer's controls and by `test/lane-b/offer-coverage.test.ts`.
- **The `operation` and panel parts** (`src/shared/host/stream-catchup.ts`): `{part: 'operation', body: OperationEvent}`, `{part: 'subagents', body: {agents}}`, `{part: 'workflow', body: {runId}}` and `{part: 'side', body: {exchanges}}`. Each is emitted by `remote-session.ts` as its event (`session.operation`, `fs.subagentsChanged`, `fs.workflowChanged`, the side store's change) under the machine's address. An older device drops an unknown part. *(m8-3, 2026-11-04.)* The device emits nothing for `side`: a side question's completion is its `operation` part, which the side store patches its log from or re-lists on. At every `subbed` after a stream's first (a `behind` reopen or the link's resubscription), main makes each panel re-list: `fs.tasksChanged` for the session's list, an `fs.subagents` read emitted as `fs.subagentsChanged` (dropped if a newer `subagents` part arrived while it was out), `fs.workflowChanged {appSessionId, runId: ''}` (both consumers re-list the session's runs whatever the `runId`), and `remote.reopened {appSessionId}` (main-owned, outside the wire manifest) for the side store's `refreshSide`. A `behind` end reopens from the cursor at once after progress; with the cursor standing it backs off from 250 ms doubling to 5 s, and after three reopens in a row with no progress the stream shows `gap` and falls back to the idle retry with its own doubling backoff, which only cursor progress resets.
- **`fs.tasks`'s input** gains `appSessionId?` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/fs.ts`), required by the `task-list` rule over RL2; a `tasks` part carries `fs.tasksChanged`.
- **`machine.folders.list`** (M9): input `{path?: string, open?: true, cursor?: string}`; output `{entries: [{name, path, protected: boolean}], cursor: string | null}`. `protected` marks a macOS TCC-protected folder, which is read inside only with `open: true`.
- **The forwarded failure** (`src/endpoint/calls.ts`): `{code, message?}`, with the message capped at 512 characters.
- **`RouteSchema`** (`src/shared/engine/route.ts`): `z.enum(['gateway', 'direct', 'machine'])`.
- **The record's `gateway`** gains:
  - `route?: Route`;
  - `observed?: {kind: 'claudeusage' | 'login' | 'host', baseUrl: string | null, login?: string, organization?: string}`.
- **The status's `gateway`** gains `route?: Route` and `observed?` (the record's shape); `mode` and `verified` keep their enums, written with the legacy route (`verified` is `routed` or `direct` on `machine`, never `mismatch`).
- **`LaunchSpec`, the setup intent, `StartOptions` and `engine.models`' input** gain `route?: Route` beside their legacy `gateway`.
- **`machine-route.json`** (new, beside `machine-settings.json`, which is unchanged): `{version: 1, defaultRoute: Route}`. The default resolves to its `defaultRoute` when the file is present, else to `direct` when `machine-settings.json`'s `defaultRoute` is `direct`, else to `machine`. *(m9-1.)* A legacy `gateway.defaultRoute` patch (an older UI's Gateway control) writes `machine-route.json` only when it changes the legacy value held, so a re-save of the same value leaves a resolved `machine` alone. Choosing `machine` writes the legacy field as `legacyRouteOf` over the machine's effective base URL. Against a `mawsd` that predates M9, main's mirror sets `gateway`/`direct` through the legacy patch and refuses `machine` before sending ("This machine's mawsd predates the machine route; choose gateway or direct until it runs this build.").
  - *Known limits (accepted by the architect, 2026-11-04):* a deliberate re-pick of Gateway in an older UI while it reads `gateway` leaves the default `machine`; and an older UI keeps showing Gateway while the route is `machine`, a display limit of older builds, not a route change.
- **`route-unsupported`**: a start refusal for `route: 'machine'` on an engine host without `engine.route-machine` when the machine names a host other than the relay. *(m9-2.)* "Names a host other than the relay" means the legacy route would not be equivalent: a resolved base URL other than `RELAY_URL`, any of the direct route's keys resolving, or an uncertain resolution. With the relay down on such a host the start refuses with `ROUTE_RELAY_UNSUPPORTED` ("ClaudeUsage's relay isn't answering, and this machine's engine host predates the machine route, so it can't start past the relay check; wait for the relay, restart MAWS there, or choose direct for this session."), and Start anyway is not offered there. Both travel as `invalid {field: 'route'}`.
- **The base-URL resolution's sources** (M9's review, 2026-11-05; `src/engine-host/models-cache.ts` `effectiveBaseUrl`, `src/shared/engine/config-dir.ts`). Lowest first, a later source winning and an empty value counting as none: the spawn environment; the global config's `env` through the engine's own file selection (`configFilePath`: `.config.json` when it exists, else `.claude.json`, or `.claude-custom-oauth.json` under `CLAUDE_CODE_CUSTOM_OAUTH_URL`), shared with the trust reader; user `settings.json`; project settings; local settings; the launch profile's `--settings` env; managed settings. `CLAUDE_CODE_RESTRICTED` empties the user, project and local sources.
  - *Fails closed* (the architect's ruling): the resolution is uncertain, so an older host refuses `route-unsupported`, when a source it does not model is present: `remote-settings.json` in the config directory (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `~/.claude`, where the engine reads it, not the managed directory), a `policyHelper` or `policyHelpers` key in a settings file it reads, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_COWORK_PLUGINS` on, or a gateway key in the global file the engine does not select where both it and the legacy `.config.json` exist. Existence and key checks only; nothing is executed.
  - Legacy direct deletes and blanks only `GATEWAY_KEYS`, so `apiKeyHelper`, `awsAuthRefresh`, `awsCredentialExport` and `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`/`VERTEX` stay in force exactly as on the machine route (`launch.test.ts`): they are not non-equivalence keys.
  - *Known limit (accepted by the architect, 2026-11-05):* a plugin's `settings.read` overlay is not told; its presence is a loaded plugin module's hook, not a file or key, and failing closed on any enabled plugin would refuse the machine route on almost every older host. No plugin installed on the owner's MacBook declares one (2026-11-05: none of the 65 cached plugins' latest versions; cua's `settings.readPaths` is unrelated). Modelling it would mean asking the engine before launch, which D-1's architecture doesn't do. The tracker line on the unmodelled sources carries it.
- **`engine.models`' `route`** (M9's review). A capable engine host resolves `machine` by `effectiveBaseUrl`: the relay gives the gateway catalogue, anything else the direct one, an uncertain resolution the direct one without gateway models. mawsd's `interceptEngine` strips `route` for an engine host that does not list `engine.route-machine` (or is not yet welcomed), sending the legacy key the resolution gives. The renderer sends `route` only for `machine`, and reads a session's default route and its daemon's support from the session's own machine (`machineOf`).
- **The machine route's guards** (m9-2; `src/services/sessions/machine-route.ts`, `start-guards.ts`). Before anything is created, a machine-route start or resume is judged on the settings its launch will read:
  - a new worktree not yet made, by the `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json` its branch tracks at the ref it will check out (a new branch's base, an existing branch's head), read from the main checkout through the read-only git runner, the local text beneath the store the worktree reads at the main checkout (`linkedWorktreeStore`); a worktree an earlier attempt made and kept, where it stands;
  - a resume, with its effective requested model (the option's, else the record's).
  The launch's own resolution in the made checkout still decides what the host is sent.
- **The record's chosen route** (m9-2) is stored at each `launching` answer from the spec's `readRoute`, before an older host's spec has `route` stripped; a status without a route no longer clears it (it clears `observed`). An explicit Gateway or Direct launch replaces it. Two nullable index columns carry `route` and `observed` (`src/services/index` `ADDED_COLUMNS`, `user_version` unmoved).
- **`startAnyway?: true`** (m9-2) on `session.start`'s and the acquire's options: the person's continuation past the advisory relay sheet on a capable host.
- **`engine.route-machine`**: an engine host capability; main sends `route: 'machine'` only to a host listing it.
- **`setupRefusal`** (`src/endpoint/policy.ts`): `addFolder`'s directory exists, its real path is under the machine's home with no symlink escape, and it is not inside a protected folder that was not opened. *(m9-3.)* It also holds `session.trust.check` (`SETUP_DIRECTORY_COMMANDS`), and the act runs on the real path it checked. The path is resolved as the kernel resolves it (a `..` climbs from where a link led; each name compared as its folder lists it, protected names case-folded), every link on the way resolves under the home, and more than 32 links is a loop. Its refusals are new reasons, `no-folder`, `outside-home`, `symlink-escape` and `protected`, passed by main's adapter as `internal` with `issues.refusal`. A protected folder counts as opened once the endpoint has answered an `open: true` listing in it for that member, kept per member for the endpoint's lifetime (latest 32), so a reconnect doesn't undo the owner's consent. `~/Library/Mobile Documents` itself is protected. The listing lists neither links nor files.
- **The setup reads before trust** (m9-3; `src/shared/machine/git-reads.ts`, `git-index.ts`, `setup-reads.ts`; the architect's ruling, 2026-11-04). No remote setup row spawns git in a folder before its trust is accepted, and nothing outside the folder is read except through the checked resolver.
  - The trust read and the registration read git's own files directly, each through the walk: the `.git` pointer, `gitdir`, `commondir` (a pointer's content resolved by the walk, not lexically), the config with no include followed (internal whitespace kept as git keeps it), the common dir's `worktrees`, HEAD, and the main checkout's `index` for the fold's content check. Discovery stops at a device change, as git does. The endpoint's own check walks the same pointers (at most 4097 bytes each) and holds the common config to the protected rule; `machine.setupTrust` (internal) guards each trust file at its read on the machine, and the global trust store is guarded before its legacy file is probed.
  - The index reader takes versions 2 to 4, at most 64 MiB and 1,000,000 entries, each decoded name at most 4096 bytes and all of them 16 MiB, and answers only plain top-level names. A split or sparse index, any mandatory extension it doesn't read, or anything malformed answers null, which folds nothing.
  - The registration's org derivation runs inside the registration, so it reads `.smns`, the pointers and the config's real path through the walk, never takes its `rev-parse` fallback (an invalid layout has no org until the refresh after trust), and makes one git call, `git config --file <checked config> --no-includes`, from the temp directory with `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` (without that isolation git read the global and the cwd repository's config, includes and all).
  - *Known limits:* `safe.directory`'s ownership check is not mirrored (the direct read answers a key where git would refuse a repository owned by another user); the fold reads the index, so a main checkout whose smallest top-level files are staged but not committed folds by the staged content; a sparse index folds nothing before trust.
  - *Outside the setup reads (the architect's scope, 2026-11-04):* an ordinary `session.spawn` without accepted trust reaches `bindTrust → trustKeyOf → rev-parse` through master's hardened runner before answering `untrusted`. The runner runs no repository code; an `include.path` into a protected folder remains, as it does for local sessions on master. A tracker line carries it.
- **The operation outcomes** (M8): `OperationTable` keeps each completed outcome by `opId`, at most 64 per session for 10 minutes; `session.operations {appSessionId, opIds}` (new, `view`, `laneB('call')`) answers `{outcomes: [{opId, event} | {opId, pending: true} | {opId, unknown: true}]}`; the `operation` part's snapshot key is `operation:<opId>`. *(m8-3.)* The device asks it at every `subbed` for the receipts its waiters hold, and at once for a receipt heard after its opening had reconciled. A completion is emitted once, and a late one once more. A waiter's first may be the late one the host kept in its place, said `late: false`. `unknown` settles as `engine_error` with the spec's sentence, and anything later for that opId is dropped. Every stream end invalidates that subscription's in-flight status and record reads.
- **The send's uploads** (M8): `session.send`'s input gains `uploads?: [{uploadId, as: 'document'}]`, behind `rl2.upload-blocks` (RL2 1.9). The endpoint reads each through `files.readUploads`: the sending session's own upload root, no link on the way, still there, within the day, a PDF within the engine's bound. A refusal is `ack refused malformed`, with nothing queued, and the engine host refuses a send that still names uploads. `SEND_UPLOADS_MAX` (100) bounds the uploads one message names, apart from `UPLOAD_FILES_MAX` (8), which bounds one `files.upload` call. The composer splits uploads across calls and refuses past the message's bound before anything goes up; the bytes stay bounded per document (32 MiB) and per message (48 MiB) *(M8's review)*. *(m8-3.)* The device sends `uploads` with the act's `requires: 'rl2.upload-blocks'` when the current peer lists it, or when there is no current channel (the act waits for one within its `sendBy`, and the channel it goes on judges it). A `not-offered` from a channel whose peer lists no `rl2.upload-blocks` is sent again, within the same bound, with the mention alone, and logged. Any other refusal is the send's error. A `malformed` refusal of a send that carried uploads is answered `invalid_input` with REMOTE_UPLOAD_REFUSED_WORDS ("The message wasn’t sent. If its attachment is more than a day old or isn’t a supported file, attach it again.", the architect's wording, since the refusal can't say which part failed) and `issues: {refusal: 'malformed', uploads: 'refused'}`.
- **`connectors.signInServer`** (M8) takes `{name, scope, cwd, appSessionId}`. `connectors.signIn`'s `path` stays required, because a request field moved from required to optional breaks an older receiver.
- **`session.spawn`'s input** gains `trust?: 'accepted'`, accepted by `spawnRefusal` only for a current machine owner, `spec-field` for anyone else.
- **The spawned-here lead** (M8, m8-4a; `src/renderer/src/sessions/spawned-here.ts`, read by the sidebar only through `leadsAsSpawned`).
  - **The mark.** A mark kept in the window's memory is set when `session.start` answers `starting` or a `session.spawn` succeeds. It records the setup phase held at that moment, so a retry's lead ignores an earlier attempt's failure.
  - **Where the lead sits.** While it holds, the session leads its group under the pinned rows, under either sort. Its tree is always shown: the cap grows by one, and Show more counts with the same tree-wide predicate.
  - **When it ends:**
    - at the first turn's end while the session is attached;
    - at an exited process, at once;
    - for a listing, at the first rest after `busy`, or when the listing says the session ended;
    - with nothing showing a first turn running, `LISTING_LEAD_MS` ends it;
    - a failed or removed start clears it.
  - *Known limit (accepted by the architect, 2026-11-04):* the mark is per window and is lost on a reload, after which the row ranks by its state.
- **Resume…'s listed rows** (M8, m8-4a). Each listed row shows under its machine (`@<name>`), and so does a remote record's row. Another member's session, in either form, shows only under a row whose People toggle is on, and a hidden row never grants People. A listed row's preview reads nothing remote.
- **The remote greying** (M8, m8-4b; `src/renderer/src/lane-b/remote-unavailable.ts`). Applies to a session reached over RL2 (`isOverRl2`: main holds a `remote.session` for it, or the org rows list its machine), whether lane A or lane B. Socket-linked and local sessions grey nothing.
  - A control greys with its REMOTE_UNAVAILABLE words, or once a `not-offered` refusal has been heard for its command on that machine (`heardRefusal`, now for every session surface).
  - It covers the composer's `!` and `#` modes, `/btw`'s Ask and Retry, the widgets, Session info, Rename and Dismiss, an archived row's Unarchive and Delete…, and the plugin panes.
  - An RL2 session's pane tabs show the reason in place of the plugin's drawing, and ask the machine nothing: no `session.ui.panes`, no show or focus, a local close. Its surface sites draw unhooked.
- **A remote PDF during a running turn** (M8, m8-4b; the architect's option (a), 2026-11-04). A send carrying `uploads` is a `session.send`, never a steer, so it goes through the queue. While a turn runs, the composer says where it will go:
  - "Joins the paused queue; Resume sends it" for any paused queue (an interrupt's pause holds a fresh send while busy, `queue.ts` `submitNow`);
  - "Sends after the queued messages" when messages are waiting;
  - else "Sends after this turn".
  A local busy send steers its document blocks into the running turn, so the remote one differs. The exit is `uploads?` on the steer path (a tracker line).
  The upload's day is one value, `UPLOAD_KEPT_MS` (`@shared/ipc/schema/files`), read by the sweep, the send-time refusal and the composer's advisory.
- **The follow log** (M8, m8-5). The engine host's FsService logs one line when a client holds an agent follow, one when it releases it, and one when the follow is dropped. Each line gives the client's tag, the first 8 hex digits of a sha256 of its id, never the raw id, and the count still holding. Nothing is logged per event.
- **`ANSWERED_IN_MAIN`** (M8, m8-5; `src/main/machines/remote/answered-in-main.ts`) is the named list of the commands main's adapters answer with no offer row, 25 at M8, each with how it is answered. `test/lane-b/offer-coverage.test.ts` holds every session command crossing a link to exactly one of: offered, REMOTE_UNAVAILABLE, or this list. It checks the list both ways against the adapters.
- **The renderer's route** (M9, m9-4; `src/renderer/src/gateway/observed-route.ts`). Features (usage, the model list, the direct fallback, the relay monitor, the routing facts) read a live status's `observed`, then its `mode`; only with no status does the record's `observed`, then `routed`, decide. The route chosen is the live `route`, then the record's `route`, then the live `mode`, so an older host's status keeps showing the machine route the record holds. Session info's Route row names what the machine reached: "The machine’s own · ClaudeUsage relay · <url>", "… · Anthropic login · <login>", or "… · <host>" (the URL's host). The new-session route choice always offers "Machine’s own". Settings › Gateway's default route reads and sets `machineSettings.*`'s `defaultRoute`; against an older `mawsd` the two-route control stays, and a dropped choice is said in main's mirror's sentence.
- **The route switch** (M9, m9-4; the architect's ruling, 2026-11-04) reaches all three routes: on `machine`, gateway and Direct; on gateway or Direct, the other, then "Back to the machine’s own…". The switch back is verified as a launch is: what `get_status` reports is recorded as `observed`, never a mismatch, and the toast names it ("now on this machine’s own setup (ClaudeUsage)"). It starts with `{route: 'machine'}` under the launch's relay check; when the machine's own settings name a relay that isn't answering, the switch asks "This machine’s relay isn’t answering. Switch anyway?" and only Switch anyway sends `startAnyway`. *Known limit:* the question comes after the old launch has stopped, since only main's guard knows whether the settings name the relay; Cancel leaves the session stopped and resumable on its old route. A tracker line carries a dry run before the stop.
- **The remote setup sheet** (M9, m9-4). The machine menu offers a machine with no checkout of the org project as "Set up this project there…"; a non-owner sees it disabled with its reason (ownership learned from the home's first `machine.folders.list` answer). The order is the folder, its trust read, the TrustCard ("Trust", or "Trust and start" at Send), then `addFolder`, so nothing is registered and no Touch ID is asked before the trust answer. Other checkouts' paths are suggested under the target's home and not probed; a private folder opens only after its confirmation, and `open: true` goes only with listings inside it. A request answering after the sheet is gone applies nothing and closes nothing. An `untrusted` refusal at Send offers the same card ("Review trust…") and sends again.
  - *A deliberate difference (2026-11-04):* the remote TrustCard has no "Start without trusting". Locally that is the decision `declined` (no attestation, no trust bootstrap); `session.spawn`'s `trust` carries only `'accepted'`. A tracker line carries a remote `declined`, owners-only exactly as `accepted` is (`acceptsTrust`, `spawnRefusal`), its exit when the spawn input carries it.
- **The owned-machine spawn's step-up** (M10, B; `src/main/device/step-up.ts`, `signsUnasked`). Main's signer judges it on the frozen action it then signs, against the held org head, never on a caller's word.
  - **No prompt.** An owned-machine spawn, trust acceptance included, signs without a prompt. The conditions are all of these:
    - the action is exactly `session.spawn`, with no session, context or digest;
    - the head lists this device and this step-up key under a member;
    - that member is among the machine's owners (`ownerMembers(machineOwners(...))`).

    The trust card's click is the confirmation (local parity). A renderer compromise already holds a promptless owner terminal on that machine, so a prompt there would not be a boundary.
  - **Unchanged.** The signature, the act's args (signed exactly as the person saw them) and `spawnRefusal`'s cap.
  - **Still prompts:** a spawn on a machine the member doesn't own (or one the head doesn't place), every approval, owner and share changes, machine admin, a non-owner's shell, `sminos.spawn` and `session.launch`, and org-state signing. Any doubt prompts too.
- **`mawsd endpoint sync [--json]`** (M10; `src/endpoint/cli.ts`). It waits for a read of the org state that begins after the call (`Endpoint.syncAfter`, the existing `#syncAfter`), then runs `promoteIfListed`, and answers as `endpoint status`. Exit 0 when org-listed, 4 while hub-confirmed, 1 when not enrolled, judged on the state after the read (an unenrollment while it was held answers 1). `mawsd`'s client waits up to 90 s for it (`SYNC_TIMEOUT_MS`), past the 10 s every other endpoint command keeps.
- **The bench agent's `watch`** (M10; `test/support/remote-bench-agent.ts`, `BenchWatch`). It holds one machine.sock link to the scratch daemon for the run. It reads id'd asks on stdin, one JSON line each: `{id, ask}` with `ask` one of `up`, `status`, `keys`, `sync`. It answers each with one line:
  - `up` is answered `{id, answer: {up: true}}`, or `{up: false, why}` while the daemon doesn't answer;
  - `status`, `keys` and `sync` are the endpoint's commands with `--json` through `daemon.endpoint`, answered `{id, answer: {code, out}}`, or `{id, error}` when the daemon doesn't answer.

  It reaches the daemon when asked and holds no link, and drops a link that fails, to reach it again at the next ask. It ends with its transport, as `serve` does. The runner (`BenchRun`) starts it at the stage's start and reads through it the daemon's answer, the rerun's check, the key and the listing. It ends it before the daemon's stop.
- **`BenchRun.load1()` and `loadPrecheck`** (M10; `test/support/remote-bench.ts`). `load1()` gives the mini's 1-minute load from `sysctl -n vm.loadavg` over its transport, or the reason it is unread; on the VM it gives null. `loadPrecheck({mac, machine})` gives the refusal's reason, or null when this Mac and the mini are both under Rule B's 5.

## Open questions for the owner

Each is built provisionally as recommended, unless it says otherwise, and changed if the owner answers differently.

- **A1. When the owner first sees remote machines in MAWS** (scheduling; for the architect and the owner). M1 runs now, but no production app can open a channel until it is signed in to the org, which is O8's sign-in (P5 Decision Log 38). *Recommendation:* split O8's sign-in into a first milestone and dispatch it now. It needs only O6 and O11, both landed, and its production `OrgSession` turns M1 on. *Alternative:* wait for O8 as planned, which also holds O9's remote lane A.
- **A2. How the app shows a machine's path to it.** *Recommendation:* show no carrier on the machine chip. The machine row's hover says "direct (tailnet)" or "via the hub". "Hub unreachable" appears once, apart from any machine's "offline" greying, as P5 §3's "hub-offline, channel-up". A device the org removed greys that machine's rows with "This Mac is no longer allowed on `<machine>`". *Alternatives:* a small relay mark on the chip; showing nothing beyond online and offline. **[owner, 2026-10-28: as recommended; P5 Decision Log 108.]**
- **A3. Whether O7's log view exists** (for the architect first). P5 Decision Logs 51, 75 and 80 name "O7's log views" as a reader of migrated history, and P5 Decision Log 51 calls it "a project-wide view of what a person's sessions said". O8's project channel reads the same log plane. *Recommendation:* fold it into O8's channel, so the log plane has one reader and O14's B4 waits on one. *Alternative:* keep it in O7 as a per-person filter of the project's agent-to-agent traffic (M6).
- **A4. A message sent to a remote session while its machine is unreachable.** *Recommendation:* hold it for 60 s, then show "Not sent: `<machine>` is offline" with Send again. M1 builds this rule. *Alternatives:* an outbox that sends typed messages whenever the machine returns, with answers and interrupts never waiting; failing at once. **[owner, 2026-10-28: as recommended; P5 Decision Log 108.]**
- **A5. The "+ Session" machine picker** (M4; to settle before it is re-planned). *Recommendation:* a machine menu beside the project picker, defaulting to this Mac, that lists the machines where the project has a checkout, with one line under it saying the lane and the inference route. *Alternatives:* the machine folded into the project row ("maws @ mini"); a two-step dialog. **[owner, 2026-10-28: as recommended; P5 Decision Log 108.]**
- **A6. Forwarded ports** (M3). *Recommendation:* a `localhost:<port>` link in a remote session opens through a forward in the in-app browser, marked "forwarded from `<machine>`", without asking. *Alternatives:* an explicit "Forward port…" command; asking each time. **[owner, 2026-10-28: as recommended; P5 Decision Log 108.]**
Added at the re-plan of 2026-10-31. Each is built as recommended unless answered otherwise; the product calls among them are marked.

- **A7. The step-up sheet's form** (M2; product). *Recommendation:* macOS's own sheet on the window (`dialog.showMessageBox`): the action's summary as its message, every field one per line as its detail, Continue and Cancel; then Touch ID with the summary. It shows even before a page loads, needs no second copy of main's words, and is the pattern a Mac user reads as a security confirmation. *Alternative:* an in-app sheet in MAWS's own style, which can show the same text with richer layout once the native one has proved the flow. **[owner, 2026-10-31: as recommended.]**
- **A8. Notifications for another machine's sessions** (M6; product). *Recommendation:* this Mac notifies for a remote session's pending item as it does for a local one, once per item, and a click opens the session; the phone's push (O13) arrives too, as it does today for local sessions. *Alternative:* count remote items in the badge and Needs you but raise no notification for them. **[owner, 2026-10-31: as recommended.]**
- **A9. A remote terminal after a new app run** (M3; product). The machine refuses a remote viewer's emulator checkpoint (O2's rule), so this app keeps it in memory for its run: a reload restores exactly, a new app run restores from the ring's tail (the last 512 KiB), which is what a local terminal shows when its checkpoint is missing. *Recommendation:* accept. *Alternative:* persist the checkpoint in this app's profile per remote pty, which keeps old state for shells that may have ended. **[owner, 2026-10-31: as recommended.]**
- **A10. Reveal in Finder and Open externally on a remote file** (M4; product). They act on the asking client, so on this Mac they can only open a copy. *Recommendation:* grey them with "on `<machine>`" until M5 routes the client port, and let "Open externally" then download the file to this Mac's temporary directory and open it. *Alternative:* hide them for remote sessions. **[owner, 2026-10-31: as recommended.]**
- **A11. Lane B records on the real machines** (M7; for the architect). The endpoint authorizes a lane B session from sminos's member record (O12's lane B observer, item 7). M7's live runs need every lane B session on the mini and the VM to have one; the e2e plays sminos with the harness's fake (§Q). *Recommendation:* confirm with the desk track that O12 item 7's records reach the endpoint on both machines at their current sminos before M7 is scheduled, and if not, make it M7's first precondition.
Added at M5's re-plan (2026-11-01). Each changes what a person may do on another machine's artifacts or services, so each is the owner's. The owner answered A12 to A15 as recommended on 2026-11-01 (P5 Decision Log 195); A16 is the architect's, deferred as board #435.

- **A12. Who may read and edit an artifact on another machine** (M5a; the grade model). An artifact belongs to no one session: it is linked to the sessions whose agents made, edited or viewed it, and some have no link at all (the person's own creations, imports, seeded design systems). *Recommendation:* the machine's owners hold `manage` on every artifact of the machine (as the machine's own app shows them everything); any other member is graded by the highest rank they hold on a session the artifact is linked to (viewing a session already shows its artifacts); an artifact with no link is the machine owners' alone. *Alternatives:* grade by the creating session alone (an artifact an agent edited into a second session, and every seeded or imported one, becomes unreadable to a non-owner); refuse every unlinked artifact remotely, for everyone (the conservative reading; the owner's own imports would then not open from the MacBook). The residual to know: a member who owns a session on the machine could, through their agent, link another person's artifact into it and gain `manage` on it; the machine's store has no per-artifact ACL and its own app allows the same today. **[owner, 2026-11-01: as recommended, by linked sessions; P5 Decision Log 195. On the residual, verbatim: "And later we're gonna introduce a multi-person coordination, and it will be completely solved then." The gap is accepted until that work.]**
- **A13. Connector widgets of a shared dashboard** (M5c; OX9's "connectors are `machine-admin`"; O4's D22 raised it). Every connector command is `machine-admin`, and a `machine-admin` act asks Touch ID each time, so a remote dashboard offered as graded would prompt at every widget refresh. *Recommendation:* the eight page-lease commands are floored at `view` on the artifact, `connectors.call` and `refresh` graded `converse` by effect: whoever may view a shared dashboard may use its widgets as its owner granted them (the grants are per artifact and per tool, the owner's), with no prompt; `converse` because driving the session's agent to call the same tool needs no more. Credential and grant management (`servers`, `connect`, `disconnect`, the grants) stay `machine-admin`. *Alternative:* keep `machine-admin` and offer none of the lease commands: a remote dashboard's widgets read "connectors on `<machine>` need its app". **[owner, 2026-11-01: as recommended; `view` opens a widget, `converse` refreshes; P5 Decision Log 195.]**
- **A14. Send to from another machine** (M5c; OX9). *Recommendation:* `delivery.status` and `delivery.receipts` at `view` on the artifact, `delivery.send` and `delivery.cancel` at `manage` on it: an artifact's owners send it where the machine's credential allows, with no prompt (`manage` never steps up); the credential is the machine's (P5 §2), so a second member with `manage` on a shared session sends through the machine owner's Notion, as that session's owner. *Alternative:* keep `machine-admin` (two Touch IDs per send: the status read and the send), or do not offer delivery remotely ("Send to on `<machine>`"). **[owner, 2026-11-01: as recommended; edit rights send; P5 Decision Log 195.]**
- **A15. Connecting a connector from another machine** (M5c; product and scope). A route D connector's consent opens in this Mac's browser and its callback lands on this Mac's loopback, where nothing listens; O4 left the seam for a relayed receiver. *Recommendation:* build the relay in M5c (a loopback listener in main for the flow's life, which outlives the act's immediate acknowledgement, its port carried in the act, the redirect relayed whole as `connectors.callback` and the flow's own reply written back to the browser), so Notion and the engine's custom-redirect sign-ins connect from the MacBook; Drive's `gws` login and an engine that keeps its own listener stay "on `<machine>`". *Alternative:* defer the relay and the client-port carriage with it: every Connect reads "Connect on `<machine>`", and the owner sits at each machine once per connector. **[owner, 2026-11-01: as recommended, the relay in M5c; P5 Decision Log 195.]**
- **A16. `show_pane` from a session on another machine** (M5; P5 §6: "it opens in the app of the device that sent the turn's input, else the most recently focused attached app"). Nothing records which device sent a turn's input, the event is gated on an app attached at the session's machine, and its answer is a `converse` act. *Recommendation:* not carried in M5: a remote viewer sees the artifact in the session's Artifacts tool and opens it; the carriage is a named follow-up (the Decision Log's M5 entry) once O16's re-homing of the attachment gate has landed and a device-of-the-turn record exists (`host.op`'s ledger has `device`). *Alternative:* carry it to every subscribed device in M5c (every app of the owner opens the pane, the first answer wins), which P5 §6 did not ask for. **[the architect, 2026-11-01: not carried; deferred as board #435; P5 Decision Log 195.]**

## Surprises & Discoveries

- Observation: the latency stub exits on any control request it holds no answer for, and a remote pane asks `list_models` (`session.listModels`) where a local one doesn't, so the bench's second turn ran on a fresh stub numbered from 1; only a hosted run showed it.
  Evidence: `test/support/latency-engine-stub.mjs` `CONTROLS` (exit 70 for an unknown subtype); M7's first hosted `e2e:remote-latency-check` run.
- Observation: `ci-gate`'s planner keeps a `MEASURED_E2E` group off the hosted lane whatever `--lane` says, so a measured group's scene can be proven before its window only by a functional twin outside `MEASURED_E2E`.
  Evidence: `.github/scripts/gate-plan.mjs`; M7's `e2e:remote-latency-check`.
- Observation: `test/support/engine-pin` runs the installed engine's `--version` when it is imported, so nothing bundled for a remote machine may import it (on the mini that is a `claude` run, which the owner forbids over ssh).
  Evidence: `test/support/engine-pin/index.ts`; M7's bench agent takes the version as an argument.
- Observation: `mawsd`'s start refuses an existing profile directory that is group- or world-accessible rather than correcting it, so anything that pre-creates a scratch profile must make it 0700.
  Evidence: `makePrivateDirectory` in the daemon's start; M7's milestone review finding 1.
- Observation: the endpoint checks freshness, grade and lane B's context before it reaches the executor's ledger, so a refused reseal does not prove that an earlier copy never ran.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/actions.ts` `#execute`: `session.status` is compared with `action.context` (`refuse('context')`) before `host.op` is dispatched.
- Observation: `openChannel` raises `protocol`, `unknown-machine`, `revoked` and `contested` from `h2` before checking its MAC and signature, so a relay can forge all four.
  Evidence: `apps/org-protocol/src/channel.ts` `openChannel`: the major and `machineKeys(...)`/`h2.keyId` checks come before `macVerifies` and `verifyInput`.

- Observation: the app holds no org state, hub session or registered device. P5 Decision Log 45's edge for M1 holds for building and testing, but a production device can't open a channel until O8's sign-in.
  Evidence: `src/main` imports `apps/org-protocol` only in `src/main/device/` (keys and step-up), and nothing calls `loadDeviceKeys` or `createStepUp` at startup. The hub's app routes (`/api/machines/<ref>/{socket,endpoint}`) take an Access-authenticated member (`apps/desk-hub/src/worker.ts`), and P5 Decision Log 38 gives that sign-in to O8.
- Observation: remote terminals and port forwarding need no O3. `pty.*` is the pty host's, and O2 forks it under `mawsd`. Diff, files and the editor do need O3: `git.*` (13), `files.*` (7) and `scm.*` (12) are all `owner: 'main'`.
  Evidence: `src/shared/ipc/schema/pty.ts` has 10 `owner: 'pty-host'`; `git.ts`, `files.ts` and `scm.ts` have only `owner: 'main'`.
- Observation: a lane A read names the caller's own subscription. The link's subscription id must therefore be stable across channels and exposed to O9.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/calls.ts` `#laneA`: `!isText(input.sub, 128)` → `malformed`; `this.ctx.subscription(channel, input.sub)`.
- Observation: a `snap` carries a page of a JSON value's text, not a value.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/subscriptions.ts` `snap()`: `pagesOf(JSON.stringify(value), …)`; "the device joins the pages' bodies and reads them".
- Observation: a step-up signature covers `nonce` and `created`, so a resealed step-up action needs a new confirmation.
  Evidence: `apps/org-protocol/src/action.ts` `actionSigningInput`: the action without `stepUp` and `webauthn` only; `src/main/device/step-up.ts` stamps a fresh nonce and `created` at confirmation.

- Observation (M1): the direct listener's per-source handshake limit (a burst of 3, 5 a minute) trips on loopback, where every handshake of a suite shares one source address, so a suite that reconnects more than three times is refused `503 busy`.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/direct-listener.ts` `HANDSHAKE_LIMITS`; the direct suite's reconnect cases failed until `test/endpoint/sub-harness.ts`'s `StartOptions.limits` raised the listener's limit and the link's `DIRECT_BUCKET` was raised to match.
- Observation (M1): `Channel.onClose` hands the link a sealed `close`'s reason and a carrier's end through one listener, so a socket close frame or a relay channel close reading `removed` looked like the machine's sealed verdict.
  Evidence: `apps/org-protocol/src/channel.ts`, the channel's close path; fixed on the device side by `carriers.ts` and the link's `sealedOnly` wrapper, which report every transport end as `carrier` (Decision Log, 2026-10-28, finding 1).
- Observation (M1): the hub's last hop to the app socket has no byte or stall bound, so the claim that a device which stops reading loses its socket holds only for the direct carrier.
  Evidence: `apps/desk-hub/src/objects/relay.ts`; P5 Decision Log 94 tracks it.
- Observation (M1): the repository's eslint `no-restricted-imports` rule keeps `src/main` from importing `src/endpoint` and `test/endpoint`, tests included, which the link's suites need for a real endpoint and its harness.
  Evidence: `eslint.config.js`; a block added for `src/main/machines/remote/*.test.ts`, `src/main/org/*.test.ts` and `test/machines/**`.
- Observation (M1): an org state that drops a machine's key must list it in `retired`, so a rotation in a test is a successor with the new key and a retired entry for the old one.
  Evidence: `apps/org-protocol` org-state validation; the direct suite's `rotation()` helper.
- Observation (re-plan): a device cannot query a `machine` act today, since org-protocol's query validator admits only `sminos`, `engine-host` and `endpoint`, while the endpoint already routes `machine` queries and acts to `machine.opQuery` and `machine.op`.
  Evidence: `apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts` `FROM_DEVICE.query`; `src/endpoint/queries.ts` "`machine` is no member of RL2's executor until O7 adds it behind a capability".
- Observation (re-plan): the endpoint places a lane B session through the engine host's `session.status`, which throws for any session the host did not launch in its run, so a released or parked session is `unknown` to a remote caller and every request on it is refused `grade`.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/targets.ts` `laneB`; `src/engine-host/ipc.ts` `sessionOf` (`table.get(id) ?? throwing(unknownSession(id))`); the engine host parks when idle (`src/mawsd/clocks.ts`).
- Observation (re-plan): `session.acquire`, the resume behind a send to a released session, is a `machine` act, so M2 needs the `machine` executor on RL2 or a released remote session could not be driven.
  Evidence: `src/services/machine-ops.ts` `claimable` over the registry's owners; the grade `{floor: 'converse', byEffect: true}` in `src/shared/ipc/grades/session.ts`; the composer's `sendHumanPrompt` calling `session.acquire` when the record has no ready process.
- Observation (re-plan): `session.launch` already exists as the engine host's launch-spec command, called by every MAWS-owned launch; its `spawn` grade line is that command's.
  Evidence: `src/shared/ipc/schema/session.ts` (`host(z.strictObject({ spec: LaunchSpecSchema, … }))`), `src/engine-host/ipc.ts`'s handler, `src/services/owner/launch.ts`.
- Observation (re-plan): a remote upload cannot be one call: the channel's message limit is 4 MiB and `files.upload` admits 32 MiB; an inline image can be 5 MiB.
  Evidence: `MESSAGE_MAX_BYTES` in `apps/org-protocol/src/channel.ts`; `UPLOAD_FILE_MAX_BYTES` in `src/shared/ipc/schema/files.ts`; `MAX_SEND_IMAGE_BYTES` in `src/shared/engine/session-state-store.ts`.
- Observation (re-plan): `git.*`'s reads take a directory, not a session, so no existing target rule can grade them; `scm.*` and `files.*` take `appSessionId` and need none; `fs.agentEvents` takes `appSessionId` and `fs.readSpill` a path under the engine's store.
  Evidence: `DirectorySchema` and `RepoRootSchema` in `src/shared/ipc/schema/git.ts`; `Session` and `SessionPathSchema` in `scm.ts` and `files.ts`; `src/shared/ipc/schema/fs.ts`.
- Observation (re-plan): the machine's checkout events never reach a device: plane B carries engine-host events only, and nothing else carries `machine`-owned events over RL2.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/plane-b.ts` `event()` (`if (owner === 'engine-host')`); `src/services/checkouts-root.ts` emitting `git.changed`, `files.changed`, `scm.remote.output`.
- Observation (re-plan): a spawn composed of MAWS's own commands cannot cross: an intent has no engine session, so `session.intent.patch` and `session.start` have no target the endpoint can place, and `spawnRefusal` admits only DX6's six fields, reading an absent mode as the ceiling while `session.create` seeds the user's default.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/targets.ts` `laneB`; `SPAWN_FIELDS` and `spawnRefusal` in `src/endpoint/policy.ts`; `src/services/sessions/service.ts`'s create; `authorize.ts` running the guard for `sminos.spawn` alone.
- Observation (re-plan): `machine.op` runs a claimed command with a `HandlerContext` holding only the client, and the endpoint sends it the claim's fingerprint, not the sealed action, so a handler cannot know who asked or keep the proof without an addition to both.
  Evidence: `src/services/machine-ops.ts` `op` (`deps.invoke(name, checked.value, context)`); `src/endpoint/actions.ts`'s `machine` branch.
- Observation (re-plan): the endpoint holds no lane B records or listings of its own: both come only from sminos's member frames, and a profile with no sminos (every e2e scene) authorizes and lists no lane B session; the harness's fake sminos imports only `node:net` and `desk-hub/protocol`, so an e2e can play it.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/endpoint.ts` `records: authorization.records ?? this.rl10.members` and `endpoint.listings` from `rl10.members.sessions()`; `test/endpoint/rl10/fake-sminos.ts`'s imports.
- Observation (re-plan): the endpoint mints no `ClientRef`, so every remote request shares the front's one `endpoint` ref, and a watch registered through it would never be released.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/endpoint.ts`'s `dispatch` calls (no `client`); `src/mawsd/front.ts` `endpointClient = endableClientRef('endpoint')`; `src/services/files/register.ts` and `git/register.ts` counting watches per client.
- Observation (re-plan): the pty's credit needs no change for a remote viewer, but `mawsd`'s guard reads the outer command name, so an attach nested in a ledger command would pass with credit; and `pty.attach`'s result carries the ring as a `Uint8Array`, which a 512 KiB replay turns into 6 MB of JSON.
  Evidence: `src/mawsd/daemon.ts`'s `credit: 'daemon' | 'app'`, its `hosts.onRaw('pty-host')` ack and its `name === 'pty.attach'` guard; `PtyAttachSchema.replay` in `src/shared/ipc/schema/pty.ts`; `src/pty-host/ring.ts`.
- Observation (re-plan): `pty.list` takes `z.void()`, and an action's `args` must be an object, so no act can carry it.
  Evidence: `src/shared/ipc/schema/pty.ts`; `apps/org-protocol/src/action.ts` `isAction`; `src/endpoint/authorize.ts` step 2.
- Observation (re-plan): a `caps` refresh at a grant change sends new grades without closing the channel, so anything authorized once and left open (a stream) would outlive a withdrawn right unless re-checked.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/channels.ts` `refreshCaps`; `src/endpoint/endpoint.ts`'s grant handling.
- Observation (re-plan): `pong` echoes the device's `at` and carries nothing of the endpoint's clock, so no exchange today can estimate a clock offset across machines; the local latency bench holds only because the stub engine and the runner share one clock.
  Evidence: `apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts` (`pong: { at }`); `src/endpoint/channels.ts`'s pong; `e2e/local-latency.spec.ts`'s header; `BENCH.clockWithinMs` in `test/support/local-latency.ts`.
- Observation (re-plan): the renderer takes no snapshot of a listed machine and reads neither `carrier` nor `refusal`, and a listed lane B row's click selects only, so M2's renderer work is the opener, the pane's started branch over a record, the overlay and the hover, not a new store.
  Evidence: `src/renderer/src/ipc/events.ts` `takeRoster` (`listedMachines.has(m.ref)` → `continue`); `src/renderer/src/sidebar/ListedRow.tsx` `activate`; `src/renderer/src/sessions/remote-openers.ts`.
- Observation (re-plan, round 2): the endpoint's lane B plane ends a subscription `unavailable` when the stream's opening read of `session.status` fails, where the front keeps its stream for the next relink; so a subscription to a session the engine host holds no row for ends at once, placement or not.
  Evidence: `src/shared/host/stream-catchup.ts`'s opening (`sink.end('unavailable')` on a failed read); `src/endpoint/plane-b.ts` ending the subscription; `src/mawsd/front-streams.ts` retaining its stream.
- Observation (re-plan, round 2): the link refuses an act with a `session` and no `context` before sending it, as `isAction` does, so a `machine` act must carry neither and be placed by its args.
  Evidence: `apps/org-protocol/src/action.ts` `isAction` (`value.session === undefined || value.context !== undefined`); `src/main/machines/remote/acts.ts`'s local `malformed`.
- Observation (re-plan, round 2): an act the channel carried and then lost is ambiguous and is queried to RL4's horizon (about 23 hours), so no 60 s hold bounds a transmitted send's outcome; only a never-sent act ends at the hold.
  Evidence: `src/main/machines/remote/acts.ts` (`onLost` leaving a sent act ambiguous; the horizon timer; the hold applied only with no copy out).
- Observation (re-plan, round 2): git's reads answer for the repository that contains the directory, not the directory: `git.status` resolves the enclosing repository, `git.diff` runs `--no-relative`, and `git.worktree.list` lists every worktree; a path rule on the input would admit a diff of a whole repository from a session rooted in its subdirectory.
  Evidence: `src/services/git/service.ts` (`status`, `diff`, `pathspec`); `src/services/git/worktrees.ts`.
- Observation (re-plan, round 2): `pty.attach`'s replay starts at the local app's checkpoint (`replayFrom = max(checkpoint.offset, ring.start)`), so a remote viewer behind that checkpoint cannot read the ring's earlier bytes through it, though the ring holds them; `ring.since(offset)` can.
  Evidence: `src/pty-host/table.ts` `attach`; `src/pty-host/ring.ts` `since` and `start`.
- Observation (re-plan, round 2): `files.watch` installs a watcher and lists nothing, so a replayed watch leaves a pane stale for what changed while disconnected; main's own reconnect emits `files.changed {kind: 'invalidated'}` and the renderer re-lists and re-watches.
  Evidence: `src/main/sessions/watch-replay.ts`'s header; `src/renderer/src/tools/code/files/store.ts`.
- Observation (re-plan, round 2): the preload envelope carries a command's machine as its third argument and nothing else, and `RemoteDispatch` is `(machine, name, input, ctx)`, so a session for a path-class call has no carrier to main today.
  Evidence: `src/preload/bridge.ts` `invoke(name, input, machine)`; `src/main/ipc/router.ts` `RemoteDispatch`.
- Observation (re-plan, round 2): an NTP-style offset from four stamps is accurate only to half the exchange's round trip, whatever the spread across exchanges: a stable asymmetric route hides its bias inside a low spread.
  Evidence: `test/support/local-latency.ts`'s clock check, which requires the whole possible interval to fit its tolerance.

- Observation (M2a): any `RL2_PROTOCOL` minor bump breaks the committed handshake vectors, since the version is inside `h1` and `h2`, which feed the handshake's secrets.
  Evidence: `apps/org-protocol/vectors/replay-channel.ts` compares outbound bytes; `handshakeSecrets` in `apps/org-protocol/src/channel.ts`.
- Observation (M2a): `mawsd` forwards an engine-host request's client as its id only and posts no `clientGone` for it, so the engine host mints a ref per remote channel that ends only with `mawsd`'s own connection to it.
  Evidence: `src/mawsd/daemon.ts` (`client && { client: client.id }` on the engine-host forward); no `clientGoneRelay` under `src/mawsd`; `src/shared/host/client-refs.ts`.
- Observation (M2a): placement now reads the index's `engineSessionId` while `actions.ts`'s context check reads the engine host's live status, so for the index's lag after an engine session change (a `/clear`) the two can name different engine sessions.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/targets.ts` against `src/endpoint/actions.ts`'s context check.

- Observation (M2b): the endpoint harness's `FakeEngineHost` events (`note`, bare permission and question shapes) are not `SessionEvent`s, so a consumer that validates drops them; `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`'s `LaneBHost` maps them to real shapes, for M3 and M4 to reuse.
  Evidence: `test/endpoint/sub-harness.ts` against `SessionEventSchema`.

- Observation (from O4, 2026-10-31, for M5): `mawsd`'s services front has no main-only gate: any `machine.sock` client past the token can send a main-only `internal` name (`connectors.windowShown` for its own pages). Remote calls reach `mawsd` only through the endpoint's offer rows, so nothing is exposed today, but wherever M5 lets a remote lease report the window, the endpoint's rows must not forward a main-only `internal` name: the front will not refuse it.
  Evidence: `src/mawsd/front.ts` (O3-D's front, unchanged); O4's report through the architect.

- Observation (M4a1): a migration that moves the index's `user_version` costs the previous build its whole index on a downgrade (it opens a newer version read-only and unavailable), so an additive column goes through `ADDED_COLUMNS` instead; two files at version 15 may now differ by such a column.
  Evidence: `src/services/index/db.ts` `openDb`; cross-build run 41237182488.
- Observation (M4b): the endpoint answers a `void` command's output as `null` (`calls.ts`, `?? null`, since O10 M8), so every such command over the channel needs main to read `null` as `undefined`.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/calls.ts`; `src/main/machines/remote/dispatch.ts`.
- Observation (M4b): the machine's git watcher keys a hold by channel client and directory with no session, so any replay that reconciles holds per session or per page undercounts when two sessions share a directory.
  Evidence: `src/services/git/watcher.ts`; M4b's two review rounds.
- Observation (M4b review): a disk history window ignores `afterSeq`, so a pager that steps the cursor past a refused row loops forever on a disk item larger than the channel; M2's legacy pager did.
  Evidence: `src/engine-host/ipc.ts`; `src/main/lane-b/remote-history.ts`.

- Observation (M3a2): the device-side `Channel` stays open after a carrier drop (`channel.closed` is false); only the endpoint's side ends, so a suite that forces a drop waits on the endpoint's `channels.size`.
  Evidence: `test/endpoint/streams.test.ts`; M3a2's report.
- Observation (M3a2): streams and subscriptions each keep their own `StreamWindow`, so up to about 512 KiB can be in a channel at once rather than 256 KiB; sharing one window would move it out of `Subscriptions`.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/streams.ts`, `src/endpoint/subscriptions.ts`.
- Observation (M3c): M3's Touches named no part of main's browser, yet `browser.*` is graded `client` and the router refused it for another machine's session, so a forward opened and its tab never appeared; the in-app browser had to answer a remote session's tabs here (the Decision Log's entry).
  Evidence: `src/main/ipc/router.ts`; `src/shared/ipc/grades/browser.ts`; M3c's report.
- Observation (M3d, M3 review): a dev server as it is commonly run is not on 127.0.0.1: python's plain `http.server` binds dual-stack `::` and prints `http://[::]:<port>/`, and Node 17+ servers bound to `localhost` often listen on `::1` alone; hence the endpoint's `::1` fallback and `[::]`, `[::1]` forwardable.
  Evidence: `e2e/remote.spec.ts`'s scene; `src/shared/machine/loopback-link.ts`.
- Observation (M3 review): `mawsd`'s supervisor exits went to a no-op `emit` and the endpoint heard only raw pty events, so a pty host's death never reached a remote terminal; and the supervisor's `crashed` (its crash limit) does not raise `restarts`, so a death read from the count alone misses it.
  Evidence: `src/mawsd/daemon.ts`, `src/endpoint/endpoint.ts`, `src/endpoint/streams.ts`; the milestone review's two rounds.
- Observation (M6a, M6 review): the index answers `session.get` from its live state but publishes record changes on a coalescing tick, so a read can be ahead of every `session.changed` heard; neither the read nor an event is known to be the newer, and preferring the heard placement disclosed a private session after two moves.
  Evidence: `src/services/index/register.ts`, `src/services/index/source.ts`; the M6a task review's repro.
- Observation (M6 review): `session.search` caps at 50 machine-wide hits, title matches first, before the endpoint's `each-session` cut, so a member who views a few sessions got an empty, `incomplete: false` answer while one of theirs matched.
  Evidence: `src/services/index/session-search.ts`; the milestone review's repro (50 private titles, one viewable transcript hit).
- Observation (M6 review): `Subscription.ev()` substitutes `{omitted: 'too-large', bytes}` for a message over the channel's limit, and a feed that records what it sent as delivered then holds a state the device never got; a consumer must read an omission as a lost feed.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/subscriptions.ts`, `src/endpoint/attention-feed.ts`, `src/main/lane-b/remote-attention.ts`.
- Observation (M6c): `connectShell` snapshots a machine the roster reports `up` before the org rows name it as a socket machine (`attention.list` with `{machine}`), which M6a's `each-session` row now answers; its late answer could undo the feed's partition until the store took the feed as the partition's owner. O8 M2's snapshot rule; harmless once guarded.
  Evidence: `src/renderer/src/ipc/events.ts`, `src/renderer/src/stores/attention.ts`.
- Observation (M6b): `Machines.onRemote` makes no initial call; a listener learns the roster from its next change, which production's asynchronous first refresh supplies after the composition. A synchronous roster would be missed here and by the `org.rows` listener in `app.ts`.
  Evidence: `src/main/machines/index.ts`; the harness `Device.roster()`.

- Observation (M5's re-plan): a stream's authorization admits only a single machine grade, so a stream at `view` on a session (an artifact page, a read-only document, O17's screencast) is refused `grade` by the act path; and streams are rechecked at a `caps` refresh and from the policy CLI, never at a record change, so a share withdrawn would not close one.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/authorize.ts` `#stream` (the single-machine-grade check); `src/endpoint/streams.ts` `recheck` and its callers (`channels.ts` `refreshCaps`, `cli.ts`); no `RecordSource.onChange` subscriber in `streams.ts`.
- Observation (M5's re-plan): `host.op` runs only a command whose input names a session this engine host holds a row for, and the endpoint's engine-host act branch refuses an act with no lane B context `malformed`, so an artifact write, which names no session, had no ledger to run under.
  Evidence: `src/engine-host/ops.ts` (the `appSessionId` and status checks); `src/endpoint/actions.ts` `#execute`'s engine-host branch; `HostOpInputSchema.context` required in `src/shared/ipc/schema/host.ts`.
- Observation (M5's re-plan): an artifact's links to sessions are a store table, many to many, which no `artifacts.*` read answers; `library.entry` answers them raw, and a library row carries the index's view of them as `sessions`.
  Evidence: `src/shared/artifacts/entity.ts` (`ArtifactSessionLink`), `src/engine-host/documents/store.ts` `links`; `LibraryEntryAnswerSchema.links`, `LibraryRowSchema.sessions` (`LibrarySessionSchema`) in `src/shared/ipc/schema/library.ts`.
- Observation (M5's re-plan): RL2 writes a message with plain `JSON.stringify`, so a `Uint8Array` in a call's output or an act's args (`artifacts.versionState`, `attributions`, `uploadAsset`, `create`'s bytes) arrives as an object the schema refuses; the machine-local socket already carries bytes in a tagged form; and a typed array can enter neither a canonical action nor an op's `JsonValueSchema` input, so the decode belongs where the handler runs, not at the endpoint.
  Evidence: `apps/org-protocol/src/channel.ts` `send`, `canonical.ts`; `src/shared/host/json-bytes.ts` (`$bytes`); `src/shared/ipc/schema/artifacts.ts`'s `Bytes`; `src/shared/ipc/schema/common.ts`'s `JsonValueSchema`; `HostOpInputSchema.input`.
- Observation (M5's re-plan, round 3): both op ledgers validate the inner command's input before the handler runs (`ops.ts:60`, `machine-ops.ts:53`), so a tagged bytes input must be decoded before that validation, not at the handler; and an OAuth flow's receiver is owned through the token exchange and released in its `finally`, after the callback it answered, so a device port released at the answer could be rebound while the machine's registration stands.
  Evidence: `src/engine-host/ops.ts`, `src/services/machine-ops.ts`; `src/services/connectors/oauth-flow.ts` (the 404 and 405 answers that settle nothing; the `finally`).
- Observation (M5's re-plan): the op ledgers bind a claim to a session id or null and their queries answer only that binding, so an act keyed by an artifact, a lease or a run has no durable target for a query after a restart unless the claim carries one.
  Evidence: `src/engine-host/ops.ts` (the target stored), `src/services/machine-ops.ts` (null without an `appSessionId`), `src/shared/host/op-ledger.ts` `query`, `HostOpQueryOutputSchema`'s `target`.
- Observation (M5's re-plan): the machine's services ask the client port for two things only, `openExternal {url}` for a consent and `openExternal {path}` or `reveal` for a machine path; `pick`, `save`, `confirm`, `notify`, `badge` and `focus` are main's own Electron port and never cross; and `show_pane` is not a port kind but an engine-host event gated on an app attached at that machine.
  Evidence: the port call sites under `src/services/` (`connectors/root.ts`, `connectors/sign-in.ts`, `delivery/index.ts`, `library/files.ts`, `files/service.ts`, `sessions/service.ts`); `src/main/client/electron-port.ts`; `src/engine-host/index.ts` (`appAttached`), `tool-server/bridge.ts`.
- Observation (M5's re-plan): the renderer names no machine on any `artifacts.*`, `library.*`, `connectors.*` or `delivery.*` call, and the artifact scheme's URL carries none, so a remote artifact's page, reads and edits all fell to this Mac; an artifact id is a UUID and the scheme's origin.
  Evidence: `src/renderer/src/artifacts/host.ts`, `tools/artifacts/viewers/ByteViewer.tsx`, `library/export.ts`, `artifacts/connectors.ts`; `src/shared/artifacts/scheme.ts`; `src/render/scheme.ts` (the handler resolves locally).
- Observation (M5's re-plan): every `connectors.*` and `delivery.*` command is `machine-admin`, and `stepUpRequired` asks a step-up for every `machine-admin` act, so a remote dashboard's widgets offered as graded would prompt Touch ID at every refresh.

- Observation (M5's re-plan): the link gives every call 10 s and `dispatchOverLink` passes no deadline, while `artifacts.export` is budgeted at 120 s, so a cold machine's export timed out on this side while the machine finished it (the tracker row of 2026-10-31, O5's landing 1).

- Observation (M5a, the first hosted run): every remote artifact write was refused "predates" while every unit test passed. `host.artifactOp` looked its command up in the handlers `registerEngineIpc` saw, and the engine host registers the artifact writes (`registerArtifacts`) on the plain `on` before that call, so the real host held none of them; the tests' fake host registered them where the lookup read.

- Observation (M5a, the milestone review): a decision taken in one continuation and an act handed on in a later one leaves a gap a revocation can fall into, whatever the branch awaits itself: the gate runs the executor after authorization's promise settles. The lane B branch asked `stands()` after its awaited status read; the artifact, `machine` and `pty-host` branches asked nothing.

- Observation (M5a, the hosted run): B's Browser notice on the hosted runner reads the browser socket's path being longer than a Unix socket allows, not "no browser attached": B's profile sits under the runner's long `/var/folders` TMPDIR. A real Mac with a long profile path could meet the same limit; the check is O5's.
  Evidence: run 41384303649's e2e:remote log; `src/mawsd/session-capabilities.ts` `browserCapability`.
- Observation (M5b): a fake again wired differently from the real thing: `FakePtyHost.serve` replaced `services.onFrame` outright, so in any harness with a pty host the fake documents' frames never arrived, and the first document cases hung at "replica synced".
  Evidence: `test/endpoint/fake-pty-host.ts` (fixed in `c44d9640a`).
- Observation (M5b): a page's referrer and a 2xx answer are not evidence of where an artifact lives: recording them let a remote machine claim a local artifact's id; every local artifact is absent from `ArtifactMachines`, so an unplaced id is not an undiscovered remote one.
  Evidence: b3's task review; `91f9cd47a`.
- Observation (M5b): a byte count of what is unsent is not a bound on memory: `subarray()` keeps a frame's backing buffer whole until its last byte drains, so a budget must count each frame whole while any of it waits.
  Evidence: the narrow re-check's reproduction (three 60 MiB frames, 1 KiB left each, 180 MiB held); `deb7fa0a9`.
- Observation (M7's sitting, 2026-11-04): a lane B session spawned from the MacBook on the mini or the VM opens in the pane but gets no row in the MacBook's sidebar, reproducibly (S1's spawn and a fresh one), though `org/listings.json` holds its listing within a second of the spawn; with the tab closed nothing reopens it (no row, no deep link: the app registers no URL protocol). *Corrected 2026-11-04 (m8-1): there was no id mismatch. The listing is keyed by the app id and the join meets it. The row was cut by the sidebar's 5-or-8 cap under the priority sort, and Resume… lists no listed rows (M8, The sidebar row).*
  Evidence: the owner's screenshots at 05:36Z and about 05:4xZ; the MacBook's `org/listings.json` read at 04:03Z.
- Observation (M7's sitting): every question on a MAWS-launched session is the MAWS ask, since the launch withdraws the native `AskUserQuestion` (`src/engine-host/launch.ts`, `--disallowedTools`; P2 Decision Log 33), and the ask panel reads and answers through `maws.asks.list` and `maws.ask.answer` (`src/renderer/src/tools/maws/ask/store.ts`), which `src/endpoint/offers.ts` doesn't offer; so a remote question shows (its `maws.ask.opened` event crosses) but can't be read or answered from another Mac. The engine's own `session.answer` is offered and never reached on these sessions.
