- Observation (M7's sitting): the session's side panels call commands the offer table lacks, and show the refusal as an error rather than a greyed state: the Subagents panel `fs.subagents`, Workflow runs `fs.workflows`, Tasks `fs.tasks` (each `not-offered`), and Reload plugins `session.reloadPlugins`, sent as a call against its act row (`act-required`). The scheduled-jobs panel's `files.read`, which is offered, answered `internal`.
  Evidence: the owner's screenshots at about 06:3xZ on 02de6c88c; `src/endpoint/authorize.ts` (`act-required` for a call against an act row).
- Observation (M7's sitting): a tool-using turn on the VM's first session left it showing "Working…" after the turn ended; the VM's own `session.status` read `engineState: idle`, `turn: null`, `activity: requesting`, so the activity wasn't cleared at the source. A later turn without a tool cleared.
  Evidence: the VM's `session.status` read by the desk track for session 6adfd847 (turn ended 05:28:12Z); the owner's fresh VM session at about 06:00Z.
- Observation (M7's sitting): `/btw` (`session.sideQuestion`, offered as a lane B act) on a remote session stayed at "Answering…" and never answered.
  Evidence: the owner's screenshot at about 06:0xZ; `src/endpoint/offers.ts` (`session.sideQuestion`).
- Observation (M7's sitting): a PDF dropped on a remote session uploads to the machine (`.claude/desk-uploads/`) and the agent reads it, but the message carries no `@` reference to it, so the agent has to find it with a tool. A new untracked file shows nothing in the diff panel.

- Observation (M7's sitting): the session's MCP widget shows a remote server that needs a sign-in with only Reconnect (`session.mcpReconnect`), which signs nothing in, and doesn't point to Settings › Connectors' Sign in, where M5c's relayed OAuth is.

- Observation (M7's bench): enrolling a scratch machine outside the runner meets two clocks the runner hides: the scratch `mawsd` exits after 10 minutes with no client, and its endpoint marks itself `org-listed` only at its own 10-minute org sync, so a listing the hub already holds is seen up to 10 minutes later and the hub link waits for it.
  Evidence: the scratch `mawsd.log` on the mini ("exited (idle, code 0)" at 04:42:59Z; enrolled 04:52:16Z, `org-listed` at 05:02:19Z); `src/endpoint/enroll.ts` `promoteIfListed`; `src/endpoint/endpoint.ts` (the hub link starts only once listed).
- Observation (M7's bench): on the mini every status poll is a fresh MAWS process (25 `machine.sock` clients in five minutes, each from a new pid, launchd at 43%), so the bench loads the machine it measures; and the mini path's stop ends only the scratch `mawsd`, leaving a detached scratch supervisor running until it exits idle.
  Evidence: the scratch `mawsd.log` on the mini, 05:00 to 05:05Z; mini-ops' read-only `ps` at 05:06:58Z; supervisor 41388 after attempt 1's teardown.
- Observation (M7's bench): attempt 1's key step read no key for 60 s from `mawsd keys --json` while the enrollment was `hub-confirmed` and the key was on disk; whether the endpoint's in-memory key lags an in-daemon enroll or the link's latency ate the window is not settled.
  Evidence: attempt 1's run log (04:26:56Z `hub-confirmed`, then "the machine posted no key to compare within 60 s"); `src/endpoint/cli.ts` `keys()`.
- Observation (M10's re-plan, 2026-11-04): attempt 1's key step was settled from the code, correcting M7's "not settled". `#keyId()` (`test/support/remote-bench.ts`) parses `{key: {keyId}}`, and `mawsd keys --json` answers flat, so a real machine always read no key. The bench's fakes carried the same shape.
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/answers.ts` (`keysAnswer`); `src/mawsd/process-endpoint.test.ts`; `test/support/remote-bench.test.ts`.

- Observation (M10's build, 2026-11-05): an endpoint read of the org state already in flight can predate the member's listing. A sync verb built on `syncNow()` alone would join that read and answer `hub-confirmed` once more. test/endpoint/enroll.test.ts says the same of `--replace` ("A read already in flight may predate the listing; the one after it doesn't").
  Evidence: `src/endpoint/endpoint.ts` (`syncNow` returns the read in flight; `#syncAfter`); `test/endpoint/enroll.test.ts`.
- Observation (M10's build): `test/endpoint/enroll.test.ts`, where `endpoint sync`'s red-first case lives, is in the `node-timing` project, not `node` (it starts the local hub), so Concrete Steps' `--project node` finds no file there.
  Evidence: `vitest.config.ts` (`TIMING_NODE_TESTS`).

## Decision Log

- Decision: Verification. This document gets one `doperpowers:adversarial-reviewer` buildability round on its execution section, focused on M1, repeated until a round yields nothing worth changing. M1's PR gets doperpowers:reviewer-high: it is the device side of RL2, it builds and step-up-signs actions, and it decides what is resealed. M2–M7 each get their own buildability round when they are re-planned after O3's cut, and the review rung that re-plan names.
  Rationale: M1 sits on the trust boundary, as O10's milestones did. The advisory milestones can't be reviewed for buildability before their dependencies exist.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7's planner.

- Decision: The first buildability round (doperpowers:adversarial-reviewer, 3 P1 and 2 P2, each verified against the code) is folded in. An ambiguous act's refused reseal is now queried, not read as "nothing ran". Handshake verdicts no longer stop the link, since they come from an unauthenticated `h2`. A trust generation closes a channel whose machine key the org retired. M1 now extends `test/endpoint/sub-harness.ts` so that a `session.send` runs and `host.opQuery` answers, and its proofs gained the four cases these need. M3's terminal rows are all acts (`pty.*` is `shell`, and a call takes only `list` or `view`), with the pty's flow control and checkpoints named.
  Rationale: each finding was real against the code; the review's verdict otherwise found the signed-out seam, the stable subscription ids, the cursor rules, the tolerant output codec and the no-bump decision sound.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7's planner.

- Decision: The second round (1 P1) is folded in. A `done` whose outcome is `{state: 'refused'}` no longer settles an ambiguous act either, because a closed ledger refuses `unavailable` before it looks the id up. Only a query settles an ambiguous act, and a query's `unavailable` keeps it ambiguous. The round found the other four fixes complete, and no new contradiction.
  Rationale: verified against `src/shared/host/op-ledger.ts` `begin`.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7's planner.

- Decision: The third round, narrowed to that fix, found nothing (approve at `edc83be65`). The review has converged: rounds of 5, then 1, then 0 findings.
  Rationale: the skill's stop rule; a round that yields nothing worth changing.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7's planner.

- Decision: M1's interface additions, checked with the architect before the tests were written. (1) `createRemoteSource` takes `local: MachineRef`, since the roster leaves the local ref out and §C's deps didn't carry it, and an optional `interfaces()` so tests inject the network watch. (2) A step-up act's 60 s send window runs from its signature, not from `opCreated`; the signer still receives and keeps `opId` and `opCreated`. (3) `MachineLink.onState` also fires when the carrier or the refusal changes under the same reported state, so a relay-to-direct switch re-emits `machines.changed` without a new generation. (4) A probe's ping is answered by any message that arrives within `PROBE_PONG_MS`, not only a `pong`. Also: the link reads `up` when `connectChannel` resolves (at `caps`), and it checks an act with `isAction` before sending and refuses one that fails as `malformed`, since the endpoint would close the whole channel on it.
  Rationale: (1) the roster can't be computed without it; (2) a slow Touch ID prompt would otherwise end a confirmed act `refused offline`, against `step-up.ts`'s rule that time at the prompt doesn't make an act stale; (3) the manager reports the carrier, and a switch changes it; (4) every message already proves the channel live, as the liveness rule counts it. The architect agreed all four and the pre-check.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor.

- Decision: Finding 1 [P1], fixed by (a). A sealed `close {reason: 'removed'}` couldn't be told from a carrier close carrying the same word, because `Channel.onClose` reports both through one listener. Every production carrier now reports its own end as `carrier` (`CARRIER_CLOSED`), whatever the close frame or the relay's channel close said, and the link wraps every duplex it hands the handshake (`sealedOnly`), so an injected dialler can't leak a transport reason either. `removed` therefore reaches the link only from inside the channel. Tests: `carriers.test.ts` (a raw WebSocket close with reason `removed`, and a relay channel close, each end `carrier`), and the direct suite's carrier ended as `removed`, which stays transient.
  Rationale: (b), an additive change to org-protocol's `Channel`, was not needed: with every carrier normalised below the channel, no transport word reaches `onClose`.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor, from the architect's review.

- Decision: Finding 2 [P2]. A query answered `refused` with the reason `unavailable` and no outcome asks again on the query backoff; any other reason with no outcome (`grade`, `class`) resolves `unknown {reason}`. §"Queries" says so now. Test: `acts.test.ts`.
  Rationale: `unavailable` is the executor's ledger closed for a moment, not the machine declining to say.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor, from the architect's review.

- Decision: Finding 3 [P2]. A call with no input sends `input: null`. Test: `link.test.ts`, a void call answered while a subscription on the same channel stands.
  Rationale: an `undefined` field vanishes in `JSON.stringify`, and the endpoint closes the channel on the malformed `call`, ending every other stream.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor, from the architect's review.

- Decision: Finding 4 [P2]. A credit refund is split into messages of at most `SUB_CREDIT_MAX`, keeping the exact total. Test: `subs.test.ts`, an overdraw beyond the cap.
  Rationale: the endpoint finds a larger `credit` malformed.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor, from the architect's review.

- Decision: Finding 5 [P2]. Liveness matches the endpoint's `#arm`: a period in which anything was heard resets the count, each silent period pings, and the third silent period in a row closes `timeout`. §"Liveness" says so now. Tests: `link.test.ts`.
  Rationale: the two sides should agree on when a quiet channel is dead.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor, from the architect's review.

- Decision: An act with no `ack` within `ACK_TIMEOUT_MS` on a live channel is marked ambiguous and queried, not resealed. A reseal goes only after the channel is lost (sent, not acked, unsigned, within its window).
  Rationale: on a live channel the first copy is most likely queued, not lost; a query costs one round trip and lets the ledger answer, and `not-received` tombstones the id so a late first copy can't run. Rejected: resealing on the ack timeout, which on a slow endpoint would put two copies in flight for the ledger to deduplicate.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor.

- Decision: Orientation's claim that a device which stops reading loses its socket is narrowed to the direct carrier; the hub's last hop has no bound (P5 Decision Log 94).
  Rationale: the architect's review, verified against `apps/desk-hub/src/objects/relay.ts`.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor.

- Decision: Both link suites run in vitest's `node-timing` project (`TIMING_NODE_TESTS`), the direct one included: it runs a real endpoint over real sockets and the handshake's WebCrypto under wall-clock timeouts.
  Rationale: the timing project's pattern for suites that run real carriers (`test/endpoint/channels.local.test.ts` and its siblings).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor.

- Decision: reviewer-high's three findings on the built branch (2 P1, 1 P2), each reproduced by the reviewer and fixed by one fix wave with a test that failed first. (1) An attempt stamped its trust with the generation read after `machineKeys` resolved, so a session change during that await could let a channel authenticated against the superseded view be adopted; the generation is now read before the session. (2) A delegated machine's certificate key lapses at its delegation's `expires` with no session change, and nothing rechecked it; the link rechecks trust and the source the roster on a timer at the next expiry (`nextCertExpiry`, `timeoutAt`, stepped at most a day). (3) An act whose channel was lost and never came back never resolved, since the horizon check lived only in the query path; each act now has its own horizon timer.
  Rationale: (1) and (2) let a channel outlive the org's trust in the key it was made under, which the trust generation exists to prevent; (3) breaks `ActHandle.outcome`'s promise to settle. An act that never had a copy out settles `refused expired` at the horizon rather than `unknown`, as `close()` distinguishes the two.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor.

- Decision: the first class 1a run was red on `link.test.ts` alone (11 of 26 on one unit shard). The suite holds the clock and lets real async work run for a fixed number of event-loop turns, and an attempt's key ids use WebCrypto's digest on libuv's thread pool, which a loaded runner shares. The suite now computes the same key ids synchronously (a `vi.mock` of `keyId`). Reproduced locally with the pool contended: 26 of 26 red before, 26 of 26 green after.
  Rationale: a test race, not a link bug; acts and subs passed under the same contention unchanged.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor.

- Decision: The review has converged. A narrow reviewer-high round on the fix commit (336d08000) found nothing, and its focused runs passed: link 28, acts 22, source 4. That makes two rounds: 3 findings, then 0.
  Rationale: the stop rule; a round with nothing worth changing.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, O7 M1's executor.

- Decision: The owner answered A2, A4, A5 and A6 as recommended. The provisional builds stand: M1's 60 s hold (A4) is unchanged; M3's and M4's re-plans build A6's forward and A5's machine menu as written; A2's carrier hover lands with the first milestone that carries a link's carrier to the renderer.
  Rationale: the owner's product calls; recorded in P5 Decision Log 108.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-28, the architect, from the owner's answers.

- Decision: The re-plan of M2 to M7 (2026-10-31), at master 33935b949 after O3-E's landing (P5 Decision Log 92's condition). Verification for the re-planned milestones: this document gets one independent spec review and one doperpowers:adversarial-reviewer buildability round on its execution section (M2 to M4 first), repeated until a round yields nothing worth changing; M2, M3 and M4 land at doperpowers:reviewer-high, M6 and M7 at medium. The split judgement: O7 stays one child. Its remaining milestones share one state owner on the app side (the adapter and the renderer's machine dimension), one proof scene (`e2e:remote` and the endpoint harness), and each of M3, M4 and M6 consumes M2's pane and adapter; the parts with other owners (the pty host's ledger, the endpoint's rows, the spine's `session.spawn`) are small and land with the milestone that needs them. The split signals that do show (M3's byte streams and ledger against M4's spine rows; M7's measured verification) are milestone boundaries, as O3-D's and O3-E's planners read theirs. Rejected: children for M2 to M4, which would put one adapter, one scene and one `src/main/index.ts` composition on three branches to reconcile.
  Rationale: decomposing's gate (one agent can own it; O9 ran ten milestones as one child) and its keep-together rule (the adapter and the scene are not meaningful apart).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: The order is M2, then M4 and M3 in either order, then M6, M5 when O4 and O5 land, M7 last. M4 before M3 by value (acceptance 4 and the file surfaces are the daily use; M3's streams are the riskiest protocol work and go after the adapter has proved itself), not by dependency; M3 does consume M4's `ClientRef` per channel, so an M3 that lands first builds that piece itself and M4 keeps it.
  Rationale: both need only M2's pane; a branch that lands second re-bumps the wire and the RL2 minor.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: Subscriptions follow the tabs, with a 10 s linger, rather than a renderer watch command (O9's `laneA.watch`) or every listed session.
  Rationale: lane B's renderer has no connect step for a session (events are broadcast and the transcript opens on the tab), and the channel's 64-subscription cap would not cover a server's listings. Rejected: a watch command (a new renderer surface for one consumer); every listed session (the cap, and the engine host woken for sessions nobody looks at).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: The remote pane runs over a record read by `session.get` and emitted as `session.changed`, with one row per address in the sidebar, rather than over the listed row with a parallel state.
  Rationale: every panel tool reads `SessionRecord | null` for the session's directory and worktree, which M4 and M3 need; P5 Decision Log 101 names the switch. Rejected: O9's `LaneAState` shape for lane B (a second record shape for the same kind of session).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: `rl2.executor.machine` lands in M2, for `session.acquire`, not in M4.
  Rationale: a send to a released remote session resumes it through `session.acquire`, a `machine` act; without it M2's conversation would stop at the first idle release. Rejected: an engine-host act for the resume (the resume is the session service's, in the spine).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: The step-up sheet is macOS's own sheet through `dialog.showMessageBox`, composed as a `Confirm` before Touch ID, and given to O8's state signer as well (A7).
  Rationale: P5 Decision Log 126 asks for the text before Touch ID and for O8's confirm hook to change with it; a native sheet shows with no page loaded and keeps main's words the only copy. Rejected: an in-app sheet (a second surface to keep in step).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: Streams are JSON message kinds with base64 data, behind `rl2.streams`, opened under an act's opId and authorized by that act's ledger binding, re-authorized with subscriptions; a binary plaintext kind is the named follow-up if M7's forward throughput needs it.
  Rationale: one facility for terminals, forwards, and M5's documents and artifact pages, with no change to the sealing layer or to O10's authorization order; the act carries the grade and the step-up. Rejected: a binary kind now (a change to `Channel` for a 33 % saving on terminal output); `pty.write` as acts (a fsynced claim per keystroke); admitting `shell` calls for the pty reads (a change to `authorize.ts`'s rule for two reads: they are acts under the pty host's ledger instead).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: The pty host keeps its own ledger (`pty.op`, `pty.opQuery`, mirroring `src/engine-host/ops.ts`) rather than `mawsd` claiming pty acts through `machine.op`.
  Rationale: the effect lands in the pty host, as lane B's lands in the engine host (P5 Decision Log 45's rule); `machine.op` claims `machine`-owned commands only and the bus's `invoke` doesn't reach the pty relay. Rejected: widening `claimable` to `pty-host` names (a second owner's commands under the spine's lock).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: `git.*`'s reads cross under a `checkout` target rule fed by an optional `session` on `call` (behind `rl2.call-session`): the endpoint takes the rank from the named session and admits the call only for a path within that session's roots.
  Rationale: the inputs name a directory, not a session, and the directory alone grades nothing. Rejected: an `appSessionId` on every git input (a wire change to every local caller); grading the reads at the machine level (`view` on any session would open every checkout).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: Remote uploads go as parts of `files.upload` (an additive optional `part`), 2 MiB per act, each claimed by `machine.op`, staged on the machine and moved into place at the last part.
  Rationale: the 4 MiB message against the 32 MiB file; a part per act keeps the reseal rule and the ledger's run-once. Rejected: M3's streams for uploads (an order dependency M4 doesn't need); one message per upload (a 2.9 MiB bound the owner's PDFs exceed).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: The remote spawn is one new command, `session.spawn`, with DX6's spawn fields and the mode required, composed on the machine from `session.create`, `intent.patch` and `start`, recording the requester and the signed action as `launchedBy` for O12's observer.
  Rationale: `spawnRefusal` admits DX6's fields only and reads an absent mode as the ceiling, which the machine's own default can exceed; an intent has no engine session for the endpoint to place MAWS's own three commands by; `session.launch` is already the engine host's launch-spec command. Rejected: three acts; a MAWS-shaped input; an optional mode; retagging `session.launch` (a changed owner is a major).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: Attention and search cross under an `each-session` target rule (the row admitted at `list`, `calls.ts` filtering the answer per session at the registry's `view`), and the live feeds are a `feed` on `sub` behind `rl2.feeds`: `checkout` per session in M4, `attention` machine-wide in M6.
  Rationale: both answer for the whole machine and name no session; a filtered answer keeps O10's rule that nothing of a session reaches a caller below its floor; the checkout events have no session cursor and nothing carried them. Rejected: `cards` subscriptions per listed session (the cap); a rank-filtered `session.list` (C5's refused alternative); polling for checkout changes.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: `e2e:remote` runs profile B's real private `mawsd` enrolled in a local org, with the harness's fake sminos on B's RL10 socket for records and listings, and A signed in through `MAWS_DEV_ORG` with a dev-only `directMap`, launched once per carrier; the measured numbers are `e2e:remote-latency`'s, on the mini.
  Rationale: the tracker row asks for the built app over a real endpoint; records and listings come only from sminos in production, and the fake speaks the real RL10; the direct carrier needs a dial map on one Mac (the listener advertises a tailnet address), read once per launch. Rejected: a fake endpoint in the spec's process (the app proven, the machine not); an `authorization.records` seam in `mawsd` (it lists nothing, so A would have no row); wall-clock bounds in `e2e:remote`.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: `fs.holders`, `fs.readSpill` and `session.deletions.list` are not offered; `git.worktree.add`, `remove` and `deleteBranch` are not offered; `files.reveal` and `files.openExternal` wait for M5's client port.
  Rationale: no remote reader for the first (the take-over flow is disabled remotely), no session-bound rule for a spill's path, and deletions are the machine owner's Settings surface; a new session's worktree is `session.spawn`'s; the client-bound two act on the asking client (A10).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: The first review round on the re-plan (doperpowers:adversarial-reviewer, 9 P1 and 4 P2; an independent spec review, 3 P1, 6 P2 and 7 P3), each finding verified against the code and folded. What moved: (1) lane B placement reads the spine's record first in `targets.ts`, since the engine host's `session.status` knows only the sessions of its own run and a released session was `unknown` (M2); (2) a send answers at its outcome, never `queued` at 5 s (M2); (3) the overlay keeps sends available while the link is down, under A4's hold (M2); (4) an image over the channel's limit is refused locally, not sent (M2); (5) `e2e:remote` lists B's sessions through the harness's fake sminos on RL10 rather than a records seam that would have listed nothing (M2); (6) A is launched once per carrier, since `directMap` is read once (M2); (7) `session.launch` is the engine host's; the remote spawn is `session.spawn`, with `permission_mode` required and step 6's guard widened to every `spawn` row (M4); (8) the claim and the sealed action reach the spawn's handler through `HandlerContext.op` and `machine.op`'s `action`, and `launchedBy` carries the action as RL9's proof (M4); (9) the checkout events cross as a `checkout` feed, `rl2.feeds` moving to M4, with watches owned by a `ClientRef` the endpoint mints per channel and replayed after a reconnect (M4); (10) `fs.agentEvents` by its session, `fs.readSpill` not offered (M4); (11) `pty.list` cannot be an act (a void input), so `pty.listFor {appSessionId}`; a claimed attach is forced `credit: false` whatever its args and answers metadata only, the ring going over the stream; `mawsd`'s seam guard reads `pty.op`'s inner name (M3); (12) open streams are re-authorized at every `caps` refresh and record change, closing `removed` on a lost grant (M3); (13) input offsets carry an `inputEpoch` per app run (M3); (14) a non-owner's prompts are stated and bounded (M3); (15) the attention feed emits empty items for a session that left the member's set (M6); (16) `pong` gains the endpoint's `received` and `sent` for the live bench's clock, the one product change M7 makes (M7); (17) the `each-session` rule's mechanism against step 4 is stated (M6); and the editorial items (the vitest project `renderer`, `GapRow` named, `launchedBy`'s file, `AuthorizeRequest.session` and `gate.ts`, the capability constants in each milestone's touches, `link.member` nullable, the route line's always-fallback, M6's two unproved claims). Findings not adopted: none; the adversarial round's "represent an unacknowledged operation as a queue entry" and the spec review's "answer at 5 s" were the same finding, resolved by waiting for the outcome.
  Rationale: each finding held against the code; the three P1s on placement, the spawn's name and the watches' transport would have let every M2 and M4 proof pass while the live scene failed.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: The second review round (doperpowers:adversarial-reviewer, 3 P1 and 8 P2; the independent spec review, 2 P2 and 10 P3), each verified against the code and folded. What moved: (1) a placed session's stream that the endpoint ends `unavailable` (the shared catch-up's opening read fails on the engine host, and plane B ends where the front retains) is read as `idle` and subscribed again with O9's backoff, so an idle session opens, shows its history and streams at its resume, and the overlay opens controls on a record rather than a status (M2); (2) acts have two shapes: an engine-host act with `session` and `context`, a `machine` act with neither (the link and `isAction` refuse the mixed form), placed by `args.appSessionId`, so `session.acquire` and M4's writes work on an idle session (M2, M4); (3) a send the channel carried and then lost is ambiguous and queried to RL4's horizon, so the renderer is answered `timeout` at 60 s with the pending words while the act keeps querying, and the queue goes on (M2); (4) the `checkout` rule's boundary is the repository each session root belongs to, resolved through `git.status`'s `repoRoot`, since git reads answer for the repository and not the directory, with the feed filtered the same way (M4; a decision for the architect's attention); (5) the session a path-class call is made from reaches main in the preload envelope's third argument and `MainContext.session` (M4); (6) a reconnect replays git watches and invalidates files watches (`files.changed {kind: 'invalidated'}`, `watch-replay.ts`'s rule), since `files.watch` lists nothing (M4); (7) a spawn with attachments sends no brief and one `session.send` after its uploads (M4); (8) a harness-only confirmation under `MAWS_E2E` lets `e2e:remote` spawn unattended, the signature real (M2, M4); (9) the `ClientRef` per channel moves to M2, so M3 and M4 are independent, and M6 is after M4 (feeds); (10) a remote viewer's ring replay reads `pty.replay {ptyId, from}` (non-crediting, offset-based), since `pty.attach`'s replay starts at the local app's checkpoint, and a claimed attach answers `{info, ringStart, nextOffset}` (M3); (11) a forward is kept per `(device, forward)` for `RESUME_MS` after a channel loss, so a reconnect needs no new act and a non-owner no new prompt, while a `tcp` stream itself is not resumed (M3); (12) the attention feed sends a newly viewable session's current items at once and never ends for an empty set (M6); (13) the live bench judges delta-to-paint over the offset's whole interval (± half the exchange's round trip) and reports `inconclusive` where the interval is wider than the allowance, so a stable asymmetric route cannot pass it falsely (M7); and the editorial items (A11's clause, `test/endpoint/targets.test.ts` new, `session.forget` in M2's row, M4's and M7's RL2 minor and constants, the `directMap` path through `MachinesDeps.linkDeps`, acceptance 10's two renderer proofs, `reachMachine`'s file, `session.spawn`'s answer shape, §I's advisory list marked superseded). Not adopted: none.
  Rationale: each finding held against the code; the three P1s (the idle stream's end, git's repository-wide reads, the checkpoint-bound replay) would have let M2's, M4's and M3's proofs pass while the scene failed or exposed more than its rule said.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: The review has converged. Two rounds: 29 findings, then 23, every one folded; the second round's findings were each one layer below the first round's fixes (the idle stream behind the placement fix, the repository behind the path rule, the checkpoint behind the attach), and no finding contradicted the design or the milestone shape. A third round would re-read the same sections.
  Rationale: the stop rule (a round that yields nothing worth changing); the second round's spec review's own verdict that no further round is needed after its two P2s.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's re-planner.

- Decision: M2 to M6's execution (from 2026-10-31) runs one branch and PR per milestone (`p5-o7-m2`, `p5-o7-m4`, `p5-o7-m3`, `p5-o7-m6`), each cut from master after the previous one lands, with the architect's wave 2 interface ledger (`docs/doperpowers/ledgers/2026-10-31-p5-wave2-interfaces.md`) binding beside this document: `WIRE_MINOR` and `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` are taken at landing (master's plus one), never reserved; a name or shared file the ledger lacks goes to the architect before its milestone lands; once O5's split lands, the app's composition is edited in `src/main/app.ts`, not `src/main/index.ts`. M2 is executed as four tasks, each reviewed before the next consumes it: (a) the machine's side and the link (org-protocol's `machine` executor, both capability constants, placement through the spine's record, the `ClientRef` per channel, the M2 offer rows, the link's `peerCapabilities`, executor and size checks, the dev session's `directMap`, `MachinesDeps.linkDeps`, the step-up sheet); (b) main's adapter (`src/main/lane-b/`, `remote.*`, the composition, `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`); (c) the renderer; (d) the e2e scene and its gate wiring. The report is `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-31-p5-o7-report.md`, carried on each milestone's branch.
  Rationale: M2 touches about fifty files across four layers that consume each other in that order; one executor per layer keeps each within one context and puts a review at each interface (the skill's dependency frontier). The per-milestone branches are the architect's dispatch.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M2a as built (commits 387f54cc6..f79a4f4f7). (1) The RL2 version enters `h1` and `h2` and so the handshake's MACs, so a minor's move broke every recorded handshake vector; `HandshakeOptions.protocol?` (the version a side announces, `RL2_PROTOCOL` by default) and `VECTOR_PROTOCOL` (1.0) pin the vectors at the version they were recorded at, for M3, M4 and M7's bumps too. Rejected: regenerating `vectors.json` at each minor (new keys and signatures throughout). (2) Placement takes the spine's record when it names an `engineSessionId`; a missing record, a null engine id or a spine error falls back to the engine host's `session.status`, so a fresh launch whose index has not heard its id is still placed and a slow spine costs no availability. (3) The per-channel `ClientRef` is `endableClientRef('<device>/<RL2 channel id>')`, not the channel table's key: the services key watches by `client.id`, and the hub reuses channel numbers, so a late end of an old ref under a reused key would release a new channel's watches. It rides every executor dispatch and authorization's own reads for the request (`Who.client`); plane B's stream reads and the subscription re-checks carry none (a subscription's `who` outlives its channel). (4) The link checks the peer's executor at each transmit and each query (the peer is known per channel): an act with no copy out is `refused not-offered`, one already sent `unknown not-offered`. The size check measures what `Channel.send` measures, `{t: 'act', action}`'s UTF-8 JSON, once as built (so no Touch ID for an act that cannot go) and again as signed. (5) `directMap` keys are `ws://` addresses and its values `ws://127.0.0.1:<port>`; a malformed map signs the dev session out; `directMap()` answers `{}` until signed in, so main reads it at dial time. (6) A `machine` call row dispatches generically, so `machine.identity` (`list`) now answers remotely. (7) The sheet does not catch a rejected `showMessageBox`: both signers already turn a throwing confirmation into a refusal.
  Rationale: each settles a gap the design left implicit, in its intent; (1) and (3) were forced by the code (the MACs; the hub's channel numbering).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7 M2a's executor, folded by the controller.

- Decision: M2a's task review (2 Important, 2 Minor), adjudicated. (1) Placement through the index's `engineSessionId` while engine-host execution goes by `appSessionId` let a member authorized on the indexed engine session read and drive a newer one the index had not caught up with (reproduced by the reviewer): authorization's engine session and the executed one must agree, checked against the live row where the host holds one, `unavailable` when they differ; M2's placement decision now says so. (2) The placement fallback: a null `engineSessionId` falls back to the host (kept, and the design text revised: the host places a fresh launch and still answers `unknown` for a never-launched session, which was the text's intent), but a failing spine answers `unavailable`, logged, rather than a silent fallback. (3) `mawsd` posts `clientGone` to the engine host for the endpoint's per-channel refs (`src/mawsd/daemon.ts`, the existing `clientGoneRelay` pattern), since M2's reason for the per-channel ref names the engine host's holds. (4) The row tests assert each admitted read reached its executor by owner and name.
  Rationale: (1) is a permission boundary; (2) keeps availability for fresh launches without hiding a spine fault as `grade`; (3) and (4) are cheap and close what the per-channel ref and the row rule promise.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, the controller.

- Decision: M2a's fix wave (deb886f12, 0ec981cc4; re-review approved). The live check sits in placement (`targets.ts`): a read, a subscription's opening, a `machine` act and a query's binding each read the live row once; an engine-host act skips it there, and `actions.ts` compares the status it already reads for the context with the placed engine session. A live row whose `engineSessionId` is null counts as different (`unavailable`). `AuthorizeContext` gains `log` for the spine-fault line. `src/shared/host/host-supervisor.ts`'s `forward` takes a `ClientRef` as well as an id and carries it into the link's `clientGoneRelay` (never on a protocol-2 link); `mawsd`'s seam passes the ref on its engine-host and pty-host forwards. A status read through `mawsd` wakes a parked engine host, so the live check wakes it for a spine read placed by a record; the pane's subscription and `session.events` wake it anyway.
  Rationale: one status read per request, none extra for an act; the relay was the existing mechanism for a forwarded client's end.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7 M2a's executor, folded by the controller.

- Decision: The owner answered A7, A8, A9 and A10 as recommended (2026-10-31, through the architect): the step-up confirmation is macOS's own sheet (A7); a remote session's pending item notifies as a local one does (A8); a remote terminal after a new app run restores from the ring's last 512 KiB (A9); Reveal in Finder and Open externally on a remote file are greyed with "on `<machine>`" until M5, then open a copy (A10). The document's provisional builds stand.
  Rationale: the owner's product calls.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, the owner, recorded by O7's executor.

- Decision: M2b as built (c660c00e3, b5013c618; the composition 9c6cc0ece in `src/main/app.ts`, after O5's split landed). (1) `CONTROL_KEYS` lives in `src/shared/ipc/schema/remote.ts` (main cannot import the renderer); the renderer re-exports it (M2c). (2) `RemoteSessionState` gains `ended` (why the stream ended: `unplaced`, `closed`, `removed`, an end reason), which drives the overlay's `reason`. (3) The record is read before subscribing; an unplaced answer retries at 0.5, 1, 2, 4 s then ends `unplaced`, while `offline`, `timeout` and `unavailable` retry on the idle backoff without spending those attempts; any `main.tabs` write re-opens every held address, so a click retries an unplaced session. (4) The cursor carries across fresh subscriptions: a first `subbed` is a gap only when it is `missing` with a cursor carried, any later non-`replay` `subbed` is a gap, so a first open and a released session resumed by a new launch draw no bar. (5) Events and parts are read one by one through the codec; one that does not read is dropped and logged. (6) The pending bound covers every engine-host act, counted from its turn (a queued send from reaching the head) and including the resume wait; an act still `waiting` at the bound waits for the link's own hold (A4). (7) The adapter's own `session.acquire` (reason `send`) is one at a time per session, and a ready answer subscribes an idle session at once. (8) `engine.models` answers from a live session's `session.listModels` on that machine (the one whose directory matches first), else the built-in list. (9) `session.changed`'s `revision` is strictly increasing across the adapter. (10) Neither the renderer's invoke nor the router's remote path times out before 60 s, so no deadline changed. (11) The layout store is built before the router in `app.ts`, since lane B reads `main.tabs`. Names beyond §J/§K: the `remote` IPC namespace's files (`src/shared/ipc/{names,grades}/remote.ts`), `remote` in `link-surface.ts`'s `CLIENT_RULES`, and test support `test/lane-b/fake-link.ts` and `FakeEngineHost.handlers`.
  Rationale: each fills a gap in M2's decisions in their intent; (4) keeps the bar for real gaps.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7 M2b's executor, folded by the controller.

- Decision: M2b's task review (1 Important, 2 Minor, and a renderer-reload gap) and its first class 1a run, fixed (cf60e1131, f183f6991). (1) One pending bound per act: `ActRequest.sendBy` (an absolute deadline) ends the link's unsent window at the adapter's deadline, and no act is made past it, so a resume's time is not given back to the send (A4's hold is one minute from the person's Send, not from the act's creation). (2) A tab of a session the org rows list as lane A is never placed by lane B (`RemoteLaneBDeps.laneOf`, refreshed at each rows change). (3) A session-only `ended('removed')` (a withdrawn session right, the channel kept) closes every control with "`<machine>` no longer lets you open this session", apart from the device's removal. (4) A reloaded or second window gets the held session's record, last parts and `remote.session` again: the pane's own `session.get` of a held remote session triggers the replay, so the renderer subscribes to its events before that call (M2c). Rejected: a window-load hook (events sent at `did-finish-load` are lost, `src/main/attention/navigation.ts`); a new renderer command. The gate fix: the app root's suites mock what the composition now takes, and `address-paths.json` gains the `remote.*` addresses.
  Rationale: (1) is A4's intent (no delayed outbox); (2)–(4) close what the tabs rule and the overlay promise.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7 M2b's executor from the task review, folded by the controller.

- Decision: M2c as built (b82993123). (1) `remote.session` is subscribed in `connectShell`; `remote-connect` mounts after it and holds a pane's `session.get` until then, so the pane subscribes before the read that triggers main's replay; the read's own answer is not applied (the record arrives as `session.changed` with its revision). (2) Try again rewrites `main.tabs` through `layout.set` (main re-opens every held address at any write), and is not offered while the link reports a refusal. (3) The gap bar is a synthesized `notice.posted` under the reserved key `maws:history-gap`, drawn as a new row kind `gap`; no wire change. (4) `REMOTE_INLINE_MAX_BYTES` and `REMOTE_TOO_LARGE_WORDS` live in `src/shared/ipc/schema/remote.ts`, and main takes its words from there. (5) A key the remote overlay closes wins over lane B's own capability. (6) A record at a listed address whose project this Mac does not know is filed under its listing's row and tab set, so the open tab does not jump when the record arrives (three of O8 M5's test expectations move with it). (7) "Hub unreachable" sits above the sidebar's tree (the sidebar has no org header), fed by `stores/hub-access.ts`. Beyond M2's Touches: `visibility.ts`, `session-actions.ts`, `menus.tsx`, `Toolbar.tsx`, `SessionRow.tsx` (the menus skip what main refuses for a remote record).
  Rationale: (1) honours M2b's replay contract; (6) keeps O8 M5's tab placement stable across the row-to-record switch, which M2's "one row per address" implies.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7 M2c's executor, folded by the controller.

- Decision: M2c's task review (5 Important), each reproduced by the reviewer and sent back as a fix: one deadline per remote send (the renderer does not acquire first for a remote session; main resumes and sends under the send's one bound), the composer's chip bindings closed with the overlay, Interrupt and Stop kept available for a remote session whose link is down and whose local status was forgotten, a `remote.gap` that arrives during hydration kept at its launch's boundary, and remote read marks kept per address in this app's memory (viewing, the keyboard toggle, Mark all read).
  Rationale: each is a contract M2 states (A4's single hold; the overlay rule; the gap's position; read marks in memory).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, the controller.

- Decision: M2c's fix waves (a24bf50b4, c8da63367). The four contract fixes as adjudicated, with one accepted departure: `connectShell` keeps a held remote lane B session's last status through a link drop (forgotten at `idle` or `ended`), so Stop and Escape stay on a running turn while the link is down (Orientation's "forgets a departed machine's statuses" no longer holds for those sessions). The gap's position: for lane B a `missing` `subbed`'s position is the launch's start (lane B streams from the launch's start and re-sends what the machine still holds), so it cannot name the hole; main holds a `missing` gap until the first event delivered after the `subbed` and emits `remote.gap {…, at: {seq, idx}}` just before it (an omitted event's gap carries its own position; a relaunch's snapshot carries none), and the renderer anchors a gap that arrives during hydration at the first event of its launch at or after `at`, before the history pager starts, so loading an older page keeps it. `at` is optional and main-owned (no wire change). Remote read marks live in `src/renderer/src/lane-b/read-marks.ts`.
  Rationale: §K's `remote.gap` gains the position M2's gap decision ("the cursor for a `missing`") needs.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7 M2c's executor from the task review, folded by the controller.

- Decision: M2d as built (8dc71d1dd..5d3c291c0, built in a side worktree beside M2c and merged). (1) B's fake tailnet preload goes on B's argv as `--require` through `profile-daemon.ts`'s new `nodeArgs` (the supervisor strips `NODE_OPTIONS`). (2) The relay launch maps B's advertised address to a closed loopback port rather than writing no map, so nothing dials a real 100.x address. (3) B's session starts the way an app starts one (`session.create`, `.intent.patch`, `.start` under `MAWS_E2E_REPLAY_FIXTURES`), so it has the spine record the adapter reads. (4) The fake sminos sends each project's presence and a log-plane hello (the endpoint lists only sessions of a project it relays), and `sminos.list` waits for B's `engine.sessions` before the member frame, under Playwright's `expect.poll`. (5) A's device is listed with an endorsement signed by the admin's device (the hub refuses an unendorsed second device, `409 endorsement-missing`). (6) Streaming is proved on A's screen: B's replay streams a strict prefix of the reply, then holds at a recorded `get_context_usage` until the test has seen the prefix and not the whole in A's pane, and the test's own read releases it; the raw bridge's two deltas are checked beside it. (7) The second launch sends nothing (sending does not depend on the carrier). (8) `e2e:remote` is a plain build guarded by `MAWS_REMOTE_E2E=1`, joins `CLASS1_E2E` (the default-specs pin moves to 102), and is not added to `HOSTED_MAC_E2E` (`--lane hosted` runs it unlisted; admission is CI-opt's). `devOrgDir` reads the chain once at the scene's start: a milestone that changes the org state later re-reads it.
  Rationale: each keeps the scene on production paths with no authorization seam and no wall-clock bound.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7 M2d's executor with its task review, folded by the controller.

- Decision: M2's milestone review (doperpowers:reviewer-high on 72fb4db85..c1cb9685f; 1 P1, 1 P2) and its fix (8584e47ae, 30675cb24; a narrow re-review). (1) Fixed: only a launch whose `process` is `ready` takes acts; an `exited` row the engine host keeps (a crash, a stop, a budget or idle release) is resumed by `session.acquire` under the send's one bound, and a `starting` or `initializing` launch is waited for. (2) Fixed for a live launch: a remote session's `session.events` is taken by main (`src/main/lane-b/remote-history.ts`) and read in pages the channel carries (`afterSeq`/`limit` from the read's start, a refused page halved), a row that alone cannot cross replaced by `remote.gap {launchId, at}`. Left open, as an unresolved finding: a window the command's inputs cannot page (`before`, `agentId`, `seek`, and a transcript the engine host reads from disk, whose history window is a 2 MiB soft budget of string length that keeps a whole newest turn up to a 16 MiB hard budget) answers `historyUnavailable` with no events when one answer exceeds the channel, so a parked session whose newest window carries large images opens with only the gap bar until it is resumed, and an oversized older page hides what precedes it. The remedy is a byte budget on `session.events` (an optional input bounding the answer's UTF-8 size, with a continuation cursor), a wire change, so it is taken with M4's `WIRE_MINOR` bump rather than making M2 take one (the ledger: M2 takes none); M4's Touches gain `src/engine-host/fs/follow.ts`, `src/shared/graph/history-window.ts` and `session.events`' input for it.
  Rationale: (1) and the live-launch half of (2) are M2's contract; the disk half needs an additive wire field, which M4 already carries a bump for.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, the controller, from the milestone review.

- Decision: M2's last fixes before landing. (1) Until M4 (acceptance 12), a remote window too large to page is told, not left bare: main emits the main-owned `remote.historyTooLarge {appSessionId, window: 'newest' | 'older'}` just before its `historyUnavailable` answer, and the renderer draws "This session's earlier history on `<machine>` is too large to show here yet; new messages appear below as the session goes on" (the newest window) or "Earlier history on `<machine>` is too large to show here yet" (an older page); a lost range keeps "Earlier history isn't available" (bf2b53b2a, d83c8890f). A sibling event rather than a reason on `remote.gap`, since the renderer draws a positioned bar for each `remote.gap` and a disk window has no launch to name. The final review round caught a first wording that promised a resume would restore the window, which it does not (a resume streams the new launch), and it was corrected. (2) The e2e scene's three esbuild bundles resolve `@shared` through an `alias`, as `scripts/catalog-summary.mjs` does, since esbuild reads `paths` only from the nearest `tsconfig.json` and O4 M1's move of `backup-exclusion.ts` to `src/shared/fs/` put an `@shared` import under `src/main/device/keys.ts` (dda418797).
  Rationale: the architect's condition that the interim bar tell the person what to do; a build break from a sibling's landing.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, the controller.

- Decision: M4's execution (2026-10-31), on `p5-o7-m4` cut from master 3adf9c979 (M2 landed, PR #410), in five tasks, each reviewed before the next consumes it: (a1) the machine's services: `session.spawn` (schema, grade, route, the spine's handler), `machine.op`'s `action` and `HandlerContext.op`, `launchedBy`, `files.upload`'s parts, and acceptance 12's `session.events` byte budget in the engine host's history window, with the one `WIRE_MINOR` bump (master's plus one, re-taken at landing); (a2) the protocol and the endpoint: `call.session` (`rl2.call-session`), feeds (`rl2.feeds`) and the `checkout` feed, the `checkout` target rule, step 6's spawn guard over every `spawn` row with the `machine` target, M4's offer rows, `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` 2; (b) main: the link's `call` session and feed subscription, `dispatch.ts`'s `call.session`, the preload envelope and `MainContext.session`, the adapter's M4 acts, uploads in parts, `session.spawn`, the checkout feed's events with the watch replay and invalidation, and `remote-history.ts` paging every window by the byte budget; (c) the renderer; (d) `e2e:remote`'s M4 scenes. Two master rules bind it (P5 Decision Logs 173 and 174, PRs #409 and #411): a spine load caused only by the engine host's events sends that host nothing unless it is ready, until a client's or the endpoint's command needs it; and a command that waits on the session service's boot (`session.intent.list`, `session.create`, `session.acquire`) wakes a parked engine host. Every M4 read and act reaches the machine as an endpoint command, so it loads wanted: `session.spawn` composes `session.create` and so wakes the host as a need; acceptance 12's disk-window and older-page reads stay endpoint commands on the wanted side. `mawsd`'s endpoint seam (`src/mawsd/daemon.ts`, `services.dispatch`) still forwards the per-channel `ClientRef` to the engine and pty hosts as M2 left it.
  Rationale: M4 spans the services, the endpoint, main, the renderer and the scene, which consume each other in that order (M2's split, which caught a defect at each interface).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M4a1 as built (`fab9962b9`..`b96b6230a`, reviewed clean after one fix wave; class 1a green with `e2e:cross-build` against the merge-base peer at wire 3.12, run 41240200433). (1) `launchedBy` rides the setup intent and is stored in the `sessions.launched_by` column; its `action` is null when the endpoint sends none. (2) The spawn handler validates `permission_mode` and `effort` itself; a refusal before the setup handoff discards the intent, a setup that fails before `launching` keeps it; the worktree branch is named exactly the `worktree` value; `session.spawn` has its own command budget. (3) Upload parts are stateless, each checked against the staged size, and a resend reuses the same `machine.op` op id; the last part's promotion copies through the verified staging descriptor into a destination created exclusive and unlinked-checked (no hard link), so a swapped staging path publishes nothing it did not check. (4) `session.events`' byte budget (`maxBytes`, at least `EVENTS_MIN_BYTES` 4096) reuses the existing `earlier` and `lastSeq` cursors rather than adding one; a single item over the budget answers `omitted` with no events and its cursor already past it; an `agentId` slice ignores the budget. The device's paging, for M4b: the newest disk window `{afterSeq: null, limit: null, maxBytes: B}`, older pages with `before: <earlier>` until none remains, live catch-up from `lastSeq` while incomplete, `B` the message bound less headroom. (5) A minor-12 `mawsd` refuses `maxBytes`, `part`, `action` and `session.spawn`, so M4a2's offer rows and M4b's calls gate on the peer's minor.
  Rationale: each is the narrowest change that carries the milestone's acceptance over the existing commands.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's executor (the controller), from M4a1's report.

- Decision: `launched_by` is an added column, not migration 16 (M4a1 fix wave 2, `6d9c4b8a3`). The index opener (`openDb` in `src/services/index/db.ts`) now takes `ADDED_COLUMNS` (`src/services/index/migrations.ts`, type `AddedColumn`): after the migrations, at any version, it adds each listed column a database lacks, leaving `user_version` where it was. Only a nullable column no build depends on being set qualifies, and one is never dropped or renamed; anything else stays a migration. The index version stays 15, so this milestone takes no migration number.
  Rationale: the first gate's cross-build downgrade (run 41237182488) showed that migration 16 put the index past what the build before reads, and a build that meets a newer `user_version` leaves the whole index unavailable, so a downgrade lost every read, not just the new field. An unknown column costs an older build nothing, since it reads and writes only the columns it names. Rejected: amending migration 15 with a repair (a released migration is never amended); teaching the downgrade scene to expect the lost index (accepting a user-facing regression).
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's executor (the controller); accepted by the architect at stop 3 (kept under O7; the three opener tests, a guard over `ADDED_COLUMNS` and the rebuild rule at the head of `migrations.ts` landed in `1533da5cc`; in wave 2 an additive nullable index column goes through `ADDED_COLUMNS`, never a numbered migration).

- Decision: M4a2 as built (`e13e81772`, `6fb8cc3d6`, `9c7feb647`; class 1a green with `e2e:cross-build` against the merge-base peer, run 41245068113). RL2 1.2: `CallMessage.session`, `SubMessage` as `SessionSubMessage | MachineFeedSubMessage`; M2a pinned the handshake vectors to `VECTOR_PROTOCOL` 1.0, so the minor's move left them unchanged. The `checkout` rule's path check is the tail of step 4, after the floor check, so a caller below `view` learns nothing of the checkout; its path is the input's `directory`, else its `repoRoot`, absolute and realpathed, equal to or under a bound; bounds are cached by the set of roots (`session.get` carries no revision), at most `CHECKOUT_BOUNDS_KEPT`. A lane A session's checkout read or feed is `no-target`. The `attention` feed is read now and refused `not-offered` until M6. A feed past its backlog ends `behind` (§R did not name it), which the device treats as a reconnect. The desk-hub acceptance probe's not-offered example moved from `files.read` (now offered) to `files.reveal`.
  Rationale: each keeps the spec's intent where the code had a narrower or different shape.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's executor (the controller), from M4a2's report.

- Decision: `files.upload` reaches `machine.op` without its sealed action (`KEPT_WITHOUT_ACTION` in `src/endpoint/actions.ts`); §O's `action` line reads "every `machine` act except `files.upload`". The action exists for RL9's proof, which O12's observer reads for spawns; an upload's own proof is its claim's fingerprint and the file it wrote, and a part's bytes (about 2.7 MiB of base64) do not belong in the services' 24 h ledger, which is rewritten at each open.
  Rationale: the architect's ruling on M4a2's concern 1. Rejected: storing the bytes with the claim; storing an action with its bytes replaced by a digest, which breaks the seal.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, the architect's ruling, recorded by O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: a `session.spawn` with a field outside the spawn fields is refused `malformed` at step 2 (M4a1's strict `SessionSpawnInputSchema`), before step 6's guard; the Proves line said `spec-field`, which still guards `sminos.spawn`. Both are refusals, so nothing a member sees changes.
  Rationale: the architect's ruling on M4a2's concern 2; the spec's own order puts step 2 first.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, the architect's ruling, recorded by O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: `session.events`' byte budget gets its own capability, `rl2.events-budget`, in both constants and §M's table; `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` already moves in this landing, so no further bump. The device sends `maxBytes` only to an endpoint listing it and otherwise keeps M2's path (no budget; `remote.historyTooLarge`); the `invalid_input` retry without `maxBytes` stays, for an older engine host behind a current endpoint. Built in M4b.
  Rationale: the architect's ruling on M4a2's concern 3, under Constraints' rule that a new optional field another peer would find malformed goes only to a peer listing its capability. Rejected: inferring the budget from `rl2.call-session`, a proxy that breaks the first time the two ship apart.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, the architect's ruling, recorded by O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M4a2's review fixes (fix waves 1 and 2, `5a8cc70d2`..`b098456d4`; class 1a green, run 41249214073). (1) `CheckoutBounds` realpaths the roots at every read, keys its cache by the canonical roots, and keeps no set in which a root failed to resolve or a read failed to answer, so a retargeted symlink or a root that appears later is read afresh. (2) The checkout feed holds `git.changed` synchronously at a change of roots and releases it only against the newest read's bounds (a read counter drops a stale one). (3) Lane B placement no longer tells a caller below the floor whether a session exists: a recorded engine session places the session, and a live row naming another engine session is the placement's `unavailable` flag (`Resolved.unavailable`), answered only after the caller stands at the floor on the recorded engine session (a caller who owns only the live one is `grade`); when the engine host can't answer (`host_unavailable`, `timeout`) every lane B id is `unavailable` before rank, recorded or not, and an engine-host act's placement reads the live row too, so the answer is machine-wide and names no session. This narrows O10's Decision Log entry of 2026-10-31 (#414, "that row's `engineSessionId` must equal the one placed, or the request answers `unavailable`"): it now holds only for a caller with rank on the recorded engine session. It applies to every lane B row, not M4's alone.
  Rationale: the task review's three Important findings and the re-review's one (an existence oracle while the host is away). Rejected: answering an unrecorded session `grade` while the host is away, which ended live subscriptions for good across a host outage (four tests caught it); fixing only the checkout branch, which left the same order on M2's rows. The architect and O10's owner agreed to the shared path.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M4b as built (main; `d84157ced`, `3d8cf6e25`, `51cf3608a`, `6c53bd8a3`, `4bd320478`, fix waves `34f30703a` and `3e427658a`; class 1a green, run 41257598409). (1) Every capability gate is judged by the link at each transmission, not where a call is made: `call.session` in `link.ts`'s `sendCall`, a feed in the `SubTable` (a feed to a peer without `rl2.feeds` ends `not-offered` unsent), and `maxBytes` through a per-call fallback input (`lacking: {capability, input}`) whose answer is marked `fellBack`, so a resend on a new channel to an older peer never carries the field and the pager restarts on M2's path at once; the link itself knows nothing of `maxBytes`. (2) `MachineLink.dispatch` takes an optional `session`. (3) A feed reopening releases and reinstalls the machine's git holds per directory across every session on that machine, one queue per machine (the machine keys a git hold by channel client and directory, not by session; files holds carry the session); a page's watches are given back when the page ends, since the endpoint's client ref is per channel. A feed ending `unavailable` reopens on a backoff; `removed` and `grade` stop it. (4) Uploads to a remote session always cross in 2 MiB parts, each its own act, a lost one resealed or queried under its opId and never re-made; progress is a new main-owned event, `remote.upload`. (5) A spawn's pending bound (210 s, `spawnPendingMs`) runs from when the act went out, not from the sheet. (6) History: a disk page that is only an omitted item is skipped to the page before it, `omitted` passed on with the rows; the budgeted path has no `too-large` fallback; M2's legacy pager ends with `remote.historyTooLarge` after three rows left out in a row, since a disk window ignores the cursor. (7) A remote command whose output schema is `void` reads the endpoint's `null` as `undefined`. (8) The spawn with attachments is the renderer's sequence (spawn without a brief, upload, one send); §O has no command carrying both.
  Rationale: each where the spec named a seam the code could not keep across a reconnect or a peer change, or left a gap; the reviews (two rounds) found the reconnect, shared-hold, latch and resend cases.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's executor (the controller), from M4b's report.

- Decision: An idle remote stream waits for its session's record, not a timer (O5's landing audit, PR #418's Residue; M4b `6c53bd8a3`, `4bd320478`). The `checkout` feed carries its session's `session.changed` (§R); main subscribes an idle stream again only on a record that says it runs (holder `self`, or a new engine session) or when the feed opens again, and a record that arrives while a try is unanswered is latched for one more try. With no feed (a peer without `rl2.feeds`, or a feed ended `not-offered`) the blind retry backs off from 1 s to 30 min (`idleProbeMaxMs`). Each blind try placed the session on the engine host and flipped interest, either of which restarts the park clock, so an open remote tab kept the machine's engine host from ever parking (D6). The parking case observes `mawsd`'s park clock through `engine-requests.ts`.
  Rationale: the cheapest liveness signal that neither reads the engine host nor reports interest. Rejected: only a longer backoff, which still wakes the host on every try.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's executor (the controller), on the architect's ruling.

- Decision: M4c as built (the renderer; `86395ab68`, `8de50c978`, `603ee98e1`, `3c3419524`, fix waves `bcaf494a0`, `7659524ef`, `9e57c7bf0`, `38fa0ff7a`; class 1a green, run 41260694746). (1) A path read names its session explicitly (`InvokeOptions.session`, lowered to the envelope's bare id), never inferred from focus. (2) Uploads go at Send, not at drop, so one path serves an existing session and a spawned one; images go inline in order while the whole message, path mentions included, fits the channel, and every PDF, every other file and every image past the inline bound uploads, in `files.upload` calls within its 8-file and 32 MiB bounds, each attachment's path cached so Send again uploads nothing twice. A local drop's mention becomes its upload once the chat turns remote, transactionally (every file read and judged first; a refused one keeps its mention and says why; Send waits for the conversion); a file picked by path alone is refused, and the @-menu offers none of this Mac's files while the target is remote. An idle worktree session's uploads are mentioned relative to its worktree. (3) Media stays available while the link is down: the upload acts wait under the link's hold as a send does (A4), then "Not sent" with Send again (M2's overlay rule). (4) The machine choice is renderer state per tab and org project, not part of the setup intent; the menu is hidden when no RL2 machine is up. (5) A spawn is one attempt per New chat tab: main keeps it by the tab's address (`InvokeOptions.from`, carried as the envelope's session; new `src/main/lane-b/remote-spawn.ts`), so Check again or Send after a timeout awaits the same act whatever the input, across a renderer reload, and two tabs are two attempts. An outcome nobody waited for becomes a tombstone (the answer only) kept until the tab reads it or 24 h; an attempt out on a link that was replaced becomes an unknown tombstone; a terminal unknown says "Couldn't confirm whether it started on <m>; check <m>'s sessions before starting again" and offers no action, so only a deliberate Send starts again. (6) A spawn with attachments hands its whole document to the new session's in-memory remote draft, which sends it once `send` is available. (7) Reveal and Open externally stay, disabled, titled "This file is on <m>, not on this Mac".
  Rationale: the spec's seams for the machine menu and the spawn (the setup intent, `composer/submit/`) were not where the code needed them; the architect's ruling on the spawn retry (never a second session) and two review rounds shaped (2) and (5). Rejected: an attempt id in `session.spawn`'s input, which the wire manifest and the endpoint's spawn fields would see.
  Date/Author: 2026-10-31, O7's executor (the controller), from M4c's report.

- Decision: M4's milestone review fixes (reviewer-high, two rounds; `6fb29ec05`..`d6afbd0a6`). (1) A spawn's answer is taken only by a page still there (main watches the requesting client's end), so a reload during Check again keeps the outcome for the tab. (2) The renderer persists the attempt (machine, checkout, input, generation) before asking, and clears it only when the session starts or the person picks another machine, so a reload at any point routes the next Send to the held remote attempt, never to the local `setup.start`; a definitive refusal keeps the key and makes the next Send a new start under it. (3) An unknown keeps its op; Check again and Send re-query it (`ActTable.requery`, `RemoteLink.requery`: a query only, never a resend); when an unknown's tombstone expires, main keeps a bounded "unresolved" marker (no op, no input, at most 256) under which a Send or Check again answers unknown; only the explicit "Start a new session on <m>" moves the tab to its next attempt key (`<tab>~n`) and mints an op. (4) The services' ledger (opt-in `orphanAtOpen`) answers a claim the last process left unfinished `expired` and reads each orphan's outcome back at open (a spawn by `launchedBy.opId`, `SessionStore.getByLaunchOp`); a late outcome replaces the unknown, so an adopted engine host's spawn reports its session and no tab starts a second. `host.op` and the endpoint's own ledger share `OpLedger` and are unchanged (residue for their owners). (5) Each session holds its own git watch. (6) Main pages `files.read` within the channel's bound and reassembles it, re-reading on a hash mismatch up to three times. (7) A staging file with more than one link is refused at append and at promotion. (8) A session this app spawned waits up to 60 s from its settlement for its machine's sminos to list it, saying "Waiting for <m> to list the new session…"; the endpoint's rank is unchanged, and ranking by `launchedBy.member` is O12's (residue). (9) `launchedBy` is written once, by the spawn handler alone: `create` takes it only as a separate parameter the spawn passes, the strict inputs of `session.create`, `intent.patch` and `session.update` refuse it, the index's update refuses the column, and a record carrying it is never yielded away as bare.
  Rationale: the milestone review's six findings and its re-review's two, each reproduced; the architect's refinements (an unknown not terminal against the act's own outcome; the list-wait; launchedBy as O12 M11's trust input).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M3's execution (2026-11-01), on `p5-o7-m3` cut from master da1cc2563 (M4 landed, PR #427), in five tasks, each reviewed before the next consumes it: (a1) the pty host: `pty.op` and `pty.opQuery` over the op ledger, `pty.listFor`, `pty.replay`, `mawsd`'s seam guard reading `pty.op`'s inner name, the one `WIRE_MINOR` bump (built at master's plus one, 16, re-taken at landing; the architect expects 18); (a2) the protocol and the endpoint, built beside a1 against §O's names: RL2's `pty-host` executor and the stream kinds (§M, §N), `rl2.streams` and `rl2.executor.pty-host` in both constants, M3's offer rows, the `pty-host` branch in `actions.ts` and `queries.ts`, `forward.open` as the endpoint's own graded action, the stream table with its pty and tcp planes (`src/endpoint/streams.ts`, `forwards.ts`), the `stream` request kind, streams re-authorized at a `caps` refresh; (b) main: the link's `openStream`, the adapter's pty acts and forward act, the local listeners (`src/main/forwards.ts`), `pty.hostState` from the link, `remote.forward.*`, a loopback link of a remote session routed to a forward; (c) the renderer: the terminal registry's `pty.listFor` per remote session, `hostReady` by machine, the forwarded browser tab and its mark; (d) `e2e:remote`'s M3 scenes. Gates: class 1a on each task's head; the PR head `--gates all` with no `--lane` (CI-opt's placement rule of 2026-11-01). Local runs are focused files only.
  Rationale: M3 spans the pty host, the endpoint, main, the renderer and the scene, which consume each other in that order (M2's and M4's split, which caught a defect at each interface).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M3a1 as built (the pty host; `81048976b`, `c1e112348`; class 1a green, run 41291565209). The pty host's ledger (`<pty host dir>/pty-ops.jsonl`) opens with `orphanAtOpen` and no read-back: a pty host's restart loses its ptys, and a `mawsd` restart adopts the host with its ledger open, so an unfinished claim is `expired`, never run again. `pty.op` keeps no sealed `action`. A remote spawn's cwd, shell and args are not constrained beyond `shell` (the grade is the machine's shell). The park edge (a new `mawsd` parks an adopted idle pty host whose only work is a remote spawn awaiting its first login-shell capture) is residue, routed by the architect to a tracker row at M3's landing.
  Rationale: the pty host's ptys don't survive its process, so a read-back would only ever answer what `expired` already says; the task review approved.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller), from M3a1's report and the architect's ruling on the park edge.

- Decision: M3a2 as built (the protocol and the endpoint; `a66f6e462`..`9739f1fb7`, fix waves `9111fc5e6`, `757454e99`, `b744254aa`; class 1a green, run 41299744139). (1) A chunk is unpadded base64url (the package's one encoding). (2) `streamRefused` gains `unavailable` (a ledger or host that can't answer now, an act still running): the device asks again; free because the kinds are new. (3) A stream's ledger read is as of now (`tombstoneIfAbsent: false`), bounded by the ledger's `KEPT_S`; past it a terminal needs a fresh act. (4) `unknown` covers an op the ledger doesn't hold as well as a forward past its window. (5) The caller's floor is checked before the ledger (below the floor learns nothing). (6) A `from` past the pty's end is `malformed`; `ringStart` is `max(from, ring start)`, and `ringStart > from` is the "[output lost]" signal. (7) Credit counts decoded bytes; the endpoint's input credit is 256 KiB. (8) The tcp plane pauses its socket rather than closing `behind`. (9) Re-checks run at every `caps` refresh and at a read-only policy change (`cli.ts`), not at record changes: a stream's grade is the machine's `shell`, which a session record can't move. (10) The binding is decided after the opening's reads and before anything is published, and the check and the publication are one synchronous step (`decide` runs a publish callback), on the pty plane, the tcp plane and forward creation; a re-check that overtakes a decision asks it again; the re-check hold is one per stream. (11) Pty input is at most once: an explicit refusal from the pty host leaves the chunk uncommitted and unrefunded (the reopen's resend is written); an unknown outcome (a throw, `timeout`, `host_unavailable`) moves the input record and its UTF-8 decoder past the chunk before closing `unavailable` (the resend is dropped). (12) The opening's buffered frames count against the 512 KiB backlog; a device's tcp `streamClose` resets the machine-side socket at once; a connection lost during its decision is refused `unreachable`. (13) The endpoint sends stream kinds only on a stream the device opened, so it needs no gate on the peer's `rl2.streams`. (14) The desk-hub acceptance's not-offered probe (`apps/desk-hub/deploy/endpoint-items.ts`, item 8) moves from `pty.spawn`, now offered, to `pty.write`; its pass condition is unchanged.
  Rationale: (11) the controller's ruling on the review's finding: a duplicated Enter runs a command, a lost keystroke shows as a missing echo, and at most once keeps RL §4.8's dedup; `host_unavailable` is answered both before a send and in flight. The rest where §N left a gap or the code differed; the task review (three rounds) found the re-check, publication, input and backlog cases, each reproduced. Rejected: at-least-once input for an unknown outcome (the executor's first choice); an endpoint gate on the peer's capability.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller), from M3a2's report.

- Decision: A forward's connection tries `::1` once when `127.0.0.1` is refused (`ECONNREFUSED` only; loopback only, no lookup; the stream's answer the second attempt's). The link rule is unchanged: a `[::1]` URL is loopback but not forwardable.
  Rationale: a forward's main use is a dev server, and Vite and other Node 17+ servers bound to `localhost` often listen on `::1` alone. Rejected: a tracker row (the common case would fail).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, the architect's ruling, recorded by O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M3b as built (main; `02f1f1289`..`aacf3c462`, fix waves `fc8602352`, `b536181b8`; class 1a green, run 41304072283). (1) The composition root is `src/main/app.ts`, not `index.ts` (the Touches were wrong). (2) The router answers `pty.hostState` for an RL2 machine through its link (`ready` or `starting` only), and `pty.openLink` and `layout.openExternal` in main for any machine. (3) A stream opened with no channel waits for the next one; a forward's waiting connections are bounded (32) and expire (15 s). (4) An attach's ring bytes are refunded in the adapter; `behind` reopens after the renderer's ack, on a backoff that starts over only on progress (a pending attach too). (5) The latest geometry per pty is sent on every stream. (6) Exit status is read by reopening from the end; `unknown` runs a fresh attach. (7) A forward's grant is kept after close; `removed`, `grade` and `not-offered` close the listener, as does the link's permanent end (`RemoteLink.ended()`); a machine-ended connection is drained, then destroyed (10 s). (8) The loopback rule is two: `isLoopbackLink` (never opened on this Mac for a remote session: `localhost`, `*.localhost`, 127/8, `0.0.0.0`, `[::1]`, `[::]`, and any IPv4-mapped address of these) and `loopbackPort` (forwardable: http(s) to `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` or `0.0.0.0`, the written port or the scheme's default).
  Rationale: where the spec left a gap or named a seam the code lacked; the task review (two rounds) found the backoff, loopback, waiting, link-end, half-open and resize cases, each reproduced.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller), from M3b's report.

- Decision: Master merged at #431 (O12 M11; `84af0afea`); M3 takes `WIRE_MINOR` 17 (re-taken at landing if O5's landing 2 lands first). With the endpoint ranking a session's spawner as owner, M4's main-side listing wait is dead and removed (`604f2fd05`): a session spawned here follows the ordinary record retries, then "can't place"; `e2e/remote.spec.ts`' first spawn step is #431's.
  Rationale: the architect's instruction at #431's landing.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: The in-app browser is this Mac's own surface, so a remote session's Browser tool holds local tabs: the router answers the Browser tool's user-facing commands here for any machine, an explicit allow-list (a `browser.*` command added later is refused for another machine until it is listed on purpose, a test pinning one unlisted command refused), never the agent-control ones (a remote session's agent does not drive this Mac's browser; M5's screencast is that surface); the tabs' events reach the renderer under the session's machine; a forwarded tab keeps its `{machine, port}` and, after an app restart, opens its forward again at its first activation (never at launch, which would prompt a non-owner).
  Rationale: M3c found the gap: `browser.*` is graded `client` and the router refused it for another machine, so the forward opened and its tab never appeared; M3's Touches had not named main's browser. Rejected: grading the Browser tool's commands for a remote machine (the tabs aren't the machine's); restoring the forward eagerly at launch.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller); accepted by the architect with the allow-list condition (it fits O17's D8: a remote agent's browser is O17's screencast).

- Decision: The in-app browser for a remote session as built (M3b; `dd5fc88ab`, `f6231ccec`, fix waves `49d772dd0`, `d5696b198`, `1972a3b2c`; class 1a green, run 41311896147). (1) The allow-list is `browser.tabs.list`, `.open` and `.select`, the only Browser-tool commands whose input names a session (the rest name a tab, which the client already sends here); a test pins an unlisted command refused. (2) Main's tab store keys another machine's session by its address and this Mac's by its bare id (local tabs and `tabs.json` unchanged); every exit (answers, events at any depth, the comment pick) says the bare id from that session's machine, an event naming two machines' sessions is not sent, and a test enumerates every session-naming browser event against the registry.
