(3) `BrowserTab.forward {machine, port, localPort}` is main's own, read from the listener that owns the URL's port at an open, at every committed navigation and at a child window's creation (a child on its opener's port taking the opener's forward once the listener is gone); a restored forwarded tab opens its forward again at its first load, a re-forward a newer navigation overtook touching nothing.
  Rationale: the task review (three rounds) found the identity, stale re-forward, provenance and nested-event cases, each reproduced.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller), from M3b's report.

- Decision: M3c as built (the renderer; `514691b33`, `d4d91bfa8`, `d19d47a9b`, `70482d857`, fix wave `e1d07a46b`, `ee3007f50`; class 1a green, run 41309336865). (1) `pty.listFor` is asked only for a restored remote tab (a new tab's label has no shell yet), kept per session until a spawn or an exit there; each tab adopts only the shell under its own label. (2) The re-adoption waits are bounded above main's whole budget through `src/shared/machine/remote-pending.ts` (`REMOTE_ACT_PENDING_MS`, `REMOTE_ATTACH_PENDING_MS`, which main reads too), plus a 5 s margin. (3) Closing a session's tab or pane runs nothing on its terminals; closing the Terminal tool's tab is the kill, as locally; a remote terminal disposed without the owner's close (its session removed or archived) is released, not killed, since a kill there is an act a non-owner would confirm with nothing asking for it. (4) "[output lost]" is drawn where a frame's offset passes the entry's end; a terminal lost to this app says so in its machine's name; `hostReady` reads the terminal's own machine (closing the tracker row at `docs/tech-debt-tracker.md:442`). (5) The forward mark reads `tab.forward` while the page is on its listener; a refused forward replaces the pane with its page and Try again; no `remote.forward.close` (a new local port would break the page's history and redirects).
  Rationale: where the spec left a gap; the task review's attach-budget finding; the reading of "closing the tab kills nothing" as the session's tab (the Terminal tool's own close is the explicit kill, as locally).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller), from M3c's report.

- Decision: Master merged at #428 (O5's landing 2; `58464c075`); M3 takes `WIRE_MINOR` 18. `[::]` and `[::1]` links are forwardable (`cdb2ed61a`), the forward still connecting 127.0.0.1 first, then `::1`; IPv4-mapped forms stay loopback and not forwardable; `e2e:remote`'s scene runs python's plain `http.server` and opens its `[::]` link (`80c121d0f`).
  Rationale: the architect's go: with the `::1` fallback in, the most common way to start a server printed a link that opened nowhere.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, the architect's ruling, recorded by O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M3's milestone review fixes (reviewer-high, two rounds; `441631eb5`, `992562204`, `199a6cd79`). (1) A forward's listener is bound only once its act is granted, so a second open during a pending act shares its outcome and no connection arrives before the grant. (2) The pty `streamOpen` gains `inputSent` (the device's end of input sent in the epoch, new in RL2 1.3): an endpoint with no record of the epoch (a `mawsd` restart, the pty host and its ledger kept) starts there, the ambiguous span dropped, so input stays at most once across a restart; main gives dropped held input's offsets back (`inputNext = inputSent`), so every open holds `inputSent >= inputFrom`. (3) `mawsd` relays its pty host's state to the endpoint (one line in `src/mawsd/daemon.ts`, beyond the plan's "the inner-name guard" alone): a rise in its restarts or `crashed` closes the host's pty streams `exit`, a reopen while it is `crashed` is refused `unknown` (final: the terminal ends lost in one round trip), and each `ready` closes only the streams whose pty is gone.
  Rationale: the review's five findings, each reproduced or traced; at most once is the input ruling's. Rejected: dedup state kept in the pty host (a change to its table the plan excludes); a new epoch at each re-bind (the endpoint never writes past `inputSent`, so the same epoch is safe).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M6's execution (2026-11-01), on `p5-o7-m6` cut from master a0a7f81a2 (M3 landed, PR #433), in four tasks: (a) the endpoint: the `each-session` target rule, §L's M6 rows (`attention.list`, `session.search`), `calls.ts`' per-session filter at the registry's grade, and the `attention` feed in `plane-feeds.ts` with its membership recomputed at every record change and new head (`subscriptions.ts`' `not-offered` answer for it retired); (b) main: `remote-attention.ts` (one feed per `up` link), `remote.attention`, the notifier's remote counts and notifications, the badge, search to each `up` machine; (c) the renderer: a listed row counts, the attention store's remote partition greyed while its machine is down, the search surface merged per machine; (d) `e2e:remote`'s M6 scenes. (a) and (b) are built side by side against §R, which org-protocol already carries (M4a2); an endpoint older than M6 answers the feed `not-offered`, which main takes as no feed on that channel. `WIRE_MINOR` moves once, to master's plus one (19, re-taken at landing) (corrected below: M6 takes no bump). Gates: class 1a on each task's head; the PR head `--gates all --class1a pool --runner macos`, no lane; flaky passes listed even when a gate is green.
  Rationale: M6 spans the endpoint, main, the renderer and the scene, consumed in that order (M2's to M3's split); the M3 retrospective's lesson, cross-layer transitions first, goes into every brief and the milestone review's.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M6 takes no `WIRE_MINOR` bump and no `RL2_PROTOCOL` bump (correcting the entry above). `remote.attention` is main-to-renderer and off the wire manifest (§K; `wire.ts`' header), and M6a changes no manifest entry, so `wire.test.ts` refuses a bump ("the wire has not changed"); `remote.attention` gains `greyed: boolean` (§K). RL2's rule moves the minor at a landing that adds a message shape or capability (§M), and M6 adds neither: `sub {feed: 'attention'}` and `MachineFeedSubMessage` are RL2 1.2's (M4a2) under `rl2.feeds`, and an endpoint without the feed answers `ended not-offered`, which the device reads per channel. M6's gates pass `--peer` a master commit at wire 3.17 (789e2b7d8), since `ci-gate` refuses equal minors. (Reversed by the milestone review: M6 takes `WIRE_MINOR` 19, below.)
  Rationale: M6b's finding, accepted by the architect.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, the architect's ruling, recorded by O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M6a as built (the endpoint). (1) The attention feed is its own file, `src/endpoint/attention-feed.ts` (`AttentionFeeds`), owned and routed to by `PlaneFeeds`, not a branch of `plane-feeds.ts`. (2) The cut places each session by its spine record alone (`session.get`, then each `session.changed`), never `targets.ts`' engine-host read, which would wake a parked host at every record change; a record that names no engine session is nobody's to rank (fail closed). (3) The member's ranks come from `Authorizer.ranks` as a synchronous predicate, so the feed and `calls.ts` check and send in one step (M3's rule); it fails closed once the head moves. (4) `Plane.accessChanged?()` runs at the end of every `Subscriptions.recheck`; the target-less feed recomputes there, admitted once and never re-authorized. (5) The feed sends one output at a time and works out the next only after it leaves; at each change of rights a queued output that no longer stands is taken back (a change unqueued, a snapshot cut again, a part-sent snapshot ends `behind`), and the bound is checked after every enqueue; `ev` `seq`s may skip and still rise within the epoch, and a slow device gets each session's latest items. (6) A placement or `attention.list` read that fails ends the feed `unavailable`. (7) A web-cards device is refused the feed (`class`), as it is `attention.list`. (8) The `ev` body is exactly `{appSessionId, items}`.
  Rationale: The task's build and its two review waves; the queue's take-back is M3's lesson (check and send in one step) applied to output held across a change of rights.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: The feed's membership as §R reads it: "left the set" sends `items: []` only for a session whose items the device holds, and "joined" sends items only for a session that has some; a viewable session with no items needs no message either way.
  Rationale: The device's state is the same; §R's wording describes the effect, not a message per membership change.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: An `each-session` call or the attention feed keeps a session only when the caller holds the grade on every placement it saw of it: the read's answer and each placement heard while the read was out; any disagreement leaves the session out (the call's `unavailable` for it). A move after the read whose notification is still in the index's coalescing tick stays unseen until it is published: the bound every event-driven revocation at the endpoint has, within §R. Rejected: a spine placement revision with uncoalesced endpoint invalidation (a services change, not M6's; a services row if a later review wants it closed).
  Rationale: The index can be ahead of what was heard (Surprises), so neither placement is known to be the newer; failing closed needs no ordering. `unavailable` is the non-disclosing answer (P5 Decision Log 186).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, the architect's ruling, recorded by O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M6b as built (main). (1) While a machine's feed follows (open, or opening again), it alone says its sessions' attention: their streams emit none, and a page's `session.get` is told the feed's cache. (2) A feed that ends `not-offered` (or `grade`, `removed`, `closed`), or whose channel sends an omitted (`too-large`) message, gives the machine back to its sessions' streams for that channel, with no reopen until a new channel; `remote.attention` then carries the streams' items (`streamItems`), live while up and greyed at down, never the ended feed's empty cache. (3) The subscription is kept across `down` and `up` (the link's `SubTable` resends it; every opening is a snapshot); a replaced link carries its items greyed until the new snapshot. (4) `remote.attention {machine, items, greyed}`; `greyed` is main's word, since items stay greyed after `up` until the next snapshot. (5) A page's announcement (`attention.pendingNavigate`) is told every following machine's state again. (6) A remote item's notification is silent only in a machine's first snapshot that arrives within 30 s of the notifier's start by this Mac's clock; never by comparing another machine's clock. (7) Remote notifications offer no reply and no Allow once (an answer is an act with step-up, through the pane); the click opens the session. (8) Search to an endpoint without the row (`not-offered`) is answered `host_unavailable`, the one omission signal. (9) The badge counts the feeds' items, cut by the endpoint, of machines not greyed.
  Rationale: The task's build and its three fix waves (the feed and the stream overwrote each other; the startup rule compared foreign clocks; an ended feed's cache erased the streams' items at the next announcement and at down; an omission left main following with nothing).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M6c as built (the renderer). (1) Live and greyed rows are two selectors (`selectNeedsYou`, `selectGreyedNeedsYou`); every count, ⌘⇧J and the Overview read the live one, only Needs you draws the greyed. (2) Greying is main's `greyed`, tracked by partition membership, never the roster; a greyed row reads "machine offline", or "reconnecting" after `up` before the snapshot, its title `text-fg-muted`. (3) A machine's first `remote.attention` makes the feed its partition's owner for the page's life: a socket-style `attention.list` still out for it writes nothing. (4) A listed session opens through M2's opener (`openListed`) from a click, ⌘⇧J, a notification, the toast and the palette; ⌘-click and split keep `openSession`. (5) The Overview's Needs-you group cards a listed session with no record from its listing and item (no directory or digest invented). (6) Conversation search is the ⌘K palette's "Conversations" scope, hits grouped under each machine (or "Sessions" while only this Mac has hits); only `host_unavailable` is left out, any other failure shown with its machine's name. (7) `remote.attention` is subscribed in `connectShell`, mounted before `connectAttention`'s announcement.
  Rationale: The task's build and its review wave (a late list undid the feed; the Overview missed what the toolbar counted, and M6's opening line puts the Overview across machines).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M6d as built (`e2e:remote`'s M6 steps, at the end of the direct launch). The waiting item is a permission on a session B started that A never opened (its own fixture journal), so the listed row stands in with no tab and no record; Needs you and the badge (`app.dock`) show it, ⌘⇧J opens it, the answer clears both. Search proves a transcript-only hit under B, B's own `session.search` the readiness wait. B's daemon stopped greys the item (`data-greyed`, "machine offline") and the badge drops. Every wait reads published state.
  Rationale: The strictest reading of "with B's tab closed" (no subscription, no record); a body hit, since a replayed transcript's first prompt is also its title.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M6 takes `WIRE_MINOR` 19 (master's plus one, re-taken at landing), reversing the no-bump entry above. `session.search`'s input gains `sessions?: AppSessionId[]` (at most 10,000; `SESSION_SEARCH_LIMIT` shared), which the store applies in SQL before its limit; the endpoint passes the sessions the caller holds `view` on, from its ranks and `session.list`'s records, and answers `unavailable` when it cannot read them; the all-placements cut stays the authorization, the list deciding only which rows are searched. A full raw page the cut still took hits from answers `incomplete: true` (more than 10,000 viewable sessions, a move during the call). No version check guards the field: the endpoint and the services it dispatches to always come from one `mawsd` build (`src/mawsd/endpoint-seam.ts`, `spine-seam.ts`, `src/services/front.ts`), so no skew exists. The landing gate's cross-build takes the default peer (master at 3.18).
  Rationale: The no-bump call was made before the milestone review found the cap before the cut hiding a member's hits (Surprises); M6 exists to make search span machines for whoever may see them. Rejected: answering `incomplete` alone (truthful, but the member's hit stays unfound).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, the architect's ruling, recorded by O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M6's milestone review fixes, after the entries above (reviewer-high, three rounds; `5d4b154b3`, `e3b8e083b`, `a41374542`, `13649a97f`, `aa83d70f9`, `827a6645d`). (1) The search's narrowing read is one more placement the call saw: `session.list`'s placement of each session joins `session.get`'s and every heard one in the send-time cut, and the heard set is registered at the call's first read, so a move during `session.list` counts too. (2) One predicate, `RemoteAttention.fed(machine)`, says who speaks for a machine's sessions: the feed once its snapshot has come on the current opening, else their streams. The streams' suppression, a page's replay, the badge and the notifier's items, `sayAgain` and the grey at down all read it, so the renderer and the dock never count different states; a returning feed keeps the streams' items (greyed while down) until its snapshot. (3) An ended feed never publishes its emptied cache over the streams' items. Known limits, in the PR: with no feed, a fallback item counts only while a tab holds its session (a machine without a feed did so before M6); a feed that reopens on a new channel shows its last cache until its new snapshot.
  Rationale: Each earlier round's fix opened the next round's defect where two layers read one state through different predicates (a placement read and not carried; a feed open and a feed fed); the rule that held: every reader of a state reads it through one predicate, and every read a decision rests on reaches the decision.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller), the last round narrow at the architect's direction.

- Decision: M5a's refresh (2026-11-01), re-pinned against master 2d27995f6 (M6 landed, PR #439; P5 Decision Log 198), after the re-plan written at a0a7f81a2. Every name M5a consumes was checked on the base and stands (`library.entry`, `LibraryRowSchema`'s `links` and `sessions`, `COMMAND_BUDGETS_MS`, `json-bytes.ts`' `toWire` and `fromWire`, the `Reads` class's `record(target, launchedBy)` in `authorize.ts`, `recordsMoved`, `machineOwnerMembers`, `HostOpInputSchema`, `MachineOpInputSchema`, `BY_EFFECT`, `CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, `remote-files-read.ts`, `BROWSER_TOOL_HERE_FOR_ANY_MACHINE`, `session.capabilities` and `session.capabilitiesChanged`); what M6 landed that M5a builds on: (1) the `artifacts` feed is a plane of its own beside `attention-feed.ts`, owned and routed to by `PlaneFeeds`, its cache dropped through `Plane.accessChanged?()` (called at every record change and new head) as well as at the events the rule names; (2) `library.list`'s per-row filter takes `calls.ts`' `each-session` shape (the rows' sessions out, the kept rows back, the cut made in one synchronous step at send time through `Authorizer.ranks`), and every placement a decision reads reaches it (M6's all-placements rule: a row is kept only when the member holds `view` at every placement read for its linked sessions); (3) `Subscription.ev` substitutes `{omitted: 'too-large'}` for a message over the limit, so the device reads an omitted `artifacts` event as a lost feed (M6's rule) rather than a delivered one; (4) `WIRE_MINOR` is 19 on master and O16 lands next at 20, so M5a builds at master's plus one and re-takes it at landing; `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` is 3, M5a's 4.
  Rationale: The spec's refresh rule for a section written before the code it consumes had landed; M6's milestone review taught that two readers of one state through different predicates is where the defects were, so M5a's feed and filter reuse M6's mechanisms rather than parallel ones.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M5a's execution (2026-11-01), on `p5-o7-m5a` cut from master 2d27995f6, in five tasks, each reviewed before the next consumes it: (a1) the machine's services: `host.artifactOp` (`src/engine-host/ops.ts`, `src/shared/ipc/schema/host.ts`; below), the claims' `target` in both ledgers (`ops.ts`, `src/services/machine-ops.ts`) with `fromWire` before their own validation, `json-bytes.ts` exporting its codec, `library.read`'s `export` form (`src/services/library/files.ts`), `artifacts.copyAsset`'s by-effect grade, the one `WIRE_MINOR` bump (master's plus one, re-taken at landing); (a2) the protocol and the endpoint, built beside a1 against §O's names: the `artifact` target rule and `ArtifactRanks` (`src/endpoint/artifacts.ts`, `targets.ts`, `authorize.ts`, `by-effect.ts`), M5a's offer rows, an engine-host act with no session (`actions.ts`), the artifact binding (`queries.ts`), `library.list`'s per-row filter (`calls.ts`), the bytes codec on calls and acts, the `artifacts` feed and `session.capabilitiesChanged` on `checkout`, `rl2.artifacts`, `rl2.bytes`, `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` 4, the harness's fake engine host rows; (b) main: per-command budgets on the link, `ArtifactMachines`, `remote-artifacts.ts` (the third act shape, the table, the capabilities substitute, `remote.openCopy`, `library.saveAs`), the device's bytes codec, the router's allow-list; (c) the renderer: the machine on every artifact-keyed invoke, the library's machine menu, open-a-copy and Reveal greyed; (d) `e2e:remote`'s M5a scenes. Gates: class 1a on each task's head; the PR head `--gates all --class1a pool --runner macos`, no lane, with `e2e:cross-build` against the default peer. Local runs are focused files only.
  Rationale: The milestone spans the services, the endpoint, main, the renderer and the scene, consumed in that order (M3's and M6's split); a1 and a2 share only §O's names and are built side by side.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: An artifact write is `host.artifactOp`, a new engine-host command carrying the claim with no `context` and served by `host.op`'s ledger, and not `host.op` with its `context` made optional (correcting M5's paragraph, §L's row and §O). The re-plan called the optional `context` additive; OX1 classes a property made optional as breaking and `classifyWireChange` refuses it at a minor (`pnpm wire:manifest`: "breaking change … `commands.host.op.input.properties.context`: it takes a major"). An older engine host answers `unknown_command`, mapped to `refused unavailable` for this command alone. `host.op` and `HostOpInputSchema` are unchanged.
  Rationale: The architect's ruling. The option that lost, amending OX1 so that a required input field made optional is additive (sound: an old receiver refuses an omitted field as it refuses a new optional one), lost on two counts: the invariant that `host.op` always carries a session context stays in the types, so no existing session-scoped handler can run with no session through a missed check, and the no-session act has its own entry authorized by the claim's `target` (A12's linked-sessions rule); and the old-host fallback is unambiguous, `unknown_command` meaning only an older host where `invalid_input` would also mean our own malformed request (more so after O16, when a newer `mawsd` talks to an older engine host across a handover). The input-direction rule goes to the PR's Residue as a proposal for P5's contracts, taken up if a second no-context case arrives.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, the architect's ruling, recorded by O7's executor (the controller).

- Decision: M5a1 as built (`1b23d6e85`, `18f709d7d`, fix wave `6372d80f1`; class 1a green, run 41367084145). (1) `host.artifactOp` runs an explicit list, `ARTIFACT_OP_COMMANDS` beside its schema, refusing any other name `unavailable` before admission (the task review showed a schema-key test let `session.launch {spec}` and `fs.sessionText {sessionId}` through). (2) Every ledger built on `OpLedger` (the engine host's, the services', the pty host's, the endpoint's) writes a result in `json-bytes`' tagged form and decodes it at reopen, so a reseal or a query after a restart answers the bytes the first answer carried; claims and actions are stored as sent. (3) `host.op` and `machine.op` decode a tagged input on a copy before validating, as `host.artifactOp` does. (4) `library.read {export}` reads link-free through the files service's own `linkFree` option (the path its own real path), the artifacts root resolved first. - Decision: M5a's known limits, accepted by the architect on 2026-11-01 and 2026-11-02: (1) opaque session ids inside CRDT and attribution bytes cross as they are, so a member can tell that one hidden session edited two artifacts, never which session; (2) the `WITHHELD_SESSION` sentinel names that a session was withheld; (3) a `linked` event's timing can show that a session the member doesn't view touched an artifact; (4) a text file that isn't valid UTF-8 can't be copied from another machine (refused, never lossy) until a raw-bytes read encoding exists (Residue). A remote html artifact's copy is its one stored file, which is the whole page by design (`src/shared/maws/tools.ts`: "an `html` page is its one file"), so it is not a limit.
  Rationale: (1) to (3) are what the projection can't reach without rewriting stored bytes or delaying events, weighed against the store's format and the feed's latency; (4) needs a wire encoding of its own.

- Decision: M5b's refresh (2026-11-02), re-pinned against master 95050efd1 (M5a landed, PR #441; P5 Decision Log 199; `WIRE_MINOR` 20, RL2 1.4). Every name M5b consumes was checked on the base and stands: M3's `src/endpoint/streams.ts` (`MAX_STREAMS`, the act path) and `authorize.ts`'s `#stream`; `STREAM_REFUSALS` (`apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts`, no `read-only` yet); `EndpointServices.documents` (`open`, `close`, `frame`, `onClosed {host | reset | upstream}`) and `onFrame`'s engine-host frames, and `artifacts.fetch {url, method, range?}`; `serveArtifacts(session, content, first?, endpoint?)` (`src/render/scheme.ts`) and main's chain in `src/main/artifacts/index.ts`; `openDocument` and `DOC_PORT_CHANNEL` (`src/main/artifacts/open.ts`); `artifacts.open`'s `readOnly` (in the schema since M5a); `artifacts.downloadAsset` (main); `remote.session`; `src/mawsd/engine-requests.ts`'s needs; `ArtifactMachines` and the renderer's table (M5a). Nothing names `rl2.read-open`, `browser.screencast` or `rl2.browser-screencast` yet. M5a's lessons bind M5b's read open: a read stream's decision and its open are one synchronous step, asked again after any wait (`stands()`); a stream's re-authorization reads the one predicate the call rows read (the `artifact` rule through `ArtifactRanks`, the projection's ranks); nothing a stream sends names a session the member doesn't view, beyond known limit 1 (opaque ids inside CRDT bytes).

Then reviewer-high on the milestone, cross-layer first. RL2 to master's minor plus one at landing (1.5 today); no manifest change.
  Rationale: The endpoint's protocol and read path are what every other task consumes; b2 and b3 touch disjoint trees, so they run side by side with each executor committing only its own paths.
