- Decision: M5b1 as built (`897b5a5fb`, `7a184395b`; class 1a green, run 41397661087). (1) A read open from a device whose caps list no `rl2.read-open` is refused `malformed` on that stream alone, the channel kept. (2) The validator ties `op` to the kind: `pty` and `tcp` name one, `http` and `document` never do. (3) The validator checks the URL only as a string of 1 to 4096 characters; the endpoint parses it with the scheme's own parser, so a bad URL is `streamRefused malformed` and the channel lives. (4) A stream request whose name is a stream row goes through `#command`'s steps; any other keeps M3's `#stream`. (5) A read open's refusal reaches the device as `grade`, but for `malformed`, `unavailable` and `read-only`, so no other refusal word says more of the target than a refusal of a call does. (6) Step 8 for a read open checks presence (`removed`) alone; nothing a stream row admits needs a step-up. (7) `Stream` moved to `src/endpoint/stream-base.ts` (a circular import from `streams.ts`); the planes plug in through `ReadOpening` and `ReadPlane` there. (8) Re-authorization re-decides only the streams whose decision no longer `stands()`; its triggers fire after each drop of the artifact cache: a record change, every `artifacts.changed` but `updated`, and `session.changed`. (9) The body is read only when nothing is queued and credit remains, so at most one 64 KiB chunk lies past the credit; HEAD, 304, 404, 416 and any answer with no body close right after `streamOpened`; a body that fails mid-read closes `unavailable`. (10) `artifact-projection.test.ts` skips stream rows; the read open's tests are `test/endpoint/read-open.test.ts`.
  Rationale: The task's own choices within M5b's rows, confirmed by the architect on 2026-11-02; each keeps the channel alive for what is one stream's fault and says no more in a refusal than a call's refusal says.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5b2 as built (`1d8168ba9`, `a78df40ec`; b1's review fixes `a6f5274c8`, `c4698d7a7`, `4cb8a09f1`, `7b574a250`; `23bbcfa74`). (1) A document read open reads `artifacts.get` first, so a reopen after a park wakes the host instead of answering `unavailable` for ever; a failed read is `unavailable`, a null artifact `grade`, a kind that isn't a document `malformed`. (2) `documents.open` is synchronous and runs inside the plane's `publish`, in the step the decision stands; the seam's one addition is `Stream.recheck`. (3) The device's bytes are refunded as they are taken in, so a frame larger than the 256 KiB credit can't deadlock; during a re-check they wait unrefunded. (4) A `view` open forwards only step 1 and awareness; past 64 KiB dropped it closes `read-only` (§N's bound). (5) Up to 8 MiB of host frames may wait past the device's credit; past that the stream closes `behind`. (6) A provisional frame's session id is rewritten to `withheld-session` (`WITHHELD_SESSION`) for every member, owners included; dropping the field was the alternative, rejected because a fixed sentinel keeps the frame's shape the same for every member and the provider's parser unchanged (the architect, 2026-11-02, under P5 Decision Log 199's projection: a device never learns a session id through a path that didn't rank it on that session). (7) The park need is held by `daemon.ts`'s `documents.open` until the channel ends (`engine-requests.ts`' hold). (8) A certificate change under the same head holds and re-checks every stream, act streams included, the artifact placements dropped first. b1's review: a read stream's bytes go only on a decision that stands, asked in the shared release path (`Stream.next()`), a moved one held and re-decided, never dropped; an http source chunk is queued a piece at a time within the credit; a body is read one ahead, so one that spends the credit exactly still closes `closed`. Frames to the device: u32be length-prefixed, 1 byte to 8 MiB, offsets contiguous from 0; `streamOpened {id, credit: 256 KiB}`; close words `host`, `reset`, `upstream`, `removed`, `read-only`, `behind`, `malformed`, `unavailable`.
  Rationale: The task and b1's review; (3) and (5) set what a slow or hostile device costs and are put to the milestone review.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5b2's review fixes (`779ffe65a`, `d80905730`, `55fd902da`; the E3 hold `3f085bc0e`). (1) A stream's re-check takes a token never reused (a counter), and a grant whose decision moved meanwhile is decided again: the shared re-check's token had been derived from a map entry a completion deletes, so a stale check could reuse it and unhold an act stream after a newer withdrawal. (2) The last park-need hold's release says the need before the requests in flight fall to zero, so the idle window starts at the release instead of a park armed on the old clock (tested on the real `DaemonClocks` and in `daemon-seam.test.ts`). (3) The document backlog's bound counts the 4-byte prefix, so a frame of the declared maximum passes. (4) An `http` read of a dataset snapshot (`assets/query-<uuid>.json`) holds it (`artifacts.holdAssets`, the E3 hold main's local Download takes) from before the fetch until the stream ends, released once on every path, so a retention sweep between the content lookup and the scheme's open serves the bytes instead of a 404; a hold that can't be taken lets the load go on, logged. Nothing of O5's content path changed (`httpPlane` takes `ctx.dispatch`).
  Rationale: b2's task review; (4) the architect's ruling of 2026-11-02 (a pane load and a Download cross the same window; the fix is in M5b's own plane).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5b3 as built (`0c6081cdf`, `f904860c3`, `fb05733d7`; the media fix `151780119`; review fixes `999635d97`, `91f9cd47a`, `5d80039dc`; class 1a green, runs 41401705079, 41403281231, 41405098524). (1) The read form is `op` absent, sent only on a channel whose peer lists `rl2.streams` and `rl2.read-open`, judged on the channel it goes out on. (2) The device's `StreamTable` enforces the credit it grants on every stream kind: a chunk past the outstanding grant closes the stream `malformed` and delivers nothing. (3) The scheme's chain is the presenter's embeds, then the remote responder by placement, then the local path, then the remote responder by referrer when the local path answers not found; the responder never writes `ArtifactMachines`. This reverses the task's first build, which recorded a referrer-routed id once its machine answered 2xx: a remote machine could then claim a local artifact's id and steer this Mac's later loads of it to itself (the task review's poisoning case); the table's one writer stays the adapter's link answers, and a nested embed falls to this Mac's store, which the stock CSP and frame policy allow for. (4) A link holds at most 8 `http` bodies, each slot kept until its body is read out, cancelled, failed or cut (not only while its stream is open), at most 1 MiB of credit each, plus Electron's read-ahead (about 64 KiB a body). (5) While a request waits for a slot, a body its frame hasn't read for 2 s (not one waiting on the machine) gives way, the oldest first, ending normally with what came (its unread bytes dropped), so a paused media element asks again by `Range` (the architect's ruling: eight paused panes would otherwise starve a machine's every load to a 504). (6) The 30 s bound runs from the request's arrival, queue time included; a carrier loss before the answer reopens within it (GET and HEAD only); after the answer it fails the body. (7) Refusals as statuses: `grade` 403, `malformed` 400, `unavailable` 502, `busy` 503, `not-offered` 501, the link's end 503 offline, each body naming the machine; `Content-Length` held both ways, offsets contiguous. (8) `artifacts.downloadAsset` routes by its target, fetches through the responder's own path whole before the dialog, the machine's 404 as `invalid_input notFound`.
  Rationale: The task, its review and the architect's rulings; one writer of which machine an artifact is on is M5a's rule, and no answer from another machine may decide where this Mac's own artifacts are read.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5b4 as built (`23a2ff0c0`, `314b21210`, `76db99763`, `105023fc1`; class 1a green, run 41406319447). (1) A remote document's reopen is the renderer's: any end of its stream closes the window's port, and the ArtifactHost's one reopen path brings a fresh port and a fresh stream, the provider's step 2 carrying what was never acked; the bridge does not reopen behind the same port, since the provider counts its frames per connection and the host acks per channel (a new endpoint channel behind an old port would mis-ack and lose edits). (2) The machine is the invoke's target, recorded in `ArtifactMachines` by the route before the bridge opens (the adapter's writer). (3) `artifacts.open` answers only once its stream is open, `edit` first, `view` after `grade` or `read-only`, so no port is posted without a stream behind it; the open is bounded at 30 s and answers offline at once while the link is down. (4) A `view` bridge forwards only step 1 and awareness itself, so no leftover local edit trips the endpoint's 64 KiB `read-only` close. (5) The machine's frames are refunded as they are taken in, after they are posted. (6) Refusals for good (`grade` on both modes, a preflight `grade` or `no-target`: not found; `malformed`, `not-offered`: invalid) reach the editor as `DocumentState.refused`, shown in place of the editor; every other word retries with the ArtifactHost's backoff, held at its 3 s step while the link isn't known down. A local document deleted while open now says it could not be opened instead of reconnecting for ever. (7) A remote replica's liveness follows `remote.session` for its machine, not `engine.hostState`, and it reads no `artifacts.status` of this Mac. (8) The length-prefix framing is one module both ends speak (`src/artifacts/provider/frame-stream.ts`).
  Rationale: The task; a reopen through the path that already carries unacked edits is the one that loses nothing.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5b4's review fixes (`7ab56b299`, `ff51fe6d2`, `3509112fe`, `559a27919`, `9fbe53547`, `b2e85c72f`; class 1a green, run 41410745886). Every open now ends. (1) Main posts no port for a stream that opened and already ended (it answers the transient refusal); the window refuses a port whose close came while it waited to be taken; a replica whose port gives no first frame within 10 s (`FIRST_FRAME_MS`, local documents too: a false trigger costs one reopen) takes the reopen path. (2) `ArtifactHost.open` takes its owner's `AbortSignal` (`ArtifactOpenOptions.signal`): an abort before the first sync closes the replica and rejects the open, so a tab closed while its document keeps failing to open stops its retries; the document and deck tabs, the suggestions doc, the presenter's route, the layouts menu and the design-system page own theirs. (3) The bridge has a stopped state: an open waiting on its preflight, and any later, is answered at once when the app stops. (4) One deadline from the open's entry bounds the preflight and both stream attempts. (5) A design system's terminal refusal replaces its page with the refusal, as a document's and a deck's do, so no field takes an edit that can't be saved. Known and accepted: the credit bounds what is in flight on the link and the one partial frame main assembles; the window's `MessagePort` queue is unbounded, as the local port model's is, so a blocked page can hold complete frames there.
  Rationale: b4's task review: the reopen design kept unacked edits, but the open's own lifetime had paths that never ended or ended silently.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5b5 as built (`8b3427e22`, `c44d9640a`, `19e7bede0`, `1c0434739`; class 1a 41411891878 and hosted e2e 41411880055 green on b2e85c72f, tree f2aef9f9, no flaky lines). `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts` gains five cases through the real link, endpoint, `routeLaneB`, main's document bridge and main's chain (`startArtifacts`, Electron mocked): an html page from a scratch store served to a `Request` with the machine's headers and bridge script, a `Range` 206, this Mac's store never asked; eight unread bodies holding every slot and a ninth served once one idled 2 s, which resumes by `Range`; two devices' edits converging with every edit acked; an edit whose ack was lost and one typed while the link was down each landing once after the reopen; a `view` member's open `readOnly`, its typing never reaching the store (its presence does), and the share's withdrawal closing its stream `removed` with the owner's untouched. Each was shown to rest on the product by a mutation (the chain without the remote responder, `IDLE_MS` at 60 s, the view filter removed at both ends, `accessMoved` removed). The machine's store in the harness is a small Yjs host in the test file (the editor's `MemoryDocumentHost` is outside the node typecheck project); B's real store is the e2e's. `e2e:remote` gains M5b's scene: B's session creates a document at A's ask; its page shows in A's frame with what B's `library.read` answers; text typed in A's editor is in B's own export of it. Not proven in e2e (the harness or unit suites carry them): a remote PDF, image or media render, `artifacts.downloadAsset`, a carrier drop mid-edit, the `view` member's read-only editor. Discovered: `FakePtyHost.serve` replaced `services.onFrame` outright, silently cutting the fake documents' frames in any harness with a pty host; it now passes other hosts' frames through.
  Rationale: The milestone's cross-layer proof; the discovery is the M5a lesson again (a fake that wires differently from the real host).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5b's milestone review (doperpowers:reviewer-high on 95050efd1..2c35aee7f) and its fix waves. Round one found six Important, all fixed: (1) the media give-way cut a download's body and download.ts saved it short: only a frame's load gives way (`fetch` is never cut), and a download's file is checked against `Content-Length` before any dialog (`0ac62abc7`, `5c589f0e0`); (2) frames were capped at the 8 MiB high water while the engine host admits payloads to 64 MiB, so a big document opened locally and reopened for ever remotely: `DOCUMENT_FRAME_MAX` (`src/artifacts/provider/frame-stream.ts`) is the host's 64 MiB on both ends, the queue threshold separate (`80ee99a9b`, `0d3d1b094`); (3) a store read-only because a newer build wrote it read writable to a `manage` member: an `edit` open reads `artifacts.status` beside `artifacts.get` and is refused `read-only`, so the bridge reopens `view` (`b9e85382f`; a status that can't be read is taken as writable, as the local editor takes it, the host still refusing the write); (4) main's document bridge held the window's frames without bound: the host socket's rule, more than 8 MiB queued closes `behind` and the reopen carries the unacked edits (`f046cda1a`); (5) a link's document streams could retain about 512 MiB: a 64 MiB per-link budget of retained document bytes (frames queued for the device and frames being assembled from it), checked as the host checks its socket, before a stream's next frame against the rest of the link without that stream's own frame in flight, so one frame up to 64 MiB always goes on a stream that holds nothing else however busy the link (`9706b5e0f`; the architect's choice between that rule and a budget above one frame); (6) the assembler kept every chunk and shifted them off: one buffer per frame, grown by doubling to its declared length (`74b58c929`). The narrow re-check of that wave found three more, the last fixed: the budget counted unsent bytes while `subarray()` kept each frame's whole buffer, so stalled tails across streams retained far past it: each host frame counts whole until its last byte goes (`deb7fa0a9`); a large frame queued behind a small head closed `behind` before it reached the device: the one admitted frame over 8 MiB is left out of its own stream's backlog and budget checks wherever it sits, counted in full by every other stream (`27edfaf5b`); main's bridge closed `behind` while a reopen's step 2 over 8 MiB drained, which on a slow link reopens for ever: the frame draining is exempt and the 8 MiB applies behind it (`be32d7723`). Each with a test red before it; the last three verified by the controller, the loop stopped there at the architect's word.
  Rationale: The review's lenses (paths only a fake exercised, decide and effect, receive credit, memory per link, placement, the give-way, every open ending, disclosure, compatibility) found nothing in the first three of its classes and these in memory and lifetime; each bound now matches the engine host's own rule, so a document that opens locally opens remotely.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5b's known limits and residue. Known limits: a download whose machine sends no `Content-Length` can't be proven whole by its length check (the transport still fails a stream that ends abnormally); the window's `MessagePort` queue is unbounded, as the local port model's is; a second oversized host frame while one is held is counted as an ordinary one; worst-case document memory per link is the 64 MiB budget plus one admitted frame per stream holding nothing else. Residue: a certificate change re-checks streams but not feed subscriptions (`[parent-impact]`, for the architect's routing); O5's `engine-requests.ts` `forward` lowers the in-flight count before it reports the need (the architect's, after O16).
  Rationale: What the reviews left that is accepted or belongs elsewhere.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5c's refresh (2026-11-02), re-pinned against master 242375483 (M5b landed, PR #445; P5 Decision Log 202; `WIRE_MINOR` 21, RL2 1.5; O16 landed before it, PR #442, P5 Decision Log 200). Every name M5c consumes was checked on the base and stands: `createClientPort`, `CLIENT_PORT_DEADLINE_MS` and the front's `route(ref)` (`src/mawsd/client-port.ts`, `front.ts`; O16 changed `front.ts` by three lines outside the port); `ListenLoopback` (`src/services/connectors/oauth-flow.ts`, `oauth-callback.ts`); `CONNECT_CALLBACK_TIMEOUT_MS`; the flow's `deps.openBrowser(url, asker)`; `connectors.windowShown`, `connectors.reset`; `exportForLibrary` and the `machine.render` welcome capability's pattern; `BY_EFFECT`; `delivery.deliver`; `MACHINE_ACTS` (`src/main/lane-b/remote.ts`). New, as planned: `MACHINE_CALLS`, `src/endpoint/client-port.ts`, `src/services/connectors/relayed-callback.ts`, `src/main/lane-b/remote-port.ts`. M5a's and M5b's lessons bind M5c: an endpoint decision and its effect in one synchronous step, asked again after every wait (a lease's re-authorization and the feed's forwarding of `connectors.watchUpdate` read the one `artifact` rule); a fake that registers or routes differently from the real host is the first suspect (the port's `route`, the flow's receiver, the broker's refresh loop); every resource a remote peer can hold (a lease, a watch, a callback listener, a pending port request) is bounded and ends on every path. `WIRE_MINOR` at landing is master's plus one, and O17 M7 bumps it too: whichever lands first takes 22, and that landing's `e2e:cross-build` (3.22 against 3.21) is the first crossing of two holder-capable builds (O16 left it unproven, P5 Decision Log 200), to be named in its PR; RL2 to master's minor plus one (1.6, `rl2.client-port`).
  Rationale: The spec's refresh rule; the architect's routing of 2026-11-02 (M5c before M7, M7's acceptance covering M5c).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5c's execution (2026-11-02), on `p5-o7-m5c` cut from master 242375483, in five tasks, each reviewed before the next consumes it: (c1) the contracts and the endpoint: RL2's `client` and `clientAnswer` behind `rl2.client-port` (RL2 1.6); the registry's grade changes for the twelve commands (`grades/{connectors,delivery}.ts`, `BY_EFFECT`), the `callback` fields, `connectors.callback`, `delivery.send` retagged `machine`, `machine.delivery`, `WIRE_MINOR` and the manifest, pushed as a first contracts commit so c2 can start on it; then the endpoint's relay (`client-port.ts`, `EndpointServices.clientPort.serve`, `src/mawsd/endpoint-seam.ts`, `front.ts`'s `route`, `daemon.ts`'s member), §L's M5c rows, `client.windowShown` as an endpoint call, the leases' re-authorization beside the streams' and the endpoint's own lease close, and the `artifacts` feed carrying the connector and delivery events; (c2) the machine's services: the relayed receiver (`relayed-callback.ts`), the receiver keyed by the asking client and the port in `oauth-flow.ts`, `sign-in.ts` and `index.ts` (busy, late callbacks, sign-ins that can't be relayed), `connectors.callback`'s handler, and `delivery.send` on the machine (export then deliver; the shim behind `machine.delivery`), after c1's contracts commit, beside c1's endpoint work; (c3) main: `remote-port.ts` (the device's port answers and the callback listener's lifetime), `MACHINE_CALLS` and the M5c acts, the window reports per machine and `connectors.reset {machine}`, after c1 and c2; (c4) the renderer: the connectors client, connector owner, delivery run and store per machine, the rebuild of the named machine only, the Connectors sheet and Send to greyed by rights and offer, beside c3; (c5) `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`'s and `e2e:remote`'s M5c scenes, after c3 and c4. Then reviewer-high on the milestone.
  Rationale: The contracts are what every other task consumes; the endpoint's relay and the machine's services touch disjoint trees once the contracts are in, as do main and the renderer.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5c as built (2026-11-02, c1 to c4 on `p5-o7-m5c`, each reviewed clean after its fixes). The endpoint (c1): the relay posts only the port's timed kinds (`openExternal`, `reveal`; every other kind `noClient` at once) and only to a device listing `rl2.client-port`; a lease is kept per channel with a generation, re-decided endpoint-wide at every `ArtifactRanks.onDropped` and once after its open, with at most one decision out per lease, a stale refusal never closing a newer generation; `LEASES_KEPT` 64 a channel, refused `busy` past it; `delivery.send`'s run ids are reserved endpoint-wide, never evicted while their run may live (256 a channel, `busy` past it), so a repeated id can't move `cancel`'s authorization to another artifact; a lease id the channel doesn't hold is refused `grade`; `via.sessionId` is withheld from every remote member's `connectors.call` and `watchUpdate`; `delivery.receipts` fails closed; admin feed events gate on `SessionRanks.holdsRight`; the callback is bounded (64 KiB of bytes, a 1-16 character method, a 4096 character URL). The machine (c2): the arming is taken in the command's synchronous step as its last check (the flow's `listen` fills it later), so a second flow on one `(client, port)` is refused `busy` at once (`why: 'busy'`, the shipped schema's field), without cancelling the first; the asker's end ends a remote flow not yet past its callback (the row freed at once, not at 120 s); relayed callbacks are routed by the armed state, and any other is 404; `delivery.send` on the machine reads its target once, before the export, reached through the root's bus (`artifacts.export`), with the e2e export hold moved to `@shared/delivery/export-hold`. Main (c3): `RemoteLink.onClient` answers on the asking channel (`noClient` without a listener) and `RemoteLink.onChannel('current' | 'lost')` beside `onState`; lease commands and the M5c acts go `oneChannel` and are never resent across channels, a sent one settling `unknown` with `channel-lost` at the loss; from a channel's loss until main's local `connectors.reset {machine}` (emitted at every channel, the first included) lease commands are refused `host_unavailable`; a window report that can't be taken refuses the lease command after two tries; the callback listener lives 331 s for a Connect and 311 s for a sign-in from acceptance (the Consent paragraph amended), ends with the channel that accepted the act, and ends at `connectors.serversChanged.ended {sourceHandle}` (the owner's new `actionEnded`, counted only once the act went out) only after a relayed request settled its flow; 16 listeners a machine and 4 relays a listener; only `content-type` and `cache-control` of the machine's answer are written back (127.0.0.1's cookies are shared by every loopback port), a header Node won't write making the answer 502; the `delivery.send` shim is a machine handler on the daemon shape behind `machine.delivery`; the modules are `remote-port.ts`, `remote-windows.ts` and `remote-connectors.ts`. The renderer (c4): O4's in-flight rule kept (main's gate yields `host_unavailable`); one owned lease a surface, closed best-effort before the rebuild's open, and `onCurrent` sends in the step that checks it; a reset counts only as main's word for that machine; a lease's machine read once at its open; unknown rights grey nothing; the offer learned from `not-offered` per `(machine, command)` and forgotten at that machine's next `artifacts.resync`; `LivePause` `notOffered` reads "Connectors on <machine> need its app"; Send to greys every action on the machine's own connections; the Connectors sheet asks once an opening with a Show button before any `machine-admin` read, so no Touch ID until Show; Customize's Configured list takes ready sessions from this Mac alone; sign-in bookkeeping keys include the machine. The moved proofs: `windows.test.ts`'s per-machine cases are in `src/main/lane-b/remote-connectors.test.ts` (the reporter is the adapter's, not the router's seam), and the two-channels case is in `src/services/connectors/relayed-connect.test.ts`.
  Rationale: Each closes a gap the spec left open with the real host's own rule (bounds, the in-flight rule, the decision discipline), and the alternatives that lost are in the task reports: an eviction that reopens a run id, a listener timed from a bind this side can't see, every header written, a reset only after a loss, greying until rights are known.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller), with the architect's rulings on c4's three concerns and Customize.
- Decision: M5c5 as built (2026-11-02): `e2e:remote`'s M5c scenes with B's observer a real engine started with no message, through an e2e-only `live` entry in `src/services/sessions/replay-seam.ts` behind exactly the seam's existing gate (`MAWS_E2E`), so a source verifies as it does in production; B has a scratch `HOME` under the scene's root; the OAuth fixture's `oauthPhase` option is retired (the scene enters the phase after the observer's pid is dead, connectors-oauth's discipline); Connect runs from a small HTML page declaring `secure`; the owner's delivery now says each live session whose report was not observed. Rejected: a replayed observer (route D answers `unidentified` for it, correctly).
  Rationale: The architect's ruling on c5's first handback; a fixture that can't verify a source proves nothing about the route.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: A remote dashboard's snapshot holds cross RL2 (2026-11-02, the architect's ruling (a) on c5's handback). `artifacts.holdAssets` (`view` on the artifact) and `artifacts.releaseAssets` (`list` on the machine, the asking channel's own hold alone, so a member who lost `view` can still let go) are main's link's calls behind `rl2.asset-holds`, inside M5c's RL2 bump, typed to the two names (`MainOverLink`, `callMainOverLink`) and never a window's; `holdDatasets`, `releaseDatasets` and `captureDatasetState` stay unoffered. The endpoint keeps them per channel under the leases' decision discipline (one decision per channel and artifact), released at the channel's end, at a lost `view` and at its stop, forgotten when the engine host's link drops, bounded at 256 a channel (`busy`). Main: the router answers `artifacts.holdDatasets` here for any machine (beside `downloadAsset`), since the renderer sends every artifact-keyed invoke to the artifact's machine, and holds on the router's `ctx.target`, not `ArtifactMachines.machineOf` (main's table may lack the id at a first hold); each remote hold records its channel, an ended channel is never asked, and a hold answered after its channel was replaced is refused. A peer without the capability draws unheld, said once per machine. The renderer's re-hold after a relink needed no change (the feed's resync at every opening invalidates that machine's holds). Rejected: (b), an unheld handle for every remote artifact, which would weaken E3 R1b's guarantee that a shown snapshot stays readable.
  Rationale: The guarantee holds across machines at the cost of one table per channel.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller), on the architect's ruling.
- Decision: A watch whose last refresh found no route wakes at a grant of its tool (2026-11-02, `e4633f360`, `src/services/connectors/broker.ts`), from this window or announced by the store from another; no retry and no longer wait. This fixed `e2e/dashboard-live-data.spec.ts:526`'s flaky line (see Surprises).
  Rationale: The architect counted the flaky line red until explained; the cause was a product race, so the fix is the product's.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5c's known limits and residue. A carrier drop mid-consent loses that consent (a new channel's ref reaches no flow; routing by ref is the isolation, and the row is freed at once). A lease decision overtaken by a drop spends a retry. A granted dashboard's first rows after a Start can wait up to 30 s when its refresh beat the server's connection (no wire event says an engine's MCP status moved). Send to's turn-end observe skips a session silently when it is not `ready` or `engine.sessions` fails (`owner-delivery.ts` `observe()`; once in e2e:remote's runs before `db71ca566`, never since). B's engine-host restart under a live remote dashboard leaves A an unbacked hold, and the device's artifact lists stale, until the next resync (the feed does not reopen on an upstream reconnect). `LiveQuery.tsx`'s sheet shows no detail line for a `notOffered:` pause. Each Servers read on another machine is a Touch ID act, made only by the owner's Show or Refresh (M5c review 1); the step-up window that would lighten it is a board ticket. The tracker rows are the architect's to write at landing.
  Rationale: What the reviews left that is accepted or belongs elsewhere.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5c review 1's fixes (2026-11-02, on `p5-o7-m5c`, `c09646e11`, `4cd2fea81`, `0116e9cbc`, `5eb1d0a1c`).
  (1) A `delivery.send` is pending on its channel from the step the endpoint receives it until it is admitted or refused (`Leases.pendRun`), counted toward `RUNS_KEPT` with the reserved runs (a second send of an id pending on the channel, or one past the bound, `busy` at once). A `delivery.cancel` is placed on the artifact its channel's pending send names, authorized by its own decision, and handed to the machine only for a run its channel holds reserved. One of a send still being decided waits on it (one per send): handed on in the step the send is admitted, before the send itself (the machine's tombstone then answers the send `cancelled`, nothing published), or refused `no-target` when the send is refused. The gate's act refusals reach the executor (`Executors.actRefused`, as `subRefused`), so every refusal settles a pending send. Rejected: main sequencing a cancel after its send's admission (one device's rule, not the channel's).
  (2) The endpoint bounds a remote acquisition (`artifacts.holdAssets`, `connectors.open`) at `ACQUIRE_WITHIN_MS` = 8 s from the call's arrival at the gate (authorization counted; `src/endpoint/acquire.ts`). That is `ACQUIRE_MARGIN_MS` = 2 s under the device's call deadline (`REMOTE_TIMING.CALL_TIMEOUT_MS` = 10 s; neither command has a budget, and both are `oneChannel`, sent at once), for the call's way to the endpoint and its answer's way back, as `discovery-wake.ts` splits the same deadline. Past it the call answers `timeout`; a hold the engine host makes after is released and a lease the machine opens after is closed at once under the channel's ref; an acquisition whose bound passed in authorization is never asked; a late one counts toward the channel's bound until its answer. A test pins the bound plus margin under the device's deadline for both names. Rejected: the device keeping a timed-out call's correlation to release a late success (two acquisition paths in main, and a channel replaced meanwhile to rule out).
  (3) Another machine's Connectors rows, once the owner's Show read them, are never read again unasked: every change that reads this Mac's rows (main's word, the page's tools, the grants, an owner action's end, the sheet ungreyed) marks them stale ("Connectors on <machine> may be out of date") beside a "Refresh" button (aria "Refresh the connectors on <machine>"), which reads them, a confirmation each; a failed read offers Refresh too. The grade stays `machine-admin`. Rejected: a step-up window per open sheet (one Touch ID covering background re-reads while it stays open), a new time-boxed admin grant and so a security design change that is the owner's call (filed by the architect as a board ticket).
  Rationale: The review's three Important findings, each closed where its resource or its rule lives, under M5c's lessons (a decision and its effect in one step, one predicate per state, every peer-held resource bounded and ended on every path).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller), on the architect's acceptance of the routes.
- Decision: M5c review 2, the narrow pass's fix (2026-11-03, `1300f2d1d`, class 1a 41559318685): `within` (`src/endpoint/acquire.ts`) judges the acquisition bound by the clock at the answer's arrival as well as by its timer. An answer or failure at or past `by` is LATE and goes to `late` once, a failure as `late(null)` as the timer's path does, because a stalled event loop runs the answer's continuation ahead of the overdue timer and would otherwise answer an id the device has already forgotten. The review loop stopped here.
  Rationale: The narrow pass's one Important, in the class the milestone review named (a peer-held resource that one path forgets).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-03, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M5c's master merge (2026-11-03, master 01d4488cf: M7 landed as PR #451 with `rl2.pong-clock` at RL2 1.6, O16's veto fix and gate as PRs #448 and #449, and the index source's scan retry and fs.scan budget as PR #452). `RL2_PROTOCOL` moves to 1.7 for `rl2.client-port` and `rl2.asset-holds` (both lists keep master's order with M5c's two appended after `rl2.pong-clock`); the link's `client` case, `clientAsked` and `dropOneChannelCalls` sit beside M7's `pong` case, `heardPong`, `ping` and `settlePings`, both run at a loss and at the relay-to-direct swap; `WIRE_MINOR` stays M5c's 22 over master's 21 (the manifest regenerated from master's is M5c's, since master changed no wire entry). PR #452 shares `src/mawsd/daemon.ts` with M5c (the engine host's `requestBudgetsMs`), which merged on its own; the endpoint's 8 s acquisition bound is the endpoint's, not the supervisor's budget.
  Rationale: The refresh rule for the second of two landings that each bump the minor (M7's Decision Log: whichever lands second takes master's plus one).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-03, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M7's live runs are booked with the desk track for roughly 2026-11-03 08:00 to 14:00Z (the architect, 2026-11-02, on a day's notice): a fresh acceptance VM reachable over the tailnet from the MacBook, lane B records on both machines (A11), and scratch-profile enrollment codes for `mini-bench` and `vm-bench`. The codes arrive as 0600 files under `~/.local/share/maws-o7-bench/codes/` on the MacBook, never in chat or a transcript; the bench reads them from there and never prints them (no echo, no log line, no argument visible to `ps`). The B4 soak on the MacBook, the mini and dc-linux171-1e68 runs to 2026-11-06T22:18Z and must not be disturbed: the scratch profiles are wholly apart from the owner's MAWS (their own profile directories, sockets and `mawsd`, nothing of the owner's user data read or written). CI-opt's mini window for `e2e:remote-latency` is booked by the architect once M7's build is green; O7 confirms a few hours before the live runs.
  Rationale: M7's preconditions, fixed before it is built so its bench step is planned around them.
- Decision: M7's bench carries each enrollment code over stdin and never at rest on the machine (2026-11-02, on the desk track's conditions relayed by the architect). The codes are minted live in the sitting at the owner's terminal (`deployment admin enroll <name>`, a 900 s TTL, the owner comparing the machine's key and typing y; `admin machine drop <name> --revoke` after), not ahead and not from the app, so this replaces "enrolled through the owner's app" in M7's live bench. The bench runs `ssh <host> '<scratch mawsd> enroll --profile <dir> --label <label> --name <name> --wait 900 --json' < ~/.local/share/maws-o7-bench/codes/<name>`: with no TTY `mawsd enroll` reads stdin to its end (`src/mawsd/cli.ts` `withSecret`), refuses the code as an argument, and logs none of it; the bench prints only the answer's state, name and org, waits out exit 4 by polling `mawsd endpoint status --json` until org-listed or the TTL, and removes the local file after. On the mini the scratch `mawsd` is started by mini-ops (its own `--profile`, `--label` and ports from M7's head) and the one ssh command only talks to its `machine.sock`; the fallback where ssh to the mini is refused is a 0600 copy in a 0700 directory that mini-ops redirects into the same command and deletes. Machine names are the bench's inputs (a name is bound to its ref for the org's life), and each scratch profile is kept until the bench is done. The bench's own session on a scratch machine gets its lane B record from the harness's fake sminos on that machine's RL10 socket (§Q's pattern), which enrolls nowhere (pending the desk track's confirmation). The bench's app is a scratch profile paired with `admin pair --class native-mac` and revoked after (O9 M10's pattern), launched with the mock keychain and with Playwright tracing off for the pairing step. The soak's refusals bind: no change to the production profiles of the MacBook, the mini or dc-linux171-1e68; linux171 not used; no default profile, `dev.maws.mawsd` label, `mawsd install` or `~/.local/bin/mawsd` for a scratch daemon; the bench's Electron runs only while no executor builds on the MacBook. Rejected: scp to the VM as the primary path (a copy at rest for no gain over the pipe); carrying a code through mini-ops' chat (a transcript).
  Rationale: The architect's question of 2026-11-02; the owner's steps and their order went to the architect for booking.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M7's refresh and execution (2026-11-02), on `p5-o7-m7` cut from master 242375483 beside M5c (the architect's ruling of 2026-11-02: the live runs of 2026-11-03 need M7's bench before M5c could land; whichever of the two lands second re-bumps `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` to master's plus one). Every name M7 consumes stands on the base: `pong {t, at}` and its reader (`apps/org-protocol/src/messages.ts`), the endpoint's answer (`src/endpoint/channels.ts`), the link's `ping` at its silence and its `probe` (`src/main/machines/remote/link.ts`), `rl2.read-open` last in both capability lists, `e2e/local-latency.spec.ts`, `test/support/latency-engine-stub.mjs`, `e2e/remote.spec.ts`, `e2e/support/remote-scene.ts` and `fake-sminos.ts`, `MEASURED_E2E` in `.github/workflows/gate.yml`. M7 moves no `WIRE_MINOR` (`pong` is RL2's alone), so it does not race O17 M7 for 22. RL2 at M7 is 1.6 with `rl2.pong-clock`, 1.7 if M5c lands first. What the bench's booking added to M7's touches (the two Decision Log entries above): the live mode's runner, which stages a scratch `mawsd` from M7's head on each machine (its own `--profile`, `--label` and ports; the VM over ssh, the mini by mini-ops under the production `mawsd`'s node runtime), starts the latency stub's session there with the harness's fake sminos on that machine's RL10 socket (§Q's pattern) for its lane B record, enrolls it by piping the code file over ssh into `mawsd enroll` (waiting out exit 4), and tears it down; the bench's app as a scratch profile signed in from a pairing code read from a file, with the mock keychain; machine names, hosts and code paths as inputs. Built as three tasks by one executor, each task-reviewed: (m7-1) `pong`'s `received` and `sent`, `rl2.pong-clock` on both sides, the link's four stamps per probe kept for the bench; (m7-2) the measured group `e2e:remote-latency` (§S) and its `MEASURED_E2E` entry; (m7-3) the live mode (`e2e:remote-latency-live`), the clock offset's interval judgement, and the runner. Then doperpowers:reviewer-medium on the milestone.
  Rationale: The spec's refresh rule; the live runs' date.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: The live runner's transport and environment, on the desk track's answers (2026-11-02). The transport is per host: plain non-TTY `ssh mini '<cmd>'` on the mini, `devcloud vm ssh <vm> -- '<cmd>'` on the VM (devcloud runs `ssh -T` with its own key and host-key alias; never raw ssh to the VM), the code on the command's stdin either way; on the mini that one command runs only the scratch `mawsd`'s enroll against its scratch socket, and an scp to a 0600 file is a fallback behind an explicit option. sminos picks its endpoint from `SMINOS_RL10`, then `$MAWS_USER_DATA/mawsd/endpoint/rl10.json`, then the default profile, so those two variables are set only in the fake sminos's and the scratch `mawsd`'s environments, never for a real sminos (its daemon or any CLI), with a fakes test of both transports and of the runner's own sminos calls running without them. The sitting's order: A11's two spawns first (the VM's is item 10's run), then the pairing and the enrollments; the desk track takes the acceptance VM down after the last item. This supersedes the earlier entry's plain ssh to the VM. Rejected: one ssh shape for both hosts (the VM's host keys are devcloud's).
  Rationale: The desk track's message 399, relayed by the architect.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: The live runner's reach on the shared machines (2026-11-02, the desk track's rulings relayed by the architect). The mini's "one ssh" is the only one carrying a code; the runner's other non-TTY calls there (staging the head, the agent's `prepare` and `serve`, the scratch daemon's status and keys) are allowed under four conditions, each enforced in code with a fakes test: every write is under the scratch profile (normalized paths under an explicit scratch root, symlink components refused), and the bench app's profile is guarded the same way; the production runtime is executed and never written, and no production socket, file, `--dir`, `app.asar` or `--unit` is named; `serve` ends with its ssh and takes the stub and the fake sminos with it, and teardown removes the profile (its head and tarball with it) only after a process check that fails closed; nothing reaches the mini in 06:45 to 07:00Z, a mandatory window enforced as a deadline on every call and on a running `serve`, with no reboot, logout or routing change. The owner's rule beneath it: no `claude` runs over ssh on the mini (the runner starts none, and no real sminos). The mini's scratch `mawsd` runs under the app's own binary as production does (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, `--disable-sigusr1`), from a head bundle built on the MacBook and copied as a tarball over the same ssh after two preflights (the binary's `process.versions.electron` equal to the bundle's, and free space); node-pty's `spawn-helper` is made executable after extraction. The VM's scratch `mawsd` is built on the VM from a `git archive` streamed over `devcloud vm ssh` (no git or GitHub credentials there), `pnpm install` with the store, caches, HOME and TMPDIR under the profile and `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD=1`, then `mawsd:pack`; this `prepare-vm` step runs before the sitting, since it loads the VM whose production `mawsd` serves it. Live resume is measured without touching routing: the bench seam closes the link's current channel, the agent writes a turn while it is down, and resume runs from the link's channel `up` again to that turn's first event, voided when it came back over the other carrier. Rejected: a CI-built Linux pack copied to the VM (the VM has the toolchain; the copy stays an unbuilt option); a network cut for the live resume (it would drop the owner's real sessions in the sitting).
  Rationale: The desk track's rulings and the m7-3 task review; the soak and the shared machines' owners set these bounds.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).
- Decision: M7 as built (2026-11-02, `p5-o7-m7`). (1) `pong` carries the endpoint's `received` and `sent` on every pong (a reader that doesn't know them passes them by), consumed only from a peer listing `rl2.pong-clock`; the link keeps the last 512 pongs' four stamps, and the bench's clock exchanges go through a separate `ping()` (one exchange per channel and millisecond, at most 16 pending, settled at a carrier swap), never `probe()`, which on the relay re-checks the direct path. (2) The measured group's bounds are per carrier, each from the same run's local number and that carrier's own round trip; the local case has no resume; the local engine start is observed in the page less its largest renderer-to-main hop, so an observation delay only narrows the allowance; each carrier's resume is timed with only its own path restored and voided when the link came back over the other. (3) `e2e:remote-latency-check` runs the measured scene on the hosted Macs with no bound, since a measured group never runs there. (4) The live mode is its own spec file (`e2e/remote-latency-live.spec.ts`, so `captureAllowed`'s `-live` rule keeps main's output out of the evidence); writes and frames are read on the wall clock, as the pong stamps are; samples are signed, their sign judged after the clock interval; the spread is taken over every exchange and voids a run above 5 ms; every bound has its own verdict, `inconclusive` read literally (the interval straddling the bound, or wider than its allowance); a case whose carrier moved while timed is void. (5) Live resume holds the link through the bench seam (a held link adopts no channel and makes no attempt; the hold is lifted in a `finally` and at the seam's disposal), reachable only with `MAWS_E2E=1` in an unpackaged build, as is the relay switch `MAWS_E2E_REMOTE_CARRIER`. (6) The runner keeps the desk track's conditions in code: every write guarded component by component against symlinks in the same shell step; a deadline on every remote command at both ends (GNU `timeout` on the VM, a perl process-group alarm on the mini) and the mini's quiet window as a hard boundary; the scratch daemon detached by `exec` and stopped only by its verified pid; the scratch hosts ended through their own sockets under the profile, then any process left by pid and start time with its argv naming the profile, never by name; teardown counting only the agent's clean exit and refusing removal while anything under the profile lives; the machine's key shown as soon as it is posted, before the listing's wait; a registered bench app signed in again rather than paired. Rejected: timing the link's probes (they move the carrier); a second test in the measured file for the live mode; a network cut for live resume (it would drop the owner's sessions); signalling hosts by name (the mini's production hosts run the same binary).
  Rationale: The executor's report and the three task reviews and the milestone review that shaped it; the desk track's conditions.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-02, O7's executor (the controller).

The residue for P5's Decision Log. Each item is decided where it binds (M1 unless marked), with the alternative it rejected:

1. M1 defines `OrgSession` and holds `signedOut()` in production, plus a development source behind `MAWS_DEV_ORG` in unpackaged builds; O8's sign-in implements it. Rejected: O7 building sign-in, so that two children build it.
2. One RL2 link per machine the held org state lists, eager. Rejected: links on first use.
3. `RemoteLink` extends O6's `MachineLink` with `call`, `act`, `subscribe`, `caps` and `probe`. It is reached only through `Machines.remote(ref)`, and `boundary.test.ts` refuses any other channel opener in main or the renderer. Rejected: a raw message pipe, which would have each consumer reimplement RL4's lifecycle; and a second link for O9 (P5 Decision Log 45).
4. Direct first: tailnet `ws://` addresses raced within 1 s, under a per-machine bucket that mirrors the listener's handshake limit; the relay otherwise; direct rechecked every 60 s and on every probe; a switch after the new handshake completes. Rejected: dialling any signed address; dialling until `busy`; overlapping two channels during a switch.
5. `up` survives a reconnect within 2 s, and is not a new generation. Rejected: `down` at every loss.
6. The only permanent verdict is a sealed `close {reason: 'removed'}`. Every handshake failure is transient and local to its carrier, because `protocol`, `unknown-machine`, `revoked` and `contested` come from an unauthenticated `h2`, and the endpoint's own refusals arrive as a bare carrier close. The link's own view drops a machine (the roster) or the device (sign-out). Rejected: stopping the link on handshake verdicts, which would let a hostile relay keep the device off the direct carrier too.
6a. A trust change closes the channel: a retired machine key or a changed device identity bumps a trust generation, re-authenticates at once, and discards older attempts. Rejected: relying on the endpoint to close channels under a retired key, which a holder of a compromised key would not do.
7. Acts: sent or resealed only within 60 s of creation; acked-without-done and older acts queried; a step-up act never resealed; `if-asked` signs after a `step-up` refusal under the same opId; a refusal (an `ack` or a refused `done`) is final only for an act that is not ambiguous, and an ambiguous act is settled by a query alone; pending acts in memory. Rejected: resealing up to `OP_HORIZON`; query-only; reading a reseal's refusal as "nothing ran"; persisting pending acts.
8. Subscriptions: the cursor moves at each delivered `ev`, and to a snapshot's position only once its last page arrives; subscription ids are stable across channels; credit is returned on delivery. Rejected: one subscription id per channel, which would break lane A reads after a reconnect.
9. Endpoint records are fetched at each attempt, verified, and cached in `<userData>/endpoint-records.json`, so a hub outage across a relaunch still dials direct. Rejected: memory only.
10. On the router path, `list`- and `view`-graded commands go as calls, and refusals map to `internal` with `issues.refusal`. Rejected: a new `IpcError` code, which is breaking.
11. `MachineState` gains optional `carrier` and `refusal`, main-to-renderer only. Rejected: a separate `machines.transport` event.
12. Both carriers use `ws`. Rejected: Node's global `WebSocket`.
13. (M3, direction) Port forwarding is graded `shell`. Rejected: per-session `view`.
14. (Schedule) M3 needs O2 and O10 M10, not O3. M2 needs O3 only for app-less machines.
15. (M1, as built) Every production carrier reports its own end as `carrier`, so only a sealed `close` names the machine's word. Rejected: an additive change to org-protocol's `Channel` that marks sealed closes, unnecessary once no transport word reaches it.
16. (M1, as built) A step-up act's send window runs from its signature. Rejected: from `opCreated`, which a slow confirmation would spend.
17. (M1, as built) An ack timeout on a live channel queries the act; a reseal follows only a lost channel. Rejected: resealing on the ack timeout.
18. (M1, as built) The direct listener's handshake limits are a `StartOptions` field of the endpoint's test harness, and the link's `DIRECT_BUCKET` an override, for suites on loopback. Rejected: a test-only bypass in the listener.
19. (M1, as built) The link's suites may import `src/endpoint` and `test/endpoint` (an eslint exception for those test files only). Rejected: copying the harness under `test/machines`.
20. (Shape) O7 stays one child; M2, then M4 and M3 in either order (M2 carries what both stand on), then M6 (after M4's feeds), M5 with O4 and O5, M7 last. Rejected: children.
21. (M2) Subscriptions follow the tabs with a 10 s linger; the pane runs over a `session.get` record emitted as `session.changed`, one row per address; lane B placement at the endpoint reads the spine's record first (a change to O10's `targets.ts`); an idle session's stream, ended `unavailable`, is subscribed again with backoff; a `ClientRef` per channel at the endpoint. Rejected: a watch command; a parallel state; placement through the engine host alone; an endpoint change to hold a stream with no host row.
22. (M2) `rl2.executor.machine` and `RL2_PROTOCOL.minor` 1 in M2, for `session.acquire`; two act shapes, a `machine` act with neither `session` nor `context`. Rejected: waiting for M4; a context on `machine` acts.
23. (M2) The step-up sheet is macOS's own, before Touch ID, for acts and for O8's state signing (P5 Decision Log 126); a harness confirmation under `MAWS_E2E` for the scenes, the signature real. Rejected: an in-app sheet.
24. (M2) The gap bar from `remote.gap` and `historyUnavailable` (closes P5 Decision Log 77's tracker row). Rejected: a toast.
25. (M2) A send answers at its outcome, or `timeout` at 60 s with the pending words while an ambiguous act keeps querying; a never-sent act ends `refused offline` at the hold; sends stay available while the link is down or the session idle; an image over the channel's limit is refused locally; drafts and read marks of a remote session stay in memory until O8 M6. Rejected: `queued` at 5 s; "not sent" for an ambiguous act; closing sends while down; `session.drafts.*` and `session.update` rows.
26. (M3) Streams as JSON kinds behind `rl2.streams`, opened under an act's opId, re-authorized with subscriptions; `pty.spawn`, `attach`, `kill` and `pty.listFor` as acts under the pty host's own ledger, an attach forced `credit: false` and answered as `{info, ringStart, nextOffset}`, the ring read by `pty.replay` from the viewer's own offset; `forward.open` as the endpoint's own graded action, the forward kept per device for `RESUME_MS` across a channel loss; input offsets under a per-run `inputEpoch`; a binary kind the named follow-up. Rejected: `pty.write` as acts; `shell` calls; a `machine` forward; `pty.list` as an act; the attach's checkpoint-bound replay; a forward per channel.
27. (M3) A remote terminal's checkpoint lives in this app's memory (A9). Rejected: persisting it in the profile.
28. (M4) The `checkout` target rule over `call.session` (`rl2.call-session`), carried from the page in the preload envelope and `MainContext.session`, bounded by the repository each session root belongs to; the `checkout` feed (`rl2.feeds`) carrying `git.changed`, `files.changed` and `scm.remote.output`; at a reconnect git watches replayed and files watches invalidated. Rejected: an `appSessionId` on git's inputs; the directory as the boundary (git answers for the repository); polling; replaying `files.watch`.
29. (M4) Uploads in 2 MiB parts of `files.upload`, each a `machine.op` claim. Rejected: streams; one message.
30. (M4) `session.spawn` (new; `session.launch` is the engine host's) with DX6's fields, `permission_mode` required, step 6's spawn guard over every `spawn` row, `launchedBy` with the sealed action for O12 carried through `HandlerContext.op` and `machine.op`'s `action`, and a spawn with attachments sending no brief and one message after its uploads. Rejected: three acts; an optional mode; retagging `session.launch`; a brief naming files not yet uploaded.
31. (M4) `session.projects.list` offered at `list` for the machine menu; `fs.agentEvents` by its session. Rejected: a dedicated checkouts read; `fs.readSpill` under the checkout rule.
32. (M6) The `each-session` rule (admitted at `list`, filtered at `view` in `calls.ts`) and the `attention` feed, a session that leaves the member's set emptied and one that joins sent its items at once, the feed open with an empty set; a listed row counts in Needs you; this Mac notifies for remote items (A8). Rejected: `cards` per listed session; a filtered `session.list`; ending the feed at an empty set.
33. (Scenes) `e2e:remote` over a real `mawsd`, a local org, the harness's fake sminos on RL10, `MAWS_DEV_ORG` with `directMap` read once per launch, `MAWS_E2E_STEP_UP=accept` for the spawn; `e2e:remote-latency` measured on the mini; the live bench against scratch machines with the clock offset from `pong`'s `received` and `sent` (`rl2.pong-clock`), judged over its interval. Rejected: a fake endpoint; a records seam; bounds in `e2e:remote`; half a round trip as the offset; a spread as the offset's accuracy.
34. (Rows) `fs.holders`, `fs.readSpill`, `session.deletions.list`, `git.worktree.add/remove/deleteBranch`, `engine.models`, `pty.list`, `pty.replay`, the drafts and `session.update` are not offered; `files.reveal` and `openExternal` wait for M5.

P5 findings for its writer (things in P5 that are wrong or incomplete):

- **F1.** The edge P5 Decision Log 45 gives M1 (O6, O10 and O11 alone) is right for building, but it omits sign-in. No production device opens a channel before O8's sign-in (Decision Log 38), so O7 M1's value and O9's remote lane A both wait on O8, and O8 is blocked by O12 for its other parts. See A1.
- **F2.** The in-app proof of any remote surface, a real endpoint inside `mawsd`, waits for O10 M10, which waits for O2. The Ordering table's O7 row doesn't show O10 M10 for M2 and M3.
- **F3.** "O7's log views" (Decision Logs 51, 75, 80; O14 §D) have no place in O7's purpose or in §5, and O8's project channel reads the same log plane. See A3.
- **F4.** O7's Edges say "blocked by O3" for everything after M1, but remote terminals and port forwarding (M3) need only O2 and O10 M10, and lane B's conversation (M2) needs O3 only on a machine with no app.
- **F5.** No child owns the rows of another machine's sessions in the sidebar. The renderer snapshots a machine through main-owned reads (`session.list`, `session.projects.list`, `session.digests`, `attention.list`), which the endpoint refuses to offer because they would list private sessions (O10 M5). Rows for an RL2 machine have to come from RL6 listings: OX8's from the hub, or `endpoint.listings`. O9 needs that source first (remote lane A), O8 owns OX8's listings, and O7 needs it for lane B. Recommendation: O8's first milestone, with sign-in, since it already fetches the listings; O7 and O9 consume it.
- **F6.** P5's list of O7's offer-table rows (lane B spawn, remote terminals and documents, `git.*`, `fs.holders`) omits rows that acceptance 4 needs: `files.*` and `scm.*` (open and edit a file), uploads (§5's drag and drop), port forwarding (§5), and the lane B panel reads (`session.getWorkspaceDiff` and the like).
- **F7.** O7's Contracts line omits OX4 (the client port across the channel, for client-bound tools and consent) and OX5 (a remote viewer's interest), both of which O7's later milestones use.
- **F8.** P5 acceptance 7's resume within 1 s after wake is carried by no child's acceptance. The link carries the app's half (M1's probes; M7 measures it), O9 lane A's subscriptions ride the same link, and O13 carries the phone's.
- **F9.** P5's O7 contracts line names `fs.holders` among O7's rows; its only caller is the take-over flow, which is disabled remotely (O9's residue 13), so O7 offers it to no reader. `[parent-impact]`: strike it from the line, or name the reader.
- **F10.** P5's O7 "Edges" and the Ordering table's O7 row still read the pre-O3 edges ("M1 now"; M2 "also by O3 for a machine with no app"; M4 "O3-E"); every one of those has landed. The edges now: M2 by nothing unlanded; M3 and M4 by M2; M6 by M2 (and O8 M1, landed); M5 by M2, O4 and O5; M7 by M2, M3, M4 and M6, with A11's precondition. The O10 status line "M11 next" is stale too (O10's document ticks M11 on 2026-10-29).
- **F11.** RL §6.3 lists a machine's rights as `spawn` and `machine-admin`; OX9, O10 and O8 add `shell`, which O7's terminals and forwards are graded by. The remote layer's text is a step behind P5's; for its writer.
- **F12.** The endpoint authorizes a lane B session only from sminos's member record (O12 item 7's observer); nothing in P5's O7 or O12 sections says O7's live proof depends on it (A11).
- **F13.** The `client` requests O7's §I promised (endpoint-to-device) are M5's and depend on a seam member of O3X6 (the front routing a non-socket client's port requests through the endpoint seam), which O3's recomposition should name as O7 M5's with the desk track.
- **F14.** O10's lane B placement (`src/endpoint/targets.ts`) reads the engine host's `session.status`, which knows only the sessions of the host's own run, so after O3-D a released or parked session is `unknown` to a remote caller; M2 changes it to read the spine's record first. For the desk track's ledger (O10's authorization placement) rather than P5's text, since O10's design says "mapped to the engine session through `session.status`".
- **F15.** The spawn's requester-as-owner (C4's remainder, "O7 M4's with O12") needs O12's observer to read `launchedBy` on the session record and to claim the member with the sealed action as `ownersProof`; nothing in P5's O12 section names it. `[parent-impact]`: a line for O12.

**M5's re-plan (2026-11-01).** The decisions of the re-plan, each where it binds in the M5 section, listed here for P5's writer with the alternative that lost. Read at master a0a7f81a2 (O7 M3 landed; `WIRE_MINOR` 18, RL2 1.3), against O4's and O5's landings and O17's R1.

- Decision: M5 is three landings, M5a (the rule and the reads), M5b (the read open, pages and documents), M5c (O4's surfaces: leases, delivery, consent, the client port), each its own branch and PR at reviewer-high, in that order; O17 M9 waits on M5b alone, and M5c may be scheduled after M7.
  Rationale: the re-check found the advisory section wrong in executor, target and scope, and each landing stands on a different contract (a target rule, §N's admission, O4's grades); landing them separately gets O17 its carriage and the owner the artifact pane soonest. Rejected: one landing (three reviews' worth of authorization change in one PR, and O17 M9 waiting on the consent relay it never needed); a fourth child (M5 shares O7's adapter, scene and renderer dimension, the re-plan's own reason for one child).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner.

- Decision: The `artifact` target rule: an artifact's grade is the machine owners' `manage`, else the highest rank over the sessions it is linked to (`library.entry`'s links; a library row's own `sessions` for the listing's filter), an unlinked artifact the machine owners' alone, each linked session placed through `laneB` alone and its record read through `Reads.record(target, launchedBy)` so O12 M11's bridge applies to artifacts (the desk track's conditions, 2026-11-01), cached per channel and artifact and dropped at a record change, a relink, a `caps` refresh and a new head (A12, answered by the owner as recommended on 2026-11-01; the linking residual accepted until the multi-person coordination work); `copyAsset`, which names two artifacts, is declared `{floor: 'manage', byEffect: true}` and graded on `to` with `view` on `from` by effect, and a copy between machines is refused by the adapter.
  Rationale: an artifact names no session, its links are many to many, and viewing a linked session already shows it; the machine-owner short cut is what the machine's own app does and keeps a listing from costing a placement per row. Rejected: `createdBy` alone (edited-into and seeded artifacts unreadable); `machine-admin` for orphans (a read there is `act-required`, and every act prompts); refusing orphans (the owner's own imports would not open remotely; A12's alternative).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner.

- Decision: Artifact writes cross as engine-host acts with no session, `host.op`'s `context` made optional and `actions.ts`'s engine-host branch skipping the status check for an `artifact`-targeted row; an older engine host's `invalid_input` (its runtime's validation of the old schema, not a ledger refusal) is answered `refused unavailable`. Bytes in a call's input or output and an act's args or result cross in `json-bytes`' tagged form behind `rl2.bytes`, encoded before sealing and decoded after verification.
  Rationale (the bytes): RL2 writes plain JSON, four artifact commands carry `Uint8Array`s, and the machine-local socket already has the one tagged form; the tagged value is a JSON value, so the sealed action, the fingerprint, the op's `JsonValueSchema` input and the ledgers' JSONL all take it unchanged, and the one decode sits where the handler runs. Rejected: base64 fields added to each schema (M4's part form, once per command; four more wire changes for one problem); decoding at the endpoint before the op (the op's input schema and the canonical form both refuse a typed array); refusing the four remotely (no remote asset upload and no version history). The binding: the claim's `target: 'artifact:<id>'` makes an artifact act's query durable across a restart. Rejected: a channel-local map of lease and run ids (gone with the channel, so a query after a restart could not be re-authorized).
  Rationale: the writes are engine-host commands keyed by `artifactId`, `host.op` is their ledger, and nothing else can claim an engine-host command. Rejected: `machine`-owned wrappers in the spine (a dozen new commands for a store that is the engine host's); `machine.op` (claims `machine`-owned commands only, by design); session-bound writes (an artifact belongs to no one session).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner.

- Decision: `artifacts.changed` and `library.changed` cross on a machine-wide `artifacts` feed behind `rl2.artifacts`, filtered per artifact by the rule; `session.capabilitiesChanged` rides the `checkout` feed; `session.capabilities` is a `view` call with the adapter's substitute for a peer that lacks the row and for a link down.
  Rationale: both artifact events are client events today and no plane carries them; the checkout feed is the session's machine-event feed already; a reader must always hold a reading. Rejected: per-artifact subscriptions (the cap of 64); polling; a feed of its own for one per-session event.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner.

- Decision: The link gives each call the command's budget from `COMMAND_BUDGETS_MS` (injected; plus one second), the socket link the same, closing the tracker row of 2026-10-31.
  Rationale: `artifacts.export` on a cold machine runs well past 10 s and the endpoint times no call; the table is the one the local machine binding already uses. Rejected: raising the default for every call (a dead read would wait two minutes); a deadline on the wire (the endpoint doesn't time calls).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner.

- Decision: An artifact's machine is a property recorded beside its id (main's `ArtifactMachines`, the renderer's twin), filled from every artifact-keyed request and answered row and carried with every row, tab and invoke; the id stays the key and the scheme's origin; the Library tool lists one machine at a time from a machine menu. `[parent-impact]`: an exception to OX2's "stores key by `{machine, id}`", kept to its intent (the machine travels with the artifact everywhere; only the key is the id); accepted by the architect, 2026-11-01, P5 Decision Log 195, OX2 amended; the alternative was address-keyed artifact stores, tabs and layout entries and a scheme URL carrying the machine.
  Rationale: artifact ids are UUIDs and the origin is the id, so a machine in the URL would change every remote page's origin and every consumer's parse for a key that is unique already. Rejected: the machine in the URL's host or userinfo; address-keyed artifact stores in the renderer.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner.

- Decision: "Open externally" on a remote file, library item or export opens a copy through main's `remote.openCopy` over the link's paged reads (`files.read`, `library.read` with a new `export` form), into the temporary directory, 64 MiB at most; Reveal stays greyed; `library.saveAs` is answered here for any machine and pages the export the same way; the five path commands of the port are not offered (A10, the owner's answer, built as recommended).
  Rationale: this app already knows the path and the session, and the port's `{path}` request would only tell it again, two round trips later; an export is a file under the artifact's directory, so `library.read` with the delivery's admission reaches it at `view`. Rejected: offering the path commands and relaying `{path}` port requests; an own-file remove command (a wire name for one tidy-up: a remote Save As exports without `ownFile` instead).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner.

- Decision: Acceptance 6's lists are RL7's two, the device's `caps` (`link.rights`) and the session's capabilities as two records (M2's control overlay and O5's `session.capabilities`); M5 defines no capability entries for O4's surfaces: they light from the member's rights and the row's offer.
  Rationale: O4 shipped no entries, and what varies for its surfaces is the member's rights and the machine's offer, which the two lists carry; an entry that is always `available: true` on a post-O4 machine would say nothing. Rejected: entries on the machine; a fourth list.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner.

- Decision: The read open (R1): `OfferRow.kind` gains `stream`; a `streamOpen` with no `op` is authorized by `authorize.ts`'s eight steps with the open's fields as the input, at the row's grade (`artifacts.fetch` at `view`, `documents.open` at `view` or `manage` by effect), rechecked at a `caps` refresh, a record change and a relink; the act path stays for `pty`, `tcp` and any open naming `op`; `browser.screencast` and `rl2.browser-screencast` are reserved by name for O17 M9; the constraint "bytes as streams opened under an act" is amended to admit a stream row where neither run-once nor step-up is due.
  Rationale: the act path exists for run-once and step-up, and a page fetch or a Yjs sync needs neither; one admission for M5's two kinds and O17's third. Rejected: a read token minted by a `view` call (R1's own rejection: a second credential); `#stream` admitting session ranks on the act path (an act per page request); an endpoint-own act for documents (no step-up is due at `manage`).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner.

- Decision: The `http` stream is one stream per request over `EndpointServices.artifacts.fetch` (status and headers in `streamOpened`, the body under credit, never `behind`), served into `serveArtifacts`'s responder chain by the artifact table and the referrer; the `document` stream is one endpoint document channel per open, the frames length-prefixed both ways, the actor the human's, a `view` open forwarding only step 1 and awareness from the device, reopened by the device with the provider's unacked updates; an open endpoint document channel is a park need.
  Rationale: `artifacts.fetch` is a request-response seam, so a request is the unit; the document channel is O2's seam as it stands, and the Yjs provider already carries unacked edits across a reconnect. Rejected: a multiplexed `http` stream per artifact (a framing over a framing); bytes in a call (the 4 MiB message); the artifact socket as a `tcp` stream (a credential the rule can't read); Yjs updates as acts; a member actor (no such kind, and attribution is O8 M6's).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner.

- Decision: O4's surfaces cross under re-graded floors (A13, A14, answered by the owner as recommended on 2026-11-01; the architect amends OX9's connector and delivery lines at this follow-up's landing): the eight page-lease commands at `view` on the artifact by effect, `connectors.call` and `refresh` `converse`; delivery's reads at `view`, `send` and `cancel` at `manage` on the artifact; credential and grant management stays `machine-admin`; `catalog.*` is not offered; the window report crosses as the endpoint's own `client.windowShown` with one owner per device; a lease is re-authorized with streams (a `caps` refresh, a new head, a record change, a relink) and closed by the endpoint when its member loses `view`, the feed forwarding its updates only while the artifact is viewable; a reconnection rebuilds a machine's leases through `connectors.reset {machine}` after the window report, every reset naming its machine (a local reset must not rebuild a live remote lease, whose watches would run on under forgotten ids); the six lease reads cross as calls by the adapter's `MACHINE_CALLS` (the dispatcher refuses a by-effect grade on its own); `delivery.send` moves to the machine behind `machine.delivery`, main's shim kept for an older daemon.
  Rationale: `stepUpRequired` prompts for every `machine-admin` act, so the surfaces offered as graded would prompt per widget and per status read; the grants are per artifact and per tool, the owner's, so a viewer uses what the owner allowed; the export already runs on the machine and O4's D14 named this step. Rejected: `machine-admin` as is (a prompt per widget); grading `connectors.call` by the tool's mutability (unreadable from the input); forwarding `connectors.windowShown` through a row (a main-only `internal` name through a front that won't refuse it); a remote lease assumed shown (the veto exists to stop hidden dashboards spending quotas); a `delivery.deliver` row with the export here (three acts, two prompts).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner. `[parent-impact]`: OX9's line on connectors and delivery grades, for P5's writer.

- Decision: The client port crosses the channel with its one real caller, the consent relay (A15, answered by the owner as recommended on 2026-11-01): `EndpointServices.clientPort.serve(ref, target)` answering a handle whose `answer` settles through the port's own identity check, `client`/`clientAnswer` behind `rl2.client-port`, `{url}` opened here, `{path}` and `reveal` answered not here; the callback relayed through a loopback listener main binds for the flow's life (the act acknowledges at once; the flow runs to `CONNECT_CALLBACK_TIMEOUT_MS`), its port carried in `callback` on `connect`, `reconnect` and `signIn`, the redirect relayed whole as `connectors.callback {port, method, url}` under the channel's ref, the armed receiver keyed by that ref and the port (ports are unique per device, and the shipped handler settles a flow before it rejects a wrong `state`), the port held on the device and the receiver armed once per `(client, port)` until the flow ends on the machine (a second flow for the port is `busy`; each flow removes only its own registration), and the flow's own reply written to the browser; Drive's login and an engine-held listener stay on the machine (the latter ending its receipt's flow with a `signInChanged` failure); `show_pane` is not carried (A16, the architect's call of 2026-11-01, deferred as board #435).
  Rationale: the machine's services ask the port only for consents and for machine paths, so the relay without the consent relay would have no caller; O4's `listen` seam is the one place the receiver is chosen; nothing records which device sent a turn's input, and the pane event is gated on an app attached at the machine. Rejected: the port relay alone; a device listener bound after registration (the URI is in the registration); page identity on the wire (one owner per device suffices); carrying `show_pane` to every subscribed device (P5 §6 did not ask for it).
  Date/Author: 2026-11-01, O7's M5 re-planner. `[parent-impact]`: P5 §6's `show_pane` rule names a device-of-the-turn no code records; the follow-up needs O16's attachment gate re-homed and a record for it (`host.op`'s ledger has `device`).

The residue of M5's re-plan, continued from item 34:

(M5a) The `artifact` target rule with the machine-owner short cut and the orphan rule (`copyAsset` on `to` with `view` on `from`); artifact writes as session-less engine-host acts; bytes in `json-bytes`' tagged form behind `rl2.bytes`; the `artifacts` feed; per-command call budgets; the artifact table (an OX2 exception, for the architect); open a copy and Save As through paged reads; three lists for acceptance 6. Rejected: `createdBy` alone; `machine-admin` for orphans; `machine`-owned wrappers; per-command base64 fields; per-artifact subscriptions; a machine in the URL; relaying `{path}` port requests.

(M5b) Stream rows and the read open at the row's grade, rechecked at record changes; `http` per request; `document` per open with the human actor and a read-only mode; `browser.screencast` reserved. Rejected: a read token; session ranks on the act path; a multiplexed `http` stream; Yjs updates as acts.
37. (M5c) Re-graded floors for page leases and delivery (A13, A14); leases re-authorized with streams and closed by the endpoint at a lost `view`; `connectors.reset {machine}` at a reconnection; the adapter's `MACHINE_CALLS`; `client.windowShown`; `delivery.send` on the machine behind `machine.delivery`; the consent relay with `callback`, a listener for the flow's life and `connectors.callback` carrying the redirect whole (A15); the client port with it, its seam answering a handle; `show_pane` not carried (A16); `catalog.*`, `delivery.connect` and the path commands not offered. Rejected: `machine-admin` as is; forwarding `connectors.windowShown`; a `delivery.deliver` row; the port relay alone; a listener for the act's life; a normalized callback.

P5 findings of M5's re-plan, continued from F15:

A13 and A14 re-grade twelve commands' floors. `[parent-impact]`: OX9's line, on the owner's answer.

For the desk track's ledger (O10's authorization), not P5's text.
