- Decision: C at the re-check, "Thought for 0s" (2026-11-05, after #480; the owner's MacBook on master 01c0b239c). The owner's session was MAWS-run (lane B, the remote session spawned on the mini), and its live label counted up; three closed thinking cells under "Internal activity" read "Thought for 0s", later ones 5 s, 4 s and 7 s.
  - **The cause, from the fixtures.** On claude-opus-5-5 a thinking block's `content_block_start` comes 0.2 to 2.9 s before its text. The text then arrives as one delta, or a burst of them, just before `content_block_stop`: within 1 to 23 ms in 10 of the 15 opus-5-5 blocks in `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/`. The normaliser emitted nothing at the start, so the row's marker and `at` were its first delta. `derive.ts`'s `completedAt - at` measured the burst, and `duration()` floors that to "0s". The live row appeared only at that delta. Replay was not the cause: RL2 carries B's engine-host `at` unchanged, and disk or reseeded history has no deltas, so it reads "Thought". W1 holds for lane B: the cells had text, since `CellLine` always draws its chevron.
  - **The fix (the architect's ruling).**
    1. The normaliser tells a thinking block's start as an `assistant.block.delta` with `thinkingDelta: ''` at its `content_block_start`. That is the existing shape, so the wire and RL2 are unchanged. A redacted block and every other kind start silently; hook-made and reseeded messages are skipped, as their deltas are. The row is drawn and measured from the block's own start, as the CLI's timer is. A textless block draws a plain live label while it streams and has no row after it closes (W1). The digest reads Thinking from the start.
    2. `thinkingLabel` shows no figure under 1 s ("Thought"), by the turn divider's threshold (`SHORTEST_FIGURE_MS`). This covers peers on older engine hosts and lane A.
  - **Lane A** gets part 2 only. The engine's hook contract hands `content_block_start` to a hook as an opaque engine chunk with no block type, so the sminos bridge first sees a thinking block at its first thinking delta (the desk track's reading). Residue: lane A's live thinking label starts at the bridge's first thinking chunk; it closes when the engine's hook contract types a block's start.
  - **Proof.** `transcript/model/thinking-duration.test.ts` runs the recorded opus-5-5 frames through the normaliser, the reducer and derive. `p4-tool-summaries-thinking-edits` reads "Thought for 2s" (it read 0s). `p4-r7-loss-stages` reads "Thought", with its textless block live from seq 194 and gone at its close. `digest.test.ts`, on the same frames, reads Thinking at seq 194, not at seq 195. `normalizer.test.ts` and `copy.test.ts` hold the shape and the threshold. All were red first. The presentation snapshots and the agents and panel-child event fixtures were regenerated: thinking rows start a few ms earlier, and "Thought for 0s" reads "Thought". reviewer-medium on the range: correct, no material findings.
  - **The re-check** rides on a later swap: the MacBook's and the mini's engine hosts get part 1 only from a new build.
  Rationale: the fixtures' timings; the owner's two screenshots and answers; the architect's ruling.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-05, O7's M10 executor.

- Decision: The live bench's clock void, and the bench fitted to the owner's sheets (2026-11-10, the architect's ruling for the first, routine for the rest). This supersedes M7's "a spread over 5 ms voids the run" (M7's *The live bench*, Concrete Steps, Interfaces' `spreadMs > 5`) and item 4 of M7's "as built" entry; those lines stand as the history of what was built.
  - **The void.** A carrier is void when its start's and its end's exchanges can't share one offset. Each exchange's interval `[sent − arrived − 1, received − at + 1]` must hold the machine's offset, so a steady offset lies at or above every lower edge and at or below every upper edge. Over both sets, the greatest lower edge less the least upper edge (`gapMs`) can be positive only by as much as the offset moved, or when a stamp is wrong; past 5 ms (`LIVE_BENCH.gapVoidMs`, formerly `spreadVoidMs`) the carrier is void, its reason giving the gap. The judged interval is unchanged: the hull of the start's and the end's narrowest intervals. `spreadMs` stays in the result as a recorded number and voids nothing. The guard on a delay under zero across the whole interval is unchanged.
  - **Why.** Each estimate is uncertain by half its own round trip, so the range of the estimates measures the path's jitter and asymmetry, not the clocks: on a path whose round trips vary by tens of milliseconds no run could pass it, and at the 2026-11-04 attempt it voided both carriers. The new rule never voids a steady offset whatever the jitter.
  - **What it can and can't see.** A wide interval moves neither edge, so a slow or held exchange neither voids a run nor hides a step. A move smaller than the fastest exchanges' round trips (plus the 5 ms) goes unseen; when it runs one way it stays inside the hull, which holds the offset at both ends, so the judgement still holds. A move that goes out and comes back between the two sets is invisible to any rule that reads only the ends, as it was before. Requiring each set's narrowest interval to meet the other's within the tolerance adds nothing: the gap over every exchange is at least that pair's.
  - **The two codes in one run.** The owner's app runs one pairing or enrollment at a time (`src/main/org/org-session.ts`, each one's `busy`); a pairing stops running once its approval is signed, once the held org state lists the device, when the sheet cancels it, when its sign-in ends, when the hub ends it, or when its code's 10 minutes pass (`src/main/org/pairing.ts`). So the enrollment code can only be made after the bench app is approved, while the bench read `codeFile` with no wait. The runner now waits, bounded, for each code file (`CODE_WAIT` in `test/support/remote-bench.ts`: the pairing file 10 minutes, one pairing code's life; the code file 30 minutes, inside the enrollment code's hour less the enroll's own 18 minutes), saying what the owner does meanwhile and how long is left; a file that never comes fails with that reason. `ENROLL_WAIT_S` (900 s from the key shown) is now the owner's time to approve, no longer the code's life (the app makes it for 3600 s).
  - **The operator's words.** Since P5 Decision Log 263 there is no `admin` verb on production: the bench's lines now name the app's sheets on the owner's Mac (Settings › Org › People, *Pair a device…* and *Remove device…*; Settings › Org › Machines, *Enroll a machine…* and *Drop…* with "Its key may be compromised" ticked), each approval with Touch ID. This document's Decision Log entry of 2026-11-02 that names `admin pair`, `admin enroll` and `admin machine drop` describes the sitting as it was then.
  - **Two small additions.** The precheck reads the VM's load too (`/proc/loadavg` through `devcloud vm ssh`, Rule B's 5, as the mini's), which closes the tracker row of 2026-11-05 and narrows the row of 2026-11-02 to the run's own samples. `MAWS_REMOTE_BENCH_REMOVE_APP=1` beside `MAWS_REMOTE_BENCH_REMOVE=1` removes the bench app's scratch profile on the runner (it holds the device's keys), checked as a scratch profile and refused while any process names it.
  Rationale: the architect's ruling of 2026-11-10 on the clock; the reading of the bench at master ed321a514; `src/main/org/pairing.ts`, `enroll.ts`, `timing.ts`; the renderer's sheets under `src/renderer/src/settings-tab/org/`.
  Date/Author: 2026-11-10, the bench-fit worker for the architect.

## Outcomes & Retrospective

**M1 (2026-10-28).** Acceptance 1 is met in tests, and nothing changes for the owner until O8's sign-in provides a production `OrgSession`.
- **What landed:**
  - main's RL2 link (`src/main/machines/remote/`) with the org session seam (`src/main/org/`);
  - the manager's second source of links, `Machines.remote`, and `machines.list`'s `carrier` and `refusal`.
- **Proof in tests:**
  - the unit suites under `src/main/machines/remote/`, `src/main/org/dev-session.test.ts` and the extended manager suite;
  - a real endpoint over the direct carrier (`test/machines/remote-link.test.ts`, 13);
  - the hub's multiplex in Miniflare (`test/machines/remote-link.local.test.ts`, 4): up over the relay; a relay drop with an act that runs once and a subscription that loses and repeats nothing; the probe's switch to direct; the hub away across a relaunch.
- **Where the build departed from the plan, each in the Decision Log:**
  - the five findings the architect raised before the gates;
  - the four interface additions;
  - the ack-timeout query;
  - reviewer-high's three trust and expiry fixes;
  - the test-race fix the first gate run exposed.
- **Retrospective:**
  - The plan's tables held: every row of the transition table became a test as written. The fixes were all at the edges the tables left implicit: transport words reaching a sealed-close listener; time as an input to trust; a timer that a lost channel cleared.
  - For later milestones: treat every `await` between reading the session and stamping trust as a window for a stale view, and give every promise the API calls "never pending" a deadline that no transport event clears.
  - The endpoint's loopback handshake limit and the eslint import boundary both cost a round. Each is now a harness option and a scoped exception, recorded for O9's suites, which will meet both.
**M2 (2026-10-31).** Acceptance 2's conversation half (stream with deltas, send, steer, interrupt, approve and deny, answer, an image), 5 (two clients), 8 (the step-up sheet), 10 (the carrier hover) and 11 (a lost range) are met in tests and in `e2e:remote` on the hosted Macs; their live run is M7's.
- **What landed:** the machine's side of lane B over RL2 (the `machine` executor at RL2 1.1, both capability constants, placement through the spine's record bound to the live engine session, a `ClientRef` per channel released through to the hosts, M2's offer rows); the link's executor, size and send-by checks; main's adapter (`src/main/lane-b/`), composed in `src/main/app.ts`; the step-up sheet before Touch ID for acts and O8's state signing; the renderer's remote pane over a record, its overlay, the gap bar, the carrier hover and "Hub unreachable", the composer's remote send and in-memory drafts and read marks; `e2e:remote` (a real `mawsd` enrolled in a local org, the harness's fake sminos on RL10, A signed in through `MAWS_DEV_ORG`, once over the hub and once directly).
- **Proof:** the endpoint suites (`test/endpoint/targets.test.ts`, `lane-b-rows.test.ts`), the link's and the adapter's unit suites, `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts` against a real endpoint (16), the renderer's suites, and `e2e:remote` with the M2 e2e set on the hosted Macs; the runs are in the report.
- **Where the build departed from the plan:** every departure is a dated Decision Log entry: the handshake vectors pinned at the version they were recorded at; placement's fallback and its binding to the executed engine session; one 60 s bound per act across a resume (`ActRequest.sendBy`); a replay of a held session on a page's `session.get`; `connectShell` keeping a held session's status through a drop; `remote.gap`'s `at`; `session.events` paged by main; `remote.historyTooLarge`.
- **Open, owned by M4:** acceptance 12 (a parked session's disk window and an older page over the channel's limit).
- **Retrospective:**
  - Splitting the milestone into four layers with a review at each interface paid: each task review found real defects at its boundary (the authorization of one engine session through another's record, a resume giving a send a second minute, the gap's position through hydration), and the milestone review still found two that spanned layers (an exited row read as live, an unbounded read over a bounded channel). Read every "the local path does X" in a remote design for the bound the channel adds.
  - Gates caught what focused runs missed: the local Miniflare suites, the app-root suites that mock `app.ts`, and a sibling's file move breaking an e2e bundle. Brief executors to run every suite that imports what they touch, and run class 1a on each task's head rather than once at the end.
  - Building the e2e scene in a side worktree beside the renderer saved most of a round of hosted runs; its UI half merged cleanly because the renderer's selectors were stable.
**M4 (2026-11-01).** Acceptance 2's diff and file items, 4 (files, the editor, source control, uploads and the spawn on another machine) and 12 (a remote session's history in pages the channel carries) are met in tests and in `e2e:remote` on the hosted Macs; their live run is M7's.
- **What landed:**
  - the machine's services: `session.spawn` with `launchedBy` (written once, by its handler alone), `machine.op`'s sealed action, `files.upload` in parts, `session.events`' byte budget and `omitted`, the services' ledger settling an orphaned claim;
  - RL2 1.2 (`call.session`, feeds) and three capabilities (`rl2.call-session`, `rl2.feeds`, `rl2.events-budget`), each judged by the link at every transmission;
  - the endpoint's M4 rows, the `checkout` target rule and feed, step 6's guard over every spawn row, and lane B placement that tells a caller below the floor nothing;
  - main's adapter (acts, uploads, the stepped-up spawn as one attempt per tab, the checkout feed with its replay, history and `files.read` paged by the channel's bound, an idle stream that waits for its record, the list-wait);
  - the renderer (the session on path reads, uploads at Send, the machine menu, the omitted bar, greyed reveal) and `e2e:remote`'s M4 scenes.
- **Proof:** the endpoint suites, the services' suites, main's lane B and link suites, `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts` against a real endpoint, the renderer's suites, and `e2e:remote` on the hosted Macs; the runs are in the report.
- **Where the build departed from the plan:** every departure is a dated Decision Log entry: `ADDED_COLUMNS` for `launched_by`; an upload's action not kept; the spawn's extra field `malformed`; `rl2.events-budget`; placement narrowing O10's #414 entry; the record on the checkout feed; the attempt identity by tab; the list-wait.
- **Retrospective:**
  - Reconnects and peer changes were where every task's first build failed: each review round found a state that one channel's lifetime hid (a resend to a different peer, a hold restored twice, a start record latched for the wrong try, a page that died while it waited). For M3 and M6, brief every executor with that list as cases to test, and test transitions, not only states.
  - A rule stated as "never a second session" needed four rounds to hold, because each fix closed one path to a new op and the next review found another (an unknown, a reload, an expiry, a reload before the first answer). The invariant held only once identity, persistence and lifetime were designed together; state such invariants with their lifecycle up front.
  - Parallel executors in one worktree worked once each committed only its own paths and one file had one writer at a time; the controller sequencing a shared file (`remote.ts`) cost minutes and saved a tangled commit.
  - The MacBook's load (70 to 160) made broad local runs unreliable and slow; focused files locally and everything else on CI, as the architect ruled mid-milestone, is the brief from now on.

**M3 (2026-11-01).** Acceptance 2's terminal item and §5's port forwarding are met in tests and in `e2e:remote` on the hosted Macs; their live run is M7's.
- **What landed:**
  - the pty host's ledger (`pty.op`, `pty.opQuery`, `orphanAtOpen`), `pty.listFor` and `pty.replay`, `mawsd`'s seam guard on `pty.op`'s inner name and its relay of the pty host's state;
  - RL2 1.3: the stream kinds behind `rl2.streams` and the `pty-host` executor behind `rl2.executor.pty-host`; the endpoint's stream table (a pty plane replaying from the device's own offset, input at most once per `inputEpoch`; a tcp plane on the machine's loopback, `::1` after a refused `127.0.0.1`), `forward.open` and its forwards kept per device for 120 s, every stream decided and published in one step and re-authorized at each `caps` refresh;
  - main's half: the link's `openStream`, the terminal adapter (acts under the pty host's ledger, a stream reopened from its offsets with no new prompt, geometry, checkpoints in memory, `pty.hostState` from the link), the forwards (listeners bound after the grant, waiting connections bounded, a link's end closing them), the loopback rule (never this Mac's loopback for a remote session; five hosts forwardable), and the in-app browser's tabs for a remote session (an allow-list, address-keyed sessions, a forward kept current and opened again after a relaunch);
  - the renderer (re-adoption through `pty.listFor` bounded above main's budget, `hostReady` per machine, "[output lost]", forwarded links, the mark, the refused page) and `e2e:remote`'s M3 scenes.
- **Proof:** `test/endpoint/streams.test.ts` against a real channel and ledger, `src/pty-host/ops.test.ts`, `src/mawsd/daemon-pty.test.ts`, main's lane B, link, forwards and browser suites, `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts` against a real endpoint and a fake pty host, the renderer's terminal and browser suites, and `e2e:remote` (a shell in B's checkout, `pwd`, a reload re-attaching it; python's `http.server` opened from its `[::]` link in the in-app browser, marked); the runs are in the report.
- **Where the build departed from the plan:** every departure is a dated Decision Log entry: at-most-once input with `inputSent`; `streamRefused unavailable`; the `::1` fallback and five forwardable hosts; the in-app browser for a remote session (main's browser was not in the Touches); the shared pending bounds; a remote terminal released, not killed, when its session goes; `mawsd`'s pty host state relayed; the composition root is `app.ts`.
- **Retrospective:**
  - The tasks' boundaries held, but the defects lived in what crossed two of them: input offsets across an endpoint restart, a pty host's death reaching a device, a forward's grant seen by a second caller. Each task review judged its own side sound; the milestone review found every one. For M6, brief the milestone reviewer on cross-layer transitions first.
  - Concurrency at the endpoint took three review rounds: a re-check, a publication and a write each had an `await` between deciding and acting. The rule that held: the check and the effect in one synchronous step, and an unknown outcome settled by the invariant the person cares about (a keystroke never twice).
  - Running a task's review beside the next task's build saved hours and cost two small contract notes; the review's fixes stayed in the reviewed task's files.
  - A spec's Touches can miss a whole surface (main's browser): the renderer's executor found it by building against the real router, which a plan read alone would not have shown.

**M6 (2026-11-01).** Acceptance 9 (attention and search across machines) is met in tests and in `e2e:remote` on the hosted Macs; its live run is M7's.
- **What landed:**
  - the endpoint: the `each-session` target rule and §L's M6 rows (`attention.list`, `session.search`), each answer cut at send time to the sessions the caller holds `view` on at every placement the call saw; the `attention` feed (`src/endpoint/attention-feed.ts`): a snapshot, then each viewable session's items, its membership recomputed at every record change and new head, output authorized as it leaves; `session.search`'s `sessions` input, applied by the store before its limit (`WIRE_MINOR` 19);
  - main: one feed per RL2 link (`remote-attention.ts`), `remote.attention {machine, items, greyed}`, one predicate (`fed`) for who speaks for a machine's sessions, the badge and the notifier counting remote items (silent only in a first snapshot within 30 s of start, by this Mac's clock), search to each `up` machine with `host_unavailable` the one omission;
  - the renderer: a listed row stands in for a session with no record in Needs you, ⌘⇧J, the toolbar and the Overview; the remote partition greyed by main's word ("machine offline", "reconnecting"); remote items open through M2's opener; conversation search grouped by machine in the ⌘K palette;
  - `e2e:remote`'s M6 steps.
- **Proof:** `test/endpoint/feeds.test.ts`, `attention-feed.test.ts` and `each-session.test.ts` (the cut's races, the two-move and narrowing-placement repros, the 50-private-titles search); `src/main/lane-b/remote-attention.test.ts`, `remote.test.ts` and `src/main/attention/notifier.test.ts` (the feed's lifecycle against the real notifier); `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts` against a real endpoint; the renderer's attention, Overview and palette suites; `e2e:remote` (a permission on a session A never opened in Needs you and the dock badge, ⌘⇧J, the answer clearing both, a transcript-only hit under B, B's daemon stopped greying the item and dropping the badge); the runs are in the report.
- **Where the build departed from the plan:** every departure is a dated Decision Log entry: the feed in its own file; placement by the record alone; membership as §R reads it; the all-placements rule; `WIRE_MINOR` 19 and the `sessions` input, reversing the no-bump entry; `fed` as the one predicate; the Overview's listed cards (M6's opening line, beyond acceptance 9's wording).
- **Retrospective:**
  - M3's lesson held and was not enough: the per-task reviews passed each layer, and the milestone review found three cross-layer defects, then two in their fixes, then one in those. Each was one state read through two predicates (an authorization's placement read and not carried to the decision; a feed open and a feed fed). The rule that closed it: every reader of a state reads it through one predicate, and every read a decision rests on reaches the decision. Brief a fix wave with the predicate it must share, not only the defect.
  - A cap applied before an authorization filter is a silent false negative; any read that answers "at most N" must be narrowed to the caller before its limit.
  - A decision made before review (no `WIRE_MINOR` bump) was reversed by a finding; recording the reason it held made the reversal one entry rather than an argument.
  - Running the e2e scene's task beside the renderer's review saved the scene's hosted runs a round; the scene found no product defect, so the unit and harness layers carried the proof.

**M5a (2026-11-02).** M5a's half of acceptance 2's artifact item, acceptance 6's tools and A10's copy are met. Tests and `e2e:remote` on the hosted Macs prove it; the live runs are M7's.
- **What landed:**
  - the machine's services: `host.artifactOp`, an engine-host command for an artifact write with no session, running only `ARTIFACT_OP_COMMANDS` under `host.op`'s ledger. Each ledger keeps byte results in `json-bytes`' tagged form. `library.read` gains its export form and reads either byte kind's stored file;
  - the endpoint: the `artifact` target rule (`ArtifactRanks`), §L's M5a rows, and the session-safe projection at send time on every answer that names a session. Hidden sessions are omitted from lists, and a hidden actor reads `WITHHELD_SESSION`. The `artifacts` feed. Every act asks its decision whether it stands before it is handed on, and again after its branch's own waits;
  - main: each call takes its command's budget; `ArtifactMachines` records which machine each artifact is on; `remote.openCopy` and Save As write exact bytes or refuse; `artifacts.resync` is sent per feed opening;
  - the renderer: artifact-keyed invokes are routed to their machine; the Library has a machine menu; links open on their source's machine; the Artifacts tool and the session card are their session's machine's; Open externally copies; Reveal is greyed with the machine named; the Browser tool's notice reads `session.capabilities`;
  - `e2e:remote`'s M5a scenes.
- **Proof:**
  - `test/endpoint/artifact-rule.test.ts`, `artifact-rows.test.ts`, `artifact-projection.test.ts` and `feeds.test.ts`;
  - `src/engine-host/ops.test.ts`, through the real registration;
  - main's remote-artifacts, remote-copy, dispatch and socket-link suites;
  - the renderer's artifacts, library and viewer suites;
  - `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`;
  - hosted `e2e:remote`, with every M5a item proven.
- **Where the build departed from the plan:** each departure is a dated Decision Log entry:
  - `host.artifactOp` rather than an optional `context` on `host.op` (OX1);
  - `library.list` on the `each-session` target;
  - the projection and its sentinel;
  - the standing check made before every executor branch, which carries O10's lane A and executor code in the desk track's shape;
  - the known limits.
- **Retrospective:**
  - The first hosted run found what no unit test could: the real engine host registered the artifact writes where the new command's lookup didn't read, while the fake host registered them where it did. A milestone's first e2e is a test of its fakes, and the review after it was briefed to hunt any other fake-only path. It found none.
  - "Decide and act in one synchronous step" was not enough as a rule: the gate itself hands an act on a continuation later, so a branch that awaits nothing is already past its decision. The rule that held is "ask whether the decision stands immediately before the effect, after every wait including the framework's". Its last instance was inside lane A's own admission wait.
  - A machine owner's short cut is an admission rule, not a disclosure rule. Every place an owner is exempted must say which of the two it is.
  - Two executors in one worktree, each committing only its own paths, held for the whole milestone with no cross-commit.

**M5b (2026-11-02).** A remote session's pages render in this Mac's frame and its documents edit here, read-only for a `view` holder, with nothing typed lost across a drop. Tests and `e2e:remote` on the hosted Macs prove it; the live runs are M7's.
- **What landed:**
  - RL2's read open (`rl2.read-open`, RL2 1.5): a `streamOpen` with no `op`, authorized as a call through the stream rows (`artifacts.fetch` at `view`, `documents.open` at `view` or `manage` by effect), re-authorized at every record change, relink, caps refresh and certificate change; `browser.screencast` reserved for O17 M9;
  - the endpoint's `http` plane (the body within credit, the E3 hold across a snapshot's read) and `document` plane (frames both ways, a `view` open forwarding only step 1 and awareness, the provisional session withheld, a 64 MiB per-link budget on the host's rule), and an open document channel as a park need;
  - main: the device's read open with its receive credit enforced on every stream kind; the scheme's chain (embeds, placement, local, referrer on a local 404) with at most 8 bodies a link and the paused-media give-way; downloads from any machine, never cut, checked whole; the document bridge, `edit` then `view`, bounded as the host bounds its socket;
  - the renderer: a read-only editor on `readOnly`, a remote replica's liveness by `remote.session`, an open its owner can cancel, a refusal for good shown in place of the editor;
  - `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`'s five cross-layer cases and `e2e:remote`'s M5b scene.
- **Proof:** `test/endpoint/read-open.test.ts`; `src/main/artifacts/remote-scheme.test.ts` and `remote-open.test.ts` through the real device stream table and chain; `src/artifacts/provider/frame-stream.test.ts`; the renderer's host, tab and editor suites; `src/mawsd/daemon-seam.test.ts`'s park case; `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`, each case shown to rest on the product by a mutation; hosted `e2e:remote`.
- **Where the build departed from the plan:** each a dated Decision Log entry: the refusal words a read open answers; the stream base shared by the planes; the responder never writing `ArtifactMachines` (reversed from the task's first build); the media give-way and the slot held until a body drains; the E3 hold; the reopen in the renderer, not behind the bridge's port; the frame cap at the host's 64 MiB and every bound on the host's own rule; the read-only store refused `read-only`.
- **Retrospective:**
  - The task reviews found what each layer got wrong, and the milestone review found where a layer's bound wasn't the host's: a frame cap, a queue rule, a store's status, a memory count. Matching the real host's rule (and reading its code for it) closed each, where a bound of our own reopened the same failure at a new size.
  - Three executors in one worktree held with explicit-path staging, but two edits of one file (the framing move during a fix, a test helper during another's fix) each needed a hand-off; the controller sequencing them by file was cheap, and splitting a fix wave by file kept it so.
  - A fake wiring differently from the real host struck again (`FakePtyHost`'s frames); briefing the scene's executor to build through the real link, endpoint and chain found it before the hosted run did.

**M7's code (2026-11-02).** The bench and its one product change are built; the numbers are not yet measured.
- **What lands:** `pong`'s endpoint clock behind `rl2.pong-clock` (RL2 1.6) and the link's stamps; the measured group `e2e:remote-latency` and its hosted twin `e2e:remote-latency-check`; the live mode `e2e:remote-latency-live` with its runner and bench agent for a scratch machine on the mini (over ssh) and on the acceptance VM (over `devcloud vm ssh`), and its `prepare-vm` step.
- **Proof:** the focused unit suites of the link, the seam, the endpoint channels, the latency library, the runner (real `sh` and `perl` runs of the guard, the deadlines and the reap, a production-shaped host left untouched) and the agent; class 1a and hosted `e2e:remote` with `e2e:remote-latency-check` green on the landing head. The measured group's numbers wait for CI-opt's mini window and the live numbers for the owner's sitting; acceptance 2, 4, 5, 8, 9, 10 and 11 hold live only once the sitting's checklist is ticked here.
- **Retrospective:**
  - A bench that drives the owner's shared machines is product code for review purposes: four review rounds each found a real way for it to escape its scratch profile, outlive its window or leave a process behind, and none of them would have shown before the sitting.
  - The desk track's conditions were cheapest as code with a fakes test each, not as operator instructions: every one of them is now a refusal the runner makes by itself.

**M5c (2026-11-03).** O4's surfaces work for another machine's artifacts: a remote dashboard's connector widgets refresh here under the grade the owner answers, its snapshots stay held while shown, Send to exports and delivers on the machine, and Connect and Sign in complete through this Mac's loopback with the consent relayed over RL2. Tests and `e2e:remote` on the hosted Macs prove it; the live M5c item is a follow-up after M5c is deployed, not a sitting item.
- **What landed:**
  - RL2's client port (`rl2.client-port`: `client` and `clientAnswer`) and the dashboard's snapshot holds (`rl2.asset-holds`), RL2 1.7 at the merge over M7's 1.6; `WIRE_MINOR` 22 (`delivery.send` retagged `machine`, the `callback` fields, `connectors.callback`, `machine.delivery`, `connectors.serversChanged.ended`);
  - the endpoint: §L's M5c rows; leases, run ids and holds kept per channel with generations and bounds (64, 256, 256), re-decided at every standing move and released at the channel's end; a send pending from its receipt so a cancel made meanwhile is honoured; acquisitions bounded at 8 s under the device's 10 s; the relay to the asking device's port;
  - the machine: the relayed OAuth receiver keyed by the asking client and port, `delivery.send` exporting then delivering, the broker waking a routeless watch at a grant;
  - main: the device's port answers and the callback listener (331 s and 311 s from acceptance, 16 a machine, only `content-type` and `cache-control` written back), `MACHINE_CALLS`, the per-machine gate and reset, window reports per machine, the dashboard's holds routed by the router's target;
  - the renderer: the per-machine connectors client and Connectors sheet (Show, then Touch ID, then Refresh for any re-read), the greying by the two lists, `notOffered` words, Customize kept to this Mac;
  - `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`'s M5c cases and `e2e:remote`'s M5c scenes, with a real observer through the replay seam's `live` entry.
- **Proof:** `test/endpoint/client-port.test.ts`, `artifact-rows.test.ts`, `asset-holds.test.ts` and `leases.test.ts`; `src/services/connectors/relayed-connect.test.ts`, `oauth-flow.test.ts`, `sign-in.test.ts`, `broker.test.ts` and `owner-delivery.test.ts`; `src/main/lane-b/remote-port.test.ts`, `remote-connectors.test.ts` and `src/main/dashboard/holds.test.ts`; the renderer's sheet, Customize and live-pause suites; `test/lane-b/remote.test.ts`; class 1a and the six hosted groups green on each fix wave's head, and the landing gate in the PR.
- **Where the build departed from the plan:** each a dated Decision Log entry: the listener's time from acceptance and its end at `serversChanged.ended`; lease commands never resent and gated per machine until the reset; the holds as a new capability (the architect's ruling (a)), routed by the router's target; the broker's wake for the `dashboard-live-data` race; the real observer; review 1's three fixes (the pending send, the acquisition bound, the Refresh gesture) and the narrow pass's clock check.
- **Retrospective:**
  - The lessons of M5a and M5b bound every brief, and the reviews still found the same class each round: a resource a peer can hold that one path forgets (a late acquisition, a cancel before its send, a stalled event loop past a deadline). Naming that class as a review lens found each before it shipped; writing every acquisition as one decision on one clock is what closed it.
  - The scene's first observer was a replay that could not verify a source, and a correct product refusal looked like a test failure; asking which layer is right before fixing the test saved a wrong fix.
  - A flaky line in an unrelated-looking spec was a product race older than this work; treating it as red until explained found it.

**M10's code (2026-11-05).** The bench is fit for a live run, and the sitting's three defects are fixed in tests and `e2e:remote` on CI. Their live re-check is the owner's minute after the PR lands.
- **The bench.** It reads the key's flat answer. It sees the listing on demand (`mawsd endpoint sync`). It holds one status client per run (the agent's `watch`). It ends the mini's hosts after mini-ops' stop. It refuses a loaded runner or mini as a recorded outcome.
- **The defects.**
  - A: a remote session ranks by its latest activity.
  - B: a spawn on a machine the member owns signs without Touch ID.
  - C: a terminal session's thinking row ticks.
- **Not proved here.**
  - The live bench run, which is carried.
  - The read-on-screen case of A: a read remote session ranks behind unread local ones, by parity.
  - A textless thinking block's final duration, which is residue.

**M10's sitting (2026-11-05).** M8's items and M9's spawn held live on both machines, with one failure, acceptance 5's row. Its cause is fixed by A below, and so is the "Thinking · 0s" the owner found (C). O7 closes on the owner's one-minute live re-check of A and C after M10's PR lands, recorded as an addendum here.
- **Setup.**
  - About 05:24Z to 06:00Z, from the MacBook against the mini and the fresh VM dc-acceptance-e071 (GCE us-west1-b). The VM was certified at boot under devcloud's `dc-` delegation, so it took no enrollment code.
  - All three machines ran master 5f2bc8964 (wire 3.27). The desk track swapped the MacBook and mini-ops swapped the mini, both on the owner's "Swap now".
  - The mini kept its MAWS project registered and trusted. On the VM the project was unregistered, and a detached worktree at `~/m10/MAWS` was left untrusted for the trust card; the original checkout was trusted at boot.
- **Passed:**
  - **Route naming.** The mini: "The machine's own · ClaudeUsage relay · http://127.0.0.1:8641". The VM: "The machine's own · mac-mini.tail112c3f.ts.net:8641", since its managed settings route through the mini's gateway.
  - **A MAWS ask answered from the MacBook:** opened 05:28:57.825Z, answered 05:29:05.214Z.
  - **Working cleared after a tool-using turn** (`turn.ended` 05:29:09.675Z).
  - **`/btw` answered.**
  - **The Subagents, Workflows and Tasks panels loaded,** each "none" rather than an error.
  - **A PDF read directly as a document.**
  - **An untracked file shown as added in Review.**
  - **The VM's remote folder sheet registered the project** (78c24e64).
  - **The to-human message tool worked in a VM session** (#466's managed scope).
  - **The MCP panel's Sign in on Notion (the VM) completed.** A Notion search returned the owner's page, and Settings › Connectors showed Notion connected.
- **Failed: acceptance 5's row.** Both spawned sessions (cb9e796a on the mini, cf463a17 on the VM) were absent from the MAWS row even with Show more, which listed rows 1 and 2 weeks old. Both were in Resume…. The cause is a remote session ranked by a stale activity time; fix A in M10's Decisions.
- **Not reached live:**
  - the trust card: the owner picked the already-trusted original checkout, so it is covered in e2e only;
  - Touch ID on Connectors' Show: not reported;
  - the concurrent second client: e2e only;
  - the live bench: carried, since its fixes were not landed at the sitting.
- **Observations:**
  - The VM's spawn took about 5 s to start, because its engine host woke from parked (05:40:54Z).
  - The owner found "Thinking · 0s" not ticking (fix C).
  - The owner's rulings at the sitting: no Touch ID for a spawn on the member's own machines (fix B); message reach as board #477; connectors switchable as board #476.

**M10's re-check and O7's close (2026-11-05).** The desk track swapped the MacBook to master 01c0b239c at 10:13Z on the owner's "Swap when ready"; the mini stayed on 5f2bc8964, since each item reads the MacBook's build.
- **B passed.** A "+ Session" on the mini asked no Touch ID (the owner: "yes, didn't ask for touchID").
- **A passed.** The owner watched the first turn end on screen, closed the tab, and the row stayed under MAWS, under the fresh-unread order (follow-up D, PR #480).
- **C passed live, with one fault fixed after.** The live label counted up, and closed blocks read "Thought for 5s", "4s" and "7s". Blocks whose text came in one burst read "Thought for 0s": a row started at its first delta, not its `content_block_start`. Follow-up C (PR #482) marks the start with an empty thinking delta and reads a figure under a second as "Thought". It reaches each machine with its next build.
- **Carried.** A terminal session's live label starts at the bridge's first thinking chunk (the engine's hook contract types no block's start); the live remote bench, never run against a real machine. Each is a tracker row.
- **O7 closes here:** M1 to M10 landed, each recorded above.

**M9 (2026-11-05).** Every machine spawns on its own terms, in tests, `e2e:remote` and `cross-build` on CI. The live proof is M10's sitting on real machines.
- **The machine route.** A machine's sessions default to `machine`: nothing injected or blanked, the machine's own settings decide the inference, and what it reaches is named (ClaudeUsage, the Anthropic login, or a host), never a mismatch. Proved in `launch.test.ts` and `session.test.ts` over the three fixtures, and in `e2e:remote`'s M9 case, whose machine reports its login.
- **Older peers.** The route travels as additive properties beside the legacy fields (`WIRE_MINOR` 27; RL2 unchanged at 1.9; `cross-build` finds no breaking change against 3.26 or 3.20). An older engine host gets the legacy route only where it is provably the same inference, and otherwise refuses `route-unsupported`; the resolution fails closed on any settings source it does not model. Proved against the real 3.20 host by `machineRouteOnOlderHost` (with `--peer 95050efd1`).
- **Acceptance 5's spawn on a machine with no setup.** The owner sets the project up on the machine from this one: the folder, its trust, then the registration, with nothing registered and no Touch ID asked before the trust answer; protected folders open only on consent, the asking member's own. Before trust no git runs in the folder but one isolated config read, and nothing outside it is read but through the checked resolver. Proved in `e2e:remote`, `setup-rows.test.ts` (through production's Index callback, in a fake home) and the setup reads' differential tests against git.
- **The renderer.** Features key on what the machine reports; the default route is a setting; the route switch reaches all three routes, its return to the machine's own held to the relay check.
- **Not proved here.** The live sitting (M10). The unmodelled engine settings sources, a remote "Start without trusting", the relay question before the stop and a pinned older-host cross-build peer are tracker lines.
- **Retrospective:**
  - The security boundary drew most of the rounds: the setup reads went from git with a hardened environment to direct reads of git's files, an object reader came and went for an index reader, and each round's reviewer found a path the last missed (an include, a lexically resolved pointer, the global config's includes, a shared consent). A probe in a fake home was the evidence each time; the rounds converged once every finding became a regression case.
  - Per-task reviews miss seams by construction. The whole-milestone review's six findings were all between tasks (a hook reading the wrong machine, a field one layer added and the next ignored), which is what it is for; the architect's bounded re-review with a stated lens then closed it in one round.
  - The wire generator refuses to fold into a written minor, so each additive change mid-branch took a new minor that the landing collapses; doing the collapse once, from master's manifest, was cheaper than fighting it.
  - Three executors in one worktree held together with fast-forwards and own-path commits; the cost was a few false `MERGE_RR.lock` exits and one push that had to wait for another executor's.


**M8 (2026-11-04).** The sitting's failures are removed in tests and `e2e:remote` on CI. Their live re-check is M10's.
- **Acceptance 4.** A just-spawned remote session leads its group under the pins and is always shown while its first turn runs. Resume… reaches listed sessions, other members' only under People. Proved in `derive.test.ts`, `resume/model.test.ts` and `e2e:remote`.
- **Acceptance 5.** A second attached app sees and opens the session by parity. An ask answered on one app settles on the other. One client's unfollow, or its quitting, leaves the other client's follow held. Proved in `e2e:remote` with two apps, read from B's follow log.
- **Acceptance 2 and 9.** The MAWS ask is answered from another Mac. `/btw`, Reload plugins and the widgets complete across the link, an operation's completion surviving a link drop exactly once. Proved in `remote-session.test.ts` and `e2e:remote`.
- **Acceptance 6.** Every session command crossing a link is offered, declared unavailable, or answered by main, held by `offer-coverage.test.ts`. A declared control greys with its reason, for lane A and lane B over RL2.
- **The rest.**
  - A remote PDF reaches the model as a `document` block (`rl2.upload-blocks`).
  - Sign in from the MCP widget.
  - Untracked files in Review.
  - "No jobs" on a machine without a schedule.
  - A forwarded failure's message.
  - An unreadable event as a gap that re-reads and heals the turn.
- **Not proved here.** Acceptance 5's 5 s bound is measured live at M10. Remote PDF steering into a running turn is not parity with local (a tracker line).
- **Retrospective:**
  - A diagnosis pass before any fix paid for itself. m8-1 withdrew the plan's id-mismatch premise: the row was cut by the cap. Without that pass, M8 would have built a join for a mismatch that didn't exist.
  - Lifecycle code drew the most review rounds: reopen and backoff, operation reconciliation, hydration order, the lead's end conditions. Each round found a real interleaving the last one missed. The rounds converged once each fix came with a reproduction ported into the suite.
  - Running disjoint tasks side by side in one worktree worked: fast-forward before each commit, and commit only your own paths. It cost only harmless ref-lock messages.
  - A product seam for observability (the follow log) was the cheapest way to make a multi-client authorization property provable end to end.

**M7's live runs (2026-11-04).** The measured acceptance is CI's: `e2e:remote-latency` on the mini, run 41573992251 (master bf6766af7, the direct-resume fix of PR #457; 2026-11-03 03:44Z; valid; the mini's load 3.26/4.33/3.69 at its start), every figure within item 3's bounds against the same run's local numbers:

| Carrier | delta-to-frame p50 / p95 | round trip p50 / p95 | open to first row | resume | link rtt p50 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| local | 25.2 / 37.8 ms | 1.3 / 1.7 ms | 107.9 ms | (engine start 179.1 ms) | |
| direct | 22.7 / 36.0 ms | 2.2 / 2.7 ms | 131.9 ms | 49.9 ms (engine start 770.0 ms) | 0.0 ms |
| relay | 23.2 / 35.6 ms | 3.2 / 4.9 ms | 522.2 ms | 80.8 ms (engine start 770.0 ms) | 1.0 ms |

The live sitting ran from the MacBook (01d4488cf; 02de6c88c for S8) against the mini and the acceptance VM `dc-acceptance-bd31` (both on 75944744d), the owner driving, from S1 at 04:01:48Z to about 06:30Z. Its running record is the desk's `sitting-record.md`; this entry is its verdict.

- **The live bench: attempted, voided by the runner's load.** `mini-bench` ran twice. Attempt 1 (04:10:39Z) stopped at the key step: the bench read no machine key within 60 s while the enrollment was `hub-confirmed`, and the owner declined. Attempt 2 (05:02:19Z to 05:05:37Z, after an enroll outside the runner) measured and was invalid on five counts: the clock spread (199.5 ms direct, 172.5 ms relay, over 5 ms), the renderer's clock bracket on both carriers, and Rule B on the runner, the MacBook's own `os.loadavg()` peaking at 97.7 (30 s pair 125.5) from other sessions' local test runs; the mini's own load stayed under 5 (3.87 at the start, 3.44 at the end), and its link was impaired (0.36 MB/s) all night. Its figures, void: direct round trip p50 73.8 ms, open 1,275 ms, resume 206 ms (bound 565), rtt p50 34 ms; relay round trip p50 119 ms, open 3,430 ms (over 1,500), resume 323 ms (bound 638), rtt p50 107 ms. `vm-bench` and the approved mini rerun were dropped at 05:23Z: with the heavy runs stopped, the MacBook still sat at a load of about 20 from its live sessions, so Rule B voids any bench run from it until it is quieted (Decision Log, 2026-11-04).
- **The checklist** (Concrete Steps' twelve items; the acceptance items they prove in brackets):

| # | Item | Mini | VM | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | an hour-idle session: history, a send resumes it, deltas [2] | not reached: the S1 session had no row on the MacBook | a fresh session streamed end to end and "Working…" cleared | partial |
| 2 | send, steer, interrupt [2] | pass ("didn't stall, in time") | not rerun (same path) | pass |
| 3 | approve and deny; answer a question [2] | approve pass; the question fails from the MacBook ("Couldn't read this session's questions", then "Couldn't deliver the answers"); answered from the mini's own app it works | approve and deny pass; the question fails the same way | fail (questions) |
| 4 | paste an image; drop a PDF [2] | both pass (the image sent in about 4 s; the PDF uploaded to the mini and read), but the attachment is not an `@` reference | image pass | pass, with a finding |
| 5 | two apps on one session [5] | the S1 session was not found in the mini's app at first and had no row on the MacBook; on the fresh session, an answer in the mini's app cleared the MacBook's count at once; streaming in both is unobservable on these sessions (the to-human output style sends a reply as one tool call) | | partial |
| 6 | the hover; Wi-Fi off [10] | "direct, tailnet" (the MacBook off its LAN, the tailnet itself over a Tailscale relay) | "direct via tailnet" (36 ms) | hover pass; Wi-Fi off skipped by the owner |
| 7 | open and edit a file; an edit on the machine shows here [2] | pass (the MacBook's edit reached the agent; the agent's append appeared live in the open editor) | not rerun | pass, with a finding (no diff for an untracked file) |
| 8 | a terminal in the worktree; `pwd` [2] | pass (a file only the mini's checkout holds) | pass (`uname -s` Linux) | pass |
| 9 | a dev server's link in the in-app browser [2] | opened in the in-app browser (the forwarded mark not reported) | not rerun | pass |
| 10 | "+ Session" on another machine: the sheet, Touch ID, the row within 5 s [4, 8] | the sheet and Touch ID passed and the session opened in the pane, but no sidebar row on the MacBook, twice (S1 and a fresh spawn) | the same (S1's VM half, after its route was set) | fail (the row) |
| 11 | a permission with no tab: Needs you, the badge [9] | the badge and Needs you listed it; answered in the mini's app, the MacBook's count dropped at once; answering from the MacBook fails (item 3's cause) | | pass, but for the answer here |
| 12 | lid closed 2 min: the stream resumes within 1 s [P5 acceptance 7] | skipped by the owner | | not run |

  Not exercised live: acceptance 2's artifact items (M5) and 11 (a lost range), and 6's capability lists beyond what the side panels showed.
- **S8, M5c on another machine (02de6c88c, about 06:25 to 06:30Z).** The surface the owner reached was the session's MCP widget, not M5c's sheet: it showed Notion `needs-auth` with only Reconnect, which is `session.mcpReconnect`, the engine's reconnect, and started no sign-in; its read asked no Touch ID, as a `view` call does. M5c's Connectors sheet (Show, then Touch ID: `connectors.servers`, `machine-admin`) and Settings › Connectors' Sign in (`connectors.signIn`, `machine-admin`) were not reached, nor the Notion send.
- **What the sitting found** (each in Surprises & Discoveries, 2026-11-04): a remote-spawned lane B session gets no row in the spawning Mac's sidebar, and once its tab closes nothing reopens it; questions on a MAWS-launched session can't be read or answered from another Mac, because `maws.asks.list` and `maws.ask.answer` aren't offered over RL2; the session's side panels call more commands the offer table lacks (`fs.subagents`, `fs.workflows`, `fs.tasks`; `session.reloadPlugins` sent as a call against its act row) and show the refusal rather than a greyed state, and the scheduled-jobs panel's `files.read` answers `internal`; a tool-using turn on the VM left its activity at `requesting`; `/btw` on a remote session never answers; an attachment isn't an `@` reference; the MCP widget offers no sign-in for a remote server that needs one; and the bench's own findings (below).
- **Retrospective:**
  - A live bench measures the runner as much as the machine: Rule B reads the MacBook, and the MacBook carries the owner's whole fleet of sessions. The measured group's one machine and one clock, on a quiet CI Mac, is where the numbers belong; a live bench needs its runner quieted and checked before its slot, which a sitting can't assume.
  - The checklist found more than the scenes did because the owner's real sessions differ from the scenes' in ways no fake models: the MAWS ask in place of the native one, the to-human output style, a project that is only discovered, a machine whose route is the gateway with no relay behind it. Each was one step away from a scene that would have found it; the next live proof should start from the owner's real session configuration.
  - The sitting ran two and a half hours where the plan said one; the bench's time went into an enrollment done by hand. The checklist items themselves took about forty minutes.
